# Daniel

## 1:1 Introduction
[Philipp Melanchthon, In Danielem Prophetam Commentarius (Leipzig, 1543). English: machine translation, The Faith Received (Mere Orthodoxy); the original follows each comment.]
ARGUMENT OF THE BOOK OF DANIEL.

Daniel has embraced many great things, useful and worthy of the consideration of good minds, which the narrowness of our talent certainly cannot unfold and explain in their entirety. Let the studious reader know that he has made much progress if he has moderately observed the principal chapters, and has learned to transfer them to pious exercises, and has seen which part instructs minds concerning true invocation, which part confirms faith, which refutes the Jews, which preaches concerning repentance, and concerning certain virtues. For it is most useful to refer the narratives to these topics.

I shall therefore review, as it were, the titles of the principal matters which are contained here, which will show what the utility of this reading is.

FIRST you will think about the entire work. The whole history of Daniel is a testimony of the Church being preserved and adorned. with immense glory, then when it seemed almost extinguished. He therefore warns that the Church is both to be chastised, and to be restored again on the cross. He likewise testifies that the Church has been defended and propagated from the very beginning not by human counsels, strengths, or ordinations, but by the singular help of God.

He warns likewise concerning promises, that God performs his promises, even if he does not perform them in the way that we had thought. As the promise was made to the tribe of Judah, The scepter shall not be taken away, nor the teacher from Judah, until the Savior shall have come. Relying on this promise, the kings of Judah rebelled so many times, and most audaciously condemned the prediction of Jeremiah concerning the overthrow of the kingdom as if it were fighting against the manifest promise of God, but Jeremiah knew both, that it would be that the people would be chastised, and yet the royal family would not be extinguished, nor would prophets be lacking.

And so it happened beyond the opinion of men, the Royal family was affected with honor even in exile, and God raised up prophets, and adorned His Church with immense glory. Indeed, He added the Gentiles to the society of the Church. A notable conversion of the Gentiles to God was made.

SECONDLY, you will observe the testimonies concerning the Messiah. As the other prophets were especially raised up for this, that the promise of the Messiah might sound through them, so also it was fitting that Daniel perform this duty, who indeed teaches many things, and prophesies concerning the time in which it was fitting for the Messiah to be born during the Jewish polity. He explicitly prophesies concerning the passion of Christ, he says it will come to pass that He is killed.

THIRDLY, you will consider the series of Monarchies, which was handed down for this reason, that the time might be indicated in which God willed Christ to be born, and the time of the resurrection of the dead and of the judgment might be signified.

FOURTHLY, the places of doctrine concerning repentance, concerning faith, concerning justification are to be observed.

FIFTHLY, examples of good and evil kings are to be extracted.

SIXTHLY, testimonies concerning the resurrection of the dead are to be considered.

SEVENTHLY, a forewarning is delivered concerning the impious kingdoms, which around the end of the world will attempt to destroy the Gospel of God. And the mournful dispersion of the Church is described, as now under the Turks and other kings, who with great cruelty defend idols, the pious are dispersed, embracing the pure doctrine of the Gospel. In such great confusion of nations there are many disputes of human reason, Whether there will be a distinction of nations or of men after this life, Whether God is about to distribute rewards and punishments to men, whether there is a certain group of men, whom God loves, the rest of men being rejected.

Epicurus denies that the souls of men survive. Other philosophers think that souls are ethereal flames, if they are anything foreign to the matter of the elements, flying off into the sublime, I know not where. Against these profane imaginations let us learn from the Gospel that the Son of God will truly resurrect the dead, so that He may adorn the pious with eternal rewards, and afflict the impious with punishments. And a famous testimony of this doctrine has been published, the resurrection of the dead; that sign is peculiar and proper to that assembly which professes the doctrine handed down through the prophets, Christ, and the Apostles. Nor has anyone else recalled the dead to life, except the professors of that doctrine. It is certain, therefore, that this doctrine is divine, which indeed alone is the first of all in the world. And this pronounces the Church or the people of God to be those who embrace this doctrine. are, and that the others are not. He teaches also that the Church is not the greatest multitude of men, but rather is often a small and miserable assembly, oppressed by servitude and many other calamities, and that empires and riches are often in the hands of the impious and the enemies of God. Since, however, this appearance offends human reason, which, ignorant of the wrath of God against sin inherent in the nature of men, dreams that those are rather dear to God to whom He gives riches and empires, therefore there is great need for us of this forewarning, which teaches how one ought to think about the Church of God, where it is to be sought, namely only in that assembly which embraces the doctrine handed down through the prophets, Christ, and the Apostles. Let not empires, not riches, not a multitude, not the length of time move you to think that those are the people of God who either [reject] the entire prophetic and Apostolic doctrine they cast away, or openly and pertinaciously defend idols.

I have briefly reviewed these chapters, so that the studious reader may see immediately in the vestibule that great and useful matters are handed down in this history. Many other places of Christian doctrine are also added incidentally. And prudence must be applied in distributing and using these matters, seeing which part contains laws, which preaches about promises and about the Gospel. For it is useful for all materials to be referred to these first chapters. It must be seen which part illustrates dogmas, as, what can be said more illustriously ἐναργέστερον against the Jews, than that Daniel explicitly affirms that Christ must be born while the Mosaic polity is enduring? Therefore that horrendous destruction of the city of Jerusalem, and the ruins of all Judea, now for 1470 years bear witness that Christ has been born, and they refute the madness of the Jews. and execrable obstinacy. We must also see what we can transfer to use in invocation. Daniel prays thus, he acknowledges sins, and seeks liberation from God with confidence in mercy, and specifically adds, FOR THE LORD'S SAKE, namely for the sake of the promised Messiah.

This is the true form of prayer in the Church of God, to approach God with confidence in the mercy promised for the sake of the Son, the mediator and intercessor. This invocation is a secret thing and unknown to the world, but it must be learned by the pious who truly fear God in their daily exercises, and it is profitable to have this form proposed in Daniel in sight, as will be said more copiously in its place. There is use in the dangers of these times and warnings, which show what the Church is, and command to avoid the society of those who attempt to blot out the name of Christ, or defend idols against the Gospel.

Latin:

ARGUMENTUM LIBIRI DANIELIS.

Multas res magnas, utiles & dignas cogitatione bonarum mentium complexus est Daniel, quas evolvere & explicare omnes ingenii nostri angustia certè non potest. Multum se profecisse studiosus lector sciat, si praecipua capita mediocriter animadverterit, & ad pia exercitia transferre didicerit, ac viderit quae pars erudiat mentes de vera invocatione, quae pars confirmet fidem, quae refutet Iudaeos, quae concionetur de paenitentia, & de certis virtutibus. Nam ad hos locos referre narrationes, utilissimum est.

Recensebo igitur quasi titulos rerum praecipuarum quae hic continentur, qui ostendent quae sit utilitas huius lectionis.

PRIMUM cogitabis de universo opere. Testimonium est tota historia Danielis servatae Ecclesiae & ornatae ingenti gloria, tum cum videretur pene extincta. Monet igitur & castigari Ecclesiam, & in cruce rursus eam instaurari. Testatur item Ecclesiam non humanis consiliis, viribus, ordinationibus, sed singulari Dei ope inde usque ab initio defensam & propagatam esse.

Monet item de promissionibus, Deum praestare promissa, etiamsi non eo modo praestat, quem nos cogitaveramus. Ut tribui Iuda facta est promissio, Non auferetur sceptrum, nec doctor à Iuda, donec venerit Salvator. Hac freti promissione reges Iuda toties rebellabant, & Ieremiae praedictionem de eversione regni audacissimè damnabant tanquam pugnantem cum manifesta promissione Dei, sed Ieremias norat utrunque, fore, ut castigaretur populus, & tamen familia regia non extingueretur, nec deessent prophetae.

Atque ita accidit praeter hominum opinionem, Regia familia etiam in exilio, honore adfecta est, Et excitavit Deus prophetas, & suam Ecclesiam ingenti gloria ornavit. Imò adiunxit ad societatem Ecclesiae Gentes. Facta est conversio Gentium ad Deum insignis.

SECUNDO observabis testimonia de Messia. Ut caeteri prophetae praecipuè ad hoc excitati sunt, ut per eos sonaret promissio Messiae, ita & Danielem hoc munere fungi oportuit, qui quidem multa docet, vaticinatur de tempore quo nasci Messiam durante politia Iudaica oportuit. Disertê vaticinatur de passione Christi, ait fore, ut occidatur.

TERTIO considerabis seriem Monarchiarum, quae ideo tradita est, ut indicaretur tempus, quo Christum Deus nasci voluit, & significaretur tempus resurrectionis mortuorum ac iudicii.

QUARTO observandi sunt loci doctrinae de paenitentia, de fide, de iustificatione.

QUINTO exempla bonorum & malorum regum excerpenda sunt.

SEXTO testimonia de resurrectione mortuorum considerentur.

SEPTIMO praemonitio traditur de regnis impiis, quae circa finem mundi Evangelium Dei delere conabuntur. Et luctuosa dispersio Ecclesiae describitur, ut nunc sub Turcis & aliis regibus, qui magna crudelitate idola defendunt, dispersi sunt pii, doctrinam Evangelii puram amplectentes. In tanta gentium confusione multae sunt humanae rationis disputationes, An sit discrimen gentium seu hominum futurum post hanc vitam, An Deus sit praemia & poenas distributurus hominibus, an sit certus hominum coetus, quem Deus amet reiectis caeteris hominibus.

Epicurus negat superstites esse animas hominum. Alii Philosophi cogitant animas esse flammas aethereas, si quid sunt alienum à materia elementorum, evolantes in sublime, nescio quò. Adversus has prophanas imaginationes discamus ex Evangelio, verè filium Dei resuscitaturum esse mortuos, ut pios ornet aeternis praemiis, & impios poenis adficiat. Et testimonium huius doctrinae editum est illustre, resuscitatio mortuorum, id signum peculiare & proprium est eius coetus qui profitetur doctrinam traditam per prophetas, Christum & Apostolos. Nec quisque alius mortuos in vitam revocavit, nisi eius doctrinae professores. Certum est igitur hanc doctrinam esse divinam, quae quidem sola prima est omnium in mundo. Et haec pronunciat Ecclesiam seu populum Dei esse eos qui hanc doctrinam amplectuntur tuntur ctuntur, caeteros non esse. Docet item Ecclesiam non esse maximam hominum multitudinem, imò saepe exiguum & miserum coetum esse, oppressum servitute & aliis multis calamitatibus, saepe penes impios & hostes dei esse imperia & opes. Cum autem haec species offendat humanam rationem, quae ignara irae Dei adversus peccatum haerens in natura hominum, somniat illos potius Deo caros esse, quibus dat opes & imperia, ideo valde opus est nobis hac praemonitione, quae docet quomodo de Ecclesia Dei sentiendum sit, ubi sit quaerenda, videlicet tantum in eo coetu, qui amplectitur doctrinam traditam per prophetas, Christum & Apostolos. Non imperia, non opes, non multitudo, non temporum diuturnitas moveat, ut populum esse Dei existimes, eos qui aut universam doctrinam propheticam & Apostolicam abiiciunt, aut palàm & pertinaciter idola defendunt.

Haec capita breviter recensui, ut studiosus lector in vestibulo statim videat, res magnas & utiles in hac historia tradi. Accedunt obiter & alii multi loci doctrinae Christianae. Estque prudentia adhibenda in distribuendis rebus & usurpandis, videndum quae pars contineat leges, quae concionetur de promissionibus & de Evangelio. Nam ad haec prima capita utile est materias omnes referri. Videndum quae pars illustret dogmata, ut, quid potest illustrius ἐναργέστερον dici contra Iudaeos, quàm quòd Daniel diserte adfirmat Christum nasci oportere, durante politia Mosaica? Ergo illud horrendum excidium urbis Ierosolymae, & ruinae totius Iudaeae, iam annis 1470 testimonium dicunt, Christum natum esse, & refutant Iudaeorum amentiam & execrandam pertinaciam. Videndum etiam quae ad usum in invocatione transferre possimus. Daniel sic precatur, agnoscit peccata, & petit à Deo liberationem fiducia misericordiae, & nominatim addit, PROPTER DOMINUM, videlicet propter promissum Messiam.

Haec est vera precationis forma in Ecclesia Dei, accedere ad Deum fiducia misericordiae promissae propter filium mediatorem ac deprecatorem. Arcana res est & ignota mundo haec invocatio, Sed piis qui verè Deum timent, in quotidianis exercitiis discenda est, ac prodest in conspectu habere hanc formam in Daniele propositam, ut suo loco copiosius dicetur. Habent usum in horum temporum periculis & ̃monitiones, quae ostendunt quae sit Ecclesia, & iubent vitare societatem eorum qui nomen Christi delere conantur, aut idola contra Evangelium defendunt.

## 1:1 Argument
CHAPTER I.

In the third year of the reign of Jehoiakim king of Judah, Nebuchadnezzar king of Babylon came to Jerusalem and besieged it, and the Lord delivered Jehoiakim king of Judah into his hand, along with part of the vessels of the house of God, and he carried them away into the land of Shinar, into the house of his god, and he brought the vessels into the treasury house of his god. And the king said to Ashpenaz the master of the eunuchs, that he should bring in some of the children of Israel, and of the king's seed, and of the tyrants, boys in whom there was no blemish, handsome in appearance, and learned in all wisdom, cautious in knowledge, and taught in discipline, and who could stand in the king's palace, that he might teach them the letters and the language of the Chaldeans. And the king appointed for them a daily provision of his own food, and of the wine from which he himself drank, that being nourished for three years, afterwards they might stand in the presence of the king. There were therefore among them of the children of Judah, Daniel, Hananiah, Mishael, and Azariah. And the master of the eunuchs imposed names upon them: to Daniel, Belteshazzar: to Hananiah, Shadrach: to Mishael, Meshach: and to Azariah, Abednego. But Daniel proposed in his heart that he would not be defiled by the king's table, nor by the wine of his drink: and he asked the master of the eunuchs that he might not be contaminated. Now God gave Daniel grace and mercy in the sight of the prince of the eunuchs. And the prince of the eunuchs said to Daniel: I fear my lord the king, who has appointed your food and drink, who if he should see your faces more lean

OF THE PROPHET DANIEL

above the other youths of your own age, you will condemn my head to the king. And Daniel said to Malasar, whom the prince of the eunuchs had appointed over Daniel, Ananias, Misael, and Azarias: Test us, I beseech you, your servants for ten days, and let legumes be given to us to eat, and water to drink, and look upon our countenances, and the countenances of the boys who eat the king's food, and as you shall see, you shall do with your servants. Who, having heard a word of this kind, tested them for ten days. But after ten days, their countenances appeared better and fatter than all the boys who were eating the king's food. Furthermore, Malasar took away the provisions and the wine of their drink, and gave them legumes. But to these boys God gave knowledge and discipline in every book and wisdom: but to Daniel the understanding of all visions.

and of dreams. Therefore, when the days were completed, after which the king had said that they should be introduced, the chief of the eunuchs introduced them into the presence of Nebuchadnezzar. And when the king had spoken with them, there were not found such among them all as Daniel, Hananiah, Mishael, and Azariah, and they stood in the presence of the king. And every word of wisdom and understanding that the king inquired of them, he found in them ten times more than all the diviners and magicians who were in all his kingdom. Now Daniel was there until the first year of King Cyrus.

Latin:

CAPUT I.

ANNO TERTIO regni Ioacim regis Iuda, venit Nabuchodonosor rex Babylonis in Ierusalem & obsedit eam, & tradidit dominus in manu eius Ioacim regem Iudae, & partem vasorum domus Dei, & asportavit ea in terram Sennaar, in domum dei sui, & vasa intulit in domum thesauri Dei sui. Et ait rex Asphenez praeposito eunuchorum, ut introduceret de filiis Israel, & de semine regio, & tyrannorum, pueros in quibus nulla esset macula, decoros forma, & eruditos omni sapientia, cautos scientia, & doctos disciplina, & qui possent stare in palatio regis, ut doceret eos literas, & linguam Chaldaeorum. Et constituit eis rex annonam per singulos dies de cibis suis, & vino unde bibebat ipse, ut enutriti tribus annis, postea starent in conspectu regis. Fuerunt ergo inter eos de fili-s Iudae, Daniel, Ananias, Misael, & Azarias. Et imposuit eis praepositus eunuchorum, nomina: Danieli, Baltassar: Ananiae, Sidrach: Misaeli, Misach: & Azariae, Ab denago. Proposuit autem Daniel in corde suo, ne pollueretur de mensa regis, neque de vino potus eius: & rogavit eunuchorum praepositum ne contaminaretur. Dedit autem Deus Danieli gratiam & misericordiam in conspectu principis eunuchorum. Et ait princeps eunuchorum ad Danielem: Timeo ego dominum meum regem, qui constituit vobis cibum & potum, qui si viderit vultus vestros macilentiores

DANIELIS PROPHETAE

prae caeteris adolescentibus coaevis vestris, condemnabitis caput meum regi. Et dixit Daniel ad Malasar, quem constituerat princeps eunuchorum super Danielem, Ananiam, Misaelem, & Azariam: Tenta nos obsecro servos tuos diebus decem, & dentur nobis legumina ad vescendum, & aqua ad bibendum, & contemplare vultus nostros, & vultus puerorum qui vescuntur cibo regio, & sicut videris, facies cum servis tuis. Qui audito sermone huiuscemodi, tentavit eos diebus decem. Post dies autem decem, apparuerunt vultus eorum meliores & corpulentiores prae omnibus pueris qui vescebantur cibo regio. Porrò Malasar tollebat cibaria, & vinum potus eorum, dabatque eis legumina. Pueris autem his dedit Deus scientiam & disciplinam in omni libro & sapientia: Danieli autem intelligentiam omnium visionum

& somniorum. Completis itaque diebus, post quos dixerat rex ut introducerentur, introduxit eos praepositus eunuchorum in conspectu Nabuchodonosor. Cumque eis locutus fuisset rex, non sunt inventi tales de universis, ut Daniel, Ananias, Misael, & Azarias, & steterunt in conspectu regis. Et omne verbum sapientiae & intellectus quod sciscitatus est ab eis rex, invenit in eis decuplum super cunctos ariolos & magos, qui erant in universo regno eius. Fuit autem Daniel usque ad annum primum Cyri regis.

## 1:1 Topics Chiefly to Be Observed
TOPICS CHIEFLY TO BE OBSERVED

in the first chapter of Daniel.

I

THE PUNISHMENT of impiety, namely the huge calamity of the Jewish people.

II

That God wonderfully fulfills promises, and preserves His Church, even when He punishes the impious.

III

Concerning the moderation of victory. Also that it is the duty of kings to see to it that youth is instructed.

IIII

The faith and perseverance of Daniel and the three youths, that they did not fall away from the divine law among the Gentiles, nor were they corrupted by the examples, or the pleasures, or the admiration of the power of the impious.

V

Bodily and spiritual rewards for that faith, perseverance, and temperance. Better health was given, favor with the king and princes, learning and prophecy. These gifts happened to them, because God wished to testify that He preserves His The Church, and the promises are not void. This example also teaches that these duties of faith and confession are pleasing to God, and merit huge rewards.

VI

It must be observed to what end temperance ought to be destined. Daniel abstains from royal foods, not because of human traditions, but because of the law of God, because many royal foods were forbidden to the Jews, such as hares, blood, and many kinds of fish.

The use of wine was not forbidden, and Daniel is read below to have drunk wine. Therefore he did not then abstain from wine out of superstition, but because of his youth, and to avoid associations, etc.

Latin:

LOCI PRAECI-PUE OBSERVANDI

in primo capite Danielis.

I

POENA impietatis, videlicet ingens calamitas populi Iudaici.

II

Quòd deus mirabiliter praestat promissiones, & servat suam Ecclesiam, etiam cum punit impios.

III

De moderatione victoriae. Item quòd regum officium sit curare, ut ivuentus instituatur.

IIII

Danielis & trium adolescentum fides, & perseverantia, quòd non defecerunt à lege divina inter Ethnicos, nec vel exemplis, vel voluptatibus, vel admiratione potentiae impiorum depravati sunt.

V

Praemia corporalia & spiritualia pro illa fide, perseverantia & temperantia. Data est melior valetudo, gratia apud regem & principes, doctrina & prophetia. Haec dona eis contigerunt, quia deus testari volebat se servare suam Ecclesiam, & promissiones non esse irritas. Docet item hoc exemplum deo placere haec fidei & confessionis officia, & mereri ingentia praemia.

VI

Observandum est ad quem finem debeat esse destinata temperantia. Daniel abstinet à regiis cibis, non propter humanas traditiones, sed propter legem dei, quia multi regii cibi interdicti erant Iudaeis, ut lepores, sanguis, et multa piscium genera.

Vini usus non erat prohibitus, & Daniel infra legitur bibisse vinum. Non ergo tum superstitione à vino abstinuit, sed propter adolescentiam, & cavenda sodalitia, & c.

## 1:1 On the First Topic
ON THE FIRST TOPIC.

TO the first and second topic almost all the sermons of Jeremiah pertain, who prophesies about punishment, and explains the causes. As in Chapter 22. Why has the Lord done thus to this city? Because they abandoned the covenant of their God, and worshipped foreign gods.

Also concerning Jehoiakim, of whom mention is made here, he says, He shall be buried with the burial of an ass, cast out beyond the gates of Jerusalem.

Let us learn therefore to fear the judgment and wrath of God, and let us flee impious cults and other flagrant crimes, because if God did not spare that people, to whom He had given most ample promises, He will much less spare the others.

II

Concerning the second place, there are also many sermons extant in Jeremiah, that promises are not void, and yet the impious are punished, and meanwhile the promises are miraculously preserved, in other ways than human wisdom thinks.

Therefore Jeremiah taxes the foolish hopes of the impious, who twisted the promises to the end that they might deny that those punishments were to come.Jerem. 7.Do not trust in lying words, saying, The temple of the Lord, the temple of the Lord.

Chapter 28, when Hananiah prophesied that the Jews would return from Babylon after two years, and had broken the chain which Jeremiah was wearing, Jeremiah rebukes him, and adds that Hananiah would die that year, because he had spoken against the Lord. And the outcome responded to the prophecy of Jeremiah.

From this history it appears that the authority of the false prophets was very great, because they were boasting promises, but wrongly twisted. Jeremiah was predicting punishments, and yet he was comforting the pious, as in the following chapters 29. & 30. and thereafter.

Chapter 30. he says, I will chastise you in judgment, lest you seem innocent to yourself. I will heal your wounds, 20. Where he teaches why God in those common corporal punishments sweeps away the saints together. Because he wishes these also to acknowledge that they have sins, and he draws away from them the confidence of their own righteousness. Thus the Prophet teaches about repentance, and afterwards promises liberation.

Chapter 31. You have chastised me O Lord, and I have been instructed, as an untamed bullock, etc. Here also he teaches that the saints are afflicted, so that repentance may grow. Therefore he comforts them in a twofold way, he teaches for what purpose afflictions are useful, and he promises liberation.

Here therefore it is very useful to observe the struggle concerning the promises. Thus all in afflictions run to the promises, and when they do not happen quickly, and in that way as human reason imagines, souls grow faint, and fall away from God, just as Saul fell away. And here many Jews fell away, thinking this people to have been deserted by God.

But let the pious first acknowledge why afflictions are imposed upon us, and that God requires obedience in them. Then also let us raise ourselves by faith, let us sustain ourselves with promises, and let us seek and expect help. AsJeremiah 29.it is said, You shall pray to me, and I will hear you, etc. AndEzek. 33.As I live, I do not desire the death of the sinner. And here there is need of prudence. Let us know that God is going to govern events, above our counsels. Therefore even if deliverance is delayed, let us not fail.This Paul teaches when he says to the Ephe. To him who is able to do more than we ask or understand, be glory, etc. Thus this people had greater glory in exile than at home. It had many prophets, Daniel, Ezekiel, Zechariah, Haggai. God testified with huge miracles that this people had the word of God, true worship, and the promise of salvation. Such was the miracle of those delivered from the furnace. Likewise the highest kings were converted to the true worship of God. The royal lineage is also preserved, and is treated royally. Therefore the promise was not void, The scepter shall not be taken away, etc. But God fulfilled the promises in other ways than the false prophets thought.

Let us therefore also learn to retain faith in affliction, to invoke and expect help, even if events do not happen according to our imaginations, and let us not grow faint, but let us expect them, as they are governed by the counsel of God. Therefore it is written, Instant in prayer. The prayer of the humble shall not depart from God.

Some here dispute about the notation of times, when Daniel was led away into captivity. But the explanation is easy. The text itself narrates that he was led away in the third year of Jehoiakim, namely seven years before the voluntary migration of Jeconiah. For Jehoiakim reigned before Jeconiah. Moreover, Nebuchadnezzar had come to Jerusalem even while Jehoiakim was reigning, when the third year of the reign of Jehoiakim had been completed, and he took away spoil, and led away some captives. But he did not snatch the kingdom from King Jehoiakim, but ordered him to be an ally, who when he had rebelled after six years, with the Chaldeans returning, was killed in his own country, and his corpse was cast out among the corpses of the common people, as Jeremiah predicted to him, cap. 22. concerning the burial of an ass.

Nor is there need here for curious calculation. The matter was not accomplished in one year. The kings of the Jews often rebelled, therefore the Chaldeans often returned.

That is to be considered, that God long delayed the final punishment, so that he might invite them to repentance, as Jeremiah preachesJeremiah 26.But when the impious thought that they had been spared from punishments, they became more contumacious, etc.

III The end of victory ought to be not devastation, but the establishment of a better state. Therefore there is need of moderation, and the sounder ones are to be preserved. Just as Nebuchadnezzar preserved Jeremiah and the king and the greatest multitude of the people.

To this pertain the precepts concerning clemency, which is to do good to the good, to discern the good from the bad, to moderate the punishments of those who are curable, but to remove from the midst the impious and the incurable.Proverbs 20.Mercy and truth preserve the king, and by clemency his throne is supported.

Furthermore, that it is the duty of Princes to take care, that citizens learn the word of God, and other arts necessary for life.

The precept testifiesDeuteronomy 17.Where he commands the King to hold in his hands the book of the law, so that he may read and learn. Therefore it is necessary for kings to take care that they have those from whom they may learn.

And to this pertains the saying of Isaiah, Kings shall be your nursing fathers, that is, God especially commands kings that they should cherish and protect the teachers of the Church.

2. Chron. 31.Hezekiah is praised because he restored the studies of the priests. Wherefore princes are to be taught, that they may know that God demands this duty from them, that they should cherish the necessary studies of the Church.

The Jews had persecuted the prophets, now a heathen king nourishes them, and he will receive great benefits from them, he is converted to piety, he will have a flourishing kingdom on account of these governors. Because God compensates this kind of duty generously. According to that, He who shall have given a drink of water, on account of doctrine, etc. shall have a reward. On the contrary tyrants, who neglect or persecute the studies of the Church, or pious ministers, will be afflicted with horrible punishments.

Then that we ought to learn privately as individuals, Peter commands, Ready for a defense, etc. Likewise concerning other arts Paul saysto Titus 3.Let our people learn to excel in good works for necessary uses, that they may not be unfruitful.

CONCERNING THE LAST place principally here it is to be observed, the temperance of Daniel that there was a need for confession, and indeed that this abstinence was commanded by the law of God, not by human traditions. Therefore Daniel abstained, so that he might testify that he did not cast away the doctrine, in which alone the word of God existed, and that he abhorred the religions of other nations.

May this example also exhort us to confession, perseverance, and temperance. Because God requires these duties, and adorns them with great rewards.

Daniel in the court was overcome neither by threats, nor by contempt, nor by the allurements of pleasures or power, so that he would fall away from true worship. Few imitate this constancy, but those who do imitate it will have huge corporal and spiritual rewards, as the text says: Those who glorify me I will glorify, &c.

Latin:

DE PRIMO LOCO.

AD primum & secundum locum penè omnes leremiae conciones pertinent, qui vaticinatur de poena, & caussas exponit. Ut Cap. 22. Quare sic fecit dominus huic civitati? Quia reliquerunt pactum dei sui, & adoraverunt deos alienos.

Item de loiakim, cuius hic fit mentio, inquit, Sepultura asini sepelictur, proiectus extra portas Ierusalem.

Discamus igitur timere iudicium & iraris dei, & fugiamus impios cultus & alia flagitia, quia si illi populo deus non pepercit, cui amplissimas promissiones dederat, multo minus caeteris parcet.

II

De secundo loco etiam extant multae conciones apud Ieremiam, quod promissiones non sint irritae, & tamen impii puniantur, & interea promissiones mirabiliter serventur, aliis modis quàm humana sapientia cogitat.

Ideo Ieremias taxat stultas spes impiorum, qui promissiones eò detorquebant, ut negarent poenas illas venturas esse.Ierem. 7.Nolite confidere in verba mendacii, dicentes, Templum domini, templum domini.

Cap. 28. cum Hananias vaticinaretur post biennium redituros esse Iudaeos ex Babylone, & rupisset catenam quam gestabat Ieremias, increpat eum Ieremias, & addit, Hananiam eo anno moriturum esse, quia adversus dominum locutus sit. Ac respondit eventus vaticinio Ieremiae.

Ex hac historia apparet, maximam fuisse autoritatem pseudoprophetarum, quia iactabant promissiones, sed male detortas. Econira Ieremias praedicebat poenas, & tamen consolabatur pios, ut in sequentibus capitibus 29. & 30. ac deinceps.

Cap. 30. inquit, Castigabo te in iudicio, ne tibi videaris innocens. Vulnera tua sanabo, 20. Ubi docet, quare deus in illis communibus paenis corporalibus sanctos unà rapiat. Quia hos quoque vult agnoscere, quod habeant peccata, & detrahit eis fiduciam propriae iustitiae. Ita Propheta docet de paenitentia, & postea promittit liberationem.

Cap. 31. Castigasti me domine, & eruditus sum, sicut ivuenculus indomitus, &c. Hîc quoque docet affligi sanctos, ut crescat paenitentia. Dupliciter ergo consolatur eos, Docet ad quid prosint afflictiones, & pollicetur liberationem.

Hîc igitur valde prodest observare certamen de promissionibus. Sic omnes in afflictionibus decurrunt ad promissiones, Et cum non contingunt cito, & illo modo sicut imaginatur ratio humana, languescunt animi, & deficiunt à deo, sicut Saul deficiebat. Et hîc multi Iudaei defecerunt, arbitrantes hunc populum à deo desertum esse.

Sed pii agnoscant primum, quare afflictiones nobis imponantur, & quòd deus in illis requirat obedientiam. Deinde etiam fide erigamus nos, sustentemus nos promissionibus, & petamus & expectemus auxilium. SicutIeremiae 29.dicitur, Orabitis me, & ego exaudiam vos, &c. EtEzech. 33.Vivo ego, Nolo mortem peccatoris. Et hic prudentia opus est. Sciamus deum gubernaturum esse eventus, supra nostra consilia. Ideo etiamsi differtur liberatio, non deficiamus.Hoc docet Paulus cum inquit ad Ephe. Ei qui potens est facere suprà quàm petimus aut intelligimus, sit gloria, &c. Ita hic populus maiorem gloriam in exilio habuit, quàm domi. Habuit prophetas multos, Danielem, Ezechielem, Zachariam, Haggaeum. Deus miraculis ingentibus testatus est hunc populum habere verbum dei, veros cultus, & promissionem salutis. Quale fuit miraculum liberatorum ex camino. Item summi reges conversi sunt ad verum cultum dei. Conservatur & regia stirps, & regaliter tractatur. Non igitur erat irrita promissio, Non auferetur sceptrum, &c. Sed deus aliis modis praestitit promissiones, quàm pseudoprophetae cogitaverunt.

Discamus igitur & nos in afflictione retinere fidem, invocare & expectare auxilium, etiamsi eventus non contingunt iuxta nostras imaginationes, nec languescamus, sed expectemus eos, sicut dei consilio gubernantur. Ideo scriptum est, In oratione instantes. Oratio humiliati non discedet à deo.

Quidam hîc litigant de annotatione temporum, quando Daniel in captivitatem abductus sit. Sed facilis est explicatio. Textus ipse narrat ab ductum esse anno tertio Ioiakim, videlicet septennio ante migrationem spontaneam Ieconiae. Nam Ioiakim regnavit ante Ieconiam. Venerat autem Nabugdonosor Ierosolymam etiam regnante Ioiakim, exacto tertio anno regni Ioiakim, ac praedam abstulit, et captivos aliquos abduxit. Regi vero Ioiakim non eripuit regnum, sed socium esse iussit, qui cum post annos sex rebellasset, redeuntibus Chaldaeis, in patria interfectus est, & cadaver inter vulgi cadaverae abiectum, ut Ieremias ei praedixit, cap. 22. de sepultura asini.

Nec opus est hîc curiosa supputaione. Res non uno anno peracta est. Saepe rebellabant reges Iudaeorum, ideo sape redibant Chaldaei.

Illud est considerandum, quòd deus diu distulit extremam poenam, ut eos|ad paenitentiam invitaret, sicut concionaturIeremias 26.Sed cum putarent impii se persunctos esse poenis, fiebant contumaciores &c.

III Finis victoriae debet esse non vastitas, sed constitutio melioris status. Ideo moderatione opus est, & saniores servandi sunt. Sicut Nabugdonosor servavit Ieremiam & regem & maximam multitudinem populi.

Huc pertinent praecepta de clementia, quae est benefacere bonis, discernere bonos à malis, moderari poenas illorum qui sunt sanabiles? impios verò & insanabiles è medio tollere.Proverb. 20.Misericordia & veritas custodiunt regem, & clementia fulcitur thronus eius.

Praeterea quod Principum officium sit curare, ut cives discant verbum dei, & alias artes vitae necessarias.

Testatur praeceptumDeute. 17.Ubi iubet Regem tenere in manibus librum legis, ut legat & discat. Ergo necesse est reges curare, ut habcant à quibus discant.

Et huc pertinet dictum Esaiae, Reges erunt nutritores tui, id est, Praecipue regibus mandat deus, ut doctores Ecclesiae foveant & tueantur.

2. Paral. 31.laudatur Ezechias, quòd studia sacerdotum restituit. Quare docendi sunt principes, ut sciant deum ab ipsis hoc officium poscere, ut studia necessaria Ecclesiae foveant.

Iudaei persecuti fuerant prophetas, nunc alit eos rex ethnicus, & magna ab eis beneficia accipiet, Convertitur ad pietatem, habebit florens regnum propter hos gubernatores. Quia deus hoc genus officii largiter compensat. Iuxta illud, Qui dederit potum aquae, propter doctrinam, &c. habebit mercedem. Econtra tyranni, qui negligunt aut persequuntur studia Ecclesiae, aut ministros pios, afficientur horribilibus poenis.

Deinde quòd privatim singuli discere debeamus, praecipit Petrus, Parati ad satisfactionem, &c. Item de caeteris artibus inquit Paulusad Titum 3.Discant nostri bonis operibus praeesse ad usus necessarios, ut non sint infructuosi.

DE POSTREMO loco principaliter hìc observandum est, Danielis temperantiam fuisse opus confessionis, & quidem hanc abstinentiam fuisse praeceptam lege dei, non humanis traditionibus. Ergo abstinebat Daniel, ut testaretur se non abiicere doctrinam, in qua sola extabat verbum dei, & abhorrere ab aliarum gentium religionibus.

Hoc exemplum nos quoque ad confessionem, perseverantiam, & temperantiam hortetur. Quia deus haec officia requirit, & ornat magnis praemiis.

Daniel in aula nec minis, nec contemptu, nec illecebris voluptatum aut potentiae victus est, ut deficeret à vero cultu. Hanc constantiam pauci imitantur, Sed qui imitantur, habebunt ingentia praemia corporalia & spiritualia, Sicut inquit textus: Glorificantes me glorificabo, &c.

## 2:1 Argument
CHAPTER II.

In the second year of the reign of Nebuchadnezzar, Nebuchadnezzar saw a dream, and his spirit was terrified, and his sleep fled from The king commanded that the soothsayers, and the magicians, and the sorcerers, and the Chaldeans should be called together, to declare to the king his dreams: and they came and stood before the king. And the king said to them: I saw a dream, and being troubled in mind I know not what I saw. And the Chaldeans answered the king in Syriac: O king, live for ever: tell thy servants the dream, and we will declare the interpretation thereof. And the king answering, said to the Chaldeans: The word is gone out from me: unless you tell me the dream and the meaning thereof, you shall be put to death, and your houses shall be confiscated. But if you tell the dream and the meaning thereof, you shall receive rewards, and gifts, and great honour at my hands: therefore tell me the dream, and the interpretation thereof. They answered the second time, and said: Let the king tell his servants the dream, and we will declare the interpretation of it. And the king answered, and said:

Certainly I know that you are buying time, knowing that the word has departed from me. If therefore you do not tell me the dream, there is one sentence for you, that you have composed a false interpretation also and one full of deception so that you may speak to me until the time passes. Therefore tell me the dream, that I may know that you also speak its true interpretation. The Chaldeans therefore answering before the king, said: There is no man upon the earth, who can fulfill your word, O king: but neither does any great and powerful king inquire a word of this kind from any soothsayer, and magician, and Chaldean. For the word which you seek, O king, is heavy: nor will anyone be found who can declare it in the sight of the king, except the gods whose conversation is not with men. Which being heard, the king in fury and in great anger commanded that all the wise men of Babylon would perish. And the sentence having gone forth, the wise men were being killed: and Daniel and his companions were sought, that they might perish. Then Daniel inquired concerning the law and the sentence from Arioch the captain of the king's guard, who had gone forth to kill the wise men of Babylon. And he asked him who had received power from the king, for what cause so cruel a sentence had gone forth from the face of the king. When therefore Arioch had indicated the matter to Daniel, Daniel having entered asked the king that he would give him time to indicate the solution to the king. And he entered his house: and he indicated the business to Ananias and Misael and Azarias his companions, that they might seek mercy from the face of the God of heaven concerning this mystery, and that Daniel and his companions might not perish with the rest of the wise men of Babylon. Then the mystery was revealed to Daniel by a vision in the night is: and Daniel blessed the God of heaven, and speaking said: Blessed be the name of the Lord from eternity and even unto eternity: for his wisdom and strength are. And he changes times and ages: he transfers kingdoms and establishes them, he gives wisdom to the wise, and knowledge to those having understanding of discipline. He reveals deep and hidden things: and knows things set in darkness: and light is with him. To thee, O God of our fathers, I confess, and thee I praise: because thou hast given me wisdom and strength: and now thou hast shown me what we asked of thee, because thou hast opened the speech of the king to us. After these things Daniel having entered to Arioch whom the king had appointed that he might destroy the wise men of Babylon, thus spoke to him: Do not destroy the wise men of Babylon: bring me into the sight of the king, and I will tell the solution to the king. Then Arioch in haste brought Daniel to the king, And he said to him: I have found a man of the children of the captivity of Judah, who will announce the solution to the king. The king answered, and said to Daniel, whose name was Belteshazzar: Do you think you can truly tell me the dream which I saw, and its interpretation? And Daniel answering before the king, said: The mystery which the king asks, the wise men, the magicians, and the soothsayers, and the diviners cannot tell the king: but there is a God in heaven revealing mysteries, who will show you, King Nebuchadnezzar, what is to come in the latter days. Your dream, and the visions of your head upon your bed, are of this kind, You, O king, began to think in your bed what should come to pass hereafter: and he that reveals mysteries, shows you what shall come to pass. To me also this secret is revealed, not by any wisdom that I have more than all living men, but that the interpretation might be made manifest to the king, and you might know the thoughts of your mind. You, O king, were seeing, and behold, as it were, one great statue: that statue, great and of sublime stature, was standing before you, and its appearance was terrible. The head of this statue was of the finest gold: but the breast and arms were of silver: furthermore, the belly and thighs were of bronze, but the legs were of iron: a certain part of the feet was of iron, but a certain part was of clay. You were seeing thus, until a stone was cut from the mountain without hands, and it struck the statue upon its feet of iron and clay, and crushed them. Then the iron, the clay, the bronze, the silver, and the gold were broken in pieces together, and were reduced as if into the chaff of the summer threshing floor, which were carried away by the wind: and no place was found for them: but the stone which had struck the statue became a great mountain, and filled the whole earth: this is the dream. We will also tell the interpretation of it We shall speak before you, O king. You are the king of kings, and the God of heaven has given you a kingdom and strength and power and glory, and all things in which the sons of men dwell and the beasts of the field, the birds of the air also He has given into your hand, and has made you ruler over all: you therefore are the head of gold. And after you shall arise another kingdom inferior to you, of silver: and a third kingdom, another of bronze, which shall rule over all the earth. And the fourth kingdom shall be as iron: as iron breaks in pieces and subdues all things, so shall it break and crush all these. Furthermore, because you saw the feet and the toes part of potter's clay and part of iron: the kingdom shall be divided, which nevertheless shall spring from the planting of iron, according to what you saw, iron mixed with clay. And the toes of the feet part iron and part earthenware: in part the kingdom shall be solid, and in part broken.

But that you saw iron mixed with clay-pottery, they shall indeed be mixed with human seed, but they shall not cleave to one another, just as iron cannot be mixed with pottery. In the days of those kingdoms, the God of heaven shall raise up a kingdom which shall not be dissipated for eternity, and its kingdom shall not be handed over to another people; it shall crush and consume all these kingdoms, and it itself shall stand for eternity. According to what you saw, that a stone was cut from the mountain without hands, and it crushed the pottery, and the iron, and the bronze, and the silver, and the gold, the great God has shown the king what things are to come hereafter: and the dream is true, and its interpretation is faithful. Then King Nebuchadnezzar fell upon his face, and adored Daniel, and commanded that they should sacrifice victims and incense to him. Therefore the king speaking, said to Daniel: Truly your God is the God of gods, and the Lord of kings, revealing mysteries: because you were able to open this sacrament. Then the king exalted Daniel on high, and gave him many and great gifts, and appointed him prince over all the provinces of Babylon, and prefect and master over all the wise men of Babylon. Daniel, however, requested from the king, and he appointed over the works of the province of Babylon, Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego: but Daniel himself was at the king's gates.

PRINCIPAL TOPICS OF THE SECOND CHAPTER.

I GOD reveals in order the four Monarchies. And he testifies that at the end of the world the everlasting kingdom of the saints will come, who truly worshipped God.

In this revelation, first, consolation and doctrine were proposed to the people. They saw that they were not deserted by God, since they had prophets such as Daniel was. They were also learning when that perpetual glory promised to the fathers was to be expected.

Then the king, and many other heathens were admonished by this doctrine, that they might acknowledge the true God, and embrace true worships and the word of God.

II An example that God heard the pious, and gives to those who ask gifts, wisdom and other gifts.

III A testimony that empires are established by divine power.

IIII An admonition that empires gradually fall into a worse state, the later ones will be increasingly turbulent, and less just, just as the Roman was much harsher than the Persian.

V A testimony that it is lawful for the saints to hold magistracies, because Daniel here obtains provinces for his companions.

Latin:

CAPUT II.

In anno secundo regni Nabuchodonosor vidit Nabuchodonosor somnium, & conterritus est spiritus eius, & somnus eius fugit ab eo. Praecepit autem rex ut convocarentur arioli, & magi, & malefici, & Chaldaei, ut indicarent regi somnia sua: qui cum venissent, steterunt coram rege. Et dixit ad eos rex: Vidi somnium, & mente confusus ignoro quid viderim. Responderuntque Chaldaei regi Syriace: Rex in sempiternum vive, dic somnium servis tuis, & interpretationem eius indicabimus. Et respondens rex, ait Chaldaeis: Sermo recessit â me: nisi indica veritis mihi somnium & coniecturam eius, peribitis vos, & domus vestrae publicabuntur. Si autem somnium & coniecturam eius narraveritis, praemia & dona, & honorem multum accipietis à me: somnium igitur, & interpretationem eius indicate mihi. Responderunt secundo, atque dixerunt: Rex somnium dicat servis suis, & interpretationem illius indicabimus. Responditque rex, & ait:

Certè novi quòd tempus redimitis, scientes quòd recesserit à me sermo. Si ergo somnium non indicaveritis mihi, una est de vobis sententia, quod interpretationem quoque fallacem & deceptione plenam composueritis ut loquamini mihi donec tempus pertranseat. Somnium itaque dicite mihi, ut sciam quòd interpretationem quoque eius veram loquamini. Respondentes ergo Chaldaei coram rege, dixerunt: Non est homo super terram, qui sermonem tuum, rex, possit implere: sed neque regum quisque magnus & potens, verbum huiuscemodi sciscitatur ab omni ariolo, & mago, & Chaldaeo. Sermo enim quem tu quaeris, rex, gravis est: nec reperietur quisquam qui indicet illum in conspectu regis, exceptis diis quorum non est cum hominibus conversatio. Quo audito, rex in furore & in ira magna praecepit ut perirent omnes saprentes Babylonis. Et egressa sententia, sapientes interficiebantur: quaerebanturque Daniel & socii eius, ut perirent. Tunc Daniel requisivit de lege atque sententia ab Arioch principe militiae regis, qui egressus fuerat ad interficiendos sapientes Babylonis. Et interrogavit eum qui à rege potestatem acceperat, quam ob causam tam crudelis sententia à facie regis esset egressa. Cum ergo rem indicasset Arioch Danieli, Daniel ingressus rogavit regem, ut tempus daret sibi ad solutionem indicandam regi. Et ingressus est domum suam: Ananiaeque & Misaeli, & Azariae sociis suis, indicavit negotium, ut quaererent misericordiam à facie dei caeli super sacramento isto, & non perirent Daniel & socii eius, cum caeteris sapientibus Babylonis. Tunc Danieli mysterium per visionem nocte revelatum est: & benedixit Daniel deum caeli, & locutus ait: Sit nomen domini benedictum à seculo & usque in seculum: quia sapientia & fortitudo eius sunt. Et ipse mutat tempora & aetates: transfert regna atque constituit, dat sapientiam sapientibus, & scientiam intelligentibus disciplinam. Ipse revelat profunda & abscondita: & novit in tenebris constituta: & lux cum eo est. Tibi deus patrum nostrorum confiteor, teque laudo: quia sapientiam & fortitudinem dedisti mihi: & nunc ostendisti mihi quae rogavimus te, quia sermonem regis aperuisti nobis. Post haec Daniel ingressus ad Arioch quem constituerat rex ut perderet sapientes Babylonis, sic ei locutus est: Sapientes Babylonis ne perdas: introduc me in conspectu regis, & solutionem regi narrabo. Tunc Arioch festinus introduxit Danielem ad regem, & dixit ei: Inveni hominem de filiis transmigrationis Iudae, qui solutionem regi annunciet. Respondit rex, & dixit Danieli, cuius nomen erat Baltassar: Putásne vere potes mihi indicare somnium quod vidi, & interpretationem eius? Et respondens Daniel coram rege, ait: Mysterium quod rex interrogat sapientes, magi & arioli, & aruspices nequeunt indicare regi: sed est deus in caelo revelans mysteria, qui indicabit tibi rex Nabuchodonosor, quae ventura sunt in novissimis temporibus. Somnium tuum, & visiones capitis tui in cubili tuo, huiuscemodi sunt, Tu rex cogitare coepisti in stratu tuo quid esset futurum post haec: & qui revelat mysteria, ostendit tibi quae ventura sunt. Mihi quoque non in sapientia quae est in me plus quàm in cunctis viventibus, sacramentum hoc revelatum est, sed ut interpretatio regi manifesta fieret, & cogitationes mentis tuae scires. Tu rex videbas, & ecce quasi statua una grandis: statua illa magna & statura sublimis stabat contra te, et intuitus eius erat terribilis. Huius statuae caput, ex auro optimo erat: pectus autem & brachia, de argento: porrò venter & femora ex aere, tibiae autem ferreae: pedum quaedam pars erat ferrea, quaedam autem fictilis. Videbas ita, donec abscissus est lapis de monte sine manibus, & percussit statuam in pedibus eius ferreis & fictilibus, & comminuit eos. Tunc contrita sunt pariter ferrum, testa, aes, argentum & aurum, & redacta quasi in favillam aestivae areae, quae rapta sunt vento: nullusque locus inventus est eis: lapis autem qui percusserat statuam, factus est mons magnus, & implevit universam terram: hoc est somnium. Interpretationem quoque eius dicemus coram te, rex. Tu rex regum es, & Deus caeli regnum & fortitudinem & imperium & gloriam dedit tibi, & omnia in quibus habitant filii hominum & bestiae agri, volucres quoque caeli dedit in manu tua, & sub ditione tua universa constituit: tu es ergo caput aureum. Et post te consurget regnum aliud minus te, argenteum: & regnum tertium alium aereum, quod imperabit universae terrae. Et regnum quartum erit velut ferreum: quomodo ferrum comminuit & domat omnia, sic comminuet & conteret omnia haec. Porrò quia vidisti pedum & digitorum partem ex testa figuli, & partem ferream: regnum divisum erit, quod tamen de plantario serri orietur, secundum quod vidisti ferrum mistum testae ex luto. Et digitos pedum ex parte ferreos & ex parte fictiles: ex parte regnum erit solidum, & ex parte contritum.

Quòd autem vidisti ferrum mistum testae ex luto, commiscebuntur quidem humano semine, sed non adhaerebunt sibi, sicuti ferrum misceri non potest testae. In diebus autem regnorum illorum suscitabit deus caeli regnum quod in aeternum non dissipabitur, & regnum eius alteri populo non tradetur, comminuet cutem & consumet universa regna haec, & ipsum stabit in aeternum. Secundum quod vidisti, quod de monte abscissus est lapis sine manibus, & comminuit testam, & ferreum, & aes, & argentum, & aurum, Deus magnus ostendit regi quae ventura sunt postea: & verum est somnium, & fidelis interpretatio eius. Tunc rex Nabuchodonosor cécidit in faciem suam, & Danielem adoravit, & hostias, & incensum praecepit ut sacrificarent ei. Loquens ergo rex, ait Danieli: Verè deus vester deus deorum est, & dominus regum, revelans mysteria: quoniam tu potuisti aperire hoc sacramentum. Tunc rex Danielem in sublime extulit, & munera multa & magna dedit ei, & constituit eum principem super omnes provincias Babylonis, & praefectum & magistrum super cunctos sapientes Babylonis. Daniel autem postulavit à rege, & constituit super opera provinciae Babylonis, Sidrach, Misach, & Abdenago: ipse autem Daniel erat in foribus regis.

SECUNDI CAPITIS LO-CI PRAECIPUI.

I DEUS revelat ordine quatuor Monarchias. Et testatur in fine mundi venturum regnum sempiternum sanctorum, qui deum verè coluerunt.

In hac revelatione primum populo proposita est consolatio & doctrina. Videbant se non desertos esse à deo, cum habebant prophetas tales, qualis fuit Daniel. Discebant etim quando expectanda esset illa perpetua gloria promissa patribus.

Deinde rex, & alii multi ethnici hac doctrina admoniti sunt, ut agnoscerent verum deum, & complecterentur veros cultus & verbum dei.

II Exemplum quòd deus exaudivit pios, & dat petentibus dona, sapientiam & alia dona.

III Testimonium, quòd imperia divinitus constituantur.

IIII Admonitio quòd imperia paulatim ruunt in deterius, Posteriora subinde magis turbulenta erunt, & minus iusta, Sicut Romanum multo fuit asperius quàm Persicum.

V Testimonium quòd liceat sanctis gerere magistratus, Quia Daniel hîc impetrat sociis provincias.

## 2:1 On the First Topic
ON THE FIRST TOPIC.

This whole miracle was exhibited on account of four causes,

Because God wished to testify that only the Jewish people possessed the word of God and true worship.

So that the Jews might have consolation.

So that they might be admonished concerning the time of the coming eternal kingdom.

So that the Gentiles also might be converted to the acknowledgment of God.

On account of these such great causes, huge miracles were set forth, and for this purpose not common persons were chosen, but the king himself and the princes.

And so that it might become more clear that the Chaldean religions are false, and that there is one true God who delivered the word to the people of Israel, therefore the wisdom of the Magi is first confounded, and their impiety and blindness is revealed.

So that this might be able to happen, God willed the king to forget the dream, and yet such was the consternation of his mind and ἔκστασις in that dream, that he sensed it was a certain divine admonition. Therefore he proposes such harsh edicts. He threatens death to the diviners, unless they narrate the dream itself, and he has a probable reason.

If interpretations are shown divinely, as the diviners boasted, it can be shown divinely not only the interpretation, but also the itself. a dream. But if the interpretation is a human conjecture, and does not proceed from God, it is ambiguous and fallacious.

Therefore, because they cannot find the dream, and Daniel finds it, it is evident that the doctrine of the Chaldeans is not from God, but that the doctrine and religion of Daniel were handed down from God. Thus the first testimony is shown, that the Chaldean doctrine and religions are false and feigned, just as the Magi were convicted in Egypt etc. Thus God universally argues against contrary doctrine in the revelation of the Gospel.

Concerning the dream I say briefly, that those dreams are certainly to be believed, to which God gives testimony that they are divinely shown, just as here the miracle of the found dream testifies that this dream is from God, and God elsewhere rendered consciences certain in another way.

After Daniel recounts the dream, he himself interprets it concerning the four Monarchies, and concerning the perpetual kingdom of Christ. This narration is not only political concerning empires, but also provides an occasion for Daniel to preach concerning the whole kingdom of Christ, concerning the final judgment, concerning the cause of sin, concerning the redemption and restoration of the human of the kind, Why there is such brevity of the world, What the future perpetual kingdom will be, Whether in this unclean nature or another, What kind of Redeemer there will be, and how one may arrive at this kingdom. Thus this brief narrative comprises the sum of the Gospel. Therefore, not only should political doctrine be observed here, but much more so the Evangelical.

It is political that we are admonished that empires are divinely established. Also that by a wonderful counsel God willed to associate nations through Monarchies, whether so that they might be better ruled by discipline, laws, judgments, and punishments, or so that by that occasion the Gospel might be more easily propagated and made known. Just as in individual Monarchies the Gospel shone for a while among the nations.

The golden head signifies the first Monarchy, which comprises the succession of the Chaldeans & Assyrians.

The silver breast signifies the second Monarchy, comprising the empire of the Medes & Persians.

The bronze belly Alexander & the more powerful successors , who were joined by a treaty.

The iron legs the Roman empire, to which He adds a description, that it was to be harsher than the others. Because in reality they ruled the nations more harshly, and waged civil wars among themselves, and destroyed the Jewish nation, and exercised most cruel persecutions against the church.

The feet partly of clay, partly of iron, signify the kingdoms born after the dissipated Monarchy, namely the French, Spanish, German, Saracen, Turkish, and among these some are more warlike than others. And although they struggle among themselves and attempt to bring about a Monarchy, yet they will not accomplish it.

Furthermore, in the fourth Monarchy, says Daniel, Christ was to come, who was to begin the eternal kingdom promised to the fathers. God willed the time of the coming of Christ to be made manifest, so that among other testimonies concerning Christ, the circumstance of time might also admonish the pious, and refute the impious. Just as the Jews now can be clearly refuted by the circumstance of time. It was necessary for Christ to be born before the dissipation of the Jewish nation in the fourth Monarchy, while the Jewish polity was still enduring, in Bethlehem, etc. Therefore it is necessary to confess that he is born, because now Bethlehem is overturned, and there is no Jewish polity.

Likewise it was profitable to hold onto pious consolations regarding time, so that in the meantime they might be able to endure afflictions, and might await the coming liberation, and might not err, thinking that the time had passed.

Finally, it is profitable for all to know that the time of the world is a small space, that the day of judgment is already pressing and is at the doors. Just as God also wished to admonish this Babylonian king concerning the brevity of the times of the world, concerning the coming judgment, concerning the salvation of the pious, concerning the eternal punishments of the impious, so that the king might not think that all things happen by chance, but might learn that there is a God saving the pious and punishing the impious, establishing empires, and helping just princes. Thus this passage embraces the doctrine of the whole religion and Gospel. I have spoken concerning time.

Thus, however, he describes this kingdom, He says, that a stone was cut out from a mountain without hands. The stone signifies Christ, who was born from the mountain, that is, from the people of Israel, but was cut out from there without hands, that is, he was not given to this people by human counsel, nor did he afterwards constitute a kingdom by human power or the strength of that people. The whole matter is conducted divinely. The Son of God comes, sent by the eternal Father, he is born without male seed, He established a kingdom, or by human counsels and forces. This is the simplest explanation, so that the mountain may be understood as the kingdom or the Jewish people, because afterwards he says again, that this stone becomes a great mountain, that is, a huge kingdom.

Others have interpreted the mountain as the mother Mary, others as heaven, but I omit these interpretations. Others also interpret 'without hands' as an unpolished stone, that is, despised and rejected, as it is said in Psalm 118, The stone which they rejected, etc. But our interpretation embraces this, 'Without hands,' that is, without human counsels, without human power.

But Daniel preaches briefly about both advents of Christ. God will raise up a kingdom while other kingdoms still endure. What kind of conflict is this? How, while the Roman Monarchy endures, will Christ nevertheless reign? Here he signifies that the kingdom of Christ will not be political, but spiritual, which will be administered through the word. And he says expressly, God will establish that kingdom. For this kingdom he himself establishes and rules immediately, through his word openly delivered, and through the holy spirit. The others, even if they are established divinely, are nevertheless established mediately through human are established and ruled by power.

This eternal kingdom will therefore be begun while the empires that will despise this kingdom and will oppose it still endure, therefore it will crush and end all kingdoms. For first it will refute by preaching, afterwards it will overturn and afflict the enemies with eternal punishments. Christ, however, remains with his Church, retains the kingdom, and enjoys eternal glory.

Thus far we have briefly run through the history and interpretation, in which, as was said before, not only political opinions but also Evangelical ones are to be considered, and it is to be thought that this history or interpretation warns us ourselves. Moreover, testimonies concerning the time of the advent of Christ confirm us, since it was necessary for Christ to be born in the fourth Monarchy, while the Mosaic polity was still enduring, it is necessary to confess that he is born.

Furthermore, because it is spoken here of an eternal kingdom, it is necessary to speak of the cause abolishing death, likewise of the cause of death, of sin, of the promises of liberation, of Christ, of our redemption through the sacrifice of Christ, of the resurrection of the dead.

How, however, are the saints to reign perpetually, Since very many have died for so many centuries, and the rest are to die hereafter unless they rise again? Therefore this passage embraces the entire doctrine of the Gospel, to this one must look, when this sermon of Daniel is read.

Meanwhile let us consider this especially concerning the stone cut from the mountain, that there is one Lord, namely Jesus Christ, who begins this eternal kingdom, who abolishes death, who conquers the entire power of the devil and the world. Nor does Daniel say anything else here than what existed before in Isaiah, who names Christ the father of everlasting life.

These sentences mean exactly the same thing; which the Gospel repeats so oftenJohn 3.He who believes in the Son has eternal life. But he who does not believe in the Son will not see life, but the wrath of God will remain upon him.

And againJohn 17.This is eternal life that they may know you the only true God, and whom you have sent JESUS Christ.

Thus therefore he begins eternal life, when we believe this stone to have been given, so that it might abolish sin and death, and we believe that on account of him the remission of sins and eternal life are given to us.

Let these things be considered chiefly in this place concerning the stone, how it begins eternal life. And let the particle Gratis be diligently observed in the doctrine of faith, as has been said often elsewhere, that the benefits of Christ are given to us freely, lest the honor of Christ be transferred to human righteousnesses. Likewise, that the certainty of faith may remain. Faith cannot exist if it hangs upon the condition of our worthiness or of the law. And what this faith is in invocation must be learned, in all the business and dangers of life. Then let us look upon Christ the mediator and high priest, and let us think that he is this stone, which destroys the power of the devil. As John says1. John 3.In this the son of God appeared, that he might destroy the works of the devil. And let us remember the particle Gratis, which Daniel himself inculcates to us below chap. 9. For we do not invoke in our own righteousnesses, but in thy many mercies.

Lastly, let testimonies concerning political affairs be excerpted. First, that empires are constituted by God, Daniel testifies here, saying, Blessed be the name of God, etc. Who removes a king, and makes him stand.

Secondly, that it is lawful for the pious to administer empires, The examples of Daniel and other saints, who presided over provinces and indeed administered justice not according to the Mosaic laws, but according to the Babylonian ones, testify here.

Latin:

DE PRIMO LOCO.

Totum hoc miraculum exhibitum est propter quatuor caussas,

Quia deus testari voluit, solum Iudaicum populum habere verbum dei, & veros cultus.

Ut Iudaei haberent consolationem.

Ut de tempore venturi regni perpetui admonerentur.

Ut converterentur etiam Gentes ad agnitionem dei.

Propter has tantas caussas miracula ingentia proposita sunt, & ad hoc delectae sunt non vulgares personae, sed rex ipse & principes.

Ac ut magis perspicuum fieret, religiones Chaldaicas falsas esse, unum esse verum deum qui tradidit verbum populo Israel, ideo primum confunditur sapientia Magorum, & patefit eorum impietas & caecitas.

Hoc ut fieri posset, deus voluit regem oblivisci somnii, Et tamen talis fuit consternatio animi &ἔκστασιςin eo somnio, ut senserit esse quandam divinam admonitionem. Ideo tam dura edicta proponit. Minatur mortem coniictoribus, nisi ipsum somnium narrent, Et habet probabilem rationem.

Si interpretationes divinitus monstrantur, ut iactitabant coniectores, potest divinitus monstrari non tantum interpretatio, sed etiam ipsum somnium. Si verò interpretatio est humana coniectura, & non proficiscitur à deo, est ambigua & fallax.

Ergo, quia non possunt reperire somnium, & Daniel reperit, constat Chaldaeorum doctrinam non esse à deo, sed Danielis doctrinam & religionem à deo traditam esse. Ita primum ostenditur testimonium, doctrinam & religiones Chaldaicas falsas & ementitas esse, sicut in Aegypto convicti sunt Magi &c. Sic universaliter in revelatione Evangelii arguit deus contrariam doctrinam.

De somnio breviter dico, Illis somniis certò credendum esse, quibus deus dat testimonium, quòd sint divinitus ostenda, sicut hîc miraculum reperti somnii testatur hoc somnium à deo esse, & deus alibi aliter reddidit certas conscientias.

Postquam autem Daniel recenset somnium, ipse interpretatur de quatuor Monarchiis, & de regno perpetuo Christi. Haec enarratio non tantum est politica de imperiis, sed etiam praebet occasionem Danieli concionandi de toto regno Christi, De novissimo iudicio, De caussa peccati, De redemptione & instauratione humani generis, Cur sit tanta mundi brevitas, Quale sit futurum perpetuum regnum, Utrum in hac natura immunda vel alia, Qualis sit futurus Redemptor, & quomodo ad hoc regnum perveniatur. Ita haec brevis narratio complectitur summam Evangelii. Ideo non tantum hîc politica doctrina observetur, sed multo magis Evangelica.

Politicum est, quòd admonemur divinitus constitui imperia. Item quòd mirabili consilio deus voluit consociare gentes per Monarchias, sive ut melius regerentur disciplina, legibus, iudiciis, poenis, sive ut per eam occasionem facilius propgari & innotescere Evangelium posset. Sicut in singulis Mondarchiis fulsit Evangelium aliquantisper apud gentes.

Aureum caput significat primam Monarchiam, quae complectitur successionem Chaldaeorum & Assyriorum.

Argenteum pectus, secundam Monarchiam Argenteum significat complectentem Medorum imperium pectus. & Persicum.

Venter aeneus Alexandrum & successores potentiores, qui erant coniuncti foedere.

Tibiae ferreae Romanum imperium, Cui adiicit descriptionem, quòd fuerit futurum asperius caeteris. Quia reipsa durius imperaverunt gentibus, & intra se gesserunt bella civilia, et deleverunt gentem Iudaicum, & exercverunt crudelissimas persecutiones in ecclesiam.

Pedes partim testacei, partim ferrei, signicant regna nata post dissipatam Monarchiam, videlicet Francicum, Hispanicum, Germanicum, Sarracenicum, Turcicum, & in his alia sunt aliis bellicosiora. Et quanquam inter se luctantur & conantur efficere Monarchiam, tamen non perficient.

Porro in quarta Monarchia inquit Daniel, venturum esse Christum, qui sit inchoaturus regnum aeternum promissum patribus. Voluit deus patefieri tempus adventus Christi, ut inter caetera testimonia de Christo etiam circumstantia temporis pios admoneret, & refelleret impios. Sicut Iudaei nunc manifesta refelli possunt per circumstantiam temporis. Oportuit Christum nasci ante dissipationem gentis Iudaicae in quarta Monarchia, durante adhuc politia Iudaica, in Bethleem, &c. Ergo necesse est fateri, quòd sit natus, quia nunc eversa est Bethleem, & nulla est politia Iudaica.

Item proderat pios tenere consolationes de tempore, ut interea afflictiones sustinere possent, & expectarent venturam liberationem, nec errarent, arbitrantes tempus praeteriisse.

Postremo prodest omnibus scire, quod mundi tempus sit exiguum spatium, quòd instet iam & sit in foribus dies iudicii. Sicut & hunc regem Babylonicum deus admonere voluit, de brevitate temporum mundi, de venturo iudicio, de salute piorum, de aeternis poenis impiorum, ne rex putaret omnia casu fieri, sed disceret esse deum salvantem pios & punientem impios, constituentem imperia, & adivuantem iustos principes. Ita hic locus complectitur totius religionis & Evangelii doctrinam. Dixi de tempore.

Sic autem describit hoc regnum, Ait, lapidem abscissum esse de monte sine manibus. Lapis significat Christum, qui de monte, id est ex populo Israel natus est, sed sine manibus inde abscissus est, id est, non humano consiliodatus est huic populo, nec postea regnum constituit humana potentia aut viribus illius populi. Tota res geritur divinitus. Filius dei venit, à patre aeterno missus, nascitur sine virili semine, Regnum constituit sive humanis consiliis & viribus. Haec est simplissima enarratio, ut mons intelligatur de regno seu populo Iudaico, quia postea rursus dicit, hunc lapidem fieri magnum montem, id est, ingens regnum.

Alii interpretati sunt montem de matre Maria, alii de caelo, sed has interpretationes omitto. Sine manibus etiam alii interpretantur, impolitum lapidem, id est, spretum & abiectum, sicut in Psalmo 118.dicitur, Lapis quem reprobaverunt, &c. Sed hanc interpretationem nostra complectitur, Sine manibus, id est, sine humanis consiliis, sine humana potentia.

Breviter autem concionatur Daniel de utroque adventu Christi. Deus suscitabit regnum durantibus adhuc aliis regnis. Qualis haec est pugnantia? Quomodo durante Monarchia Romana, tamen regnabit Christus? Hîc significat regnum Christi non futurum politicum, sed spirituale, quod per verbum administrabitur. Et dicit disertè, Deus constituet id regnum. Nam hoc regnum immediate constituit & regit ipse, per verbum suum palàm traditum, & per spiritum sanctum. Caetera etiamsi divinitus constituuntur, tamen mediate per humanam potentiam constituuntur & reguntur.

Inchoabitur ergo hoc sempiternum regnum durantibus adhuc imperiis, quae id regnum contemnent, & ei adver sabuntur, Ideo conteret et finiet omnia regna. Primum enim arguet praedicatione, postea evertet & afficiet hostes aeternis poenis. Christus autem cum sua Ecclesia permanet, retinet regnum & fruitur aeterna gloria.

Hactenus breviter percurrimus historiam & interpretationem, in qua, ut ante dictum est non tantum politicae sententiae, sed etiam Evangelicae considerandae sunt, & cogitandum, quod haec historia vel, interpretatio nos ipsos moneat. Confirmant autem nos testimonia de tempore adventus Christi, Cum in quarta Monarchia, durante adhuc politia Mosaica, Christum nasci oportuerit, necesse est fateri quòd sit natus.

Praeterea, quia hîc de regno aeterno dicitur, necesse est dici, de caussa abolente mortem, item de caussa mortis, de peccato, de promissionibus liberationis, de Christo, de redemptione nostra per sacrificium Christi, de resurrectione mortuorum.

Quomodo autem regnaturi sunt sancti perpetuò, cum plurimi sint mortui tot seculis, & deinceps morituri reliqui nisi resurgant? Ergo hic locus complectitur universum Evangelii doctrinam, ad hanc respiciendum est, cum haec concio Danielis legitur.

Interim hoc praecipue consideremus de lapide praeciso à monte, Unum esse dominum, scilicet Iesum Christum, qui hoc regnum aeternum in choat, qui abolet mortem, qui vincit universam diaboli et mandi potentiam. Nec aliud hìc dicit Daniel, quàm quòd antea extabat apud Esaiam, qui nominat Christum patrem perpetuae vitae.

Hae sententiae prorsus idem volunt; quod toties repetit EvangeliumIoan. 3.Qui credit in filium, habet vitam aeternam. Qui autem non credit in filium, non videbit vitam, sed ira dei manebit super eum.

Et rursusIoan. 17.Haec est vita aeternam agnoscant te solum verum deum, & quem misisti IESUM Christum.

Sic ergo inchoat vitam aeternam, cum credimus hunc lapidem datum esse, ut aboleat peccatum & mortem, & credimus nobis propter eum donari remissionem peccatorum & vitam aeternam.

Haec de lapide hoc loco praecipuè considerentur, quomodo inchoet vitam aeternam. Et diligenter observetur in doctrina de fide, particula Gratis, sicut alias saepe dictum est, quòd gratis donentur nobis beneficia Christi, ne transferatur honos Christi in iustitias humanas. Item, ut maneat fidei certitudo. Non potest fides existere, si pendeat ex conditione nostrae dignitatis aut legis. Et quid sit haec fides in invocatione, discendum est, in omnibus vitae negotiis & periculis. Tunc intueamur Christum mediatorem & pontificem, & cogitemus eum hunc esse lapidem, qui destruit potentiam diaboli. Sicut Ioannes inquit1. Ioan. 3.In hoc apparuit filius dei, ut destruat opera diaboli. Et meminerimus particulae Gratis, quam ipse Daniel nobis inculcat infra cap. 9. Neque enim in iustificationibus nostris invocamus, sed in miserationibus tuis multis.

Postremo excerpantur testimonia de politicis negotiis. Primum quòd imperia divinitus constituantur, testatur hîc Daniel, inquiens, Sit nomen dei benedictum, &c.Qui transfert regem, & consistere facit.

Secundo, quòd piis liceat administrare imperia, testantur hîc exempla Danielis, & aliorum sanctorum, qui praefuerunt provinciis, & quidem dixerunt ius non ex legibus Mosaicis, sed ex Babylonicis.

## 3:1 Argument
CHAPTER III.

King Nebuchadnezzar made a golden statue of sixty cubits in height, and six cubits in breadth, and set it up in the plain of Dura, in the province of Babylon. Therefore King Nebuchadnezzar sent to gather together the satraps, magistrates, and judges, the generals and tyrants, and the governors, and all the princes of the provinces, that they might come together to the dedication of the statue which King Nebuchadnezzar had set up. Then the satraps, magistrates, and judges, the generals and tyrants, and the great men who were established in power, and all the princes of the provinces were gathered together, that they might come together to the dedication of the statue which King Nebuchadnezzar had set up. But they were standing in the sight of the statue which King Nebuchadnezzar had placed, and a herald was crying out mightily, It is said to you, O peoples, tribes, and languages: In the hour in which you shall hear the sound of the trumpet and pipe and harp, sackbut, and psaltery, and symphony, and of every kind of musical instruments, falling down, adore the golden statue which King Nebuchadnezzar has set up. But if anyone does not, prostrate, adore, in the same hour he shall be cast into the furnace of burning fire. After these things, therefore, immediately as all the peoples heard the sound of the trumpet, pipe and harp, sackbut and psaltery, symphony, and of every kind of musical instruments, all the peoples, tribes, and languages falling down adored the golden statue which King Nebuchadnezzar had set up. And immediately at that very time, the Chaldean men approaching they accused the Jews, and said to King Nebuchadnezzar: O king, live forever: you, O king, have set forth a decree that every man who shall hear the sound of the trumpet, pipe, and harp, sackbut, and psaltery, and symphony, and all kinds of music, shall prostrate himself, and adore the golden statue: but if anyone not falling down shall adore, let him be cast into the furnace of burning fire. There are therefore Jewish men, whom you have set over the works of the province of Babylon, Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego, these men have despised, O king, your decree, they do not worship your gods, and they do not adore the golden statue which you have erected. Then Nebuchadnezzar in fury and in anger commanded that Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego be brought: who were immediately brought into the sight of the king. And King Nebuchadnezzar pronouncing, said to them: Is it true, O Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego, that you do not worship the gods do you not worship my gods, and do you not adore the golden statue which I have set up?
