Now therefore if you are ready, at whatever hour you hear the sound of the trumpet, flute, harp, sackbut, and psaltery and symphony, and all kinds of music, prostrate yourselves, and adore the statue which I have made: but if you do not adore, in the same hour you shall be cast into the furnace of burning fire, and who is the god that shall deliver you out of my hand? Sidrach, Misach, and Abdenago answering, said to king Nabuchodonosor: We do not need to answer thee concerning this matter. For behold our God whom we worship, is able to save us from the furnace of burning fire, and to deliver us out of thy hands, O king. But if he will not, be it known to thee, O king, that we do not worship thy gods, and we do not adore the golden statue which thou hast set up. Then Nabuchodonosor was filled with fury, and the aspect of his face of that one was changed concerning Sidrach, Misach, and Abdenago: and he commanded that the furnace be heated seven times more than it was accustomed to be heated. And he ordered the strongest men from his army to bind the feet of Sidrach, Misach, and Abdenago and cast them into the furnace of burning fire. And immediately those men were bound with their trousers, and turbans, and shoes, and garments, and were cast into the midst of the furnace of burning fire: for the king's command was urgent: but the furnace was heated exceedingly. Moreover, the flame of the fire killed those men who had cast in Sidrach, Misach, and Abdenago. But these three men, that is, Sidrach, Misach, and Abdenago, fell down into the midst of the furnace of burning fire, bound. And they walked in the midst of the flame praising God, and blessing the Lord. Then King Nabuchodonosor was astonished, and rose up in haste, and said to his nobles: Did we not Did we not cast three men bound into the midst of the fire? They answering the king, said: It is true, O king. The king answered, and said: Behold I see four men loose, and walking in the midst of the fire, and there is no hurt in them, and the form of the fourth is like the son of God. Then Nabuchodonosor came to the door of the burning fiery furnace, and said: Sidrach, Misach, and Abdenago, you servants of the most high God, go ye forth, and come. And immediately Sidrach, Misach, and Abdenago went out from the midst of the fire. And the satraps, and the magistrates, and the judges, and the great men of the king being gathered together, considered these men, that the fire had no power in their bodies, and that not a hair of their head had been singed, nor their garments altered, and the smell of the fire had not passed on them. And Nabuchodonosor breaking forth, said: Blessed be their God, namely Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego, who sent his angel, and delivered his servants who believed in him, and changed the king's word, and yielded their bodies that they might not serve nor worship any god, except their own God. Therefore, a decree is made by me, that every people, tribe, and language, which shall speak blasphemy against the God of Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego, shall perish, and his house shall be destroyed: for there is no other God that can save in this way. Then the king promoted Shadrach, Meshach, and Abednego in the province of Babylon. Nebuchadnezzar, the king, to all peoples, nations, and languages, who dwell in all the earth, peace be multiplied unto you. The most high God has wrought signs and wonders with me. It has pleased me therefore to preach his signs, because they are great, and his wonders, because they are mighty, and his kingdom, an everlasting kingdom, and his power is from generation to generation.

Latin:

CAPUT III.

Nabuchodonosor rex fecit statuam auream altitudine cubitorum sexaginta, latitudine cubitorum sex, & statuit eam in campo Dura provinciae Babylonis. Itaque Nabuchodonosor rex misit ad congregandos satrapas, magistratus & iudices, duces & tyrannos, & praefectos, omnesque principes regionum, ut convenirent ad dedicationem statuae quam erexerat Nabuchodonosor rex. Tunc congregati sunt satrapae, magistratus & iudices, duces & tyranni, & optimates qui erant in potestatibus constituti, & universi principes regionum, ut convenirent ad dedicationem statuae quam exexerat. Nabuchodonosor rex. Stabant autem in conspectu statuae quam posuerat Nabuchodonosor rex, & praeco clamabat valenter, Vobis dicitur populis, tribubus & linguis: In hora qua audieritis sonitum tubae & fistulae & citharae, sambucae, & psalterii, & symphoniae, & universi generis musicorum, cadentes adorate statuam auream quam constituit Nabuchodonosor rex. Si quis autem non prostratus adoraverit, eadem hora mittetur in fornacem ignis ardentis. Post haec igitur statim ut audierunt omnes populi sonitum tubae, fistulae & citharae, sambucae & psalterii, symphoniae, & omnis generis musicorum, cadentes omnes populi, tribus, & linguae adoraverunt statuam auream quam constituerat Nabuchodonosor rex. Statimque in ipso tempore accedentes viri Chaldaei accusaverunt Iudaeos, dixeruntque Nabuchodonosor regi: Rex in aeternum vive: tu rex posuisti decretum ut omnis homo qui audierit sonitum tubae, fistulae & citharae, sambucae, et psalterii, & symphoniae, & universi generis musicorum, prosternat se, & adoret statuam auream: si quis autem non prócidens adoraverit, mittatur in fornacem ignis ardentis. Sunt ergo viri Iudaei, quos constituisti super opera regionis Babyloniae, Sidrach, Misach, & Abdenago, viri isti contempserunt, rex, decretum tuum, deos tuos non colunt, & statuam auream quam erexisti non adorant. Tunc Nabuchodonosor in furore & in ira praecepit, ut adducerentur Sidrach, Misach, & Abdenago: qui confestim adducti sunt in conspectu regis. Pronunciansque Nabuchodonosor rex, ait eis: Veréne Sidrach, Misach, et Abdenago deos meos non colitis, & statuam auream quam constitui non adoratis? Nunc ergo si estis parati, quacunque hora audieritis sonitum tubae, fistulae, citharae, sambucae & psalterii & symphoniae, omnisque generis musicorum, prosternite vos, & adorate statuam quam feci: quòd si non adoraveritis, eadem hora mittemini in fornacem ignis ardentis, & quis est deus qui eripiet vos de manu mea? Respondentes Sidrach, Misach, & Abdenago, dixerunt regi Nabuchodonosor: Non oportet nos de hac re respondere tibi. Ecce enim deus noster quem colimus, potest eripere nos de camino ignis ardentis, & de manibus tuis, ô rex, liberare. Quòd si noluerit, notum sit tibi, rex, quia deos tuos non colimus, & statuam auream quam erexisti, non adoramus. Tunc Nabuchodonosor repletus est furore, & aspectus faciei illius immutatus est super Sidrach, Misach, & Abdenago: & praecepit ut succenderetur fornax septuplum quàm succendi consueverat. Et viris fortissimis de exercitu suo iussit ut ligatis pedibus Sidrach, Misach & Abdenago mitterent eos in fornacem ignis ardentis. Et confestim viri illi vincti cum braccis suis, & tiaris, & calceamentis & vestibus, missi sunt in medium fornacis ignis ardentis: nam iussio regis urgebat: fornax autem succensa erat nimis. Porrò viros illos qui miserant Sidrach, Misach, & Abdenago, interfecit flamma ignis. Viri autem hi tres, id est Sidrach, Misach, & Abdenago, ceciderunt in medio camino ignis ardentis, colligati. Et ambulabant in medio flammae laudantes deum, & benedicentes domino. Tunc Nabuchodonosor rex obstupuit, & surrexit properè, & ait optimatibus suis: Nonne tres viros misimus in medium ignis compeditos? Qui respondentes regi, dixerunt: Verè rex. Respondit rex, & ait: Ecce ego video quatuor viros solutos, & ambulantes in medio ignis, & nihil corruptionis in eis est, & species quarti similis filio dei. Tunc accessit Nabuchodonosor ad ostium fornacis ignis ardentis, & ait: Sidrach, Misach, & Abdenago servi dei excelsi, egredimini & venite. Statimque egressi sunt Sidrach, Misach, & Abdenago de medio ignis. Et congregati satrapae, & magistratus, & iudices, & potentes regis, contemplabantur viros illos, quoniam nihil potestatis habuisset ignis in corporibus eorum, & capillus capitis eorum non esset adustus, etiam sarabala eorum non fuissent immutata, & odor ignis non transisset per eos. Et erumpens Nabucho donosor, ait: Benedictus deus eorum, Sidrach videlicet, Misach, & Abdenago, qui misit angelum suum, & eruit servos suos qui crediderunt in eum, & verbum regis immutaverunt, & tradiderunt corpora sua ne servirent & ne adorarent omnem deum, excepto deo suo. A me ergo positum est hoc decretum, ut omnis populus, tribus & lingua, quaecunque locuta fuerit blasphemiam contra deum Sidrach, Misach, & Abdenago, dispereat, & domus eius vastetur: neque enim est alius deus qui possit ita salvare. Tunc rex promovit Sidrach, Misach, & Abdenago in provincia Babylonis. Nabuchodonosor, rex, omnibus populis, gentibus, & linguis, qui habitant in universa terra, pax vobis multiplicetur. Signa & mirabilia fecit apud me deus excelsus. Placuit ergo mihi praedicare signa eius, quia magna sunt, & mirabilia eius, quia fortia, et regnum eius, regnum sempiternum, & potestas eius in generatione & generationem.

## 3:1 Topics of Chapter III
TOPICS OF CHAPTER III.

I. AN EXAMPLE of human blindness and audacity, instituting new forms of worship without the word of God. Which God here shows himself to disapprove.

II. The doctrine is that impious forms of worship ought to be rebuked.

III. That one ought to prefer the commandment of God to all human things, to power, to human laws, to peace, to tranquility, to our life.

IIII. Blasphemy is to be refuted, as the saying of the king is refuted here, Who is the god that can deliver you?

V. What kind of faith there ought to be concerning bodily deliverance, namely with the condition, if it pleases God, and concerning the distinction of promises. Some have a condition, others do not have one.

The glorification of the pious against blasphemy, and the punishment of the impious, especially of the henchmen who are ministers of another's fury.

VII. Testimony that in dangers God adds angels to the pious.

VIII. The conversion of the king, following the sermon and the glorification of the pious.

IX. An edict which teaches that it pertains to the office of princes to prohibit and punish blasphemies.

Latin:

LOCI III. CAPITIS.

I. EXEMPLUM humanae caecitatis & audaciae, instituentis novos cultus fine verbo dei. Quos hîc ostendit se deus improbare.

II. Doctrina est, quòd oporteat reprehendi impios cultus.

III. Quod oporteat mandatum dei anteferre omnibus rebus humanis, potestati, legibus humanis, paci, tranquillitati, vitae nostrae.

IIII. Blasphemia refutanda est, ut hîc refutatur dictum regis, Quis deus potest vos liberare?

V. Qualis debeat esse fides de corporali liberatione, videlicet cum conditione, si deo placet, & de discrimine promissionum. Aliae habent conditionem, aliae non habent.

VI.

Glorificatio piorum contra blaspemiam, & poena impiorum praesertim satellitum, qui alieni furoris ministri sunt.

VII. Testimonium quòd in periculis deus addat angelos piis.

VIII. Conversio regis, sequens concionem & glorificationem piorum.

IX. Edictium quod docet pertinere ad officium principum, prohibere & punire blasphemias.

## 3:1 On the First Topic
ON THE FIRST TOPIC.

SINCE the magnitude of these things cannot be explained in words, I am briefer, and I desire only to admonish the readers. First, therefore, let the pious learn here that it is a huge and horrible sin against the first commandment to institute and defend worship without the word of God.

And let us know that not one Nebuchadnezzar is signified here, but the idolatry of the whole world. Just as Nebuchadnezzar with horrible audacity, and yet with the highest opinion of religion, instituted worship, so many have done in other times. Empires for the most part defend idolatry. In the Church But the impious pontiffs establish kingdoms for themselves, and fortify them under the pretext of new cults, just as Jeroboam did. And the counsel of Nebuchadnezzar looked to this, that he might unite the nations by his own cult, etc.

Therefore it must be considered that it is a huge sin of pontiffs and princes, who defend cults instituted without the word of God, and fighting against the word of God, the Mass, monasticism, and other traditions. As Isaiah 29. it is said, The Lord has mingled for you a spirit of deep sleep, etc. because they worshipped me with the commandments of men, etc.

And Jer. 2. My people have done two evils, they have forsaken me, the fountain, and have made for themselves cisterns.

These things must be considered, lest we pollute ourselves by the society of those defending impious cults, lest we assent to them, lest we help them, etc.

II. Sentences are to be collected which exhort us to rebuke impious cults, as the first commandment says, You shall have no other gods.

Item, he who will not confess me before men, I will confound him before my heavenly Father.

Item, that the commandment of God is to be preferred to human laws and power. According to that, It is necessary to obey God rather than men.

Likewise concerning scandals, Let them alone, they are blind and leaders of the blind.

4 Kings not only commanded that honor be held for the statue, but added a manifest blasphemy, by which he prefers his own power before the divine, and at all times tyrants mock the pious, expecting divine aid. Because they are either Epicureans, or they persuade themselves that they are the chief members of the people of God, just as the Pharisees thought of themselves.

God, however, for the most part vindicates the glory of his name with notable examples and bodily liberation, as4. Reg. 19.concerning Sennacherib, You have raged against me, your pride has ascended to my ears, etc.

And after the mockings in the history of the passion of Christ, immediately follow earthquakes, an eclipse, and the very nature of things testifies that it abhors those blasphemies. Thus a glorious vengeance is accustomed to follow.

Furthermore, the pious ought not to dissimulate such blasphemies, but openly refute them, just as they say here, It is our God whom we worship, who is able to deliver us. But they say explicitly, whom we worship, so that they might signify that they preach that He alone is God, who delivered this word and these forms of worship which the people of Israel had.

Wherefore let us learn that God is neither acknowledged nor worshipped except in that word which He Himself has set before us, and that forms of worship and opinions about God outside of this word are to be rejected, just as Christ teaches, saying, He who sees me, sees the Father.

V. Here one must inquire about the promises, and the nature of faith must be considered, how it expects deliverance. For the text openly proclaims the faith of those confessing these things. I answer, All promises require both, namely that we believe that God is both able and willing to help us. But concerning the will there is a distinction. The promise of grace demands that we believe by name that this itself is granted, namely the remission of sins and the imputation of righteousness, and eternal life. For here God has expressed His will, and these things are to be sought and expected by name without any condition, just as the text says, Everyone who believes in the Son has eternal life.

And this faith must always precede and govern the expectation of bodily benefits.

Although, however, the promises of bodily benefits also require that we believe, not only concerning the power of God, but also concerning the will, that God wills to help, yet faith concerning present liberation adds a condition, namely, if it shall please God, He will liberate me in the present. Nor does this condition fight with the nature of faith, but it provides obedience, Because these things are required at the same time, that we be prepared for obedience, that we may invoke, ask, & expect help, but according to the counsel of God.

Thus David prays, If the Lord shall will, He will bring back me, if He shall not will, let the will of the Lord be done.

Thus they say & in this place those confessing, It does not behoove us to answer, that is, Whether God is about to exempt us from this furnace we do not know, as if they were saying, The outcome & event & manner of liberation we permit to the counsel of God, we are also prepared for obedience. But we defend the glory of God, we affirm that He is able to liberate us, & that this is truly the God whom we worship, that is, who has handed down to us the word & worship, & meanwhile their faith nevertheless expects from God whatever consolation & help, according to the counsel of God.

VI. And it must be diligently observed here that bodily afflictions are imposed upon us for this reason, that we may be stirred up to invocation and faith. As it is said Esaiae 26. Distress is your discipline for them to cry out. Therefore, one must not indulge in distrust, nor think that invocation is done in vain, or that the promises are void, even if that condition is added. For God delivers when we call upon Him, so that He may show us the testimonies of His presence. As He says in the Psalm, I will deliver you, and you shall glorify me.

And when He does not wish to remove the present calamity, He nevertheless adds consolation, wherefore the promise is never void. Christ therefore commands us to pray incessantly, that is, even if deliverance is deferred so that we may be exercised, nevertheless do not cease to pray, because invocation is not void.

But this is difficult for reason to do, when God does not deliver, spirits are broken, they think they are not heard, they begin to be indignant and to fall away entirely from God, just as Saul fell away.

Faith ought to resist this temptation, so that it may grow and ask more ardently, and await help, even if it is not immediately, or entirely removed the present calamity. For because God hears this invocation, He will bring aid either by present consolation and mitigation of the calamity, or by establishing a good outcome through His wonderful counsel, just as Paul says, God is faithful, who with the temptation will provide the outcome &c.

VII. A miracle was performed, so that the king might be converted to the acknowledgment of God, who, having been converted, also issues a confession, and prohibits blasphemies.

Here let us learn that it pertains to the duty of princes, to abolish impious doctrine and impious worship, and to take care that pious doctrine and pious worship be set forth. For the magistrate is the guardian of the whole law, as far as it pertains to discipline. Just as he prohibits murders, even if he does not change hearts, so he ought to prohibit external idolatry, blasphemies, and impious worship.

For although the magistrate is not the ministry of the spirit, that is, although he does not convert hearts, nevertheless he is the minister of maintaining the external discipline of the whole law, and as a chief member of the Church, he ought to assist the remaining members, so that pious doctrine may be propagated.

Latin:

DE PRIMO LOCO.

CUM magnitudo harum rerum non possit verbis explicari, sum brevior, & tantum admonere lectores cupio. Primum igitur hîc discant pii, ingeus & horribile peccatum esse contra primum praeceptum, instituere & defendere cultus sine verbo dei.

Ac sciamus non unum Nabogdonosor hîc significari, sed idolatriam totius mundi. Sicut Nabogdonosor horribili audacia, & tamen summa opinione religionis, instituit cultum, ita multi fecerunt aliis temporibus. Imperiae plaerunque defendunt idolatriam. In Ecclesia verò impii pontifices regna sibi constituunt, & muniunt praetextu novorum cultuum, sicut Ieroboam fecit. Et eò spectavit consilium Nabogdonosor, ut suo cultu consociaret gentes 2c.

Ideo cogitandum est, ingens peccatum esse pontificum & principum, qui defendunt cultus institutos sine verbo dei, & pugnantes cum verbo dei, Missam, monachatus, & alias traditiones. Ut Esaiae 29.dicitur, Miscuit vobis dominus spiritum soporis, & c. quia coluerunt me mandatis hominum, &c.

EtIere. 2.Duo mala fecit populus, Me dereliquerunt fontem, & sibi fecerunt cisternas.

Haec cogitanda sunt, ne nos polluamus societate defendentium impios cultus, ne eis assentiamur, ne eos adivuemus, &c.

II. Colligendae sunt sententiae quae hortantur nos, ut reprehendamus impios cultus, ut primum praeceptum ait, Non habebis deos alienos.

Item, qui non confitebitur me coram hominibus, confundam eum coram patre caelesti.

Item, quòd anteferendum sit mandatum dei legibus humanis, & potestati. Iuxta illud, Oportet deo magis obedire quàm hominibus.

Item de scandalis, Sinite illos, caeci sunt & duces caecorum.

IIII. REX non solum praecepit honorem statuae haberi, sed addidit manifestam blasphemiam, qua suam potentiam antefert divinae, & omnibus temporibus tyranni derident pios, expectantes auxilium divinum. Quia aut sunt Epicuraei, aut sibi persuadent se esse praecipua membra populi dei, sicut Pharisaei de sese sentiebant.

Deus autem plaerunque insignibus exemplis & corporali liberatione vin dicat gloriam nominis sui, ut4. Reg. 19.de Senacherib, Insanisti adversus me, superbia tua ascendit ad aures meas, &c.

Et post subsannationes in historia passionis Christi, statim sequuntur terraemotus, Eclipsis, & ipsa rerum natura testatur, se abhorrere ab istis blasphemiis. Ita solet sequi gloriosa vindicta.

Porro pii tales blasphemias dissimulare non debent, sed apertè refutare, sicut hîc dicunt, Est deus noster quem colimus, qui potest nos liberare. Disertè autem dicunt, quem colimus, ut significent, se praedicare illum solum esse deum, qui hoc verbum & hos cultus tradidit, quos populus Israel habebat.

Quare discamus deum non agnosci neque coli nisi in illo verbo, quod ipse nobis proposuit, & repudiandos esse cultus & opiniones de deo, extra hoc verbum, sicut docet Christus inquiens, Qui me videt, videt patrem.

V. Hîc de promissionibus quaerendum est, & natura fidei consideranda, quomodo expectet liberationem. Nam textus aperte praedicat fidem horum confitentium. Respondeo, Promissiones omnes requirunt utrunque, scilicet ut credamus deum & posse & velle nobis opitulari. Sed de voluntate discrimen est. Promissio gratiae postulat, ut nominatim credamus hoc ipsum tribui, videlicet remissionem peccatorum & imputationem iustitiae, & vitam aeternam. Nam hîc expressit deus suam voluntatem, & haec petenda & expectanda sunt nominatim sine aliqua conditione, sicut textus inquit, Omnis qui credit in filium, habet vitam aeternam.

Et haec fides semper debet praecedere & gubernare expectationem corporalium beneficiorum.

Quanquam autem promissiones corporalium beneficiorum etiam requirunt, ut credamus, non solum de potentia dei, sed etiam de voluntate, quod deus velit ivuare, tamen fides de praesenti liberatione addit conditionem, scilicet, si deo placuerit, me in praesentia liberabit. Neque haec conditio pugnat cum natura fidei, sed praestat obedientiam, Quia simul requiruntur ista, ut simus parati ad obedientiam, ut invocemus, petamus, & expectemus auxilium, sed iuxta consilium dei.

Ita orat David, Si dominus volet, reducet me, si non volet, fiat voluntas domini.

Ita dicunt & hoc loco isti confitentes, Non oportet nos respondere, id est, An deus sit nos exempturus ex hoc camino non scimus, quasi dicant, Exitum & eventum & modum liberationis consilio dei permittimus, sumus etiam parati ad obedientiam. Sed gloriam dei defendimus, affirmamus eum nos posse liberare, & hunc verè deum esse quem colimus, id est, qui nobis verbum & cultus tradidit, & interea ipsorum fides tamen expectat à deo qualemcunque consolationem & auxilium, iuxta consilium dei.

VI. Ac diligenter hîc observandum est, ideo nobis imponi afflictiones corporales, ut excitemur ad invocationem & fidem. SicutEsaiae 26.dicitur, Angustia ad clamandum disciplina tua eis. Ideo non est indulgendum diffidentiae, nec cogitandum, frustra fieri invocationem, aut promissiones esse irritas, etiamsi illa conditio additur. Liberat enim deus, cum invocamus, ut nobis testimonia ostendat suae praesentiae. Sicut inquit in Psalmo, Eripiam te, & glorificabis me.

Et cum non vult praesentem calamitatem tollere, tamen addit consolationem, Quare nunquam promissio est irrita. Iubet igitur Christus incessanter orare, id est, etsi differtur liberatio ut exerceamur, tamen orare non desinas, quia invocatio non est irrita.

Sed hoc agere difficile est rationi, cum deus non liberat, franguntur animi, existimant se non exaudiri, incipiunt indignari ac prorsus deficere à deo, sicut Saul defecit.

Huic tentationi fides repugnare debet, ut crescat & ardentius petat, & expectet auxilium, etiamsi non statim, aut prorsus tollitur praesens calamitas. Nam quia deus hanc invocationem exaudit, opitulabitur vel praesenti consolatione ac mitigatione calamitatis, vel constituens suo mirabili consilio bonum eventum, sicut Paulus inquit, Fidelis deus, qui dat cum tentatione eventum &c.

VII. Miraculum editum est, ut rex converteretur ad agnitionem dei, qui conversus etiam edit confessionem, & prohibet blasphemias.

Hîc discamus ad officium principum pertinere, ut impiam doctrinam & impios cultus aboleant, & curent piam doctrinam, & pios cultus proponi. Nam magistratus est custos totius legis, quod ad disciplinam attinet. Sicut prohibet homicidia, etiamsi non mutet corda, ita prohibere debet externam idolatriam blasphemias, impios cultus.

Quanquam enim magistratus non est ministerium spiritus, id est, quanquam non convertit corda, tamen est minister tuendae externae disciplinae totius legis, & ut membrum Ecclesiae praecipuum, adivuare reliqua membra debet, ut pia doctrina propagetur.

## 4:1 Argument
CHAPTER IV.

I Nebuchadnezzar was at rest in my house, and flourishing in my palace, I saw a dream which frightened me, and my thoughts upon my bed, and the visions of my head troubled me. And by me a decree was proposed, that all the wise men of Babylon should be brought in before me, and that they should show me the interpretation of the dream. Then came in the soothsayers, the magicians, the Chaldeans, and the astrologers, and I told the dream before them, and they did not show me the interpretation thereof: until at last Daniel came in before me, whose name is Belteshazzar according to the name of my god, who has the spirit of the holy gods in himself, and I spoke the dream before him. Belteshazzar prince of the soothsayers, because I know that the holy spirit you have the spirit of the holy gods in you, and every mystery is not impossible for you: tell me the visions of my dreams which I saw, and the interpretation of them. The vision of my head in my bed, I was seeing, and behold, a tree in the midst of the earth, and the height thereof was exceeding great. The tree was great, and strong, and the height thereof reached unto heaven, the sight thereof was even to the ends of all the earth. Its leaves were most beautiful, and its fruit exceeding much, and in it was food for all: under it the animals and the beasts dwelt, and in its branches the birds of the air had their habitation, and all flesh was fed from it. I was seeing in the vision of my head upon my bed, and behold, a watcher and a holy one came down from heaven: He cried aloud, and said thus: Cut down the tree, and cut off its branches, shake off its leaves, and scatter its fruit, let the beasts flee that are under it, and the birds from of its branches. Nevertheless, leave the germ of its roots in the earth, and let it be bound with a band of iron and bronze among the grasses that are outside, and let it be drenched with the dew of heaven, and let its portion be with the wild beasts in the grass of the earth. Let his heart be changed from a human one, and let the heart of a beast be given to him, and let seven times pass over him. By the sentence of the watchers is the decree, and the word of the holy ones, and the petition, until the living know that the Most High rules in the kingdom of men, and to whomever He wills, He will give it, and He will set the lowliest man over it. This dream I, King Nebuchadnezzar, saw: you, therefore, Belteshazzar, tell the interpretation quickly, because all the wise men of my kingdom are not able to declare the solution to me: but you are able, because the spirit of the holy gods is in you. Then Daniel, whose name was Belteshazzar, began to think silently within himself as if for one hour, and his thoughts were troubling him. But the king answering, said: Baltassar, let not the dream and the interpretation thereof trouble thee. Baltassar answered, and said: My lord, may the dream be to them that hate thee, and the interpretation thereof to thine enemies. The tree which thou sawest high and robust, whose height reacheth to the heaven, and the sight thereof unto all the earth, and the branches thereof most beautiful, and the fruit thereof exceeding much, and food for all in it, under it dwelling the beasts of the field, and in the branches thereof dwelling the birds of the heaven: Thou art the king, who hast been magnified, and hast grown strong, and thy greatness hath grown, and reached even unto the heaven, and thy power unto the ends of the whole earth. But whereas the king saw a watcher, and a holy one descend from heaven, and say: Cut down the tree, and destroy it, yet leave the germ of the roots thereof in the earth, and let it be bound with iron and with the dew of heaven let him be sprinkled, and let his pasture be with the wild beasts, until seven times are changed over him: this is the interpretation of the sentence of the most high, which has come upon my lord the king: They shall cast you out from men, and your dwelling shall be with the beasts and the wild animals, and you shall eat hay like an ox, and you shall be sprinkled with the dew of heaven: and seven times shall be changed over you, until you know that the most high rules over the kingdom of men, and gives it to whomsoever he will. But that he commanded that the stump of its roots, that is, of the tree, should be left: your kingdom shall remain for you after you have known that the power is heavenly. Wherefore, O king, let my counsel be pleasing to you, and redeem your sins with alms, and your iniquities with mercies to the poor, * perhaps God will forgive your offenses. All these things came upon Nabuchodonosor king. After the end of twelve months, he was walking in the palace of Babylon. And the king answered, and said: Is not this the great city of Babylon, which I have built into the house of the kingdom, by the strength of my might, and in the glory of my excellence? And while the word was yet in the king's mouth, a voice came down from heaven: To thee it is spoken, O King Nebuchadnezzar, Thy kingdom shall pass from thee, and they shall cast thee out from men, and thy dwelling shall be with beasts and wild animals, thou shalt eat grass like an ox, and seven times shall pass over thee, until thou knowest that the Most High ruleth in the kingdom of men, and giveth it to whomsoever he will. The same hour the word was fulfilled upon Nebuchadnezzar, and he was cast out from among men, and did eat grass as an ox, and his body was wet with the dew of heaven, until his hairs grew like the feathers of eagles, and his nails like the nails of birds. Therefore after the end of the days I Nebuchadnezzar lifted up my eyes to heaven, and my sense was restored to me, and I blessed the most high, and I praised and glorified him that liveth for ever, because his power is an everlasting power, and his kingdom is to generation and generation: And all the inhabitants of the earth are reputed as nothing before him: for he doth according to his will, as well in the powers of heaven, as among the inhabitants of the earth: and there is none that can resist his hand, and say to him: Why hast thou done it? At the same time my sense returned to me, and I came to the honor of my kingdom, and I attained to my beauty, and my figure returned to me, and my nobles, and my magistrates sought me, and I was restored to my kingdom, and greater magnificence was added to me. Now therefore I Nebuchadnezzar I praise and magnify, and glorify the King of heaven, because all His works are true, and all His ways are judgments, and those who walk in pride He is able to humble.

Latin:

CAPUT IIII.

EGO Nabuchodonosor quietus eram in domo mea, & florens in palatio meo, somnium vidi quod perterruit me, & cogitationes meae in stratu meo, & visiones capitis mei conturbaverunt me. Et per me propositum est decretum, ut introducerentur in conspectu meo cuncti sapientes Babylonis, & ut solutionem somnii indicarent mihi. Tunc ingrediebantur arioli, magi, Chaldaei, & aruspices, et somnium narravi in conspectu eorum, & solutionem eius non indicaverunt mihi: donec tandem ingressus est in conspectu meo Daniel, cui nomen Baltassar secundum nomen dei mei, qui habet spiritum deorum sanctorum in semetipso, & somnium coram ipso locutus sum. Baltassar princeps ariolorum, quoniam ego scio quòd spiritum sancto rum deorum habeas in te, & omne sacramentum non est impossibile tibi: visiones somniorum meorum quas vidi, et solutionem earum narra. Visio capitis mei in cubili meo, Videbam, & ecce, arbor in medio terrae, & altitudo eius nimia. Magna arbor, & fortis, & proceritas eius contingens caelum, aspectus illius erat usque ad terminos universae terrae. Folia eius pulcherrima, & fructus eius nimius, & esca universorum in ea: subter eam habitabant animalia & bestiae, & in ramis eius conversabantur volucres caeli, & ex ea vescebatur omnis caro. Videbam in visione capitis mei super stratum meum, & ecce, vigil & sanctus de caelo descendit: Clamavit fortiter, & sic ait: Succidite arborem, & praecidite ramos eius, excutite folia eius, & dispergite fructus eius, fugiant bestiae quae subter eam sunt, & volucres de ramis eius. Veruntamen germen radicum eius in terra sinite, & alligetur vinculo ferreo et aereo in herbis quae foris sunt, & rore caeli tingatur, & cum feris pars eius in herba terrae. Cor eius ab humano commutetur, & cor ferae detur ei, & septem tempora mutentur super eum. In sententia vigilum decretum est, & sermo sanctorum, & petitio, donec cognoscant viventes quoniam dominatur excelsus in regno hominum, & cuicunque voluerit, dabit illud, & humilimum hominem constituerit super eum. Hoc somnium vidi ego Nabuchodonosor rex: tu ergo Baltassar interpretationem narra festinus, quia omnes sapientes regni mei non queunt solutionem edicere mihi: tu autem potes, quia spiritus deorum sanctorum in te est. Tunc Daniel, cuius nomen Baltassar, coepit intra semetipsum tacitus cogitare quasi una hora, & cogitationes eius conturbabant eum. Respondens autem rex, ait: Baltassar, somnium & interpraetatio eius non conturbent te. Respondit Baltassar, & dixit: Domine mi, somnium his qui te oderunt, & interpretatio eius hostibus tuis sit. Arborem quam vidisti sublimem atque robustam, cuius altitudo pertingit ad caelum, & aspectus illius in omnem terram, & rami eius pulcherrimi, & fructus eius nimius, & esca omnium in ea, subter eam habitantes bestiae agri, & in ramis eius commorantes aves caeli: Tu es rex, qui magnificatus es, & invaluisti, & magnitudo tua crevit, & pervenit usque ad caelum, & potestas tua in terminos universae terrae. Quôd autem vidit rex vigilem, & sanctum descendere de caelo, & dicere: Succidite arborem, & dissipate illam, attamen germen radicum eius in terra dimittite, & vinciatur ferro & aere in herbis foris, & rore caeli conspergatur, & cum feris sit pabulum eius, donec septem tempora mutentur super eum: haec est interpretatio sententiae altissimi, quae pervenit super dominum meum regem: Eiicient te ab hominibus, & cum bestiis ferisque erit habitatio tua, & foenum ut bos comedes, & rore caeli infundêris: septem quoque tempora mutabuntur super te, donec sciasque dominetur excelsus super regnum hominum, & cuicunque voluerit, det illud. Quòd autem praecepit ut relinqueretur germen radicum eius, id est arboris: regnum tuum tibi manebit postquam cognoveris potestatem esse caelestem. Quam ob rem rex, consilium meum placeat tibi, & peccata tua eleemosynis redime, & inquitates tuas misericordiis pauperum, * forsitan ignoscet deus delictis tuis. Omnia haec venerunt super Nabuchodonosor regem. Post finem mensium duodecim, in aula Babylonis deambulabat. Responditque rex, & ait: Nonne haec est Babylon civitas magna, quam ego aedificavi in domum regni, in robore fortitudinis meae, & in gloria decoris mei? Cumque sermo adhuc esset in ore regis, vox de caelo ruit: Tibi dicitur Nabuchodonosor rex, Regnum tuum transibit à te, & ab hominibus eiicient te, & cum bestiis & feris erit habitatio tua, foenum quasi bos comedes, & septem tempora mutabuntur super te, donec scias quòd dominetur excelsus in regno hominum, & cuicunque voluerit, det illud. Eadem hora sermo completus est super Nabuchodonosor, & ex hominibus abiectus est, & foenum ut bos comedit, & rore caeli corpus eius infectum est, donec capilli eius in similitudinem aquilarum crescerent, & ungues eius quasi ungues avium. Igitur post finem dierum ego Nabuchodonosor oculos meos ad caelum levavi, & sensus meus redditus est mihi, & altissimo benedixi, & viventem in sempiternum laudavi, & glorificavi, quia potestas eius potestas sempiterna, & regnum eius in generatione & generationem: Et omnes habitatores terrae apud eum in nihilum reputati sunt: iuxta voluntatem enim suam facit tam in virtutibus caeli quàm in habitatoribus terrae: & non est qui resistat manui eius, & dicat ei: Quare fecisti? In ipso tempore sensus rneus reversus est ad me, & ad honorem regni mei, decoremque perveni, & figura mea reversa est ad me, & optimates mei, & magistratus mei requisierunt me, & in regno meo restitutus sum, et magnificentia mea amplior addita est mihi. Nunc igitur ego Nabuchodonosor laudo & magnifico, & glorifico regem caeli, quia omnia opera eius vera, & omnes viae eius iudicia, & gradientes in superbia potest humiliare.

## 4:4 The Fourth Chapter of the Loci
THE FOURTH CHAPTER OF THE LOCI.

I. EXAMPLE, which indicates that those who have been converted are afterwards subjected to horrendous afflictions. Just as King Nebuchadnezzar after his conversion fell here into the most bitter calamity. According to that, Judgment begins from the house of God. Likewise, Humble yourselves under the mighty hand of God. Nor is there any doubt that there were many who said that he was paying the penalties for a changed religion.

II. The punishment of security, pride, and negligence in prosperous things, because the text says, He was flourishing in the palace. And afterwards, Is not this great Babylon, that I have built? And the angel himself says, that this example is proposed, so that all nations may acknowledge God, and know that he resists the proud, and protects the humble.

III The image of the kingdom, the King, is a tree, under which living creatures feed. Therefore, princes should not be tyrants, but should provide shade, that is, defense for their subjects, and grant them that they may be able to seek their livelihood and enjoy their own possessions. Likewise, an angel is added as a guardian of the king.

IV. Mitigation of punishment. Daniel commands the king to do penance, therefore threats can be mitigated, as the example of Nineveh testifies. And it is written, As I live, I do not desire the death of the sinner, but that he be converted and live. Nor are the disputes about Stoic fate to be brought in here.

V. A sermon on penance, Free yourself from sins through justice and benefits toward the oppressed, and there will be long-suffering for your salvation, or there will be a healing of your offenses.

This sermon embraces both, the doctrine of contrition, namely the law, which convicts of sins, and the promise of grace or absolution.

VI Testimony that the Gospel pertains to the nations. Likewise concerning Evangelical liberty, that the observances of the law were not necessary for grace.

VII Since the promise is delivered here, there will be healing, it follows necessarily that faith is required. Wherefore they do not understand this saying, who twist it against the doctrine concerning faith.

VIII. Since faith does not rely upon our dignity, but looks upon the promise, it is necessary that it be understood that the remission is gratuitous.

IX. Nor is it enough for the saying to be understood concerning the remission of temporal punishment, because punishments are not mitigated without true repentance and the remission of guilt. Therefore, let the remission of guilt be understood here first.

X. After the sermon, an interval of a whole year is granted to the king for repentance, but security and pride remain, therefore punishment follows.

XI. Punishments are inflicted so that they may admonish us concerning repentance. Just as Paul says, We are chastened by the Lord, that we may not be condemned with this world.

XII. After the punishment, the king here performs true contrition, tion, faith, confession, and invocation. It is the voice of contrition, He humbles those who walk in pride. Of faith, I have lifted up my eyes to heaven. Of invocation and confession, I praise and glorify the king of heaven.

Thus, therefore, in punishments the wrath of God against sin must be acknowledged, and again the mercy must be acknowledged, that God punishes, not that He may destroy, but that He may call to repentance, etc.

XIII. Let both consider, especially princes, then also others, namely, that punishments hang over the secure, the proud, and those endowed with excellent gifts. As Christ says, That which is sublime among men is an abomination before God.

Again, also let the example of mercy be considered, that so great an idolater, so proud a tyrant, is received into grace, and indeed is even freed from corporal punishments.

And this example consoles the pious against the temptation concerning the magnitude of sin, that they may learn that grace abounds over sin, as Paul says.

The prophet has briefly embraced the image and duties of a pious prince, he requires first piety, the knowledge of God, and faith, then beneficence toward the oppressed, that is, justice in administration, the defense of the pious and of the Church against tyrants.

Latin:

LOCI IIII. CAPITIS.

I. EXEMPLUM, quod indicat conversos postea subiici horrendis afflictionibus. Sicut rex Nabogdonosor post conversionem hîc in acerbissimam calamitatem incidit. Iuxta illud, Iudicium à domo dei incipit. Item, Humiliamini sub potenti manu dei. Nec dubium est, fuisse multos, qui dixerunt eum poenas dare mutatae religionis.

II. Poena securitatis, superbiae, & negligentiae in secundis rebus, quia textus dicit, Erat florens in palatio. Et postea, Nonne haec est Babylon, quam ego aedificavi? Et angelus ipse dicit, hoc exemplum proponi, ut omnes gentes deum agnoscant, & sciant eum resistere superbis, & humiles tueri.

III Imago regni Rex est arbor, sub qua pascuntur animantia. Ergo principes non sint tyranni, sed praebeant umbram, id est, defensionem subditis, & concedant eis, ut quaerere victum, & frui suis possint. Item, angelus additur custos regis.

IIII. Mitigatio poenae. Daniel iubet regem agere paenitentiam, ergo comminationes mitigari possunt, ut testatur exemplum Ninives. Et scriptum est, Vivo ego, nolo mortem peccatoris, sed ut convertatur & vivat. Nec sunt huc afferendae disputationes de Stoico fato.

V. Concio de paenitentia, Libera te à peccatis per iustitiam & beneficia erga oppressos, & erit longanimitas ad tuam salutem, vel erit sanatio delictorum tuorum.

Haec concio utrunque complectur, doctrinam contritionis, scilicet legem, quae arguit peccata, & promissionem gratiae seu absolutionem.

VI Testimonium quod Evangelium pertineat ad gentes. Item de libertate Evangelica, quòd cultus legis non fuerint necessarii ad gratiam.

VII Cum hîc tradatur-promissio, erit sanatio, necessario sequitur requiri fidem. Quare non intelligunt hoc dictum, qui detorquent contra doctrinam de fide.

VIII. Cum fides non nitatur nostra dignitate, sed intueatur promissionem, necesse est intelligi, gratuitam esse remissionem.

IX. Nec satis est intelligi dictum de remissione poenae temporalis, quia poenae non mitigantur sine vera paenitentia & remissione culpae. Ideo prius hîc intelligatur remissio culpae.

X. Post concionem conceditur intervallum regi ad paenitentiam totus annus, sed manent securitas & superbia, ideo sequitur poena.

XI. Poenae infliguntur, ut admoneant nos de paenitentia. Sicut unquit Paulus, A domino corripimur, ne cum hoc mundo damnemur.

XII. Post poenam hîc rex praestat veram contritionem, tionem, fidem, consessionem & invocationem. Vox est contritionis, Gradientes in superbia ipse humiliat. Fidei, Levavi oculos meos ad caelum. Invocationis & confessionis, Laudo et glorifico regem caeli.

Sic igitur in poenis agnoscenda est ira dei adversus peccatum, Et rursus agnoscenda misericordia, quòd puniat deus, non ut perdat, sed ut vo cet ad paenitentiam, &c.

XIII. Utrunque considerent, praescipuè principes, deinde & alii, videlicet, poenas impendere securis, superbis, & praeditis excellentibus donis. Sicut inquit Christus, Abominatio est coram deo, quic quid est sublime apud homines.

Rursus etiam misericordiae exemplum consideretur, quod tantus idolatra, tam superbus tyrannus, recipitur in gratiam, & quidem liberatur etiam corporalibus poenis.

Et consolatur pios hoc exemplum contra tentationem de magnitudine peccati, ut discant exuberare gratiam supra peccatum, ut inquit Paulus.

Breviter complexus est propheta imaginem & offitia pii principis, requirit primum pietatem, agnitionem dei, & fidem, Deinde beneficentiam erga oppressos, hoc est, iustitiam in administratione, defensionem piorum & Ecclesiae adversus tyrannos.

## 4:4 Concerning the Second Topic
CONCERNING THE SECOND TOPIC.

THE TEXT shows the sins of the king to have been security and pride, both of which are hidden, and the sins of many saints, and they creep in more easily in favorable circumstances, and especially in excellent men, who have admirable successes, immense glory, great gifts of God. When the mind is delighted by these advantages, and does not perceive the signs of the wrath of God, the fear of the wrath and divine judgment is gradually extinguished; the mind begins to admire and love these goods immoderately, and forgets God, nor does it think that those successes are from God, but looks upon its own wisdom, its own virtues, its own power. By the help of these things it judges that it is performing and accomplishing such great things, and trusts that it will perform similar ones forever. It trusts that it is fortified by these protections against all the accidents of fortune, just as Pompey used to say that by the stamping of his foot he would fill all of Italy with an army. And Timotheus, the Athenian general, said in an assembly, This I have done, not fortune. After that utterance, they write that nothing succeeded for him afterward.

Furthermore, a haughty mind with that confidence in itself, that it can accomplish great things by itself, that by its own wisdom it is fortified against all accidents, begins to despise other men, loves and admires itself, and permits itself the license of many crimes. It wishes to be worshipped and magnified, and becomes harsh to those who do not worship it. Illustrious examples are seen in Alexander, Pompey, and Caesar. They think that by their own wisdom, their own virtue, and power, so many victories were won, and that they are invincible. Therefore they despise others, permit themselves license, and rush into various crimes, and wish to be worshipped, by no means with familiar association.

Thus also negligence creeps upon the saints, as upon Samson and David, because David in the present does not feel the signs of the wrath of God, fears judgment less, and yields to the allurements of pleasures.

But for the most part in great men, pride accompanies security, that is, confidence in one's own wisdom, justice, power, and forgetfulness of God; afterwards, anger, cruelty, and other vices follow. Since, however, God especially punishes pride, that is, confidence in one's own virtue, horribly, the examples of all histories contain the saddest falls of great men, such as Saul, Hercules, Theseus, Miltiades, Themistocles, Pompey, Caesar, &c.

And among Euripides, Oedipus says, I who excelled all kings in wisdom, now what am I, if not a vanishing shadow, or a corpse, or a fleeting dream? This is the voice of a great king, dragged down from the highest rank into the most bitter calamities.

Such was the image of David in the rebellion, when he himself considered for what great crime he was paying the penalty.

Such was the image of this Babylonian king, who not only becomes furious, but so that his madness might be more celebrated, wanders among the beasts. In this calamity he not only lost his kingdom and authority, but what is much more, his mind as well.

Let us therefore consider what his sin is, so that we may learn to fear God, and not to nourish security and pride, which beget many other sins and punishments, as it is written, The people sat to eat and to drink, and rose up to play. Likewise, when they shall say, Peace and security, &c.

Latin:

DE SECUNDO LOCO.

TEXTUS ostendit peccata regis fuisse, securitatem & superbiam, quae ambo sunt arcana, & multorum sanctorum peccata, & facilius obrepunt in rebus secundis, ac praecipue excellentibus viris, qui habent admirabiles successus, ingentem gloriam, magna dei dona. His commodis cum delectatur animus, nec sentit significationes irae dei, paulatim extinguitur timor irae & iudicii divini incipit animus immodice mirari & amare haec bona, & obliviscitur dei, nec cogitat successus illos à deo esse, sed intuetur suam sapientiam, suas virtutes, suam potentiam. Harum rerum ope iudicat se tanta res gerere & efficere, & confidit similes se perpetuò gesturum esse. Confidit se munitum esse his praesidiis contra omnes fortunae casus, sicut Pompeius dicebat se supplosione pedis Italiam totam completurum esse exercitu. Et Timotheus dux Atheniensis in concione dixit, Hoc ego feci, non fortuna. Post eam vocem scribunt ei postea nihil successisse.

Porrò clatus animus illa fiducia sui, quod per sese res magnas efficiat, quòd sua sapientia sit munitus adversus omnes casus, incipit alios homines contemnere, sese amat, & admiratur, sibi permittit licentiam multorum scelerum. Vult coli & magnifieri, & fit asper non colentibus. Exempla illustria conspiciuntur in Alexandro, Pompeio & Caesare. Existimant sua sapientia, sua virtute & potentia, tot victorias partas esse, & se invictos esse. Ideo contemuunt caeteros, sibi permittunt licentiam, & ruunt in varia scelera, volunt coli nequaquam familiari consuetudine.

Sic etiam sanctis obrepit negligentia, ut Samsoni & Davidi, quia David in praesentia non sentit significationes irae dei, minus timet iudicium, & obsequitur illecebris voluptatum.

Plaerunque autem in magnis viris securitatem comitatur superbia, id est, fiducia propriae sapientiae, iustitiae potentiae oblivio dei, postea accedunt iracundia, crudelitas, & alia vitia. Cum autem deus praecipuè superbiam, id est, fiduciam propriae virtutis horribiliter puniat, exempla omnium historiarum continent tristissimos casus magnorum virorum, ut Saulis, Herculis, Thesei, Miltiadae, Themistoclis, Pompei, Caesaris, &c.

Et apud Euripidem inquit Oedipus, Ego qui excellui omnes reges sapientia, nunc quid sum, nisi evanescens umbra, vel cadaver, vel volatile somnium? Haec est magni regis vox, ex summo gradu detracti, in acerbissimas calamitates.

Talis fuit imago Davidis in seditione, cum ipse cogitaret quanti sceleris poenas daret.

Talis fuit imago huius Babylonici regis, qui non solum fit furiosus, sed ut insania sit celebratior, vagatur inter bestias. In hac calamitate non solum amisit regnum, & autoritatem, sed quod multo plus est, mentem etiam.

Consideremus igitur quale sit eius peccatum, ut discamus timere deum, & non alere securitatem & superbiam, quae pariunt multa alia peccata & poenas, sicut scriptum est, Sedit populus manducare & bibere, & surrexit ludere. Item, cum dixerint, Pax & securitas, &c.

## 4:4 Concerning the Fourth Place
CONCERNING THE FOURTH PLACE.

A SERMON of repentance must be diligently considered. For as I said, both parts are briefly proposed here, the doctrine of the law and the promise.

The first part is a sermon of the law, Remove your sins by righteousness, and by benefits toward the miserable. This is the voice of the law, which commands concerning repentance or contrition. Nor is it anything other than what Isaiah says, Cease to do evil, and learn to do good. Likewise, if you shall do repentance, your sins are forgiven &c.

It requires repentance, yet it does not hold that works merit forgiveness. For repentance or contrition is necessary, but it is not the cause nor the merit of the forgiveness of sins. Nor does Daniel say this, but he commands cessation. Take away your sins by righteousness, that is, cease, stop sinning, and begin to do just things. This is a simple and not a perplexed interpretation, but it is manifest that this is the voice of the law, and a sermon concerning repentance or contrition.

But with the adversaries omitted, let us speak of the matter. Although Daniel does not wish for works to merit forgiveness, nevertheless, to this very sermon there is an implicit promise concerning the forgiveness of sins. For unless forgiveness were proposed, he would not say, Take away your sins. For certainly he wishes this, that you change yourself, and being converted you shall have forgiveness. Thus, promises are often implicit in the doctrine of works, And yet it is necessary to distinguish faith from works, and from merit, just asIsaiah chapter 1.For faith cannot exist if it relies upon the condition of our worthiness.

But the following part expressly adds the promise, Behold, there shall be a healing of your transgressions. Furthermore, every promise requires faith, and indeed a promise of reconciliation requires faith, which establishes that sins are forgiven freely. Because it would become uncertain and would be shaken from souls if it depended upon the condition of our worthiness. Therefore, no one skilled can deny that faith is required here by the promise proposed. Wherever, therefore, a promise is delivered either openly or covertly, let us know that faith is required at the same time. Just as Paul teachesRom. 4.Therefore it is of faith freely, that the promise might be firm.

The adversaries foolishly understand these most serious discourses, nor do they distinguish promises from the law, nor do they understand the use of promises and the struggles of faith; they only dream that these are childish precepts about morals. Let us therefore lead our minds away from their ravings, and let us consider that a serious and most grave discourse is proposed here concerning the greatest matter, concerning the remission of sins, concerning eternal life.

But Daniel in this discourse has undoubtedly embraced the whole doctrine concerning the promises. For this absolution is not a human voice, but a divine sentence, taken from the promises, in which Daniel knew that on account of the coming Christ, the remission of sins was promised, and that the benefit did not pertain only to the Jews, but also to the Gentiles, that is, to all who believe in the promise concerning Christ. Nor was it of common wisdom to understand the promises in such a way that they also pertain to the Gentiles. And at the same time Daniel teaches us concerning the calling of the Gentiles, concerning the abrogation of the law, concerning gratuitous justification. For he shows that Jewish ceremonies are not righteousness, nor do they merit the remission of sins, because he does not impose these upon the king. He has embraced all these things in this example.

Latin:

DE QUARTO LOCO.

CONCIO paenitentiae diligenter consideranda est. Nam ut dixi, utraque pars hîc breviter proponitur, doctrina legis & promissio.

prima pars est concio legis, Remove peccata tua per iustitiam, & per beneficia erga miseros. Haec est vox legis, quae praecipit de paenitentia seu de contritione. Nec aliud est quàm quod dicit Esaias, Desinite malefacere, & discite recta facere. Item, si egeritis paenitentiam, peccata vestra remittuntur &c.

Requirit paenitentiam, nec tamen sentit opera mereri remissionem. Necessaria enim est paenitentia seu contritio, sed non est caussa nec meritum remissionis peccatorum. Nec hoc dicit Daniel, sed praecipit cessationem. Aufer peccata tua per iustitiam, id est, cessa, desine peccare, & incipe iusta facere. Haec est simplex & non perplexa interpretatio, manifestum est autem hanc esse vocem legis, & concionem ae paenitentia seu contritione.

Sed omissis adversariis, de re dicamus. Quanquam non vult Daniel opera mereri remissionem, tamen huic ipsi concioni est implicita promissio de remissione peccatorum. Nisi enim esset proposita remissio, non diceret, Aufer peccata tua. Certè enim hoc vult, mutes te, & conversus habebis remissionem. Ita saepe implicitae sunt promissiones doctrinae operum, & tamen discerni fidem ab operibus, & merito necesse est, sicutEsaiae capite 1.Non potest enim existere fides, si nitatur conditione nostrae dignitatis.

Sed sequens pars expresse addit promissionem, Ecce, erit sanatio delictorum tuorum. Porrò omnis promissio requirit fidem, & quidem promissio reconciliationis requirit fidem, quae statuat gratis condonari peccata. Quia fieret incerta & excuteratur animis, si penderet ex conditione dignitatis nostrae. Ergo nemo peritus negare potest, quin hîc requiratur fides proposita promissione. Ubicunque igitur vel aperte vel tectè promissio traditur, simul fidem requiri sciamus. Sicut docet PaulusRoma. 4.Ideo ex fide gratis, ut sit firma promissio.

Adversarii insulse intelligunt has gravissimas conciones, nec discernunt promissiones à lege, nec intelligunt usum promissionum & certamina fidei, tantum somniant haec esse puerilia praecepta de moribus. Ab ducamus igitur animos ab eorum deliramentis, & cogitemus, hîc proponi seriam & gravissimam concionem de re maxima, de remissione peccatorum, de vita aeterna.

Ac Daniel in hac concione haud dubiè complexus est totam doctrinam de promissionibus. Nam haec absolutio non est humana vox, sed sententia divina, ex promissionibus sumpta, in quibus norat Daniel propter venturum Christum, promissam esse remissionem peccatorum, & beneficium non tantum ad Iudaeos pertinere, sed etiam ad gentes, hoc est, ad omnes qui promissioni de Christo credunt. Nec fuit vulgaris sapientiae, sic intelligere promissiones, quod etiam ad gentes pertineant. Et sunul docet nos Daniel de vocatione gentium, de abrogatione legis, de gratuita iustificatione. Ostendit enim ceremonias Iudaicas non esse iustitiam, nec mereri remissionem peccatorum, quia has non imponit regi. Haec omnia complexus est in hoc exemplo.

## 4:4 Concerning the Fruits of Penitence or Good Works
CONCERNING THE FRUITS OF PENITENCE or good works.

BUT AS in conversion or penitence new obedience must always follow, just as John says, Bring forth fruits worthy of penitence, here Daniel adds the doctrine concerning good works. And first see what works he demands, namely not monastic works, not human traditions, but the works which God commands. And he preaches specifically concerning the office of one's calling. When he commands him to perform justice, universal obedience is to be understood, namely the acknowledgment of God, fear, faith, love of God, justice in ruling the empire, continence, and the other virtues which God demands.

But specifically the particle is added concerning the chief work of one's calling, for individuals ought especially to perform the works of their own calling. Just as Paul1. Thes. 4.commands individuals to excel in this endeavor, that they may do their duty. Likewise, Obedience is better than sacrifice. Therefore here Daniel preaches to the king concerning his own function, he commands him to show beneficence toward the oppressed, here he embraces generally judgments, punishments, wars, and every defense of the innocent.

But he especially admonishes him concerning the defense of the true Church, which was then being cruelly treated in exile by the impious. Therefore Daniel exhorts the king, that he may not allow the Church of God to be destroyed, but that he may preserve, adorn, and assist it with his benefits. This is the chief duty of kings and princes, as Isaiah says, Kings shall be your nursing fathers, etc.

Let us remember, therefore, this sermon, so that we may propose it to princes even at this time, when most unjust cruelty is exercised against the true church. For although not all tyrants can be bent toward piety, nevertheless pious princes are strengthened by these sayings of the prophets, and some enemies are recalled to the way, just as here Nebuchadnezzar, who although he had been an enemy, nevertheless is converted to the acknowledgment of God, and defends the pious. Daniel preaches especially here about due duties, and let us know that this royal sermon pertains to all times and all princes.

Let us know also that, that these works commanded by God are the true fruits of repentance, or new obedience, in which faith shines and is exercised. For because the king now adorns the Church, he shows a sign of confession, that he acknowledges there is one God, the creator of heaven and earth, who wishes to be acknowledged through the word which he had handed down to this one people Israel, who had given promises concerning the remission of sins, hears, and saves. He testifies that other gods and cults outside this word are to be rejected. In this confession, the king invited many to piety. He also exercised faith in dangers, because there is no doubt that there were impious princes, who opposed and plotted against him. But he himself endured these difficulties, and awaited defense from God.

Daniel preaches about these most serious matters, about repentance, promise, faith, about the true fruits of repentance, about the proper office of kings, about the exercises of faith in the dangers of administration.

But monks have defiled this passage in many ways, they transfer it to satisfactions and undue works. Because the king is afflicted here, they argue that sins are not remitted without satisfactions. Likewise, because the interpreter here names alms, they twist the saying to certain ceremonies of that office, and they pretend that they are satisfactions, and merit the remission of guilt and punishment. These things can easily be refuted.

First, therefore, it must be known that the whole delusion about satisfactions, which they call undue works, must be rejected, according to this saying, In vain do they worship me with the commandments of men. But after repentance, new obedience must follow, commanded by God. Alms also pertain to this, which are to be given according to the measure of one's means. And although these works do not merit the remission of guilt; or of eternal punishment, yet the promise of God is certain, and is to be believed, that God truly forgives those doing penance, believing, and calling upon him, and wills to take away or mitigate bodily punishments. These benefits are to be sought by faith and expected when we do penance. And there would be fewer public and private calamities, if we sought mitigation by this faith and did penance. According to that of Zechariah, Turn to me, namely doing penance, and believing and calling upon me, and I will turn to you, namely by giving benefits.

But as to what is objected concerning punishments, Adam, David, Uzziah, Manasseh, Nebuchadnezzar, and many are punished daily, Therefore since sins are not remitted without punishments, satisfaction is necessary. I answer, The consequence must be denied, for those punishments are neither satisfactions, nor compensations, nor do they merit the remission of guilt or of damnation, but they are singular works of God, by which he calls the fallen to penance or exercises the saints. Just as it is written, Judgment begins from the house of God. And although God wills those punishments to be examples to all, which may admonish all concerning the wrath and judgment of God, yet he spares many, as the Ninevites, and in the Gospel he forgives everywhere without punishments.

Examples of punishments are testimonies of the judgment of God, that God will truly at some time punish all the impious, as the first sermon says, If you do evil, your sin will rest, until it slips back. That is to say, although the punishment is deferred for some time, and you will be of a secure and idle mind, yet at length God will judge and punish.

AndExod. 32.But in the day of my visitation I will visit their sins. That is to say, although I defer punishments, yet do not on that account think that I forget crimes, I do not establish punishments by human counsel, but by my own, I give space for repentance, therefore I wish to be feared, and I call you to repentance.

Thus it must be thought concerning punishments, not that they are satisfactions, but that they are testimonies of the judgment of God, and call us to repentance or exercise the saints.

Other examples where punishments have been mitigated or removed are most sweet; consolations, which testify to those doing repentance, that the punishment is forgiven or mitigated, as Esaias cap. 1. teaches.

But the examples of the saints, such as Abel, Isaiah, Jeremiah, John the Baptist, and the like, are not They are not punishments, but exercises, as was said before, because it is necessary for the Church to be subject to the cross, so that it may acknowledge the magnitude of the sin clinging in the flesh, and again, with the flesh abolished, spiritual newness may be begun among afflictions.

I have added these things briefly concerning afflictions, so that readers, being warned, may reject the ravings about satisfactions, and may know that neither punishments divinely inflicted, nor our works, are satisfactions.

But here they object, the text says, Redeem, therefore it signifies that works are a price or merit. I answer briefly, the word signifies cessation, not compensation, as if he were to say, Cease from sins, and do rightly, or be liberated from sins, which is the same thing, Cease from sins, etc. But I will not be prolix in treating this grammatical cavil. Let other illustrious testimonies be opposed which speak of gratuitous remission, and of faith in Christ, and of the sacrifice of Christ.

Origen transformed this history into an altogether untimely allegory about the devil. He wrote that it is signified by the Babylonian king, the devil cast out through pride, etc. And they say he added an error about the liberation of the devil, but these portentous delusions are to be disapproved, and absurd allegories are to be fled.

Let us retain the history, and let us remember that an illustrious example of repentance is set forth, in which we are admonished concerning security, concerning punishments, concerning the gratuitous remission of sins, concerning the calling of the Gentiles, concerning the promise, concerning true faith, concerning the fruits of repentance, concerning the duties of princes, etc.

It is the voice of true faith, by which he here recognizes not only the power of God, but also His will, namely that He punishes the proud, and remits sins to those doing penance, and hears and saves those who call upon Him. This recognition is true faith, which he here professes, when he says, Those who walk in pride He humbles. Therefore he recognizes God not as idle, but as truly punishing.

He also adds, All His ways are judgments, that is, he proclaims God to be just, punishing those who despise Him, and receiving into grace those who do penance, just as the prophet had said to him. For one must look back to the prophet's sermon. The king recognizes that he was punished by God, and now liberated from punishment. Therefore he gives thanks for the liberation, and remembers the promise, he believes in the remission of sins etc.

Latin:

DE FRUCTIBUS PAE-nitentiae seu bonis operibus.

UT AUTEM in conversione seu poeni tentia semper sequi debet nova obedientia, sicut Ioannes inquit, Facite fructus dignos poeni tentiae hîc adiicit Daniel doctrinam de bonis operibus. Ac primum vide quae opera flagitet, videlicet non opera monastica, non humanas traditiones, sed opera quae deus praecipit. Et nominatim concionatur de offitio voca tionis. Cum iubet cum praestare iustitiam, intel ligenda est universalis obedientia, scilicet agnitio dei, timor, fides, dilectio dei, iustitia in regendo imperio, continentia & caeterae virtutes, quas deus postulat.

Nominatim verò additur particula de praecipuo opere vocationis, singuli enim praecipuè debent opera suae vocationis praestare. Sicut Paulus1. Thes. 4.iubet singulos in hoc studio excellere, ut suum officium faciant. Item, Melior est obedientia quàm victima. Ideo hîc Daniel regi de propria functione concionatur, iubet eum praestare beneficentiam erga oppressos, hîc complectitur generaliter iudicia, poenas, bella, & omnem defensionem innocentum.

Sed praecipue admonet eum de defensione verae Ecclesiae, quae tunc in exilio ab impiis crudeliter tractabatur. Hortatur ergo Daniel regem, ne sinat Ecclesiam dei deleri, sed ut eam conservet, ornet, & adivuet suis beneficiis, Hoc est praecipum munus regum, & principum, sicut inquit Esaias, Reges erunt nutritores tui, &c.

Meminerimus ergo hanc concionem, ut eam principibus etiam hoc tempore proponamus, quo exercetur iniustissima crudelitas contra veram ecclesiam. Quanquam enim non omnes tyranni flecti ad pietatem possunt, tamen pii principes confirmantur his dictis prophetarum, et aliqui hostes revocantur in viam, sicut hîc Nabogdonosor, qui etsi fuerat hostis, tamen convertitur ad agnitionem dei, & defendit pios. Debis offitiis praecipuè hîc concionatur Daniel, & hanc regiam concionem sciamus ad omnia tempora, & omnes principes pertinere.

Sciamus & illud, haec opera à deo mandata, veros fructus paenitentiae esse, seu novam obedientiam, in qua lucet & exercetur fides. Quia enim rex iam ornat Ecclesiam, ostendit signum confessionis, quòd agnoscat unum esse deum, conditorem caeli & terrae, qui velit agnosci per verbum, quod huic uni populo Israel tradiderat, qui dederit promissiones de remissione peccatorum, exaudiat, & salvet. Testatur caeteros deos & cultus extra hoc verbum reiiciendos esse. In hac confessione multos invitavit rex ad pietatem. Exercuit & fidem in perticulis, quia non dubium est, fuisse impios principes, qui ei adversati & insidiati sunt. Sed ipse pertulit has difficultates, & expectavit à deo defensionem.

De his gravissimis rebus concionatur Daniel, de paenitenia, promissione, fide, de veris fructibus paenitentiae, de proprio regum offitio, de exercitiis fidei in periculis administrationis.

Sed monachi multis modis contaminaverunt hunc locum, transferunt ad satisfactiones & opera indebita. Quia hîc rex affligitur, disputant, non remitti peccata sine satisfactionibus. Item, quia interpres hîc nominat eleemosynas, ipsi detorquent dictum ad ceremonias certas eius offitii, et fingunt eas esse satisfactiones, & mereri remissionem culpae & poenae. Haec facile refutari possunt.

Primum igitur sciendum est, totum deliramentum de satisfactionibus, quas vocant opera indebita reiiciendum esse, iuxta hoc dictum, Frustra colunt me mandatis hominum. Sed post paenitentiam sequi debet nova obedientia, à deo praecepta. Ad hanc etiam eleemosynae pertinent, quae dandae sunt pro modo facultatum. Et quanquam haec opera non merentur remissionem sionem culpae; vel poenae aeternae, tamen promissio dei certa, & credenda est, quòd deus verè ignoscat agentibus paenitentiam credentibus, & invocantibus ipsum, & velit tollere aut mitigare poenas corporales. Haec beneficia fide petenda & expectanda sunt cum agimus paenitentiam. Ac minus esset calamitatum publicarum & privatarum, si hac fide peteremus mitigationem & ageremus paenitentiam. Iuxta illud Zachariae, Convertimini ad me, scilicet agentes paenitentiam, & credentes & invocantes me, & convertar ad vos, scilicet dandis beneficiis.

Quod autem de poenis obiicitur, Adam, David, Osias, Manasses, Nabogdonosor, & multi quotidie puniuntur, Ergo cum peccata non sine poenis remittantur, necessaria est satisfactio. Respondeo, Neganda est consequentia, nam poenae illae nec satisfactiones, nec compensationes sunt, nec merentur remissionem culpae nec damnationis, sed sunt singularia dei opera, quibus vocat lapsos ad paenitentiam aut exercet sanctos. Sicut scriptum est, Iudicium à domo dei in cipit. Et quanquam deus vult paenas illas exempla esse omnibus, quae admoneant omnes de ira & iudicio dei, tamen multis parcit, ut Ninivitis, & in Evangelio passim remittit sine poenis.

Exempla poenarum sunt testimonia iudicii dei, quòd vere deus aliquando omnes impios puniturus sit, sicut prima concio inquit, Si malefeceris, peccatum tuum quiescet, donec reve labitur. Q. d. Etsi aliquandiu differtur poena, & eris securo ac ocioso animo, tamen tandem deus iudicabit & puniet.

EtExod. 32.Ego autem in dic visitationis meae visitabo peccata eorum. Q. d. Etsi differo poenas, tamen non propterea existimate me oblivisci scelera, non humano consilio, sed meo constituo poenas, Do spatium paenitentiae, ideo metui volo, & vos ad paenitentiam voco.

Sic de poenus sentiendum est, non quòd sint satisfactiones, sed sint testimonia iudicii dei, & nos vocent ad paenitentiam aut exerceant sanctos.

Alia exempla ubi mitigatae aut sublatae sunt poenae, sunt dulcissimae; consolationes, quae testantur agentibus paenitentiam, remitti aut mitigari poenam, sicut docetEsaias cap. 1.

Caeterum exempla sanctorum, ut Abel, Esaiae, Ieremiae, Ioannis Baptistae, & similium, non sunt poenae, sed exercitia, ut antè dictum est, quia oportet Ecclesiam subiectam esse cruci, ut agnoscat magnitudinem peccati haerentis in carne, & rursus abolita carne, inchoetur novitas spiritualis inter afflictiones.

Haec breviter de afflictionibus adieci, ut lectores admoniti repudient deliramenta de satisfactionibus, & sciant nec poenas divinitus irrogatas, nec nostra opera satisfactiones esse.

Sed hîc obiiciunt, Textus inquit, Redime, Ergo significat opera esse precium seu meritum. Respondeo breviter, Vocabulum significat cessationem, non compensationem, Quasi dicat, Cessa à peccatis, & recte facito, seu liberate à peccatus, quod idem est, Cessa à peccatis, & c. Sed non ero prolixus in hac Grammatica cavillatione tractanda. Opponantur alia illustria testimonia quae loquuntur de gratuita remissione, & de fide in Christum, & de sacrificio Christi.

Origenes transformavit hanc historiam in allegoriam prorsus intempestivam de diabolo. Scripsit significari per regem Babylonicum diabolum eiectum per superbiam, & c. Et aiunt addidisse errorem de liberatione diaboli, sed haec portentosa deliria improbanda sunt, & fugiendae absurdae allegoriae.

Nos historiam retineamus, & meminerimus illustre exemplum paenitentiae propositum esse, in quo admonemur de securitate, de poenis, de gratuita remissione peccatorum, de vocatione gentium, de promissione, de vera fide, de fructibus paenitentiae, de principum officiis, &c.

Verae fidei vox est, qua hîc cognoscit non solum potentiam dei, sed etiam voluntatem, scililicet quòd puniat superbos, & remittat peccata agentibus paenitentiam, exaudiat & salvet invocantes. Haec agnitio est vera fides, quam hîc profitetur, cum ait, Gradientes in superbia humiliat. Ergo agnoscit deum non ociosum, sed vere punientem.

Addit etiam, Omnes viae eius iudicia, id est, praedicat deum esse iustum, punientem eos qui ipsum contemnunt, & recipientem in gratiam agentes paenitentiam, sicut ei propheta dixerat. Nam ad prophetae concionem respiciendum est. Agnoscit rex se à deo punitum esse, nunc poena liberatum. Agit igitur gratias pro liberatione, & recordatur promissionis, credit remissionem peccatorum &c.

## 5:1 Argument
CHAPTER V.

King Belshazzar made a great feast for a thousand of his nobles, and each one drank according to his age. Therefore, already intoxicated, he commanded that the gold and silver vessels be brought which Nebuchadnezzar his father had carried away from the temple that was in Jerusalem, so that the king and his nobles, his wives and his concubines, might drink from them. Then the gold vessels were brought which he had carried away from the temple that had been in Jerusalem, and the king and his nobles, his wives and his concubines, drank from them. They drank wine, and praised their gods of gold and silver, of bronze, of iron, and of wood and stone. In the same hour, fingers appeared, as if of the hand of a man writing opposite the lampstand upon the surface of the wall of the royal palace: and the king was looking at the joints of the writing hand. Then the king's countenance was changed, and his thoughts troubled him, and the joints of his loins were loosed, and his knees struck one against another. The king therefore cried out aloud that they should bring in the wise men, the Chaldeans, and the soothsayers. And the king speaking said to the wise men of Babylon: Whosoever shall read this writing, and shall make known to me the interpretation thereof, shall be clothed with purple, and shall have a chain of gold about his neck, and shall be the third in my kingdom. Then came in all the king's wise men, but they could neither read the writing, nor declare the interpretation thereof to the king. Whereupon king Balthasar was troubled exceedingly, and his countenance was changed: and his lords also were troubled. Now the queen, by reason of what had happened to the king and his lords, came into the banquet house, and speaking said: O king, in Live forever: let not your thoughts disturb you, nor let your countenance be changed. There is a man in your kingdom who has the spirit of the holy gods in him, and in the days of your father knowledge and wisdom were found in him: for King Nebuchadnezzar your father appointed him prince of the magicians, enchanters, Chaldeans, and soothsayers, your father I say, O king: Because a more excellent spirit, and prudence, and intelligence, and the interpretation of dreams, and the showing of secrets, and the solution of knots were found in him, that is in Daniel: to whom the king gave the name Belteshazzar: now therefore let Daniel be called, and he will tell the interpretation. Therefore Daniel was brought in before the king, to whom the aforementioned king said: Are you Daniel of the children of the captivity of Judah, whom my father the king brought from Judea? I have heard of you that you have the spirit of the gods, and knowledge, and greater intelligence and wisdom have been found in you. And now the wise men, the magicians, have entered into my presence, that they might read this writing, and declare its interpretation to me: and they could not declare the meaning of this speech. Moreover, I have heard concerning you, that you can interpret obscure things, and dissolve things that are bound. If therefore you are able to read the writing, and to declare its interpretation to me, you shall be clothed in purple, and you shall have a golden chain around your neck, and you shall be the third prince in my kingdom. To which Daniel responding, said before the king: Let your gifts be for yourself, and give the rewards of your house to another, but I will read the writing to you, O king, and I will show you its interpretation. O king, the most high God gave the kingdom and magnificence, glory and honor to Nebuchadnezzar your father. And because of the magnificence which He had given all peoples, tribes, and languages trembled and feared him: whom he wished, he killed: and whom he wished, he struck: and whom he wished, he exalted: and whom he wished, he humbled.
