But when his heart was lifted up, and his spirit was hardened unto pride, he was deposed from the throne of his kingdom, and his glory was taken away, and he was cast out from the sons of men, but also his heart was placed with the beasts, and his dwelling was with the wild asses: he also ate hay like an ox, and his body was wet with the dew of heaven, until he knew that the most high had power in the kingdom of men, and whomsoever he will, he shall raise up over it. You also, his son Belshazzar, have not humbled your heart, although you knew all these things, but you have been lifted up against the Lord of heaven: and the vessels of his house have been brought before you, and you, and your nobles, and your wives your wives, and your concubines have drunk wine in them: you have also praised gods of silver, and of gold, and of brass, of iron, and of wood, and of stone, which do not see, nor hear, nor feel: but the God who has your breath in His hand and all your ways, you have not glorified. Therefore, there was sent by Him the part of the hand, which wrote this that is inscribed. And this is the writing that was set down: MANE, THECEL, PHARES. And this is the interpretation of the word. MANE: God has numbered your kingdom, and has finished it. THECEL: you are weighed in the balance, and are found wanting. PHARES: your kingdom is divided, and is given to the Medes and Persians. Then by the king's command, Daniel was clothed with purple, and a golden chain was put about his neck: and it was proclaimed of him that he should have power as the third in his kingdom. In the same night Balthasar the Chaldean king was killed. And Darius the Mede succeeded to the kingdom, sixty-two years old.

Latin:

CAPUT V.

Balsasar rex fecit grande convivium optimatibus suis mille, & unusquisque secundum suam bibebat aetatem. Praecepit ergo iam temulentus, ut afferrentur vasa aurea & argentea quae asportaverat Nabuchodonosor pater eius de templo quod fuit in Ierusalem, ut biberent in eis rex & optimates eius, uxoresque eius & concubinae. Tunc allata sunt vasa aurea quae asportaverat de templo quod fuerat in Ierusalem, & biberunt in eis rex & optimates eius, uxores & concubinae illius. Bibebant vinum, et laudabant deos suos aureos & argenteos, aereos, ferreos, ligneosque & lapideos. In eadem hora apparuerunt digiti, quasi manus hominis scribentis contra candelabrum in superficie parietis aulae regiae: & rex aspiciebat articulos manus scribentis. Tunc facies regis commutata est, & cogitationes eius conturbabant eum, & compages renum eius solvebantur, & genua eius ad seinvicem collidebantur. Exclamavit itaque rex fortiter ut introducerent magos, Chaldaeos, & aruspices. Et proloquens rex ait sapientibus Babylonis: Quicunque legerit scripturam hanc, & interpretationem eius manifestam mihi fecerit, purpura vestietur, & torquem auream habebit in collo, & terrius in regno meo erit. Tunc ingressi omnes sapientes regis, non potuerunt nec scripturam legere, nec interpretationem eius indicare regi. Unde rex Balsasar satis conturbatus est, & vultus illius immutatus est: sed & optimates eius turbabantur. Regina autem pro re quae acciderat regi & optimatibus eius, domum convivii ingressa est, & proloquens ait: Rex in aeternum vive: non te conturbent cogitationes tuae, neque facies tua immutetur. Est vir in regno tuo, qui spiritum deorum sanctorum habet in se, & in diebus patris tui scientia & sapientia inventae sunt in eo: nam & rex Nabuchodonosor pater tuus principem magorum, incantatorum, Chaldaeorum, & aruspicum constituit eum, pater inquam tuus ô rex: Quia spiritus amplior, & prudentia, intelligentiaque, & interpretatio somniorum, & ostensio secretorum, ac solutio ligatorum inventae sunt in eo, hoc est in Daniele: cui rex posuit nomen Baltassar: nunc itaque Daniel vocetur, & interpretationem narrabit. Igitur introductus est Daniel coram rege, ad quem praefatus rex ait: Tues Daniel de filiis captivitatis Iudae, quem adduxerat pater meus rex de Iudaea? Audivi de te quoniam spiritum deorum habeas, & scientia, intelligentiaque ac sapientia ampliores inventae sunt in te. Et nunc introgressi sunt in conspectu meo sapientes magi, ut scripturam hanc legerent, & interpretationem eius indicarent mihi: & nequiverunt sensum huius sermonis edicere. Porrò ego audivi de te, quòd possis obscura interpretari, & ligata dissoluere. Si ergo vales scripturam legere, & interpretationem eius indicare mihi, purpura vestiêris, & torquem auream circa collum tuum habebis, & tertius in regno meo princeps eris. Ad quae respondens Daniel, ait coram rege: Munera tua sint tibi, & dona domus tuae alteri da, scripturam autem legam tibi, rex, & interpretationem eius ostendam tibi. O rex, Deus altissimus regnum & magnificentiam, gloriam & honorem dedit Nabuchodonosor patri tuo. Et propter magnificentiam quam dederat ei, universi populi, tribus & linguae tremebant, & metuebant eum: quos volebat, interficiebat: & quos volebat, percutiebat: & quos volebat, exaltabat: & quos volebat, humiliabat. Quando autem elevatum est cor eius, & spiritus illius obfirmatus est ad superbiam, depositus est de solio regni sui, & gloria eius ablata est, & à filiis hominum eiectus est, sed & cor eius cum bestiis positum est, & cum onagris erat habitatio eius: foenum quoque ut bos comedebat, & rore caeli corpus eius infectum est, donec cognosceret quòd potestatem haberet altissimus in regno hominum, & quencunque voluerit, suscitabit super illud. Tu quoque filius eius Balsasar, non humiliasti cor tuum, cum scires haec omnia, sed adversum dominatorem caeli elevatus es: & vasa domus eius allata sunt coram te, & tu, & optimates tui, & uxores tuae, & concubinae tuae vinum bibistis in eis: deos quoque argenteos, & aureos, & aereos, ferreos, ligneósque, & lapideos qui non vident, neque audiunt, neque sentiunt, laudasti: porrò deum qui habet flatum tuum in manu sua & omnes vias tuas, non glorificasti. Idcirco ab eo missus est articulus manus, qui scripsit hoc quod exaratum est. Haec est autem scriptura quae digesta est: MANE, THECEL, PHARES. Et haec est interpretatio sermonis. MANE: Numeravit deus regnum tuum, & complevit illud. THECEL: appensus es in statera, & inventus es minus habens. PHARES: divisum est regnum tuum, & datum est Medis & Persis. Tunc iubente rege, indutus est Daniel purpura, & circúndata est torques aurea collo eius: & praedicatum est de eo quòd haberet potestatem tertius in regno suo. Eadem nocte interfectus est Balsasar rex Chaldaeus. Et Darius Medus successit in regnum annos natus sexagintaduos.

## 5:1 Places of the V. Chapter
PLACES OF THE V. CHAPTER.

ABOVE he proposed a king doing penance, and propagating true worships, whom God also adorned with rewards. Now he adds the contrary example of an impious king, restoring idolatry, not doing penance, whom God punishes and strips of the kingdom.

But first the history must be considered. Nebuchadnezzar reigned 45 years. The son Evil-Merodach imitated the piety of the father, who granted royal honor to Jeconiah king of Judah, and affected the people of God with great benefits. But Evil-Merodach reigned for 23 years.

To him succeeded Balthasar, unlike his ancestors, and relapsed to idolatry, who, that he might testify that he was an enemy of true doctrine, and might confirm idolaters, profaned the Jewish cups, and with manifest contempt insulted God. Just as Rabshakeh and Sennacherib4. Kings 18. and 19.And just as the Jews mocked Christ, If he is the son of God, let him descend from the cross. These blasphemies are quickly followed by punishments, according to the second commandment, God will not hold him guiltless, etc.

This history first warns us of the horrible power of the devil, who in all ages abuses the highest princes to confirm idolatry and to tear apart the Church. Wherefore few princes retain the true worships of God, and against this scandal it is necessary for the minds of the pious to be forewarned, and it is profitable for the pious to understand the sins of princes, who corrupt true doctrine for this reason, that they may adapt religions to their own utilities, which is truly and simply to mock Christ. And this age has many examples of this thing.

Secondly, this history exhorts princes to piety, and warns of the punishments of the impious.

Thirdly, it testifies that the changes of kingdoms happen divinely, on account of the various sins of princes. Here God punishes idolatry and blasphemy, as in Manasseh, and the kings of Israel. In David he punished adultery and the counsel of slaughter.

But it usually happens that the sins of the people and of the princes concur, as it is said in Hosea 4., Like priest, like people. Nor is there any doubt that most of the calamities of the world now happen on account of manifold idolatry, in masses, the invocation of saints, and other terrors and impious cults. Hence are the Turkish successes, intestine wars, and other dissipations.

Latin:

LOCI V. CAPITIS.

SUPRA proposuit regem agentem paenitentiam, & propagantem veros cultus, quem deus etiam ornavit praemiis. Nunc addit contrarium exemplum regis impii, restituentis idolatriam, non agentis paenitentiam, quem deus punit & regno exuit.

Primum autem historia consideranda est. Nabogdonosor regnavit annos 45. Patris pietatem imitatus est filius Evilmerodach, qui leconiae regi Iuda tribuit regium honorem, & magnis beneficiis affecit populum dei. Regnavit autem Evilmerodach annos 23.

Huic successit Balthasar dissimilis maiorum, & relapsus ad idolatriam, qui, ut testaretur se hostem esse verae doctrinae, & confirmaret idolatras, prophanabat Iudaica pocula, & manifesto contemptu insultabat deo. Sicut Rapsaces & Senacherib4. Reg. 18. & 19.Et sicut Iudaei Christum deridebant, Si filius dei est, descendat de cruce. Has blasphemias. cito sequuntur poenae, iuxta secundum praeceptum, Non habebit deus insontem, &c.

Haec historia primum admonet nos de horribili potentia diaboli, qui omnibus seculis abutitur summis principibus ad confirmandam idolatriam, & lacerandam Ecclesiam. Quare pauci principes retinent veros cultus dei, & contra hoc scandalum necesse est praemunitos esse animos piorum, & prodest piis intelligere principum peccata, qui ideo corrumpunt veram doctrinam, ut attemperent religiones suis utilitatibus, quod est vere & simpliciter illudere Christum. Et huius rei habet haec aetas multa exempla.

Secundo, haec historia hortatur principes ad pietatem, & admonet de poenis impiorum.

Tertio, testatur mutationes regnorum fieri divinitus, propter varia peccata principum. Hîc punit deus idolatriam & blasphemiam, sicut in Manasse, & regibus Israel. In Davide punivit adulterium & consilium caedis.

Sed plaerunque fit ut concurrant peccata populi et principum, sicutOseae 4.dicitur, Qualis sacerdos, talis populus. Nec dubium est plaerasque calamitates mundi nunc accidere propter multiplicem idolatriam, in missis, invocatione sanctorum, & aliis terroribus ac impiis cultibus Hinc sunt Turcici successus, bella intestina, & aliae dissipationes.

## 5:1 Concerning Sacred Things
CONCERNING SACRED THINGS.

FOURTHLY, it is asked here concerning sacred things, whether it is not permitted to transfer them to profane uses? I answer, that things consecrated without the word of God are not to be distinguished from profane things, as far as consecration pertains, because there is no consecration without the word of God. Indeed, it is impiety to feign a consecration without the word of God, just as in Acts chap. 10. it is clearly said, What God has purified, do not you call common, &c.

Wherefore the pious can use those things which were dedicated to our temples, if they otherwise hold them, without theft or injury to others. Indeed, they sin who do not use them on account of superstition, as if they were sacred things to which God wishes a peculiar reverence to be attributed, because without the word of God that honor ought not to be attributed to any things. Furthermore, they do rightly who profane things consecrated to idols, just as Rachel snatched the golden images from her father.

It is another matter concerning things consecrated by the word of God, just as in the Mosaic law there were certain things consecrated, from which the people were obliged to abstain on account of the word of God, which pertained to those times and those things. And yet even there it was permitted for the pious in necessity to use those things, as the example of David eating the loaves of proposition shows, etc.

But Belshazzar here profanes things consecrated by God, and adds blasphemies; for he profanes them for this reason, that he may show himself to be an enemy of God. Just as the text clearly says, They praised the gods of gold, of wood, etc. For just as Julian attempted to restore idolatry, so this Belshazzar had joined to himself the enemies of true doctrine, and was creating hope for the restoration of idolatry. Wherefore the deeds of Rachel and Belshazzar must be prudently distinguished, so that similar things may be judged.

He does not sin who transfers the things of temples to profane uses, namely, if it be done without theft and injury to others. For just as it is theft if a private person takes away the things of the public treasury of the state, without a legitimate contract, so it is theft if a private person takes away anything from ecclesiastical things, without a just contract. However, ecclesiastical things can be transferred through contracts by those who legitimately administer them, for a probable cause, just as other things of the treasury.

Furthermore, the superior lord, as he is the defender of other public goods, so is he the defender of ecclesiastical goods. And as it is in the interest of the prince, if the city manages the goods of the treasury poorly, to ensure that they are placed more diligently and usefully, so concerning ecclesiastical faculties it is in the interest of princes to ensure that suitable teachers and scholars are nourished from them. And if enemies of the Gospel or idle men enjoy them, they ought to ensure that these being removed, suitable men are substituted, just as is commanded in the C. And Scripture says, He who does not work, let him not eat.

Wherefore individual princes in their territories ought to cast out the idle and enemies of the Gospel, Bishops, Canons, and Monks from the possessions of ecclesiastical faculties, and ensure that suitable ministers with their wives and children are honestly nourished. Likewise that poor scholars be sustained from Ecclesiastical faculties, to preserve the studies necessary for the Church, just as Paul says, No one serves as a soldier without wages. Wherefore sustenance is owed not only to those teaching, but also to those learning. And it is most unjust to supply the luxury of Canons, and meanwhile to neglect not only the pastors, but also the very studies, which cannot be preserved without public aid.

Nor do superior lords have the power of transferring to themselves those ecclesiastical goods, the Churches having been defrauded; that is, they ought to bestow as much as is necessary upon the Churches. They ought to do this even if it had to be given from their own property, how much more so are things previously acquired to be preserved for the Churches.

But if there is a surplus or there are goods which the Church cannot administer, because political powers are not fitting for Bishops, the superior lord rightly transfers these to himself, as superior lords ought to have transferred the powers, dominions, and armies of the Bishops to themselves, but in the meantime take care that provision be made for the Churches out of the goods.

FIFTH, In the change of a kingdom God does not forget His Church, just as Christ consoles us,Matt. 24.When you hear of rumors of wars, do not be troubled. For the kingdoms of the world must be prudently distinguished from the eternal kingdom, which is the Church. And although wars shake the Churches in the changes of kingdoms, nevertheless the pious ought to look upon this consolation, that the Church is an eternal kingdom, they ought not to fall away into despair to the impious, as is happening now in Greece. Against this weakness Christ wished to console the pious, When you shall hear of wars and rumors of wars, be not troubled.

And so here in the change of the kingdom God again raises up Daniel, and attributes to him greater authority than he had before, so that the new kings also may be converted to the acknowledgment of God, so that the Church may be preserved, and public miracles may exist, which testify that this people has the word of God, and true worship.

Not only do the words written on the wall denounce destruction to this king but the example in general signifies the manner of changes in all times.

The first word Mane, that is, he has numbered, signifies that God establishes limits for tyrants, beyond which they cannot proceed. He therefore consoles all the pious, in all times, so that they may know that cruelty is neither perpetual nor long-lasting, as the Psalm says, Let his days be few, etc. Wherefore the first word admonishes princes concerning the judgment of God, that he has established limits for them. He consoles the pious that cruelty will not be lasting, as the histories of all times testify.

The remaining two voices speak of punishments. Now the first punishment of kings and governors is the loss of authority, as it is saidPsalm. 7.Contempt is poured out upon princes, and, He made them wander in the pathless waste, and not in the way, and he helped the poor out of affliction, and fed the families like sheep. For since empires stand by divine will, God adds authority to governors, as1. Re. 10.And a part of the army went with him, whose hearts God had touched. And in another Psalm, Who gives salvation to kings. And the examples of all ages testify that heroic men have prevailed by authority, and have not retained wills by human means or forces. Therefore, whenever a change impends, authority is changed. The consensus of the good judges otherwise, minds incline elsewhere than before, as now appears concerning the pontifical state.

The second punishment is the final one, namely, the loss of the kingdom, as David succeeds Saul, and here the Medes and Persians succeed the Chaldeans and Assyrians. And thus is the end of the first Monarchy, and of the golden kingdom.

Let tyrants therefore cease now also to trust in their wealth and power, and let them consider that they can lose their empires by an easy fall, as that The Persians, who initially had very small forces, occupied a most flourishing kingdom. They acknowledged, therefore, that they had taken possession of Babylon by divine power. For Xenophon also writes that the city was captured at such a time when festival days were being celebrated and banquets were being held with great security. Alluding undoubtedly to this banquet described by Daniel.

He also writes that there were two princes from the kingdom of Belshazzar with Cyrus, Gobryas and Gadatas, who were the first to enter the city and the citadel, and they slaughtered the king, for King Belshazzar had unjustly killed the son of Gobryas during a hunt out of envy. From the accounts of these men and similar ones, Daniel undoubtedly began to become known to the two kings, Darius and Cyrus. And although the Greeks do not name this Darius the Mede before Cyrus, he nevertheless seems to be the very one who is called by Xenophon, Cyaxares the Mede, whom Cyrus succeeded.

Latin:

DE REBUS SACRIS.

QUARTO hîc de sacris rebus quaeritur, an non liceat eas transferre ad prophanos usus? Respondeo, Consecratas sine verbo dei non esse discernendas à prophanis, quod ad consecrationem attinet, quia nulla est consecratio sine verbo dei. Imò impietas est fingere consecrationem sine verbo dei, sicut inAct. cup. 10.clare dicitur, Quôd deus purificavit, tu ne commune dixeris, &c.

Quare pii rebus illis uti possunt, quae fuerunt dedicatae templis nostris, si alioqui teneant eas, sine furto aut aliorum iniuria. Imò peccant, qui non utuntur propter superstitionem, quasi sint res sacrae, quibus velit deus peculiarem reverentiam tribui, quia sine verbo dei non debet ille honos tribui ullis rebus. Praeterea rectè faciunt qui idolis consecrata prophanant, sieut Rachel patri simulachra aurea rapuit.

Aliud est de consecratis verbo dei, sicut in lege Mosaica erant certae res consecratae, à quibus populus debebat abstinere propter verbum dei, quod ad illa tempora & illas res pertinebat. Et tamen ibi quoque licuit piis in necessitate uti illis rebus, ut ostendit exemplum Davidis comedentis panes propositionis, &c.

Sed Balthasar hîc prophanat res divinitus consecratas, & addit blasphemias, Ideo enim prophanat, ut ostendat se esse hostem dei. Sicut textus clarè dicit, Laudabant deos aureos, ligneos, &c. Sicut enim Iulianus restituere conatus est idolatriam, ita hic Balthasar adiunxerat sibi hostes verae doctrinae, & faciebat spem restituendae idolatriae. Quare prudenter discernenda sunt facta Rachel & Balthasaris, ut similia iudicari possint.

Non peccat qui res templorum ad usus prophanos transfert, scilicet, si id sine furto & aliorum iniuria fiat. Ut enim furtum est si privatus res fisci civitatis auferat, sine legitimo contractu, ita furtum est si privatus aliquid auferat ex rebus ecclesiasticis, sine iusto contractu. Possunt autem res ecclesiasticae per contractus transferri ab iis qui legitimè administrant eas, ex probabili caussa, sicut aliae res fisci.

Porrò superior dominus, ut defensor est aliorum publicorum bonorum, sic est defensor ecclesiasticorum bonorum. Et ut principis interest, si civitas bona fisci malè administrat, curare ut diligentius & utilius collocentur, ita de facultatibus ecclesiasticis interest principum curare, ut inde alantur idonei doctores et scholastici. Et si fruuntur eis hostes Evangelii aut ociosi, debent curare ut his remotis homines idonei substituantur, sicut in C. praecipitur. Et scriptura inquit, Qui non laborat, non manducet.

Quare singuli principes in suis ditionibus debebant ociosos & hostes Evangelii, Episcopos, Canonicos & Monachos eiicere ex possessionibus facultatum ecclesiasticarum, & curare ut idonei ministri cum coniugibus & liberis honestè alerentur. Item ut pauperes scholastici ex Ecclesiasticis faculutibus sustentarentur, ad conservanda studia Ecclesiae necessaria, sicut inquit Paulus, Nemo militat sine stipendiis. Quare victus non solum docentibus, sed etiam discentibus debetur. Et iniustissimum est suppeditare luxui Canonicorum, et interea negligere non solum pastores, sed etiam ipsa studia, quae sine publicis auxiliis conservari non possunt.

Nec habent potestatem superiores domini transferendi ad se illa bona ecclesiastica defraudatis Ecclesiis, hoc est, quantum opus est, Ecclesiis impertire debent. Id debebant facere etiamsi de proprio esset largiendum, quanto magis antè partae res Ecclesiis conservandae sunt.

Sed si superest aut sunt bona quae Ecclesia non potest administrare, quia Episcopis non congruunt politica imperia, haec supertor dominus rectè ad se transfert, ut superiores domini, debebant imperia, ditiones, exercitus Episcoporum ad se transferre, sed interea curare, ut ex bonis prospiceretur Ecclesiis.

QUINTO, In mutatione regni deus non obliviscitur Ecclesiae suae, sicut Christus nos consolatur,Matth. 24.Cum audietis rumores bellorum, ne turbemini. Prudenter enim discernenda sunt regna mundi à regno aeterno, quod est Ecclesia. Et quanquam bella concutiunt Ecclesias in mutationibus regnorum, tamen pii debent intueri hanc consolationem, quòd Ecclesia sit aeternum regnum, non debent desperatione desciscere ad impios, sicut fit nunc in Graecia. Contra hanc infirmitatem consolari pios Christus voluit, Cum audietis rumores bellorum, ne turbemini.

It aque hìc in regni mutatione deus rursus evehit Danielem, & attribuit ei maiorem autoritatem, quàm antea habuit, ut novi reges etiam convertantur ad agnitionem dei, ut servetur Ecclesia, & extent publica miracula, quae testentur hunc populum habere ucrbum dei, & veros cultus.

Non tantum huic regi denunciant exitium verba scripta in pariete, sed exemplum in genere significat modum mutationum omnibus temporibus.

Prima vox Mane, id est, numeravit, significat deum constituere metas tyrannis, ultra quas progredi non possunt. Consolatur itaque omnes pios, omnibus temporibus, ut sciant saeviciam nec perpetuam nec diuturnam esse, sicut Psalmus inquit, Fiant dies eius pauci, &c. Quare prima vox admonet principes de iudicio dei, quòd metas eis constituerit. Consolatur pios quod non sit duratura saevitia, sicut omnium temporum historiae testantur.

Reliquae duae voces loquuntur de poenis. Est autem regum & gubernatorum prima poena, autoritatis amissio, sicut diciturPsalm. 7.Effusus est contemptus super principes, &, Errare fecit eos in invio, & non in via, & adivuit pauperem ex afflictione, & pavit familias ut oves. Cum enim imperia stent divinitus, deus addit gubernatoribus autoritatem, ut1. Re. 10.Et abiit cum eo pars exercitus, quorum deus tetigerat corda. Et alio Psalmo, Qui dat salutem regibus. Et exempla omnium aetatum testantur heroicos viros autoritate valuisse, & non humanis modis aut viribus retinuisse voluntates. Ideo quandocunque impendet mutatio, autoritas mutatur. Aliter iudicat consensus bonorum, aliò inclinant animi, quàm antè, ut nunc de statu pontificio apparet.

Altera poena est ultima, videlicet, regni amissio, Ut Sauli succedit David, Et hîc Chaldaeis & Assyriis succedunt Moedi & Persae. Atque ita finis est primae Monarchiae, & aurei regni.

Desinant igitur nunc quoque tyranni opibus & potentia sua confidere, & cogitent se facili casu posse amittere sua imperia, ut illud florentissimum regnum occupaverunt Persae, qui admodum exiguas vires initio habuerunt. Agnoscebant igitur divinitus se potitos esse Babylone. Nam & Xenophon scribit, captam esse urbem tali tempore, cum celebrarentur dies festi, & agitarentur convivia, magna securitate. Alludens haud dubiè ad hoc convivium à Daniele descriptum.

Scribit etiam apud Cyrum fuisse duos principes ex regno Balthasaris, Gobiam & Gadatam, qui primi ingressi sunt urbem atque arcem, & regem trucidaverunt, nam Gobiae filium in venatione rex Balthasar iniustè per invidiam occiderat. Ex horum & similium sermonibus, haud dubiè Daniel coepit innotescere duobus regibus, Dario & Cyro. Etsi autem hunc Darium Medum ante Cyrum Graeci non nominant, tamen videtur ille ipse esse, qui à Xenophonte appellatur, Cyaxares Medus, cui successit Cyrus.

## 6:1 Argument
CHAPTER VI.

It pleased Darius, and he appointed over the kingdom one hundred and twenty satraps, that they might be in his whole kingdom. And over them three princes, of whom Daniel was one, that the satraps might render an account to them, and the king might not suffer annoyance. Therefore Daniel surpassed all the princes and satraps, because a more ample spirit of God was in him. Furthermore the king was thinking of appointing him over the whole kingdom: whence the princes and satraps were seeking an occasion that they might find against Daniel from the side of the kingdom, and they could find no cause and suspicion, for the reason that he was faithful, and every fault and suspicion could not be found in him. Therefore those men said: We shall not find any occasion against this Daniel, unless perhaps in the law of his God. Then the princes and the satraps stole away to the king, and spoke to him thus: King Darius, live forever: all the princes of your kingdom, the magistrates and satraps, the senators and judges, have taken counsel that an imperial decree should go forth, and an edict, that whoever shall ask any petition of any god or man, for thirty days, except of you, O king, shall be cast into the den of lions. Now therefore, O king, confirm the sentence, and write the decree, that what is established by the Medes and Persians may not be changed, nor may it be lawful for anyone to transgress it. Furthermore, King Darius proposed the edict, and established it. When Daniel had discovered this, that is, the established law, he entered his house: and with the windows open in his upper chamber, toward Jerusalem, three times a day he bent his knees, and prayed, and gave thanks before his God, just as he had been accustomed to do before. Those men, therefore, inquiring more curiously, they found Daniel praying and beseeching his God. And approaching, they spoke to the king concerning the edict: O king, have you not established that every man who should ask anything of any of the gods and men for thirty days, except you, O king, should be cast into the lions' den? To whom the king responding, said: The word is true according to the decree of the Medes and Persians, which it is not permitted to violate. Then responding they said before the king. Daniel, of the sons of the captivity of Judah, has not cared for your law and for the edict which you have established, but three times a day he prays with his supplication. Which word when the king had heard, he was saddened enough: and he set his heart for Daniel to deliver him, and even until the setting of the sun he labored to rescue him. But those men understanding the king, said to him: Know, O king, that it is the law of the Medes and Persians, that every decree which the king has established, it is not permitted to be changed. Then the king gave the order, and they brought Daniel, and threw him into the den of lions. And the king said to Daniel: Your God whom you worship always, He himself will deliver you. And one stone was brought, and placed over the mouth of the den, which the king sealed with his own ring, and with the ring of his nobles, so that nothing might be done against Daniel. And the king went away to his house, and slept without supper, and food was not brought before him, and furthermore sleep departed from him. Then the king rising at the first light of dawn, went in haste to the den of lions: and approaching the den, he called out to Daniel with a tearful voice, and addressed him: Daniel servant of the living God, your God whom you serve always, do you think He has been able to deliver you from the lions? And Daniel answering the king, says: O king live forever, my God sent his angel his own, and he shut the mouths of the lions, and they did not hurt me: because before him justice was found in me: but also before you, O king, I have done no offense. Then the king rejoiced greatly over him, and he commanded Daniel to be brought up out of the den: and Daniel was brought up out of the den, and no injury was found in him, because he believed in his God. And by the king's command, those men who had accused Daniel were brought, and they were cast into the lions' den, they and their children, and their wives: and they did not reach the floor of the den, until the lions seized them, and crushed all their bones. Then King Darius wrote to all peoples, tribes, and languages dwelling in the whole earth: May peace be multiplied unto you. By me a decree is established, that in my whole empire and kingdom they shall tremble and fear the God of Daniel: for he is the living and eternal God in ages: and his kingdom shall not be destroyed, and his power shall be even to eternity. He is the deliverer and savior, doing signs and wonders in heaven and in earth: who delivered Daniel out of the lions' den. Moreover Daniel continued even unto the reign of Darius, and the reign of Cyrus the Persian.

I FIRST the example warns that the constitution of the republic ought to follow victories, and especially the restoration of the true religion, just as here the provinces are ordered, governors are read, and Daniel is set over as a teacher of the true religion. And so in turn God adorns the Persians with great glory, according to that, Those who glorify me, I will glorify.

II the second example shows that persecution is not lacking to the church, and that the minds of princes are artfully ensnared. There were two kings, Darius the elder, and Cyrus the younger, and not born of a father king, but superior in glory. This dissimilarity most easily produces suspicions and rivalries. Just as Xenophon writes that Cyaxares, offended by the glory of Cyrus, even expostulated with him while weeping, and complained that he was being despised, even though he was appeased by the wonderful diligence and loyalty of Cyrus. It was therefore easy for cunning and courtly minds to find a trick to incite the king, who did not want Cyrus to grow. Therefore he allows an edict to be made, that all benefits should be sought from him.

The devil has such ministers, who, having seized opportunities, cunningly turn the minds of kings away from the truth, where snares are laid under the highest appearance of duty and virtue. Thus here, while the good old man thinks that his honor is being consulted against a colleague, he does not see how much wickedness he admits, because in the edict even the invocation of God is prohibited.

This example therefore warns that princes should be cautious in observing such snares, and especially in establishing laws and edicts.

III THIRDLY, an example of weak faith is proposed. But one must diligently distinguish between the weak and the persecutors. The weak is he who loves and embraces the doctrine, and desires to propagate it, allows himself to be taught, and strives to progress. He also desires the teachers to be safe, and

he confesses in some way, even if he does not defend it freely enough nor strongly enough, nor yet does he deny or oppose the doctrine. Such a one Paul commands to be received, and he adds, God has received him, and he is able to confirm him. Such were the Apostles at the time of the death of Christ.

And indeed God so governs the saints, that they both have and acknowledge their own infirmity, from which they finally struggle out. Therefore Isaiah says, He does not extinguish a smoking flax. And Psalm. 144. The Lord lifts up all who are falling, and raises up all who are crushed.

Thus also in this place Darius is weak, he embraces the doctrine, he desires to defend the one confessing, he even helps him with his testimony, and with his confession. These are signs of a pious mind, nor does he doubt concerning the cause, but he doubts concerning his own ability. He judges that he alone, especially in this newness of the kingdom, cannot obtain what he wanted, nor is it permitted for him alone to disapprove the opinion of all the princes. And these men did not fight lightly, they contend for the whole day, they urge the authority of the law, they say the example will be pernicious, and against the kingdom, if the edicts are retracted. Finally therefore the king, conquered, doubting either concerning his own authority, or concerning his power, permits the accusers to prevail.

Although therefore Darius sinned, yet he fell through infirmity, and against the fury of the accusers he sustains himself with a certain spark of faith, which shows that not he himself, but the princes were the authors of the punishment, even if he himself had not sufficiently strongly repressed them. He shows therefore his confession, saying, Your God will deliver you. God lifts up such infirm ones, as appears here. For there follows immediately the most bitter repentance of the king, and then such strength of faith, such greatness of soul, that he even punishes the accusers.

On the other hand, however, they are persecutors who do not love nor embrace the doctrine, nor suffer themselves to be taught, nor strive to progress, nor protect the teachers, nor desire to propagate those things which they understand, but would rather also bury and overwhelm them, and they signify hatred. Those who do these things, having known the doctrine, let them fear the judgment of God concerning blasphemers. And the rule of Paul is to be observed concerning them, A man that is an heretic after the first and second admonition avoid, because he is condemned by his own testimony.

Now let us by the example of Darius acknowledge our own infirmity and strive to progress, and let us ask as heMark 9.I believe Lord, but help my unbelief.

And you will remember in this place that a double consolation was exhibited to the king, namely, Not only was Daniel delivered from his danger, but also the king was delivered from the contagion of parricide, and obtained the remission of sins. This consolation, which pertained properly to his own conscience, was no less a miracle than the other.

IIII Fourthly, the example concerning the retracting of unjust laws is most to be observed at this time.

V. Fifthly, the confession of Daniel exhorts us to oppose impious edicts, because he prayed publicly with open windows. It exhorts likewise to pray, and indeed signifies the manner, that looking toward Jerusalem, he said his prayers, that is, relying on the promise given to the true church. Because3. Kings 8.the exiles are commanded while praying to turn themselves toward Jerusalem. Thus Daniel turned himself toward the south-west, that is, thinking of the command and the promise, that God wills to save this people, and to hear them on account of the promised Lord. Just as the Gospel teaches us to look upon the promise while praying, and to seek and expect help for the sake of Christ, so they, looking upon the temple, were thinking about the promise and about the Lord who was to come.

VII. Seventh, the danger of Daniel depicts the strength and violence of the enemies of Christ. Just as the weak Daniel is exposed to lions, so the whole Church has most powerful enemies, the devil, kings, the mighty, the proud, those excelling in authority and riches in the world.

VIII. Eighth, the liberation of Daniel is a testimony that God is present with the saints, and saves them by his judgment, at one time in body, at another in spirit.

There is, however, a twofold liberation here: Daniel is liberated from death, the king is liberated from sin and from eternal death; he learns here the remission of sins, and is confirmed in faith. Daniel also especially desired that liberation, that God might be glorified, and the king might be recalled to the way, and the king himself understands that it is much greater than the safety of Daniel was. Therefore, with a most ardent heart, he gives thanks to God, and publishes testimonies of confession.

IX. Ninth, there are three notable sentences in the text concerning faith and works. The principal one is concerning faith, when he says, He believed in his God.

Although the sentence is brief, nevertheless it admonishes the reader concerning the whole doctrine of faith, that God requires faith, and that faith obtains immense gifts. He believed, namely, at the beginning when the impious edict was proposed, he did not cast away faith and profession, but constantly confessed that he invoked the God of Israel, and by this faith he sought and expected help from God, for the king and for himself, namely that true doctrine might not be destroyed in the kingdom.

Faith therefore signifies here as elsewhere, the confidence which dares to confess constantly, and determines that God receives us through mercy, and wills to save, and expects help for the Church, for us, for those who are to be converted. God requires this faith, and this obtains benefits. According to that of Isaiah, He who trusts in the Lord shall not be put to shame. Likewise, Matt. 21. Whatever you ask believing you shall receive. As therefore they obtained help by faith, Moses at the Red Sea, Isaiah in the siege of the city, and other saints in true dangers, so here Daniel obtains liberation by faith.

May these examples also rouse us in confession and in all dangers, and exhort us to faith and invocation. And these testimonies must be opposed to the adversaries, who clamor much about works, and reject the doctrine concerning faith.

The remaining two sentences speak of the righteousness of works, which ought to be present in believers both before God and before men. Daniel says explicitly, Righteousness was found in me before God. Then he adds also, Before you, O king, I have done no evil.

You will observe, therefore, that these three things are proposed: faith, and also begun obedience, which, even though it is unclean, yet pleases God in believers, who are righteous by faith. For it is necessary that the righteousness of a good conscience be present in us, as Paul says2. Corin. 1. This is our boast, the testimony of our conscience.

Thus here Daniel says, Righteousness was found in me before God. So that this may be rightly understood, add the other sentence concerning faith, He believed in his God. For Daniel by no means wishes himself to be righteous on account of his own dignity or his own virtues, but through mercy by faith. And yet he knows that is required.

to please God is the righteousness of a good conscience in believers.

Thirdly, righteousness toward men is added, for it is also necessary that innocence be seen before men for the sake of example. According to that saying, Let your light shine, etc.

And it must be diligently observed that, although he acted against the edict, he nevertheless affirms that he has done nothing evil. Let us transfer this voice of Daniel to ourselves at this time. For although we act against the edicts of the Emperor, yet let us know that we do nothing evil against him, nor are we seditious, nor do we injure the majesty of our superiors. For it is necessary that such edicts be violated, which they themselves ought to hold as vain. Thus the Nicomedian patrician, who tore down the publicly posted edict, was doing nothing evil against the Emperor. Let us therefore oppose the voice of Daniel to those who accuse us of contumacy and blame us as if we were seditious. To these things let us protest that we do nothing evil against the Emperor.

X. TENTH, the punishment of calumniators warns the enemies of the Gospel of the judgment of God, and exhorts them to repentance.

XI. ELEVENTHLY, as the example of Nebuchadnezzar above teaches concerning the duty of princes, that they ought to abolish impious cults, etc., so the example of Darius in this place teaches the same, who also sets forth an edict concerning the worshipping of the God of Daniel.

XII TWELFTHLY, it is to be observed in the edict of the king an evangelical sermon, he says, that this God of Daniel is a deliverer and savior. He names the God of Daniel, that is, who is acknowledged and worshipped by the faith of the word, which Daniel preaches, because the word divinely delivered distinguishes the church from the nations. For God does not wish to be acknowledged or worshipped without some word and testimony of his. Therefore he is called the God of Abraham, that is, who gave this word, these promises to Abraham, etc. So he is called the God of Israel, who led you out of the land of Egypt. So we invoke the God and Father of our Lord Jesus Christ. The king therefore testifies that he is the true God, whose word Daniel preaches.

Then he adds another distinction, that this God is a deliverer and savior. For this also is a distinction of true religion from all others.

Where there is only the knowledge of the law, doubt remains, and the afflicted cannot determine that God truly wills to show mercy, to hear, to help. But the Gospel cries out that God receives us freely as believers. Also that He truly wills to hear and to save, that He requires this faith, that He wills to be worshipped especially by this worship. Therefore, the voice of the Gospel and the promise must be opposed to natural doubt and heathen opinions, and faith must be stirred up, lest we accuse God of a lie. Wherefore this proclamation in the edict, which preaches that God is a deliverer and savior, is truly Evangelical, just as those in the Psalm, He who heals the broken in heart, and binds up their wounds. This faith must be exercised in daily invocation.

Latin:

CAPUT VI.

Placuit Dario, & constituit super regnum satrapas centum viginti, ut essent in toto regno suo. Et super eos principes tres, ex quibus Daniel unus erat, ut satrapae illis red derent rationem, & rex non sustineret molestiam. Igitur Daniel superabat omnes principes et satrapas, quia spiritus dei amplior erat in illo. Porrò rex cogitabat constituere eum super omne regnum: unde principes & satrapae quaerebant occasionem ut invenirent Danieli ex latere regni, nullamque causam & suspicionem reperire potuerunt, eo quòd fidelis esset, & omnis culpa & suspicio non inveniretur in eo. Dixerunt ergo viri illi: Non inveniemus Danieli huic aliquam occasionem, nisi fortè in lege dei sui. Tunc principes & satrapae trapae surripuerunt regi, & sic locuti sunt ei: Dari rex, in aeternum vive: consilium inierunt omnes principes regni tui, magistratus & satrapae, senatores & iudices, ut decretum imperatorium exeat, & edictum, ut omnisqui petierit aliquam petitionem à quocunque deo & homine, usque ad triginta dies, nisi à te, rex, mittatur in lacum leonum. Nunc itaque rex confirma sententiam, & scribe decretum, ut non immutetur quod statutum est à Medis & Persis, nec praevaricari cuiquam liceat. Porrò rex Darius proposuit edictum, & statuit. Quod cum Daniel comperisset, id est constitutam legem, ingressus est domum suam: & fenestris apertis in cenaculo suo, contra Ierusalem tribus temporibus in die flectebat genua sua, & adorabat, confitebaturque coram deo suo sicut & antè facere consueverat. Viri ergo illi curiosius inquirentes, invenerunt Danielem orantem, & obsecrantem deum suum. Et accedentes, locuti sunt regi super edicto: Rex, nunquid non constituisti ut omnis homo qui rogaret quenquam de diis & hominibus usque ad dies triginta, nisi te, rex, mitteretur in lacum leonum? Ad quos respondens rex, ait: Verus est sermo iuxta decretum Medorum atque Persarum, quod praevaricari non licet. Tunc respondentes dixerunt coram rege. Daniel de filiis captivitatis Iudae, non curavit de lege tua & de edicto quod constituisti, sed tribus temporibus per diem orat obsecratione sua. Quod verbum cum audisset rex, satis contristatus est: & pro Daniele posuit cor ut liberaret eum, & usque ad occasum solis laborabat ut erueret illum. Viri autem illi intelligentes regem, dixerunt ei: Scito, rex, quia lex Medorum atque Persarum est, ut omne decretum quod constituerit rex, non liceat immutari. Tunc rex praecepit, & adduxerunt Danielem, & miserunt eum in lacum leonum. Dixitque rex Danieli: Deus tuus quem colis semper, ipse liberabit te. Allatusque est lapis unus, & positus est super os laci, quem obsignavit rex annulo suo, & annulo optimatum suorum, ne quid fieret contra Danielem. Et abiit rex in domum suam, & dormivit incoenatus, cibique non sunt allati coram eo, insuper & somnus recessit ab eo. Tunc rex primo diluculo consurgens, festinus ad lacum leonum perrexit: appropinquansque lacui, Danielem voce lachrymabili inclamavit, & affatus est eum: Daniel serve dei viventis, deus tuus cui tu servis semper, putásne valuit te liberare à leonibus? Et Daniel regi respondens, ait: Rex in aeternum vive, Deus meus misit angelum suum, & conclusit ora leonum, & non nocuerunt mihi: quia coram eo iustitia inventa est in me: sed & coram te, rex, delictum non feci. Tunc vehementer rex gavisus est super eo, & Danielem praecepit educi de lacu: eductusque est Daniel de lacu, & nulla laesio inventa est in eo, quia credidit deo suo. Iubente autem rege, adducti sunt viri illi qui accusaverunt Danielem, & in lacum leonum missi sunt, ipsi & filii, & uxores eorum: & non pervenerunt usque ad pavimentum laci, donec arriperent eos Ieones, & omnia ossa eorum comminuerunt. Tunc Darius rex scripsit universis populis, tribubus, & linguis habitantibus in universa terra: Pax vobis multiplicetur. Ame constitutum est decretum, ut in universo imperio & regno meo tremiscant, & paveant deum Danielis: ipse est enim deus vivens & aeternus in secula: & regnum eius non dissipabitur, & potestas eius usque in aeternum. Ipse liberator atque salvator, faciens signa & mirabilia in caelo & in terra: qui liberavit Danielem de lacu leoneum. Porrò Daniel perseveravit usque ad regnum Darii, regnumque Cyri Persae.

I PRIMUM exemplum monet quòd victorias sequi debeat constitutio reipub. & praecipuè verae religionis restitutio, sicut hîc provinciae ordinantur, leguntur praesides, & praeficitur Daniel doctor verae religionis. Itaque vicissim ornat deus Persas ingenti gloria, Iuxta illud, Glorificantes me, glorificabo.

II secundum exemplum ostendit, non deesse ecclesiae persecutionem, & artificiose irretiri principum animos. Erant duo reges, Darius senior, & Cyrus iunior, & non natus patre rege, sed gloria superior. Haec dissimilitudo facilimè parit suspiciones & simultates. Sicut & Xenophon scribit Cyaxaren offensum gloria Cyri, etiam lachrymantem cum eo expostulasse, & questum esse, se contemni, tamet si placatur mirifica diligentia & fide Cyri. Facile igitur fuit astutis & aulicis ingeniis invenire technam ad impellendum regem, qui nolebant crescere Cyrum. Ideo patitur fieri edictum, ut à se omnia beneficia petantur.

Tales habet diabolus ministros, qui captatis occasionibus, regum animos astutè à veritate avertunt, ubi summa offitii & virtutis specie insidiae struuntur. Ita hîc dum bonus senex, suo honori adversus collegam consuli arbitratur, non videt quantum admittat sceleris, quòd in edicto etiam dei invocatio prohibetur

Monet igitur hoc exemplum, ut cauti sint principes in observandis talibus insidiis, ac praesertim in legibus & edictis condendis.

III TERTIO, proponitur exemplum infirmae fidei. Diligenter autem discernendum est inter infirmos & persecutores. Infirmus est, qui amat & amplectitur doctrinam, & propagare cupit, patitur se doceri, ac studet proficere. Cupit etiam salvos esse doctores, &

confitetur aliquo modo, etiamsi non satis libere nec satis fortiter propugnat, neque tamen negat aut oppugnat doctrinam. Talem iubet Paulus recipi, & addit, Deus eum recepit, & potest eum confirmare. Tales erant Apostoli tempore mortis Christi.

Et quidem ita gubernat deus sanctos, ut suam infirmitatem & habeant & agnoscant, ex qua tandem eluctantur. Ideo dicit Esaias, Linum fumigans non extinguit. EtPsalm. 144.Allevat dominus omnes qui corruunt, et erigit omnes elisos.

Sic & hoc loc infirmus est Darius, amplectitur doctrinam, cupit defendere confitentem, adivuat etiam eum suo testimonio, & sua confessione. Haec sunt signa piae mentis, nec dubitat de caussa, sed de facultate sua dubitat. Arbitratur se unum, praesertim in hac regni novitate, non posse obtinere quod volebat, nec licere soli sententiam omnium principum improbare. Et isti non leviter pugnaverunt, toto die contendunt, urgent legis autoritatem, dicunt exemplum fore perniciosum, & contra regnum, si retractentur edicta. Tandem igitur victus rex dubitans vel de autoritate sua, vel de facultate, permittit ut vincant accusatores.

Quanquam igitur peccavit Darius, tamen infirmitate lapsus est, & contra furorem accusatorum sustentat se quadam scintilla fidei, quae ostendit, non ipsum, sed principes esse supplicii autores, etiamsi ipse non satis fortiter eos represserat. Ostendit igitur suam confessionem, Deus tuus, inquiens, liberabit te. Tales infirmos deus sublevat, ut hîc apparet. Sequitur enim statim acerbissima paenitentia regis, ac deinde tantum fidei robur, tanta magnitudo animi, ut puniat etiam accusatores.

Econtra verò persecutores sunt, qui non amant nec amplectuntur doctrinam, nec doceri se patiuntur, nec proficere student, nec tuentur doctores, nec propagare cupiunt ea quae intelligunt, sed mallent quoque sepelire & obruere, ac significant odium. Haec qui faciunt cognita doctrina, timeant iudicium dei de blasphemis. Estque de eis servanda regula Pauli, Haereticum hominem post unam atque alteram monitionem vit abis, quia est suo ipsius testimonio condemnatus.

Iam nos exemplo Darii agnoscamus nostram infirmitatem & proficere studeamus, ac rogemus sicut illeMarci 9.Credo domine, sed adivua incredulitatem meam.

Et memineris hoc loco duplicem consolationem regi exhibitam esse, videlicet, Non solum Daniel liberatus est suo periculo, sed etiam rex liberatus est à contagio parricidii, & consecutus remissionem peccatorum. Haec consolatio, quae ad ipsius conscientiam propriè pertinuit, non minus miraculum fuit quam alterum.

IIII Quarto, exemplum de iniustis legibus retractandis, hoc tempore maximè observandum est.

V. Quinto, Danielis confessio hortatur nos, ut adversemur impiis edictis, quia publicè oravit apertis fenestris. Hortatur item ad precandum, & quidem significat modum, quòd spectans ad Hierusalem, dixerit suas preces, id est, fretus promissione data verae ecclesiae. Quia3. Reg. 8.iubentur exules orantes se vertere versus Hierosolymam. Sic se vertit Daniel versus occasum meridianum, hoc est, cogitans de mandato & promissione, quòd deus velit scruare hunc populum, et exaudire propter promissum dominum. Prorsus sicut Evangelium docet nos orando intueri promissionem, & petere ac expectare auxilium propter Christum, sic illi templum intuentes, de promissione & de venturo domino cogitabant.

VII. Septimo, periculum Danielis pingit robur & violentiam hostium Christi. Sicut Daniel imbecillis obiicitur leonibus, sic tota Ecclesia habet hostes validissimos, diabolum, reges, potentes, superbos, praestantes autoritate & opibus in mundo.

VIII. Octavo, liberatio Danielis est testimonium, quòd deus adsit sanctis, & servet eos suo iudicio, aliàs corpore, aliàs spiritu.

Est autem hîc duplex liberatio, Daniel liberatur à inorte, rex liberatur à peccato & à morte aeterna, discit hîc remissionem peccatorum, & confirmatur in fide. Eam liberationem & Daniel praecipuè optavit, ut deus glorificaretur, & rex revocaretur in viam, & intelligit rex ipse multo esse maiorem quàm erat incolumitas Danielis. Ideo ardentissimo pectore agit gratias deo, ac edit testimonia confessionis.

IX. Nono, tres sunt insignes sententiae in textu, de fide & operibus. Praecipua est de fide, cum inquit, Credidit deo suo.

Quanquam autem brevis est sententia, tamen admonet lectorem de tota doctrina fidei, quòd deus requirat fidem, & quòd fides impetret ingentia dona. Credidit, videlicet initio proposito impio edicto, non abiecit fidem & professionem, sed constanter confessus, se deum Israel invocare, & hac fide petivit & expectavit à deo auxilium, pro rege & pro se, videlicet ne deleretur in regno vera doctrina.

Significat igitur fides hîc ut alibi, fiduciam quae audet constanter confiteri, & statuit deum nos per misericordiam recipere, & velle salvare, & expectat auxilium pro Ecclesia, pro nobis, pro iis qui sunt convertendi. Hanc fidem requirit deus, & haec impetrat beneficia. Iuxta illud Esaiae, Qui confidit in dominum, non pudefiet. Item,Matth. 21.Quicquid petieritis credentes accipietis. Ut igitur fide impetrarunt auxilium, Moyses ad mare rubrum, Esaias in obsidione urbis, & alii sancti in veris periculis, ita hîc Daniel fide impetrat liberationem.

Haec exempla nos quoque in confessione & omnibus periculis exuscitent, & ad fidem ac invocationem adhortentur. Suntque opponendae haec testimonia adversariis, qui de operibus multum vociferantur, & reiiciunt doctrinam de fide.

Reliquae duae sententiae loquuntur de iustitia operum, quae & coram deo in credentibus esse debet, & coram hominibus. Disertè inquit Daniel, Inventa est mihi iustitia coram deo. Deinde addit etiam, Coram te rex, nihil malifeci.

Observabis igitur haec tria proponi, fidem, item inchoatam obedientiam, quae etiamsi est immunda, tamen placet deo in credentibus, qui fide iusti sunt. Oportet enim adesse in nobis iustitiam bonae conscientiae, ut inquit Paulus2. Corin. 1.Haec est gloriatio nostra, testimonium conscientiae nostrae.

Ita hîc Daniel ait, Inventa est mihi iustitia eoram deo. Quod ut rectè intelligatur, adiunge alteram sententiam de fide, Credidit deo suo. Nequaquam enim vult Daniel se esse iustum propter propriam dignitatem aut suas virtutes, sed per misericordiam fide. Et tamen scit requiri &

placere deo iustitiam bonae conscientiae in credentibus.

Tertio additur iustitia erga homines, nam & coram hominibus conspici innocentiam oportet exempli caussa. Iuxta illud, Luceat lux vestra &c.

Et diligenter observandum est, quòd quanquam fecit contra edictum, tamen affirmat se nihil mali fecisse. Hanc vocem Danielis ad nos hoc tempore transferamus. Quanquam enim contra edicta Imperatoris facimus, amen sciamus nos nihil mali contra eum facere, nec esse seditiosos, nec laedere maiestatem superiorum. Talia enim edicta violari necesse est, quae quidem ipsi pro inanibus habere debent. Ita nihil mali contra Imperatorem faciebat Nicomedensis patritius, qui edictum publicè propositum concerpsit. Opponamus igitur vocem Danielis, his qui accusant nos contumaciae, & vituperant tanquam seditiosos. Ad haec reclamemus, nos nihil mali contra Imperatorem facere.

X. DECIMO, poena calumniatorum admonet hostes Evangelii de iudicio dei, & adhortatur eos ad paenitentiam.

XI. UNDECIMO, ut suprà exemplum Nabogdonosor de offitio principum docet, quòd debeant abolere impios cultus & c. sic Darii exemplum hoc loco idem docet, qui etiam edictum proponit de colendo deo Danielis.

XII DUODECIMO, observanda est in edicto regis evangelica concio, ait, hunc deum Danielis esse liberatorem & salvatorem. Nominat deum Danielis, id est, qui agnoscitur et colitur fide verbi, quod praedicat Daniel, quia verbum divinitus traditum, discernit ecclesiam à gentibus. Deus enim non vult agnosci aut coli sine aliquo suo verbo & testimonio. Ideo dicitur deus Abraham, id est, qui dedit hoc verbum, has promissiones Abrahae, &c. Sic dicitur deus Israel, qui eduxi te de terra Aegypti. Sic nos invocamus deum patrem domini nostri Iesu Christi. Testatur igitur rex illum deum esse verum deum, cuius verbum praedicat Daniel.

Deinde addit alterum discrimen, hunc deum esse liberatorem & salvatorem. Nam & hoc discrimen est verae religionis à caeteris omnib.

Ubi tantum est legis notitia, manet dubitatio, & afflicti non possunt statuere, deum verè velle misereri, exaudire, ivuare. Sed Evangelium clamitat, quòd deus gratis nos recipiat credentes. Item quòd verè velit exaudire & salvare, quòd hanc fidem requirit, quod hoc cultu praecipue coli velit. Opponenda est igitur vox Evangelii, & promissio naturali dubitationi & ethnicis opinionibus, & excitanda fides, ne arguamus deum mendacii. Quare haec concio in edicto, quae praedicat deum esse liberatorem et salvatorem, verè Evangelica est, sicut illae in Psalmo, Qui sanat contritos corde, & alligat contritiones eorum. Haec fides in quotidiana invocatione exercenda est.

## 7:1 Argument
CHAPTER VII.

In the first year of Belshazzar king of Babylon, Daniel saw a dream: and the vision of his head upon his bed, and writing the dream, he comprehended it in a brief discourse, and summarizing it, he says: I was seeing in my vision by night, and

Behold, the four winds of heaven were fighting on the great sea. And four great beasts were ascending from the sea, different from one another. The first was like a lioness, and it had the wings of an eagle: I was looking, until its wings were plucked off, and it was lifted up from the earth, and it stood upon its feet like a man, and the heart of a man was given to it. And behold, another beast, like a bear, stood on one side: and there were three rows in its mouth, and in its teeth, and thus they were saying to it: Arise, eat much flesh. After these things I was looking, and behold, another like a * leopard, and it had wings like a bird, four upon it: and there were four heads on the beast, and power was given to it. After these things I was looking in the vision of the night, and behold, a fourth beast, terrible and wonderful, and exceedingly strong: it had great iron teeth, eating and crushing, and trampling the rest with its feet: but it was unlike the other beasts which I had seen before it, and it had ten horns. I was considering the horns, and behold, another little horn arose from the midst of them: and three of the first horns were plucked out from before its face: and behold, eyes like the eyes of a man were in that horn, and a mouth speaking great things. I was looking, until thrones were placed, and the Ancient of Days sat: his garment was white as snow, and the hair of his head like clean wool: his throne, flames of fire: his wheels, a burning fire. A swift river of fire was issuing from before his face: thousands of thousands were ministering to him, and ten times a hundred thousand were standing before him: the judgment sat, and the books were opened. I was looking because of the voice of the great words which that horn was speaking: and I saw that the beast was slain, and its body had perished, and it had been delivered to be burned with fire: of the other beasts also the power would be taken away, and the times of life would be established for them up to a time and * a time. I was watching therefore in the vision of the night, and behold, with the clouds of heaven one like a son of man was coming, and he reached up to the ancient of days, and in his sight they offered him. And he gave him power, and honor and a kingdom: and all peoples, tribes and tongues shall serve him: his power is an eternal power which shall not be taken away, and his kingdom that which shall not be corrupted. My spirit shuddered, I Daniel was terrified in these things, and the visions of my head troubled me. I approached one of those standing by, and I was seeking the truth from him concerning all these things. Who told me the interpretation of the words, and taught me, These four great beasts, are four kingdoms, which shall rise from the earth. But the saints of the most high God shall receive the kingdom, and they shall possess the kingdom even unto the age and the age of ages. After this I wished to learn diligently about the fourth beast, which was very unlike all, and exceedingly terrible: its teeth and claws were of iron: it was eating and breaking in pieces, and trampling the rest with its feet: and about the ten horns which it had on its head: and about the other which had sprung up, before which three horns had fallen: and about that horn which had eyes, and a mouth speaking great things, and was greater than the others. I was looking, and behold, that horn was making war against the saints, and was prevailing against them, until the ancient of days came, and gave judgment to the saints of the most high, and the time arrived, and the saints obtained the kingdom. And thus he says: The fourth beast, the fourth kingdom shall be on the earth, which shall be greater than all kingdoms, and shall devour the whole earth, and shall trample, and shall break it in pieces. Moreover the ten horns of the kingdom itself, there shall be ten kings: and another shall rise up after them, and he shall be more powerful than the former: and he shall humble three kings. And he shall speak words against the Most High, and shall wear out the saints of the Most High: and he shall think that he can change times and laws, and they shall be delivered into his hand until a time and times and half a time. And judgment shall sit, that power may be taken away, and be broken in pieces, and perish even to the end. But the kingdom and power, and the greatness of the kingdom which is under all the heaven, may be given to the people of the saints of the Most High, whose kingdom is an everlasting kingdom, and all kings shall serve him and obey. Hitherto is the end of the word. I Daniel was much troubled in my thoughts, and my countenance was changed in me, but I kept the word in my heart.

HERE Daniel begins his own visions concerning the succession of empires, and concerning the advent of Christ, and the struggle of the Church, which was to be in the last times. For this vision was exhibited for this reason, that the time of the advent of Christ might be signified, lest on account of the delay and on account of afflictions the pious should despair of the promises.

Furthermore, again in these prophecies I admonish what was said above, that the church is manifoldly instructed and confirmed. First, they testify that God is the author of empires, likewise that the church is defended and preserved among these empires.

Secondly, since the predictions concerning all empires have come to pass, it is certain without doubt that they are divine. Wherefore it must be confessed that those prophets truly had the word of God, and that this doctrine which they have handed down to us is truly divine.

Thirdly, they also warn us concerning future dangers, that we may observe what kind of thing the antichrist is, lest incautiously we assent to those warring against the true church, nor on account of scandals fall away from the faith.

ENARRATION.

But as he established four Monarchies above, Thus this vision shows the same Monarchies in another form.

The Lion signifies the Chaldeans and Assyrians. Two wings signify the two principal nations, which reigned by turns, the Chaldeans and the Assyrians. It is lifted up above the earth, and is changed into the figure of a man, that is, the kingdom becomes very glorious, and lays aside its ferocity, because it is called to the acknowledgment of God.

The Bear signifies the Persians. Three long teeth in its mouth signify three principal kings, Cyrus, Darius, Artaxerxes.

The Leopard signifies Alexander the Macedonian. Four wings and four heads are the successors, for after huge struggles four leaders after Alexander obtained the greatest kingdoms, Seleucus Syria and the further regions, Ptolemy Egypt, Antigonus Asia Minor, Antipater Macedonia.

The nature of the leopard or panther suits the Greek dispositions, they are spotted, fragrant, with a terrible head, they hunt beasts by their scent, and with their head hidden, they fight most courageously for their young and for their offspring, fearing no weapons, they are captured when inebriated with wine.

The fourth beast signifies the Roman empire, which signifies that it would be the most ferocious of all, either because it waged the most difficult wars abroad, or because it had very many civil wars, or because it raged most against the church, or because it destroyed the Jewish nation.

But this particle is especially to be observed, that he says, It trampled the residue with its feet. This picture signifies that the final part of the empire would have more cruelty and calamities. As it appears that Italy has now been miserably torn apart for almost nine hundred years, first by the Goths, the Greeks, afterwards by the Lombards, then by our Emperors up to this time. The residue, that is, the remaining part of the empire. The feet, that is, the final Emperors were trampling it most.

But the meaning is not only to be applied to civil matters, but also to the church. The final kings will most trample the church, just as it has appeared for six hundred years now, that kings are the satellites of the popes, and exercise horrible cruelty against the church for the defense of idolatry.

The ten horns signify the greatest provinces of the empire. Nor should one dispute anxiously about the number, because the number seven and the number ten are customarily used in the scriptures to signify a large number, as in Job, You confound me ten times. Likewise in the Gospel Matt. 25. The kingdom of heaven is like unto ten virgins. He understands therefore many large kingdoms, and more than the previous Monarchies had.

But yet if anyone wishes to count, the enumeration of the greatest provinces fits not ineptly, such as Italy, Spain, Gaul, Germany, Illyricum, Greece, Africa, Egypt, Asia, Syria. These were the most ample provinces of the empire, and to each of these neighboring parts are to be added, as Pannonia to Illyricum, Macedonia to Greece.

Moreover, a huge horn arises in the forehead, with three other horns plucked out. He says that that horn will be more powerful than the other neighboring ones, will change the religion of Christ, and will have huge successes, and will wage war with the saints.

These things must necessarily be understood concerning some kingdom arisen in the dissipation of the Roman Monarchy, which is more powerful than the other kingdoms, and professes a religion and doctrine fighting against the Gospel, kills the saints, and has huge successes.

Since, however, it is certain that the time has long since passed in which the Roman Monarchy collapsed, let us consider the history of the world, and let us take the interpretation from the event itself. Especially since these very prophecies were handed down primarily so that they might warn and confirm the pious, whose age falls into those dangers which that new dominion, rising after the collapsed Roman Monarchy, threatens to the church.

WHAT is, however, the kingdom born immediately upon the collapse of the Roman Monarchy, proposing a dogma which is openly insulting to the son of God, and openly abolishes prophetic and apostolic scripture, and forces other nations by arms to embrace a new, fictitious, and impious doctrine, and excels all kingdoms in power? The thing itself speaks, that this is the Mahometan kingdom, that is, the Saracen and Turkish. For the Turkish arose from the Saracen, or is a part of it.

Nor do I mix the pontifical dominion into this passage, for this is described in another place, as I shall indicate later. Mention is made here of a kingdom established by arms, but the pontifical dominion grew by the deceptions of ceremonies and hypocrisy, and more it is fortified more by the foreign arms of kings than by its own. However, another passage follows below, which, since Paul transfers it to pontifical dominion, I too shall follow the interpretation of the Apostle, as is fitting.

Now I shall speak briefly concerning the prophecy of this passage. The time, doctrine, power, and place correspond to the Saracen kingdom.

Concerning the time, the histories are well known. The Franks were already holding Gaul, the Lombards Italy, when in the East, in the time of Heraclius, Mahomet stirs up sedition around the year from the birth of Christ 623. Therefore, with the Roman Monarchy collapsing, the Saracen kingdom was established, and indeed in a short time great accessions were made.

The doctrine is described in the text, when it says, that the horn had the eyes of a man, and a mouth speaking great things, and blasphemous things against God.

It is evident that Mahomet, with horrible audacity, abolishes all the writings of the prophets and apostles. Moreover, the most true and principal sign of the church is the profession of prophetic and apostolic doctrine. Wherefore it is sufficiently clear that that kingdom wages war against God, which abolishes the writings of the prophets and apostles. Next and utterly destroys the principal articles of the Gospel. He feigned that Christ is a teacher similar to Moses or other prophets, nor does he acknowledge Him to be the Savior and victim for the sins of men. He rejects entirely those things which are said in the Gospel concerning the Son of God. He is entirely ignorant of the doctrine concerning faith and true invocation; finally, he destroys the proper doctrine of the Gospel. He retains only a particle of civil precepts, although he corrupts these as well. He grants wicked lusts, he commands that nations be compelled by arms to embrace an impious doctrine, and that those who oppose be killed. This is truly to command impious and infinite robbery.

Concerning power, also, the histories are well known. All of Africa, a great part of Asia, and a great part of Europe is held by those who defend the opinion of Muhammad. And now the Turks dominate most widely of all, whose empire here is not distinguished from the Saracen.

But Ezekiel and the Apocalypse speak by name concerning this barbarity, which Ezekiel writes will come from the north in the final age of the world, and he attributes to them the names Gog and Magog, because in the Scythian manner they carry their dwellings around, nor do they have fixed seats. It is certain, however, that the Turks, having advanced from the cliffs of the Caucasus, afterwards wandered in Asia and gradually occupied Galatia and the coast of Pontus. Ezekiel and the Apocalypse prophesy that this barbarism will rule in the end, and indeed will widely devastate the churches of God, and will finally perish in the mountains of Israel, as I shall say later.

Regarding the place, it must be observed that Daniel says by name that the former or anterior horns were plucked out. He signifies that the Roman provinces situated in the east were about to fail. And three horns are named. For the Saracens immediately joined these three regions, Egypt, Syria, and the coast of Cilicia. But the fact that more provinces were conquered afterwards does not conflict with the text, which describes the beginning of the kingdom in this place.

AFTER I have explained what kingdom is described, let us now consider the reasons why God has revealed these things. Human minds are greedy for the knowledge of future things, by a certain perverse curiosity. But God revealed the greatest things at the beginning, the Advent of his son, the future judgment, the resurrection of the dead, the eternal punishments of the impious, the eternal glory of the pious, of kingdoms the succession or order of the principal things before the last judgment. Since God has not shown these great things in vain, His will must be diligently considered. And then indeed He instructed Daniel and the others with this doctrine concerning eternal life and the coming judgment. The same doctrine ought to confirm us even more, for we see that the events have corresponded; the four Monarchies have appeared in order, now a nation hostile to God dominates, which openly destroys the prophetic and apostolic writings. Let us not doubt, therefore, that the rest which the vision shows will also come to pass, namely concerning the judgment, concerning the punishments of the wicked, and concerning the glory of the pious. Let us know for certain that this doctrine, which Daniel professed, is divine, and let us not allow ourselves to be torn away from it. This passage, however, contains doctrine, threatening, and consolation.

The doctrine pertains most especially to this last age. Men are greatly moved by fortune in both directions. But since there have been huge successes and admirable victories of the Turkish kingdom, and the Churches have been so widely devastated, men follow fortune; many defect to the Turks and embrace the impious doctrine.Others seek the society and συμμαχίαν with the Turks, and although they do not embrace their doctrine, yet they assist the impious kingdom. God therefore warns us, lest, captured by admiration of power, or broken by our own miseries, we fall away from the Gospel. The manifest marks of the impious kingdom, which are described here, are that the doctrine is insulting to God, and slaughters the saints. But let it be an evident sign that we know the prophetic and apostolic writings are utterly abolished by Mahomet. To these marks God has added this testimony, that He is about to condemn and cast into eternal punishments this beast, that is, the whole impious kingdom.

The threat is in the text, which I have just reviewed, which denounces destruction to the Mahometan kingdom.

But another threat also is to be observed here, which preaches about the calamities of the church. It predicts that the Mahometan kingdom will stir up wars against the saints, and that there will be devastations of churches, and slaughters of the saints. This is a sad sermon and foreign to human judgments. Why does God grant empire and victories to an impious nation, openly insulting to Christ, openly destroying the prophetic and apostolic doctrine?

Does He allow us who call upon God and the mediator the Son of God, who retain the prophetic and apostolic doctrine, to be either destroyed or oppressed?

Nor does the outcome show that Daniel is preaching about a small or brief calamity. In all of Africa and a great part of Asia, the name of Christ has been destroyed. In Europe, although in the Turkish empire there are still churches here and there, yet they are pressed by such a cruel and barbaric servitude that the studies of letters cannot be cultivated, and there are churches without teachers, and many little by little cast off the Christian name. The tyrant himself also tears away a certain number of children from their parents, whom he trains for military service and his own rites. These things are sadder than the Egyptian servitude, and yet certain madmen seek this empire.

But what are the causes of such great calamities of the church? If human judgments are consulted here, will they not doubt whether God truly loves this oppressed assembly? Whether He has redeemed it with the blood of His Son?

But here the appearance of the church is to be looked at from the beginning. When our first parents had seen the testimony exhibited from heaven in the sacrifice of Abel, by which they were led into the hope of the propagation of of the church to posterity, soon Abel is killed, and one impious Cain remains. The impious posterity of this man also founds a city, and rules, while the first parents are still alive, who were despised.

After the flood, with Babylon founded, the nations again openly despise the teaching of Noah and Shem, they fall away from God, and a small group of the pious remains, which wanders miserably in uncertain dwellings, namely the family of Abraham.

Then in Egypt how atrocious a cruelty is exercised against the posterity.

But when they had occupied Palestine, how great were the disasters, changes of kingdom, and dissipations that followed, and how small a part of the world were the Israelites, when meanwhile the nations ignorant of God held the highest empires.

Since such has for the most part been the appearance of the church, let us acknowledge the wrath of God against the world, and let us not fall away from God, even if in this last time we see the church harshly shaken, but let us hold that hypothesis fixed in our minds, that by the wonderful counsel and will of God the church is subjected to the cross. The causes are also indicated in the doctrine of the prophets and apostles, which I do not all recount now.

Let us consider only this regarding the final old age of the world, that the prophets and apostles predict that the world will pay the penalty, because after the Gospel was spread, tyrants rage against the members of Christ, and then also by those very ones who govern the church, the church has been polluted with idols, false dogmas, the murders of saints, and lusts. And history shows that the Mahometan plague arose from these seeds.

When Arabia and Egypt were being torn apart by many monstrous dogmas, a confusion of opinions moved fickle men to applaud the new dogma of Mahomet, which removed old controversies and proposes only political opinions and those pleasing to human judgments. Therefore Daniel says that the horn has human eyes.

You see therefore that by these very beginnings dissensions were punished, factions were punished, the Samosatene, the Arian, the Manichaean. For those very places were filled with all these ravings and more. The Turkish power grew afterwards, so that the world might be punished on account of idols, the cult of saints, and the profaned supper of the Lord, and impure celibacy.

And see how the times agree. The decree concerning transubstantiation, as they call it, which confirmed the horrible ἐιδωλομανίαν, it was done in the year from the birth of Christ 1215, Innocent III being pope. Immediately followed the auspices of the Ottoman kingdom, in the year from the birth of Christ 1250.
