Soon therefore after the confirmation of the idol-mania, a kingdom began to arise elsewhere, and to bring forth arms towards the west to punish these stains of the church.

These things are to be considered in the threatening in this place in Daniel, and errors are to be amended, and morals corrected, and true doctrine, and the true invocation of God restored in the church. If we shall do that, God will mitigate the punishments, and will repress the Turkish arms. For He will not allow the church to be utterly destroyed.

Consolation is added, because Daniel signifies that there will not be a fifth Monarchy. Wherefore the Turkish power will not be equal to the Roman, it will not oppress the whole of Europe.

CONCERNING THE TIME the words are obscure, until a time, and times, and half a time. But in general I understand, a long time, and then a sudden and unexpected decline. Because he says, Half a time, as if Let him say, When the Turkish power shall have reached its summit, and shall already hope that it will possess the empire of the whole world, and shall promise itself dominion without end, some sudden decline will occur. And soon that most joyful day will dawn, on which the son of God will resurrect the dead, and will grant life and eternal glory to his church, and will cast all the impious into eternal torments.

The same things are said concerning Gog and Magog in Ezekiel and in the Apocalypse. Ezekiel says that Gog and Magog, having committed huge devastations, will finally perish on the mountains of Israel.

Although it can be understood concerning the final judgment of Christ, nevertheless I think that the battles are signified which the pious will have in this last old age of the world with the Turks, who will be conquered on the mountains of Israel, that is, either in the places of the church in which the Gospel truly sounds, or by the peoples truly calling upon God in the faith of his son Jesus Christ. For the Turks will not be conquered except by the son of God fighting for his church, as he says clearly belowDaniel, chapter 12.Michael will stand, that is, Christ the great leader for the children of the people. But also in Daniel and in Ezekiel great devastations are denounced, which let us seek with our whole heart that God may mitigate.

Latin:

CAPUT VII.

Anno primo Balsasar regis Babylonis, Daniel somnium vidit: visio autem capitis eius in cubili suo, & somnium scribens, brevi sermone comprehendit, summatimque perstringens, ait: Videbam in visione mea nocte, &

ecce, quatuor venti caeli pugnabant in mari magno. Et quatuor bestiae grandes ascendebant de mari diversae inter se. Prima quasi leaena, & alas habebat aquilae: aspiciebam, donec evulsae sunt alae eius, & sublata est de terra, & super pedes quasi homo stetit, & cor hominis datum est ei. Et ecce bestia alia similis urso in parte stetit: & tres ordines erant in ore eius, & in dentibus eius, & sic dicebant ei: Surge, comede carnes plurimas. Post haec aspiciebam, & ecce, alia quasi * pardus, & alas habebat quasi avis, quatuor super se: & quatuor capita erant in bestia, & potestas data est ei Post haec aspiciebam in visione noctis, & ecce, bestia quarta terribilis atque mirabilis, & fortis nimis: dentes ferreos habebat magnos, comedens atque comminuens, & reliqua pedibus suis conculcans: dissimilis autem erat caeteris bestris quas videram ante eam, & habebat cornua decem. Considerabam cornua, & ecce cornu aliud parvulum ortum est de medio eorum: & triae de cornibus primis evulsa sunt à facie eius: & ecce oculi, quasi oculi hominis erant in cornu isto, & os loquens ingentia. Aspiciebam, donec throni positi sunt, & antiquus dierum sedit: vestimentum eius candidum quasi nix, & capilli capitis eius quasi lana munda: thronus eius, flammae ignis: rotae eius, ignis accensus. Fluvius igneus rapidusque egrediebatur à facie eius: millia millium ministrabant ei, & decies millies centena millia assistebant ei: iudicium sedit, et libri aperti sunt. Aspiciebam propter vocem sermonum grandium, quos cornu illud loquebatur: & vidi quoniam interfecta esset bestia, & perisset corpus eius, & traditum esset ad comburendum igni: aliarum quoque bestiarum ablata esset potestas, & tempora vitae constituta essent eis usque ad tempus & * tempus. Aspiciebam ergo in visione noctis, & ecce, cum nubibus caeli quasi filius hominis veniebat, & usque ad antiquum dierum pervenit, & in conspectu eius obtulerunt eum. Et dedit ei potestatem, & honorem & regnum: & omnes populi, tribus & linguae ipsi servient: potestas eius, potestas aeterna quae non auferetur, & regnum eius quod non corrumpetur. Horruit spiritus meus, ego Daniel territus sum in his, & visiones capitis mei conturbaverunt me. Accessi ad unum de assistentibus, & veritatem quaerebam ab eo de omnibus his. Qui dixit mihi interpretationem sermonum, & docuit me, Hae quatuor bestiae magnae, quatuor sunt regna, quae consurgent de terra. Suscipient autem regnum sancti dei altissimi, & obtinebunt regnum usque in seculum & seculum seculorum. Post hoc volui diligenter discere de bestia quarta, quae erat dissimilis valde ab omnibus, & terribilis nimis: dentes & ungues eius ferrei: comedebat & comminuebat, & reliqua pedibus suis conculcabat: & de cornibus decem quae habebat in capite: & de alio quod ortum fuerat, ante quod ceciderant tria cornua: & de cornu illo quod habebat oculos, & os loquens grandia, et maius erat caeteris. Aspiciebam, & ecce, cornu illud faciebat bellum adversus sanctos, & praevalebat eis, donec venit antiquus dierum, & iudicium dedit sanctis excelsi, & tempus advenit, & regnum obtinuerunt sancti. Et sic ait: Bestia quarta, regnum quartum erit in terra, quod maius erit omnibus regnis, & devorabit universam terram, & conculcabit, & comminuet eam. Porrò cornua decem ipsius regni, decem reges erunt: & alius consurget post eos, & ipse potentior erit prioribus: et tres reges humiliabit. Et sermones contra excelsum loquetur, & sanctos altissimi conteret: & putabit quòd possit mutare tempora & leges, & tradentur in manu eius usque ad tempus et tempora & dimidium temporis. Et iudicium sedebit, ut auferatur potentia, & conteratur, & dispereat usque in finem. Regnum autem & potestas, & magnitudo regni quae est subter omne caelum, detur populo sanctorum altissimi, cuius regnum, regnum sempiternum est, & omnes reges servient ei & obedient. Hucusque finis verbi. Ego Daniel multum cogitationibus meis conturbabar, & facies mea mutata est in me, verbum autem in corde meo conservavi.

HIC Daniel inchoat proprias visiones de successione imperiorum, et de adventu Christi, ac certamine Ecclesiae, quod postremis temporibus futurum erat. Ideo enim haec visio exhibita est, ut significaretur tempus adventus Christi, ne propter moram & propter afflictiones pii desperarent de promissis.

Porrò iterum in his vaticiniis admoneo, quod suprà dictum est, multipliciter erudiri et confirmari ecclesiam. Primum testantur deum esse autorem imperiorum, item defendi & servari ecclesiam inter haec imperia.

Secundo, cum praedictiones de omnibus imperiis evenerint, constat haud dubiè divinas esse. Quare fatendum est, istos prophetas vere habuisse verbum dei, & hanc doctrinam quam nobis tradiderunt, verè divinam esse.

Tertio monent etiam nos de futuris periculis, ut observemus, qualis sit antichristus, ne incauti assentiamur belligerantibus cum vera ecclesia, nec propter scandala deficiamus à fide.

ENARRATIO.

Ut autem suprà quatuor Monarchias constituit, ita haec visio easdem Monarchias ostendit alia specie.

Leo significat Chaldaeos & Assyrios. Duae alae significant duas praecipuas gentes, quae per vices regnaverunt, Chaldaeos & Assyrios. Elevatur supra terram, & mutatur in figuram hominis, id est, regnum valde fit gloriosum, & deponit immanitatem, quid vocatur ad agnitionem dei.

Ursus significat Persas. Tres longi dentes in ore significant tres praecipuos reges, Cyrum, Darium, Artaxerxen.

Pardus significat Alexandrum Macedonem. Quatuor alae & quatuor capita successo res sunt, nam post ingentia certamina quatuor duces post Alexandrum obtinuerunt regna maxima, Seleucus Syriam & ulteriora, Ptolemeus Aegyptum, Antigonus Asiam minorem, Antipater Macedoniam.

Natura pardi vel pantherae convenit Graecis ingeniis, sunt maculosae, fragrantes, terribili capite, venantur bestias odore, & occultato capite, animosissime dimicant pro natis & pro sobole, nulla tela formidantes, capiuntur vino inebriatae.

Quarta bestia significat Romanum imperium, quod significat omnium fore truculentissimum, vel quia foris difficilima bella gessit, vel quia habuit plurima civilia bella, vel quia maximè saeviit in ecclesiam, vel quia delevit gentem Iudaicam.

Sed praecipuè observanda est haec particula, quòd inquit, Residuum pedibus conculcabat. Haec pictura significat postremam imperii partem habituram plus crudelitatis & calamitatum. Ut apparet Italiam nunc annis ferè nongentis miserabiliter dilaceratam esse, primum à Gottis, Graecis, postea à Longobardis, deinde à nostris Imperatoribususque ad hoc tempus. Residuum, id est, partem imperii reliquam. Pedes, id est, postremi Imperatores maximè conculcabant.

Sed non solum accommodanda est significatio ad civilia, verum etiam ad ecclesiam.Reges postremi maximè conculcabunt ecclesiam, sicut iam annis sexcentis apparet, reges esse satellites pontificum, & crudelitatem horribilem in ecclesiam exercere ad defensionem idolatriae.

Decem cornua significant maximas provincias imperii. Nec de numero anxiè disputan dum est, quia septenarius & denarius usitatè in scripturis significant magnum numerum, ut in Hiob, En decies me confunditis. Item in EvangelioMatth. 25.Simile est regnum caelorum decem virginibus. Intelligit igitur multa ampla regna, ac plura quàm superiores Monarchiae habuerunt.

Sed tamen si quis volet numerare, non ineptè quadrat enumeratio maximarum provinciarum, ut Italia, Hispania, Gallia, Germania, Illyricum, Graecia, Africa, Aegyptus, Asia, Syria. Hae fuerunt amplissimae imperii provinciae, ac singulis vicinae partes addendae sunt, ut Illyrico Pannonia, Graeciae Macedonia.

Exoritur autem in fronte ingens cornu, tribus aliis cornibus evulsis. Id cornu ait potentius fore caeteris vicinis, mutaturum religionem Christi, & habiturum ingentes successus, bellaturum cum sanctis.

Haec necesse est intelligi de regno aliquo exorto in dissipatione Romanae Monarchiae, quod est potentius caeteris regnis, & profitetur religionem ac doctrinam pugnantem cum Evangelio, interficit sanctos, & habet ingentes successus.

Cum autem constet multo antè praeteriisse tempus, quo Romana Monarchia collapsa est, historiam mundi consideremus, & ex ipso eventu sumamus interpretationem. Praesertim cum haec ipsa vaticinia maximè tradita sint, ut moneant & confirment pios, quorum aetas incidit in ea pericula, quae minitatur ecclesiae nova illa dominatio, oriens post collapsam Romanam Monarchiam.

QUOD est autem regnum statim natum collabente Romana Monarchia, proponens dogma, quod apertè contumeliosum est in filium dei, et apertè abolet scripturam propheticam & apostolicam, & cogit armis alias gentes ad amplectendam novam, commentitiam & impiam doctrinam, & potentia omnibus regnis antecellit? Res loquitur ipsa, id esse regnum Mahometicum, hoc est Sarracenicum & Turcicum. Nam Turcicum ortum est ex Sarracenico, seu pars est.

Nec admisceo huic loco pontificiam dominationem, nam haec alio loco describitur, ut postea indicabo. Fit hîc mentio regni armis constituti, At dominatio pontificia praestigiis ceremoniarum & hypocrisi crevit, & magis alienis armis regum quàm suis munita est.Sequitur autem alius locus infra, quem cum Paulus ad pontificiam dominationem transferat, sequar & ego Apostoli interpretationem, ut decet.

Nunc de vaticinio huius loci dicam breviter. Conveniunt ad Sarracenicum regnum, tempus, doctrina, potentia, & locus.

De tempore notae sunt historiae. Iam Galliam Franci tenebant, Italiam Longobardi, cum in oriente Mahometus Heraclii tempore seditionem movet circiter annum à natali Christi 623. Collabente igitur Monarchia Romana Sarracenicum regnum constitutum est, et quidem brevi magnae factae sunt accessiones.

Doctrina describitur in textu, cum ait, cornu habere oculos hominis, & os loquens grandia, & contumeliosa in deum.

Constat Mahometum, horribili audacia abolere omnia prophetarum & apostolorum scripta. Est autem verissimum & praecipuum signum ecclesiae, professio propheticae & apostolicae doctrinae. Quare satis perspicuè constat, id regnum deo bellum inferre, quod abolet scripta prophetarum & apostolorum. Deinde & articulos praecipuos Evangelii penitus delet. Finxit Christum esse doctorem similem Moysi aut aliis prophetis, nec agnoscit esse salvatorem & victimam pro peccatis hominum. Abiicit prorsus ea quae de filio dei in Evangelio dicuntur. Ignorat prorsus doctrinam de fide & vera invocatione, denique propriam Evangelii doctrinam delet. Tantum particulam retinet civilium praeceptorum, quanquam haec quoque corrumpit. Concedit flagitiosas libidines, iubet armis cogi gentes ad amplectendam impiam doctrinam, & interfici adversantes. Hoc verè est impium & infinitum latrocinium praecipere.

De potentia etiam notae sunt historiae. Tota Africa, magna pars Asiae, magna Europae tenetur ab iis qui Mahometi sententiam defendunt. Et nunc Turci omnium latissime dominantur, quorum imperium hîc non distinguitur à Sarracenico.

Sed Ezechiel et Apocalypsis nominatim de hac barbarie concionantur, quam scribit Ezechiel venturam ab aquilone postrema mundi aetate, & tribuit eis adpellationes Gog & Magog, quôd Scythico more tecta circumferant, nec habeant certas sedes. Constat autem Turcos à Caucasi rupibus progressos, postea in Asia vagatos esse ac paulatim Galatiam & Ponti oram occupasse. Hanc barbariem vaticinantur Ezechiel & Apocalypsis postremo imperaturam esse, & quidem late vastaturam esse ecclesias dei, & tandem in montibus Israel perituram esse, ut postea dicam.

De leco observandum est, quòd nominatim inquit Daniel, evulsa esse priora vel anteriora cornua. Significat defecturas fuisse provincias Romanas in oriente sitas. Et nominantur tria cornua. Primum enim statim coniunxerunt Sarraceni has tres regiones, Aegyptum, Syriam, & Ciliciae oram. Quòd vero postea plures provinciae domitae sunt, id non pugnat cum textu, qui hoc loco initium regni describit.

POSTQUAM exposui, quod regnum describatur, iam caussas consideremus cur deus haec patefecerit. Humanae mentes sunt avidae cognitionis futurorum, quadam perversa curiositate. Sed deus maximas res initio patefecit, Adventum filii sui, futurum iudicium, resuscitationem mortuorum, aeternas poenas impiorum, aeternam gloriam piorum, regnorum praecipuorum successionem seu ordinem ante ultimum iudicium. Has tantas res cum non frustra monstrarit deus, voluntas eius diligenter consideranda est. Ac tunc quidem Danielem et caeteros hac doctrina erudiit de vita aeterna, et venturo iudicio. Nos magis etiam confirmare eadem doctrina debet, videmus enim respondisse eventus, extiterunt ordine quatuor Monarchiae, nunc dominatur gens inimica deo, quae palàm delet scripta prophetica & apostolica. Non dubitemus igitur eventura & caetera quae visio ostendit, videlicet de iudicio, de poenis impiorum, de gloria piorum. Sciamus certò hanc doctrinam, quam Daniel professus est, divinam esse, nec ab ea avelli nos sinamus. Continet autem hic locus doctrinam, comminationem & consolationem.

Doctrina ad hanc ultimam aetatem maximè pertinet. Valde moventur homines fortuna in utramque partem. Cum autem fuerint ingentes successus, & admirandae victoriae regni Turcici, Ecclesiae verò tam latè vastatae sint, sequuntur homines fortunam, multi ad Turcos deficiunt, & amplectuntur impiam doctrinam.Alii expetunt societatem &συμμαχίανcum Turcis, & quanquam non amplectuntur eorum doctrinam, tamen regnum impium adivuant. Praemonet igitur nos deus, ne capti admiratione potentiae, aut fracti nostris erumnis deficiamus ab Evangelio. Manifestae notae sunt impii regni, quae hîc describuntur, quòd doctrina est contumeliosa in deum, & sanctos trucidat. Sed evidens signum sit, quòd scimus à Mahometo prorsus aboleri scripta prophetica & apostolica. Ad has notas addidit deus hoc testimonium, quòd sit damnaturus & in supplicia aeterna abiecturus hanc bestiam, id est, totum regnum impium.

Comminatio est in textu, quam modo recensui, quae regno Mahometico exitium denunciat.

Sed alia etiam comminatio hîc observanda est, quae de calamitatibus ecclesiae concionatur. Praedicit regnum Mahometicum bella moturum esse adversus sanctos, & futuras vastationes ecclesiarum, & caedes sanctorum. Haec est tristis concio & aliena à iudiciis humanis. Cur imperium & victorias tribuit deus genti impiae, palàm contumeliosae in Christum, palàm delenti doctrinam propheticam & apostolicam?

Eur nos qui invocamus deum & mediatorem filium dei, qui retinemus doctrinam propheticam & apostolicam, sinit aut deleri aut opprimi?

Nec de exigua aut brevi calamitate concionari Danielem eventus ostendit. In tota Africa & magna parte Asiae, deletum est nomen Christi. In Europa etsi in imperio Turcico adhuc pasim reliquae sunt ecclesiae, tamen adeo crudeli & barbarica servitute premuntur, ut studia literarum coli non possint, & sint ecclesiae sine doctoribus, et multi paulatim nomen Christianum abiiciant. Ipse etiam tyrannus à parentibus abstrahit certum numerum liberorum, quos ad militiam & suos ritus instituit. Haec tristiora sunt aegyptiaca servitute, & tamen quidam amentes expetunt hoc imperium.

Quae sunt autem causae tantarum calamitatum ecclesiae? Si hîc humana iudicia consulantur, nonne dubitabunt, an verè hunc oppressum coetum diligat deus? an sanguine filii redemerit?

Sed hîc intuenda est ecclesiae species indeusque ab initio. Cum primi parentes vidissent testimonium de caelo exhibitum in sacrificio Abel, quo in spem adducebantur propagandae ecclesiae ad posteritatem, mox Abel interficiotur, & reliquus est unus Cain impiu. Huius etiam posteritas impia civitatem condit, & dominatur, vivis adhuc primis parentibus, qui contemnebantur.

Post diluvium condita Babylone rursus gentes palàm contempta doctrina Nohe & Sem, deficiunt à deo, & exiguus coetus piorum reliquus manet, qui miser vagatur incertis sedibus, videlicet familia Abrahae.

Deinde in Aegypto quàm atrox Saevitia in posteritatem exercetur.

Cum verò occupassent palestinam, quantae clades, regni mutations, & dissipationes secutae sunt, & quantula mundi pars erant Israelitae, cum interim gentes ignarae dei summa imperia tenuerint.

Talis cum plaerunque fuerit ecclesiae species, agnoscamus iram dei adversus mundum, & non deficiamus à deo, etiamsi hoc postremo tempore videmus ecclesiam duriter quassari, illam autem hypothesin teneamus infixam animis, mirabili dei consilio & voluntate eccleseam subiectam esse cruci. Caussae etiam indicantur in doctrina prophetarum & apostolorum, quas omnes nunc non recenseo.

Tantum hoc de postrema senecta mundi consideremus, quòd praedicunt prophetae & apopostoli, mundum poenas daturum esse, quod post sparsum Evangelium, tyranni saeviant in membra Christi, deinde & ab illis ipsis qui gubernant ecclesiam, polluta sit ecclesia, idolis, falsis dogmatibus, parricidiis sanctorum & libidinibus. Et ex his seminibus ortam esse Mahometicam pestem historia ostendit.

Cum Arabia & Aegyptus multis monstrosis dogmatibus dilacerarentur, opinionum confusio movit leves homines, ut novo dogmati Mahometi adplauderent, quod tollebat controversias veteres, & tantum politicas sententias & gratas humanis iudiciis proponit. Ideo dicit Daniel, cornu habere oculos humanos.

Vides igitur his ipsis exordiis punita esse dissidia, punitas factiones, Samosatenam, Arianam, Manichaeam. Nam his deliriis omnibus & pluribus illa ipsa loca referta erant. Crevit postea pocentia Turcica, ut puniretur mundus propter idola, cultus sanctorum & prophanatam cenam domini, & impurum celibatum.

Ac vide quomodo congruant tempora. Decretum de transsubstantiatione, ut vocant, quod confirmavit horribilemἐιδωλομανίαν,factum est anno à natali Christi 1215. Innocentio tertio pontifice. Statim secuta sunt auspicia regni Othomanici, anno à natali Christi 1250. Mox igitur post confirmationem idolomaniae, regnum alibi exoriri coepit, & proferre arma versus occidentem ad has ecclesiae labes puniendas.

Haec in comminatione hoc loco apud Danielem consideranda sunt, & errores emendandi, & mores corrigendi, ac vera doctina, vera invocatio dei in ecclesia restituenda. Id si faciemus, deus mitigabit poenas, & reprimet arma Turcica. Non enim sinet funditus deleri ecclesiam.

Consolatio est addita, quòd Daniel significat non futuram quintam Monarchiam. Quare Turcica potentia non erit par Romanae, non opprimet totam Europam.

DE TEMPORE verba sunt obscura, usque ad tempus, & tempora, & dimidium temporis. Sed in genere intelligo, longum tempus, & deinde subitam ac inexpectatam inclinationem. Quia dicit, Dimidium temporis, quasi dicat, Cum pervenerit ad fastigium Turcica potentia, & iam sperabit se potituram imperio totius orbis, & pollicebitur sibi dominationem sine fine, subita aliqua fiet inclinatio. Et mox illucescet laetissimus ille dies, quo filius dei resuscitabit mortuos, & ecclesiae suae vitam, & gloriam aeternam tribuet, ac impios omnes abiiciet in aeternos cruciatus.

Eadem de Gog & Magog apud Ezechielem & in Apocalypsi dicuntur. Ezechiel ait, Gog & Magog, factis ingentibus vastationibus, tandem in montibus Israel perituros esse.

Etsi de iudicio ultimo Christi intelligi potest, tamen arbitror significari praelia quae piis erunt in hac ultima mundi senecta cum Turcis, qui vincentur in montibus Israel, id est, seu in locis ecclesiae in qua verè sonat Evangelium, vel à populis verè invocantibus deum in fide filii eius Iesu Christi. Non enim vincentur Turcae nisi per filium dei dimicantem pro sua ecclesia, ut infra clarè inquitDaniel, capite 12.Stabit Michael, id est, Christus dux magnus pro filiis populi. Sed & apud Danielem & apud Ezechielem magnae vastationes denunciantur, quas ut deus mitiget, toto pectore petamus.

## 8:1 Argument
CHAPTER VIII.

In the third year of the reign of King Belshazzar, a vision appeared to me, I Daniel, after that which I had seen in the beginning, I saw in my vision, when I was in Shushan the palace which is in the province of Elam: and I saw in the vision that I was by the gate of Ulai. And I lifted up my eyes, and saw: and behold, one ram stood before the marsh, having high horns, and one higher than the other and growing up. Afterwards I saw the ram pushing with his horns against the west, and against the north, and against the south: and all beasts could not withstand him, nor be delivered out of his hand: and he did according to his will, and became great. And I was understanding: and behold, a he-goat came from the west upon the face of the whole earth, and did not touch the earth: moreover the goat had a notable horn between his eyes. And he came even to that horned ram, which I had seen standing b before the gate, and ran at him in the fury of his power. And when he had approached near the ram, he was enraged against him, and struck the ram, and broke his two horns, and the ram could not resist him: and when he had cast him to the ground, he trampled him, and no one could deliver the ram from his hand. But the goat of the goats became exceedingly great: and when he had grown, the great horn was broken and there arose four horns under it toward the four winds of heaven. And out of one of them came forth one little horn: and it became great against the south and against the east, and against c the strength. And it was magnified even to the d strength of heaven: and it cast down of the strength, and of the stars, and trampled them. And it was magnified even to the prince of strength: and from him the continual sacrifice was taken away, and he cast down the place of his sanctuary. g And strength was given him against the continual sacrifice because of sins: and truth shall be cast down on the ground, and he shall do, and shall prosper. And I heard one of the saints speaking: and one saint said to another, I know not to whom speaking: How long shall be the vision, and the continual sacrifice, and the sin of the desolation that is made: and the sanctuary, and h the strength be trodden under foot? And he said to him: Unto evening and morning, two thousand three hundred days: and the sanctuary shall be cleansed. And it came to pass when I Daniel saw the vision, and sought the understanding: behold, there stood in my presence as it were the appearance of a man. And I heard the voice of a man between Ulai: and he called, and said: Gabriel, make this man to understand the vision. And he came, and stood near where I stood:

And when he had come, I fell terrified upon my face, and he said to me: Understand, O son of man, for in the time of the end the vision shall be fulfilled. And as he was speaking to me, I fell flat on the ground: and he touched me, and set me on my standing, and said to me: I will show thee what things are to come in the last of the indignation: for the time hath its end. The ram, which thou sawest to have horns, is the king of the Medes and Persians. And the he-goat, is the king of the Greeks, and the great horn that was between his eyes, he is the first king. But whereas when that was broken, there arose up four for it: four kings shall rise up of his nation, but not with his strength. And after their kingdom, when iniquities shall be grown up, there shall arise a king shameless in face, and understanding dark sentences: and his power shall be strengthened, but not by his own force: and beyond what is believed he can, he will lay waste to all things, and he will prosper, and he will act. And he will kill the strong, and the people of the saints according to his will, and deceit will be directed in his hand, and he will magnify his heart, and in the abundance of all things he will kill very many: and he will rise up against the prince of princes, and he will be broken without hand. And the vision of the evening and the morning, which has been spoken, is true: you therefore seal the vision, because it will be after many days. And I Daniel languished, and was sick for days: and when I had risen, I did the works of the king, and I was astonished at the vision, and there was no one who could interpret it.

It has been said several times, for what purpose it is useful to hold the predictions concerning the series of Monarchies, and of all times up to the final judgment. For the church has need of this doctrine and consolation, lest it despair among so many afflictions and scandals.

There is also need of admonition, that we may consider the causes let us fear afflictions. Here he says expressly in chapter 8, that on account of the sins of the people it is permitted that Antiochus should exercise such great cruelty. As Paul says,2. Thes. 2.Because they did not love the truth, God will send them an effective deception, that they may believe a lie. May these atrocious threats rouse us, that we may be more diligent in preserving the purity of doctrine, and in life, lest God allow greater darkness to arise.

Furthermore, since in the next chapter Daniel described all the Monarchies and the final kingdom, warring with the true church, he now returns to describe more clearly two Monarchies, the Persian and the Greek, for these were the nearest. And it was profitable for the pious to foreknow the horrible dissipation which Antiochus Epiphanes was about to cause, so that they might have consolation, and not fail, nor think that it would come to pass that the people of God would be entirely blotted out.

Moreover, it is a clear prophecy, and declared by the son of God to the angel who was asking. I will briefly recite the sum. Daniel under Belshazzar was absent from the court, and was not in the city of Babylon, but in Persia, which the Hebrews call Elam, in the citadel of Susa, which has its name from roses, by the river Ulai, which Strabo and Pliny call Eulaeus. Since therefore the end of the first Monarchy was already approaching, Daniel saw that the Persians were about to possess the empire, therefore this very vision is shown to him in Persia.

He sees a ram standing in Persia, that is, the leader Cyrus, who led both armies, the Median and the Persian, therefore two horns are added to the ram, which signify the kingdom of the Medes and Persians. And the Persian Monarchy lasted about two hundred years. Afterwards Alexander occupied Asia, who is signified here by the goat. The four horns signify the successors of Alexander, who, variously joined by treaties, held the greatest power. This is the sum of the history which is recited here, up to Antiochus Epiphanes.

There is no doubt, however, that certain meanings are contained in the pictures, as I said briefly above concerning the leopard, which is both sagacious and spirited and endowed with remarkable speed, it dares to encounter an armed hunter and by its speed it escapes the arrows. Nor is it taken by force, but it is overcome while drunk, for because leopards are greedy for wine, the hunter pours wine in a place near the lair of that beast, afterwards at a longer interval, he places bowls full of wine.

Immediately the scent draws the leopards from their dens, which, when they have come to the bowls, having drunk the wine, they are inflamed, run about, play together, and mate. Then, drunk and exhausted from mating, they fall asleep; there the hunter overcomes them while they are asleep. A clear description of this matter is in Oppian, book four κυνηγετικῶν,, where these verses are also read.

λάπτουσι διώνυσον, ἐπʼ ἀλλήλοισι δὲ πᾶσαι σκιρτῶσι μεὲν πρῶτα χοροιτυπέουσιν ὅμοιαι..

What image, however, can be thought of more illustrious than the sagacity, that is, the cleverness and counsel, of very many Macedonian kings? Scarcely any leaders surpassed Philip and Alexander. Furthermore, what they had decided upon after considering the matter and looking out for opportunities, they undertook most swiftly, which virtue prevails greatly in leaders.

Moreover, they were most fierce and invincible in fighting. Yet, in the end, they perished through pleasures, the one by Venus, the other by drunkenness. Such also were Demetrius and the later Philip afterwards.

Nor does it rarely happen that excellent men, when they have been most fierce in conducting affairs, and have succumbed neither to any dangers nor to labors, afterwards nevertheless, broken by pleasures, they had calamitous and tragic ends.

I could recite recent examples of great leaders. In Francesco Sforza, how great was his virtue at first, how great his greatness of soul? Yet as an old man, as if his nature had been changed by the poisons of Circe, he deformed and overwhelmed his former honors with lusts.

But I omit examples, which the reader, once warned, may consider himself in common life. For Daniel also proposed these pictures, so that he might warn great minds not to indulge themselves, but to moderate their impulses and desires, so that they do not summon punishments upon themselves, as those very ones whom I have recounted did: Philip, Alexander, Demetrius, and countless others.

In this place there is another picture of the Greek nation. Daniel calls them goats, for just as these have exceptional skill in climbing and delight in constant leaping, so the curiosity of the Greek minds was wonderful in testing new things, arts, and counsels. I also think that stage plays are being censured, to which that nation was especially devoted, which indeed produced and confirmed many great outrages.

I return, however, to the narration of the prophecy. A long catalog of the successors of Alexander will be recounted below by Daniel himself. Now, with the others omitted, he speaks of Antiochus Epiphanes, who was sprung from Seleucus, as I shall explain below, and held the kingdom of Syria. He began to reign, however, in the 137th year after the death of Alexander, as the first book of Maccabees testifies, that is, in the second year of the one hundred and forty-ninth Olympiad.

It is customary, however, as the histories of all ages show, for kings to adapt and bend religions to their own advantage. And so, since Epiphanes knew that the Jews, on account of their religion, would not easily endure foreign empires, and yet saw that he had obtained an opportunity for both things, of taking possession of Judea and of changing their religion—a man who was an atheist, and who considered all religions to be equally fabulous, and who rushed with immense insolence into lusts and into all flagrant crimes, whither he was carried by a blind impulse—he applied himself with all his might to both, so that he might both reduce Judea to servitude and utterly abolish the ancient doctrine concerning God received from the prophets.

The crimes of the high priests, however, had offered an opportunity. When a pious man held the high priesthood Onias, his brother Jason having set out to Antiochus, gave him money under the auspices of the kingdom, so that, having captured his brother, he might hand over the high priesthood to him. By a similar fraud, another, Menelaus, later snatched the high priesthood from Jason, and both introduced Greek games into the city, they despised and neglected the study of the divine law, they imitated ethnic splendor, delights, ethnic customs, etc.

Menelaus having later been killed, Alcimus succeeded him as high priest, who also was an enemy of the Maccabees. These crimes of the high priests were the beginnings of the calamities of that time.

Moreover, two expeditions of Epiphanes are described, by which he came to Jerusalem. For twice having set out into Egypt, on his return he came to Jerusalem. He sought Egypt, however, boasting that he was the guardian of his kinsman and neighbor, the young king, so that under the pretext of guardianship he might occupy Egypt. And in the first expedition he imposed garrisons on some cities, still feigning friendship with his young kinsman. Returning, however, he was summoned to Jerusalem by Jason, where, having plundered the temple, and having made a great slaughter, he departed again, not yet changing the republic of the Jews. These things happened when Epiphanes was already reigning in his sixth year, namely after the death of Alexander in the 143rd year.

AFTER two years Antiochus returned into Egypt. For Philometor had expelled the garrisons of Antiochus, and had sought help from the Romans. Therefore Antiochus came to wage war against his kinsman. But the Roman legate Popilius forbade war to be waged against Egypt, and reminded him that the senate would defend Egypt. And when Antiochus answered ambiguously, Popilius having drawn a circle around Antiochus, said that he demanded that he answer clearly whether he was going to depart from Egypt and lead away the army, or not, before he should step out of this circle. There, terrified, Antiochus promised peace.

Returning therefore now from Menelaus he is called to Jerusalem, there he celebrates the Bacchanalia, with Menelaus as his helper, he profanes the temple, having placed there the idol of Olympian Jupiter, everywhere before houses altars are built, and idols are placed in the manner of the gentiles, he prohibits the law and circumcision. And he added to Menelaus the high priest a cohort, which might protect the royal edicts. Sacred books are burned, many pious men are killed, the greatest part of the people defected to the heathens. These things happened in the year 145 after the death of Alexander.

Here God stirs up the old man Mattathias from the priestly tribe, the father of the Maccabees, who, having gathered an army, began to overturn idols everywhere, and to fight with the cohort of Antiochus. After the father, Judas Maccabaeus led the army. These small forces, gathered by private counsel, recovered the temple, and destroyed the armies of Antiochus in many battles. Therefore it says below, They shall be helped with a little help. And the temple was recovered in the 3rd year after Antiochus had placed an idol and garrisons there, namely in the year 148 after the death of Alexander. The rest of the history should be sought from the books of the Maccabees. For I have recited this part because of the numbers placed here, and so that the words of the prophecy might be weighed more diligently.

DANIEL sets the time here, one thousand three hundred days, that is, six years, three months and twenty days. I judge that this time begins from the second expedition of Epiphanes, that is, from the year 145 up to the year 151, in which the whole region was recovered, with the army of Nicanor destroyed. And you see the event most correctly correspond to the prophecy.

Some pious men understood this consolation, by which they felt it was promised that this calamity would not be long-lasting. And so to this inter The number fits the interpretation, so that the months also correspond. For the statue was placed in the temple in the year 145, in the month of Casleu, which for us is November. Then Nicanor was defeated in the year 151, in the month of Adar, which for us is February. There are therefore six whole Jewish years, which began from the vernal equinox. To these add the months following the placement of the statue in the temple, December, January, February, and also some days, for the statue was placed in the temple in the month of November. Therefore, the time of Daniel agrees most exactly with this event.

That this prophecy properly preaches about the history of Antiochus Epiphanes is testified by the angel Gabriel, the interpreter in the text, who indeed, speaking with the son of God, received the interpretation from him.

FURTHERMORE let this passage admonish us concerning the presence of Christ, namely that the son of God was always present to the fathers, and that he truly is present now also to the church, hears and governs those calling upon him, just as he says in the Gospel, Behold, I am with you, etc.

Likewise, wherever two or three are gathered in my name, there am I in the midst of them.

Item, He ascended, that he might give gifts to men, &c.Gen. 48.Jacob says concerning Christ, The Angel who delivered me from all evil, bless the boys, &c.

AndJohn 1.it is said, All things were made through him, &c.

And here he is present at the conversation of the angels in Daniel.

And Paul says, All drank from the spiritual rock accompanying them, and the rock was Christ.

Thus we ought to establish that Christ is present, hears, helps, and governs us. And scripture calls this the kingdom of Christ. But the weakness of the human mind cannot thus understand the kingdom of Christ, it thinks of him as if he were absent, not doing anything with us. But one must fight against these shadows of human reason, and according to the testimonies of the promises, and according to these examples, it must be believed that he is truly present with us, hears, and helps those calling upon him.

I have added these things briefly concerning the interpreter of the prophecy, whom he here calls Palmoni, that is, a certain wonderful one just as elsewhere Christ is called wonderful counselor.

It is also to be considered that the Son of God is present when mention is made of the greatest calamity, so that He may signify that He Himself will be on watch in that very calamity, just as He says below, At that time Michael, who stands for the children of the people, etc. This is a great consolation, especially at this time, to know that Christ is on watch for us.

Latin:

CAPUT VIII.

Anno tertio regni Balsasar regis, visio apparuit mihi, ego Daniel, post id quod videram in principio, Vidi in visione mea, cum essem in Susan castro quod est in Aelam civitate: vidi autem in visione esse me a super portam Ulai. Et levavi oculos meos, & vidi: & ecce, aries unus stabat ante paludem, habens cornua excelsa, & unum excelsius altero atque succrescens. Postea vidi arietem cornibus ventilantem contra occidentem, & contra aquilonem, et contra meridiem: & omnes bestiae non poterant resistere ei, neque liberari de manu eius: fecitque secundum voluntatem suam, & magnificatus est. Et ego intelligebam: ecce autem hircus caprarum veniebat ab occidente super faciem totius terrae, & non tange bat terram: porrò hircus habebat cornu insigne inter oculos suos. Et venit usque ad arietem illum cornutum, quem videram stantem b ante portam, & cucurrit ad eum in impetu fortitu dinis suae. Cumque appropinquasset propè arietem, efferatus est in eum, & percussit arietem, et comminuit duo cornua eius, & non poterat aries resistere ei: cumque eum misisset in terram, conculcavit, & nemo quibat liberare arietem de manu eius. Hircus autem caprarum magnus factus est nimis: cumque crevisset, fractum est cornu magnum & orta sunt quatuor cornua subter illud per quatuor ventos caeli. De uno autem ex eis egressum est cornu unum modicum: & factum est grande contra meridiem & contra orientem, & contra c fortitudinem. Et magnificatum est usque ad d fortitudinem caeli: & deiecit de fortitudine, & de stellis, & conculcavit eas. Et usque ad principem f fortitudinis magnificatum est: & ab eo tulit iuge sacrificium, & deiecit locum sanctificationis eius. g Robur autem datum est ei contra iuge sacrificium propter peccata: & prosternetur veritas in terra, & faciet, & prosperabitur. Et audivi unum de sanctis loquentem: et dixit unus sanctus alteri nescio cui loquenti: Usquequo visio, & iuge sacrificium, & peccatum desolationis quae facta est: & sanctuarium, & h fortitudo conculcabitu? Et dixit ei: Usque ad vesperam & mane, dies duo millia trecenti: & mundabitur sanctuarium. Factum est autem cum viderem ego Daniel visionem, & quaererem intelligentam: ecce, stetit in conspectu meo quasi species viri. Et audivi vocem viri inter Ulai: & clamavit, & ait: Gabriel, fac intelligere istam visionem. Et venit, & stetit iuxta ubi ego stabam:

cumque venisset, pavens corrui in faciem meam, & ait ad me: Intellige fili hominis, quoniam in tempore finis complebitur visio. Cumque loqueretur ad me, collapsus sum pronus in terram: & tetigit me, & statuit me in gradu meo, dixitque mihi: Ego ostendam tibi quae futura sunt in novissimo maledictionis: quoniam habet tempus finem suum. Aries quem vidisti habere cornua, rex Medorum est atque Persarum. Porrò hircus caprarum, rex Graecorum est, et cornu grande quod erat inter oculos eius, ipse est rex primus. Quòd autem fracto illo surrexerunt quatuor pro eo, quatuor reges de gente eius consurgent, sed non in fortitudine eius. Et post regnum eorum cum creverint iniquitates, consurget rex impudens facie, & intelligens propositiones: & roborabitur fortitudo eius, sed non in viribus suis: & supra quàm credi potest, universa vastabit, & prosperabitur, & faciet. Et interficiet robustos, & populum sanctorum secundum voluntatem suam, & dirigetur dolus in manu eius, & cor suum magnisicabit, & in copia rerum omnium occider plurimos: & contra principem principum consurget, & sine manu conteretur. Et visio vespere & mane, quae dicta est, vera est: tu ergo visionem signa, quia post multos dies erit. Et ego Daniel langui, & aegrotavi per dies: cumque surrexissem, faciebam opera regis, & stupebam ad visionem, et non erat qui interpretaretur.

Aliquoties dictum est, ad quid prosit tenere praedictiones de serie Monarchiarum, & omnium temporam usque ad extremum iudicium. Est enim ecclesiae hac doctrina & consolatione opus, ne inter tot afflictiones & scandala desperet.

Est etiam admonitione opus, ut caussas cogitemus gitemus afflictionum. Hîc disertè in cap. 8. inquit, propter peccata populi permitti ut tantam crudelitatem Antiochus exerceat. Sicut Paulus inquit,2. Thes. 2.Quia veritatem non dilexerunt, mittet eis deus efficacem deceptionem, ut credant mendacio. Hae atroces comminationes exuscitent nos, ut simus diligentiores in conservanda puritate doctrinae, & in vita, ne deus sinat exoriri maiores tenebras.

Porrò cum proximo capite Daniel omnes Monarchias & postremum regnum, belligerans cum vera ecclesia, descripserit, nunc redit ad duas Monarchias clarius describendas, ad Persicam & Graecam, nam hae erant proximae. Et proderat piis praescire horribilem dissipationem, quam Antiochus Epiphanes facturus erat, ut haberent consolationem, nec deficerent, néve putarent fore, ut omnino deleretur populus dei.

Est autem perspicuum vaticinium, & à filio dei declaratum angelo interroganti. Summam breviter recitabo. Daniel sub Balthasaro abfuit ab aula, nec fuit in urbe Babylone, sed in Persia, quam Hebraei vocant Elam, in arce Susis, quae à rosis appellationem habet, ad amnem Ulai, quem Strabo & Plinius Euleum vocant. Cum igitur iam instaret finis primae Monarchiae, vidit Daniel Persas imperio potituros esse, ideo haec ipsa visio ei in Persia exhibetur.

Videt arietem in Persia stantem, id est, ducem Cyrum, qui utrunque exercitum, Medicum & Persicum duxit, ideo duo cornua adduntur arieti, quae significant regnum Medorum & Persarum. Ac duravit Monarchia Persica circiter ducentos annos. Postea Alexander Asiam occupavit, qui hîc significatur per hircum. Quatuor cornua significant successores Alexandri, qui foederibus variè coniuncti summam potentiam habuerunt. Haec est summa historiae, quae hîc recitatur, usque ad Antiochum Epiphanem.

Non dubium est autem in picturis contineri certas significationes, ut supra de par dali breviter dixi, quae & sagax est & animosa & celeritate insigui praedita, audet occurrere armato venatori & celeritate exeunt tola. Nec vi capitur, sed opprimitur ebria, quia enim pardalides sunt avidae vini, venatoriis loco vicino eius bestiae latebris vinum fundit, postea longiore intervallo, collocat cymbia plena vini.

Statim odor elicit ex antris pardalides, quae cum ad cymbia ventum est, hausto vino accensae discurrunt, ludunt unà, & coeunt. Deinde ebriae & coitu defatigatae sopiuntur, ibi sopitas opprimit venator. Huius rei luculenta descriptio est apud Oppianum libro quarto κυνηγετικῶν, ubi & hi versus leguntur.

λάπτουσι διώνυσον, ἐπʼ ἀλλήλοισι δὲ πᾶσαι σκιρτῶσι μεὲν πρῶτα χοροιτυπέουσιν ὅμοιαι..

Quae potest autem cogitari imago illustrior Regum Macedonicorum plurimorum sagacitate, hoc est, solertia & consilio? Vix ulli duces antecelluerunt Philippo & Alexandro. Deinde quod decreverant re cogitata, & occasionibus prospectis, celerrime suscipiebant, quae virtus in ducibus plurimum pollet.

Erant antem & in dimicando acerrimi, & invicti. Postrimo verò voluptatibus periere, alter Venere, alter temulentia. Tales & postea fuerunt Demetrius, & posterior Philippus.

Nec raro accidit, ut viri praestantes, cum in rebus gerendis acerrimi fuissent, & nec periculis ullis nec laboribus succubuissent, postea tamen voluptatibus fracti, calamitosos & tragicos exitus habuerint.

Possem recentia exempla magnorum ducum recitare. In Francisco Sphortia, quanta primum virtus fuit, quanta magnitudo animi? Senex vero, quasi Circes venenis natura eius mutata esset, priora decora libidinibus deformavit atque obruit.

Sed omitto exempla, quae lector admonitus consideret ipse in communi vita. Nam & Daniel has picturas proposuit, ut magna ingenia admoneat, ne sibi indulgeant, sed impetus & cupiditates moderentur, ne poenas sibi accersant, ut illi ipsi quos recensui fecerunt, Philippus, Alexander, Demetrius, & alii innumerabiles.

In hoc loco alia est pictura Graecae gentis. Capras vocat Daniel, ut enim hae eximiam habent in scandendo solertiam, & saltationibus assiduis gaudent, ita Graecorum ingeniorum mira fuit in experiundis novis rebus, artibus & consiliis curiositas. Taxari etiam exist imo ludos scenicos, quibus ea gens praecipue dedita fuit, qui quidem multa magna flagitia pepererunt & confirmaverunt.

Redeo autem ad narrationem vaticinii. Longus catalogus successorum Alexandri infra ab ipso. Daniele recensebitur. Nunc caeteris omissis de Antiocho Ephiphane dicit, qui fuit ortus à Seleuco, ut infra exponam, ac regnum Syriae tenuit. Coepit autem regnare anno post Alexandri mortem 137. ut testatur primus liber Machabaeorum, hoc est, secundo anno Olympiadis centesimae quadragesimae nonae.

Usitatum est autem, ut omnium aetatum historiae ostendunt, reges attemperare atque infiectere religiones ad suam utilitatem. Itaque cum Epiphanes sciret Iudaeos propter religionem non facile pati externa imperia, & videret tamen se nactum esse occasionem utriusque rei, potiundae Iudaeae, & mutandae religionis, homo atheos, & qui omnes religiones pariter fabulosas esse ducebat, & immani petulantia ruebat in libidines, & in omnia flagitia, quò caeco impetu rapiebatur, in utrunque incubuit omnibus viribus, ut & Iudaeam in servitutem redigeret, & veterem de deo doctrinam à prophetis acceptam, prorsus aboleret.

Obtulerant autem occasionem scelera pontificum. Cum teneret pontificatum vir pius Onias, frater eius Iason profectus ad Antiochum, in auspiciis regni pecuniam ei dedit, ut capto fratre, pontificatum sibi traderet. Simili fraude alius postea Menelaus eripuit pontificatum Iasoni, & uterque invexit Graecos ludos in urbem, contemnebant & negligebant studium divinae legis, imitabantur ethnicum splendorem, delitias, mores ethnicos, &c.

Menelao postea interfecto, successit Alcimus pontifex, qui etiam fuit hostis Machabaeorum. Haec scelera pontificum fuerunt initia calamitatum eius temporis.

Describuntur autem duae expeditiones Epiphanis, quibus venit Hierosolymam. Bis enim in Aegyptum profectus, in reditu venit Hierosolymam. Petivit autem Aegyptum iactitans se tutorem esse cognati, & vicini regis adolescentis, ut praetextu tutelae Aegyptum occuparet. Et in prima expeditione aliquot urbibus praesidia imposuit, adhuc simulans amicitiam cum cognato adolescente. Rediens autem, accersitus est Hierosolymam à Iasone, ubi spoliato templo, & facta magna caede, rursus discessit, nondum mutans Iudaeorum rempub. Haec acciderunt cum iam sexto anno regnaret Epiphanes, videlicet post Alexandri mortem 143. anno.

POST biennium rediit Antiochus in Aegyptum. Nam Philometor expulerat praesidia Antiochi, & petiverat auxilium à Romanis. Venit igitur Antiochus bellum illaturus cognato. Sed Romanus legatus Popilius vetuit bellum inferri Aegypto, et commemoravit senatum defensurum esse Aegyptum. Cumque Antiochus ambigue responderet, Popilius circum ducto gyro circum Antiochum, inquit, se postulare, ut disertè respondeat, an ex Aegypto discessurus sit, & ab ducturas exercitum, aut non, priusquam ex hoc circulo egrederetur. Ibi perterritus Antiochus, promittit pacem.

Rediens igitur iam à Menelao vocatur Hie+rosolymam, ibi celebrat Bachanalia, adiutore Menelao, prophanat templum, collocato ibi idolo Iovis Olympii, ubique ante domos extruuntur arae, & imponuntur idola gentilium more, prohibet legem & circumcisionem. Et addidit Menelao pontifici cohortem, quae tueatur regia edicta. Cremantur sacri libri, multi pii interficiuntur, maxima pars populi defecit ad ethnicos. Haec acciderunt anno 145. post mortem Alexandri.

Hîc deus excitat senem Mathathiam ex tribu sacerdotali, patrem Machabaeorum, qui collecto exercitu, coepit passim evertere idola, & dimicare cum cohorte Antiochi. Post patrem Iudas Machabaeus duxit exercitum. Hae parvae copiae privato consilio collectae, recuperarunt templum, & deleverunt Antiochi exercitus multis praeliis. Ideo infra dicit, Sublevabuntur auxilio parvulo. Estque templum recuperatum 3. anno postquam Antiochus ibi collocaveret idolum & praesidia, videlicet anno post mortem Alexandri 148. Reliqua historia petatur è libris Machabaeorum. Nam hanc partem propter numeros hîc positos recitavi, & ut vaticisii verba diligentius expendantur.

DANIEL tempus ponit hîc, dies mille trecentos, hoc est, sex annos, tres menses & viginti dies. Id tempus inchoari iudico, à secunda expeditione Epiphanis, hoc est, ab anno 145. usque ad annum 151. quo tota regio recuperata fuit, deleto Nicanoris exercitu. Et vides eventum rectissime respondere vaticinio.

Hanc consolationem aliqui pii intellexerunt, qua promitti senserunt, non fore hanc calamitatem diuturnam. Adeoque ad hanc inter pretationem quadrat numerus, ut menses etiam congruant. Statua enim in templo posita est anno 145. mense Casleu, qui nobis est November. Deinde victus est Nicanor anno 151. mense Adar, qui nobis est Februarius. Sunt igitur anni Iudaici integri sex, qui inchoabantur à vernò aequinòctiò. His adde menses proximos post statuam in templo collocatam, Decembrem, Ianuarium, Februarium, item aliquot dies, nam meise Novembri statua in templo collocata est Exactissime igitur convenit tempus Danielis cum hoc eventu.

Quòd autem hoc vaticinium propriè concionetur de historia Antiochi Epiphanis, testatur angelus Gabriel, interpres in textu, qui quidem colloquens cum filio dei, accepit ab eo interpretationem.

PORRO hic locus admoneat nos de praesentia Christi, quòd videlicet filius dei semper affuerit patribus, quodque verè nunc quoque ecclesiae adsit, exaudiat & gubernet invocantes ipsum, sicut dicit in Evangelio, Ecce, ego vobiscum sum, &c.

Item, ubicunque duo aut tres congregati sunt in nomine meo, ibi sum in medio eorum.

Item, Ascendit, ut det dona hominibus, &c.Gene. 48.inquit Iacob de Christo, Angelus qui eripuit me ex omni malo, benedicat pueris, &c.

EtIoan. 1.dicitur, Omnia per ipsum facta sunt, &c.

Et hîc interest colloquio angelorum apud Danielem.

Et Paulus inquit, Omnes bibebant de spirituali petra eos comitante, petra autem erat Christus.

Sic nos statuere debemus adesse Christun, exaudire, ivuare, & gubernare nos. Idque vocit scriptura regnum Christi. Sed infirmitas himanae mentis, non potest sic intelligere regni Christi, cogitat de eo, tanquam de absente, non agente aliquid nobiscum. Sed his tenebris humanae rationis repugnandum est, & iuxta testimonia promissionum, & iuxta haec exempla credendum, quòd verè adsit nobis, exaudiat, & ivuet invocantes ipsum.

Haec breviter adieci de interprete vaticinii, quem hîc vocat Palmoni, id est, admirabilem quendam, sicut alibi Christus vocatur admirabilis consiliarius.

Est & illud considerandum, quòd adest silius dei, cum fit mentio summae calamitatis, ut significet se in illa ipsa calamitate futurum esse in excubiis, sicut infra inquit, In illo tempore Michael, qui stat pro filiis populi, &c. Haec est magna concolatio praesertim hoc tempore, scire, quòd Christus sit in excubiis pro nobis.

## 8:1 On the Accommodation to the Kingdom of the Antichrist
ON THE ACCOMMODATION to the kingdom of the Antichrist.

Although the prophecy concerning Antiochus and the Jewish people is preached first, yet at the same time the prophet signifies that a similar calamity would come to the church before the end of the world, after Christ had suffered and the Gospel had been scattered among the nations. That Daniel at the same time depicted the image of an impious kingdom, warring in the church against Christ, is testified by Paul, who expressly accommodates the words spoken about Antiochus to the Antichrist,2. Thes. 2.And since we have Paul as a witness of this accommodation, let us follow his authority, omitting the cavils of others who do not wish these things to be accommodated to the Antichrist.

Then let us consider, as it profited the pious among the Jews to be forewarned, lest they should assent to the kingdom of Antioch, so let us know that we too are warned, lest we assent to the kingdom of Antichrist, or the impious kingdom ruling in the church.

By the name of Antichrist are comprehended both kingdoms, the Mahometan and the Roman pontificate, and Scripture preaches about both, and condemns both. As appears in Revelation 13, where it prophesies about both, when it says that Gog and Magog, the beast and the false prophet, are to perish. Here it embraces the Turkish kingdom, and the other kingdom, in which the false prophet, that is, the Roman pontiff, rules.

Since, however, there is some difference between these two kingdoms, the Mahometan or Turkish one was acquired by arms, nor does it hold the title of the church of Christ, therefore I spoke of it above, where empires acquired by arms were described. But the pontifical kingdom was established by fraud and superstition, and it retains the title of the church of Christ.

Since therefore Paul adapts the image of Antiochus to the kingdom that rules in the church, that is, which retains the title of the church, I myself will follow Paul, and I will adapt this prophecy concerning Antiochus to the pontifical kingdom, by which name not only the Roman pontiff I understand, but all bishops everywhere in the world defending his idolatry, whether they are otherwise joined to him, or are not joined.

And below two marks are attributed to Antiochus, which especially agree with the pontifical kingdom, namely, that he places an idol in the temple, and worships with gold and silver. Also, that he does not treat women with honor.

These are two properties of the papacy, Idols are manifest in the impious mass, the worship of saints and statues, which are displayed in gold and silver, so that they may be worshipped there. That is done nowhere, except in the pontifical kingdom. Also, in no kingdom was legitimate marriage ever prohibited, except in the pontifical. Therefore it agrees with that which Daniel says, that women would not be in honor. Furthermore, where there is no honor for marriage, wandering lusts and manifold filthiness follow. These most openly agree with the pontifical kingdom. Wherefore there is no doubt that Daniel and Paul are preaching about the pontifical kingdom. Since this is so, let us know that we are warned, lest we assent to the impiety of the pontiffs, or help and establish it.

NOW I return to the description of Antiochus in the text, in which there are many particles, which contain most sorrowful meanings, which the attentive reader may weigh for himself, looking into these our times.

Truth, he says, shall be cast down to the ground. We see now the Bishops and their satellites defending most manifest outrages against conscience, such as the prodigious abuses of the masses, the worship of saints, celibacy, and lusts. It is evident, therefore, that they hold the truth in mockery, and truly trample it underfoot, etc.

Concerning Antiochus the text says thus, A king of shameless face shall arise, and understanding propositions, that is, bold, a contemner of God, like Mezentius, and yet crafty. He shall grow, not by his own strength, but by the treachery of the Jewish high priests, and the fickleness of the people. And beyond what can be believed, he shall lay waste all things, that is, he shall abolish true worships, he shall introduce idolatry and propagate it widely, he shall lead the people to the rites and pleasures of the heathens, he shall have great successes, he shall kill the pious, and he shall entice many to himself with bribes, and he shall keep them ensnared with great advantages and allurements of wealth, dignities, and pleasures.

But at length he shall be broken without hand, because Antiochus was not killed by the Jews, but in the preparation of a new war, he died at home from a fall, when he already saw that many nations had defected from him, which, because of his cruelty and the baseness of his morals, hated and despised him; for he used to dance on the stage among actors, he would revel through all the brothels, he would scatter money at the crossroads, so that by many he was called not ἐπιφανῆς, but ἐπιμανῆς .

Let this image be compared to the pontiffs; these, thinking about the kingdom, see that kings and the people are held most of all by two things, namely superstition and utility. Astutely, therefore, they devised cults, which the princes and the people greatly admire. Minds are moved by those prodigious praises of the mass, because it merits every kind of good things, victories, gains, prosperous outcomes in all business. And because an impious mind cannot expect help from God, more gods are sought, and more placable ones; therefore the cult of the saints is most pleasing to men.

Afterwards, temples began to be filled with statues of the saints, and a completely ethnic idolomania followed. At the same time, the doctrine concerning the justice of faith and true cults was also buried, and were received. superstitious traditions, vows, celibacy, distinctions of foods, of garments, etc. And in devising these they used both craftiness and audacity. For it is the height of audacity, having spurned the word of God, to institute new cults, against the command of God, or without a command. And it is craftiness to see by what arts the minds of men may be captured, Item, to see the snares for increasing power and gain. Little by little, therefore, riches grew in wondrous ways.

Wherefore now those addicted to the Roman pontiff are retained partly by superstition, partly by the magnitude of the benefits. To this pertain the words of Daniel, The abundance of things will destroy many, that is, the pontiffs will invite and retain many ensnared by riches, dignities, splendor, pleasures, as is manifest.

Nor was the pontifical dominion acquired or fortified by its own strength or arms, as other kingdoms, but partly by the superstition of kings and peoples, partly because impious men of their own accord seek and defend those huge advantages. Just as at this time the nobility fights most fiercely, that it may retain in its hands the colleges, for the sake of its own advantage. Kings defend the pomp of the pontiffs, that they may gratify the nobility. Item, that they themselves may have courts more frequent and more splendid. Therefore Daniel says, It shall grow not by its own power.

And indeed popes and kings trade mutual services, just as Menelaus had invited Antiochus, offering him plunder from the temple and the kingdom, and in turn Antiochus added a cohort to Menelaus. Thus now kings, princes, and nobles enjoy ecclesiastical wealth, while truly ecclesiastical duties are neglected. But in turn, kings add a cohort to the popes, that is, for their sake they exercise cruelty, even against conscience. As at this time it is evident that many kings and princes, even if they do not approve of the idols and tyrannies of the popes, nevertheless for the sake of the popes exercise horrible cruelty. And meanwhile they flatter themselves beautifully, with a vain excuse invented, that it is beneficial to defend the authority of the church.

But as for what Daniel says, Deceit shall prosper in his hand, this extends widely. First, it signifies false dogmas, commended to the world with great appearance, as I said above. Then it signifies other tricks, by which they have obligated kings to themselves, or diminished the power of kings, or drawn kingdoms to themselves. Now also with wonderful cunning they paint abuses anew, and solicit kings by wondrous arts to exercise cruelty. He embraces all these things, when he names deceit.

Lastly, because he says, he shall be broken without hand, he signifies that before the final judgment there will come a renewal of the Gospel, in which the authority of the pontiffs will be shaken without arms, that is, by teaching the pontifical errors will be refuted, with the light of the Gospel renewed, just as also below he says, The learned among the people shall teach many, and shall fall by the sword. And to the Thess. Then that wicked one shall be revealed, whom the Lord shall destroy with the spirit of his mouth, for he signifies that it will come to pass, that a certain refutation of the errors of Antichrist shall precede the final judgment, just as the dawn precedes the sun.

Furthermore, it appears quite clearly that this prophecy pertains to this our age, and to the doctrine divinely revealed at this time, just as Daniel and Paul signify, that there will be final struggles concerning doctrine. Wherefore let both sides contemplate themselves here, and not despise the admonition of the Holy Spirit.

The name of the church moves and detains many, from opposing the pontiffs. But the Holy Spirit in Daniel and Paul clearly testifies, that that kingdom, namely the pontifical one, which He defends εἰδωλομανίας, & kills the pious, that it is not the church, but a faction rejected by God, he urges all, that they should desert it, just as the angel cries out in the Apocalypse, Go out from Babylon.

On the other hand he consoles the pious, who rebuke errors, so that they may know that they have a command of God, that this diligence pleases God, & that they defend the true Church. Just as Daniel says clearly, The learned among the people shall teach many, and they shall fall, &c. And yet he promises that the true church will remain, even if many will be slaughtered. For thus he says, The people knowing their God shall prevail, &c.

Let us not therefore be deterred from the profession of true doctrine, nor let us be broken in spirit, even if the adversaries shout that we are seditious, & incite all kings to exercise cruelty. Nor yet will the whole pontifical kingdom fall before the last day, but some part will remain, as Paul says, He will abolish him by his illustrious coming. The Apocalypse signifies the same thing concerning the false prophet cast into the abyss.

Latin:

DE ACCOMMODATIONE ad regnum Antichristi.

Quanquam vaticinium de Antiocho & de populo Iudaico, primum concionatur, tamen simul propheta significat similem calamitatem ecclesiae venturam ante finem mundi, postquam Christus passus esset, & Evangelium inter gentes sparsum. Quòd autem Daniel simul depinxerit imaginem impii regni, in ecclesia belligerantis cum Christo, testatur Paulus, qui verba de Antiocho dicta, expressè accommodat ad Antichristum,2. Thes. 2.Cumque testem habeamus Paulum huius accommodationis, autoritatem eius sequamur, omissis aliorum cavillationibus, qui haec nolunt accommodari ad Antichristum.

Deinde cogitemus, ut piis profuit apud Iudaeus, esse praemonitos, ne assentirentur regno Antiochi, ita sciamus nos quoque commoneri, ne assentiamur regno Antichristi, seu regno impio dominanti in ecclesia.

Nomine Antichristi comprehenduntur ambo regna, Mahometicum & pontificatus Romanus, & de utroque concionatur scriptura, & utrumque damnat. Ut Apocalysis 13. apparet, ubi de utroque vaticinatur, cum ait, perituros esse Gog & Magog, bestiam & pseudoprophetam. Hîc complectitur regnum Turcicum, & alterum regnum, in quo dominatur pseudopropheta, id est, Romanus pontifex.

Cum autem aliquid intersit inter haec duo regna, Mahometicum seu Turcicum armis partum est, nec tenet titulum ecclesiae Christi, ideo de eo supra dixi, ubi imperia describebantur armis parta. Sed pontificium regnum fraude & superstitione constitutum est, & retinet titulum ecclesiae Christi.

Cum igitur Paulus accommodet imaginem Antiochi ad regnum, quod in ecclesia dominatur, hoc est, quod retinet titulum ecclesiae, sequar & ipse Paulum, & hoc vaticinium de Antiocho accommodabo ad pontificium regnum, quo nomine non tantum Romanum pontificem intelligo, sed omnes episcopos ubique terrarum eius idolatriam defendentes, sive ei alioqui sint coniuncti, sive non sint coniuncti.

Ac infrà notae duae tribuuntur Antiocho, quae praecipuè conveniunt ad pontificium regnum, videlicet, quòd idolum in templo collocet, & auro ac argento colat. Item, quòd non adficiat honore mulieres.

Haec sunt duo propria pontificatus, Idola manifesta sunt impiae missae, cultus sanctorum & statuae, quae in auro & argento ostenduntur, ut ibi colantur. Id nusquam fit, nisi in pontificio regno. Item, in nullo regno unquam prohibitum fuit legitimum coniugium, nisi in pontificio. Ergo convenit ad id, quod ait Daniel, non futuras in honore mulieres. Porrò ubi non est honos coniugio, sequuntur vagae libidines & multiplex turpitudo. Haec apertissimè conveniunt ad regnum pontificium. Quare nihil dubium est, Danielem & Paulum concionari de regno pontificio. Quod cum ita sit, admoneri nos sciamus, ne assentiamur impietati pontificum, néve eam adivuemus ac stabiliamus.

IAM redeo ad descriptionem Antiochi in textu, in quo sunt multae particulae, quae tristissimas significationes continent, quas attentus lector per sese expendat, intuens in haec nostra tempora.

Prosternetur, inquit, veritas in terra. Videmus iam Episcopos & eorum satellites manifestissima flagitia contra conscientiam defendere, ut, prodigiosos missarum abusus, cultus sanctorum, caelibatum & libidines. Constat igitur eos veritatem ludibrio habere, & verè conculcare pedibus, &c.

De Antiocho sic inquit textus, Surget rex impudens facie, & intelligens propositiones, id est, audax, contemptor dei, ut Mezentius, & tamen astutus. Is crescet, non suis viribus, sed pontificum Iudaicorum perfidia, & populi levitate. Et suprà quàm credi potest, omnia vastabit, id est, abolebit veros cultus, invehet idolatriam & latè propagabit, traducet populum ad ritus & voluptates ethnicorum, habebit magnos successus, interficiet pios, & deliniet sibi multos largitionibus, & retinebit inescatos magnis commodis & illecebris opum, dignitatum, ac voluptatum.

Tandem verò sine manu conteretur, quia Antiochus non est interfectus à Iudaeis, sed in apparatu novi belli, domi ex lapsu mortuus est, cum iam multas nationes à se defecisse videret, quae eum propter crudelitatem, & morumtur pitudinem oderant & contemnebant, saltabat enim in scena inter histriones, bacchabatur per omnes ganeas, spargebat pecuniam in compitis, ut à multis nonἐπιφανῆς,sedἐπιμανῆςdiceretur.

Haec imago conferatur ad pontifices, hi de regno cogitantes, vident reges & populum duabus rebus maximè teneri, videlicet superstitione & utilitatibus. Astutè ergo excogitaverunt cultus, quos valde admirantur principes & populus. Moventur animi illis prodigio sis laudibus missae, quòd mereatur omnis generis bona, victorias, lucra, prosperos eventus in omnibus negotiis. Et quia animus impius non potest à deo expectare auxilium, quaeruntur plures dii, & placabiliores, ideo gratissimus est hominibus cultus sanctorum.

Postea coeperunt templa compleri statuis sanctorum, & secuta est prorsus ethnica idolomania. Simul etiam obruta est doctrina de iustitia fidei, & veris cultibus, receptae sunt superstitiosae traditiones, vota, caelibatus, discrimina ciborum, vestium, &c. Et in his excogitandis simul & asturia & audacia usi sunt. Est enim summa audacia, spreto verbo dei, novos cultus instituere, contra mandatum dei, vel sine mandato. Et calliditas est videre, quibus artibus capiantur hominum animi, Item, videre aucupia augendae potentiae & quaestus. Paulatim igitur opes creverunt miris modis.

Quare nunc addicti Romano pontifici, retinentur partim superstitione, partim magnitudine utilitatum. Huc pertinent verba Danielis, Copia rerum multos interficiet, id est. pontifices invitabunt & retinebunt multos inescatos opibus, dignitatibus, splendore, voluptatibus, sicut palàm est.

Nec propriis viribus sive armis, ut caetera regna, parta est, aut munita pontificia dominatio, sed partim superstitione regum & populorum, partim quia ultro homines impii expetunt & defendunt ista ingentia commoda. Sicut hoc tempore nobilitas acerrimè dimicat, ut retineat in manibus collegia, sui commodi caussa. Reges defendunt pompam pontificum, ut nobilitati gratificentur. Item, ut ipsi habeant aulas frequentiores & splendidiores. Ideo inquit Daniel, Crescet non suis viribus.

Et quidem tradunt mutuas operas pontifices & reges, sicut Menelaus invitârat Antiochum offerens ei praedam ex templo & regnum, & vicissim Antiochus addidit cohortem Menelao. Ita nunc reges, principes et nobiles fruuntur opibus ecclesiasticis, neglectis officiis vere ecclesiasticis. Vicissim autem reges addunt pontificibus cohortem, hoc est, in eorum gratiam exercent saeviciam, etiam contra conscientiam. Ut hoc tempore constat multos reges & principes, et si non probant pontificum idola & tyrannides, tamen in pontificum gratiam exercere horribilem crudelitatem. Et Interea sibi pulchre blandiuntur, conficta inani excusatione, quòd prosit tueri autoritatem ecclesiae.

Quòd verò Daniel inquit, Dolus prospera bitur in manu eius, hoc latè patet. Primum significat falsa dogmata, magna specie commendata mundo, ut suprà dixi. Deinde significat alias technas, quibus reges sibi obligaverunt, dut regum potentiam imminuerunt, aut regna ad se traxerunt. Nunc quoque mira calliditate de novo pingunt abusus, & sollicitant reges miris artibus ad exercendam saeviciam. Haec omnia complectitur, cum nominat dolum.

Postremo, quòd inquit, sine manu conteretur, significat ante extremum iudicium venturam renovationem Evangelii, in qua autorit aspontificum labefactabitur sine armis, hoc est, docendo reprehendentur errores pontificii, renovata luce Evangelii, sicut & infrà inquit, Docti in populo docebunt multos, & ruent in gladio. Et ad Thes. Tunc revelabitur ille iniquus, quem destruet dominus spiritu oris sui, significat enim futurum, ut quaedam reprehensio errorum Antichristi praecedat extremum iudicium, sicut aurora solem praecedit.

Porrò satis perspicuè apparet, hoc vaticinium pertinere ad hanc nostram aetatem, & doctrinam hoc tempore divinitus patefactam, sicut Daniel & Paulus significant, postremo futura certamina de doctrina. Quare hîc utraque pars se contempletur, nec contemnat admonitionem spiritus sancti.

Nomen ecclesiae multos movet ac detinet, quo minus adversentur pontificibus. Sed spiritus sanctus apud Danielem & Paulum clarè testatur, id regnum, videlicet pontificium, quod defenditεἰδωλομανίας,& interficit pios, non esse ecclesiam, sed factionem à deo reiectam, hortatur omnes, ut eam deserant, sicut in Apocalypsi clamat angelus, Exite ex Babylone.

Econtra consolatur pios, qui reprehendunt errores, ut sciant se habere mandatum dei, deo placere hanc sedulitatem, & defendere se veram Ecclesiam. Sicut disertè inquit Daniel, Docti in populo docebunt multos, et ruent, &c. Et tamen pollicetur mansuram esse veram ecclesiam, & si multi trucidabuntur. Sic enim inquit, Populus sciens deum suum vincet, &c.

Non igitur deterreamur à professione verae doctrinae, nec frangamur animis, etiamsi adversarii vociferantur, nos seditiosos esse, & concitant omnes reges ad exercendam crudelitatem. Neque tamen ruet totum regnum pontificium ante novissimum diem, sed pars aliqua manebit, ut Paulus inquit, Abolebit eum illustri adventu suo. Idem significat Apocalypsis de pseudopropheta abiecto invoragine.

## 9:1 Argument
CHAPTER IX.

In the first year of Darius the son of Ahasuerus of the seed of the Medes, who reigned over the kingdom of the Chaldeans: In the one year of his reign, I Daniel understood by books the number of the years, concerning which the word of the Lord was made to Jeremiah the prophet, that the desolation of Jerusalem should be accomplished in seventy years. And I set my face to the Lord my God to pray and make supplication in fastings, and sackcloth and ashes. And I prayed to the Lord my God, and I made my confession, and said, I beseech thee, O Lord God, great and terrible, keeping the covenant and mercy to them that love thee, and to them that keep thy commandments. We have sinned, we have committed iniquity, we have done wickedly, and have revolted, and have declined from thy commandments and from thy judgments. We have not hearkened to thy servants prophets, who spoke in your name to our kings, our princes, our fathers, and to all the people of the land. To you, O Lord, is justice: but to us confusion of face, as it is this day to the man of Judah, and to the inhabitants of Jerusalem and to all Israel, to those who are near, and to those who are far off in all the lands, to which you have cast them out because of their iniquities, in which they have sinned against you. O Lord, to us is confusion of face, to our kings, to our princes, and to our fathers who have sinned: But to you, the Lord our God, are mercy and propitiation, because we have departed from you, and have not heard the voice of the Lord our God, to walk in his law which he set before us by his servants the prophets. And all Israel have transgressed your law, and have turned away so as not to hear your voice: and the curse and the detestation which is written in the book of Moses has distilled upon us. servants of God, because we have sinned against Him. And He confirmed His words which He spoke against us and against our princes who judged us, that He might bring upon us a great evil, such as never was under all the heaven, according to what has been done in Jerusalem. As it is written in the law of Moses, all this evil has come upon us: and we have not entreated thy face, O Lord our God, that we might turn from our iniquities, and think on thy truth. And the Lord watched over the malice, and brought it upon us: the Lord our God is just in all his works which he has done: for we have not heard his voice. And now, O Lord our God, who hast brought forth thy people out of the land of Egypt with a strong hand, and hast made thee a name as at this day, we have sinned, we have committed iniquity. O Lord, according to all thy justice, let be turned away I beseech thee, let thy anger and thy fury be turned away from thy city Jerusalem, and from thy holy mountain. For because of our sins, and the iniquities of our fathers, Jerusalem and thy people are a reproach to all that are round about us. Now therefore, O our God, hear the prayer of thy servant, and his supplications, and show thy face upon thy sanctuary which is desolate, for thy own sake. Incline, O my God, thy ear, and hear: open thy eyes, and see our desolation, and the city upon which thy name is called: for it is not for our justifications that we present our prayers before thy face, but for the multitude of thy tender mercies. Hear, O Lord, be appeased, O Lord: hearken and do: delay not for thy own sake, O my God: because thy name is invoked upon thy city and upon thy people.

And while I was yet speaking and praying, and confessing my sins and the sins of my people Israel, that I might prostrate my prayers in the sight of my God, for the holy mountain of my God: As I was yet speaking in my prayer, behold the man Gabriel, whom I had seen in the vision at the beginning, flying swiftly, touched me at the time of the evening sacrifice. And he instructed me, and spoke to me, and said: Daniel, now I have come forth to teach thee, and that thou mightest understand. From the beginning of thy prayers the word came forth: but I have come to show thee, because thou art a man of desires: do thou therefore mark the word, and understand the vision. Seventy weeks are shortened upon thy people and upon thy holy city, that transgression may be finished, and sin may receive an end, and iniquity may be blotted out, and everlasting justice may be brought, and the vision may be fulfilled and prophecy: and the holy of holies may be anointed. Know therefore and attend: from the going forth of the word, that Jerusalem may be built again, unto Christ the leader, there shall be seven weeks and sixty-two weeks: and the street shall be built again, and the walls in straitness of times. And after sixty-two weeks Christ shall be slain, * and there shall not be for him (a people). And the people with the leader that shall come shall destroy the city and the sanctuary, and the end thereof shall be waste, and after the end of the war the appointed desolation. But he shall confirm the covenant with many for one week: and in the half of the week the victim and the sacrifice shall fail, and there shall be * in the temple the abomination of desolation: and even unto the consummation and the end the desolation shall persevere.

Although the individual chapters contain very rich doctrine, yet this chapter far excels the others. For it hands down an illustrious testimony concerning the time of the coming of Christ, concerning the death of Christ, concerning the justice of the new testament, concerning the destruction of the Jewish polity.
