Et nominatim dicit, Exaudi deus orationem servi tui, & aspice templum tuum desertum, propter dominum, id est, exaudi, aspice propter dominum. Nam apud Hebraeos haec particula sic inseritur, ut Daniel loquens ad deum secunda persona, Exaudi deus, aspice, &c. postea adiiciat tertiam personam in fine, Propter dominum. Ideoque Iudaei interpretati sunt, propter Abraham.

Sed haec interpretatio aliena & repudianda est. Sciebat enim Daniel, non Abraham esse dominum populi, sed promissum liberatorem, sicut in Psalmo dicitur, Dixit dominus domino meo, sede à dextris meis, &c.

Nec dubium est quin hîc Daniel Christum intellexerit, inquiens, Propter dominum, id est, propter Christum. Ac similes conciones de contritione & fide passim extant in prophetis, utIerem. cap. 14.Si iniquitates nostrae responderint nobis, Domine, fac propter nomen tuum, tibi peccavimus, Salvator noster in tempore tribulationis non relinquens nos, &c.

Discamus autem & nos, & exerceamus utramque doctrinam, agnoscamus peccata, & petamus veniam, excitent nos publicae & privatae calamitates, quia haec exempla in prophetis proposita sunt, ut intelligamus & consideremus ecclesiae calamitates, & petamus liberationem. Nam praesertim postremis temporibus non est futura Ecclesiae minor calamitas, quàm fuit illud exilium Babylonicum istius populi. Et haec Danielis precatio recitanda est tanquam communis precatio pro ecclesia his temporib.

Hîc admonendi sunt lectores, ut observent hanc concionem Danielis, confirmandae sententiae caussa, quae traditur à Paulo de iustitia fidei. Hîc enim proponitur certum & perspievum testimonium doctrinae Pauli. Nam astuti quidam conantur inflectere Pauli sententiam ad iudicium humanum de iustitia operum, & clamitant, horridius eam à nobis accipi. Negant illas propositiones, omnes homines sub peccato esse, nostra opera non mereri reconciliationem, naturam hominis vitiosam esse, non posse satisfacere legi, item gratis per misericordiam homines reputari iustos propter Christum, non propter opera.

Haec paradoxa, inquiunt aliena esse à communi sensu. Ideo quaerunt cavillationes ad eludenda Pauli dicta, & ita mitigant ea. Fatentur ceremoniis detrahi iustitiam, sed aiunt propter moralia opera homines iustos esse. Sicut Philosophi dicunt, hominem iustum esse propter civiles mores. Quia enim habent securos & otiosos animos, admirantur illam civilem iustitiam, & putant eam satisfacere legi dei, putant se sine peccato esse. Ideo consolationes alias nec quaerunt nec intelligunt.

Sed in veris pavoribus experimur opus esse doctrina de gratuita misericordia. Ac nisi hac fide sustentemur, animi ruunt in desperationes & blasphemias, non possunt deum invocare, non possunt expectare aut petere auxilium.

Quare pii non sinant se decipi ab illis securis, qui obruunt doctrinam Pauli de fide. Fateamur nos esse auditores Pauli, ab ipso discamus, non arrogemus nobis censuram contra eius dicta.

Deinde illud consideremus, Cum Paulus concionetur de iustitia coram deo, necesse est eius sententiam congruere cum prophetis ac patribus. Atque ita est profecto, Ordine omnes prophetae idem docent. Testantur omnes homines sub peccato esse, accusant humanam iustitiam. Et rursus proponunt gratuitam misericordiam revelatam in promissionibus à deo propter promissum liberatorem. Et hanc misericordiam iubent fide accipi, ita affirmant nobis ignosci.

Haec est una & consentiens vox omnium prophetarum. Ideo Petrus inquit, Huic omnes prophetae testimonium perhibent, remissionem peccatorum accipere per nomen eius, omnes qui credunt in eum. Et haec est liberatio, promissa per Messiam, liberari à peccato, & restitui integram iustitiam, & vitam aeternam. At Iudaei somniant se esse iustos lege, & expectant liberationem non à peccato, & à morte, sed ab exilio & inopia. Hunc errorem prophetae & apostoli taxabant.

Quod autem congruat Paulus hîc cum Daniele, manifestum est. Daniel inquit, Tibi domine iustitia, nobis autem confusio faciei nostrae. Agnoscit totam ecclesiam habere peccata. Et nominatim dicit, Non in iustitia nostra precamur, &c. Item, ne quis fingat Synecdochen, loqui eum de multis, non de omnibus (Sicut Origenes corrumpit dictum Danielis) nominatim dicit, Cum confiterer peccata mea, et peccata populi. Sed ita est, peccati onus tantum sentiunt pii, alii securi contemnunt.

Ut igitur Paulus tollit meritum, & contendit omnes sub peccato esse, item, inculcat particulam gratis, Ita hîc docet Daniel, inquiens, Non in iustitia nostra, &c. Deinde, ut Paulus inquit, remitti peccata per gratiam, ita hic clamitat (confugimus ad misericordiam quae magna est) & nominat mediatorem, inquiens, Propter dominum. Porrò quoties fit mentio misericordiae, correlativè intelligenda est fides, qua statuimus nobis contingere misericordiam. Omnino igitur cum Paulo congruit Daniel.

Ita prodest in caeteris prophetis consentientes sententias considerare, ut in Psalmis. Quòd omnes sunt rei, testantur haec dicta, Non iustificabitur in conspectu tuo omnis vivens. Item, Delicta quis intelligit. Item, Si iniquitates observaveris domine, domine quis sustinebit?

Rursus de misericordia et remissione gratuita Dixi confitebor adversum me iniquitatem meam. Sic Esaias uno versiculo utramque partem complexus, accusat omnes, & proponit misericordiam, Omnes nos quasi oves erravimus unusquisque in suam viam, & posuit dominus in eo iniquitates omnium nostrum.

Sic agnoscebant prophetae venturum liberatorem, longè maius beneficium allaturum esse quàm voluptates & opes, videlicet destructurum esse opera diaboli, id est peccatum, quo natura humana oppressa est, tyrannidem diaboli, & mortem aeternam.

Quare legentes Paulum, intueamur animo omnes patrum ac prophetarum conciones, inde usque ab initio, & de peccati magnitudine cogitemus, & sciamus non esse levia beneficia, quae filius dei attulit. Haec breviter adieci, ut confirmentur studiosi adversus Sophisticam, qua nonnulli cavillantur & corrumpunt Pauli dicta de iustitia fidei.

VATICINIUM DE 70. hebdomadibus.

SEQUITUR vaticinium de septuaginta hebdomadibus traditum Danieli, quod est perspicuum testimonium contra Iudaeos, de tempore adventus Christi, & de fine politiae Iudaicae. Ac multa breviter complexus est Daniel, ut fit in vaticiniis. Cum loquitur de Christi adventu, simul monet de beneficiis, quae allaturus est. Interpretatur promissiones, videlicet, futurum ut aboleatur peccatum, & inchoetur perpetua iustitia, & nova ac aeterna vita. Addit etiam Christum moriturum esse, et tamen multis ex populo daturum sua beneficia, et postea finem fore huius politiae Iudaicae. Haec est summa vaticinii, in qua satis apparet proponi testimonia de multis articulis contra Iudaeos.

Primum refutat hic locus errorem de lege retinenda, & de regno politico Christi. Si erit perpetua iustitia, item, si Christus occidetur, sequitur legem Mosaicam non retinendam esse, nec fore mundanum regnum.

SECUNDO tradit testimonium de passione Christi.

TERTIO, cum politia iam defierit, ita ut nullos habeat duces, nullos prophetas, nulla tribuum discrimina, constat impletum esse dictum Iacob, Non auferetur sceptrum de Iuda, donec venerit Salvator. Necesse est igitur venisse Salvatorem.

Estque diligenter consideranda diuturnitas temporis post excidium Hierosolymorum, & conferenda cum duratione legis ante excidium. Nec est consentaneum, velle deum iacere deletam politiam diutius, quàm florere, si eam iam approbaret. Sunt autem anni à data lege usque ad excidium Hierosolymorum, capta urbe à Tito, mille quingenti sexagintaquatuor. Nunc e contra iacet politia deleta annis 1467. usque ad hunc annum, quo iam numeramus à nato Christo 1542. Cum igitur tam diu iaceat politia Iudaica, necesse est vaticinium Iacob impletum esse, ac Christum venisse.

Ac voluit deus hanc politiam deleri, tum ut esset exemplum irae adversus impios, tum, quia non potuisset eximi persuasio hominibus de iustitia legis, si durasset ea politia. Voluit autem deus ostendere, ritus illos non esse iustitiani. Item, iustitiam legis non esse iustitiam, de qua concionantur promissiones. Item, voluit exaequare gentes Iudaeis, ut intelligeremus perspicuè gratuitam reconciliationem.

Porrò Danieli etiam numerus annorum revelatus est, usque ad Christi praedicationem & resurrectionem. Nam postea post missionem spiritus sancti, & inchoatam Evangelii praedicationem, populus Iudaicus definit esse populus dei. Ideo non loquitur de anno excidii, sed de prioribus usque ad Christi resurrectionem & circiter.

Nominat autem septuaginta hebdomadas, quae faciunt annos quadringentos & nonaginta. Sunt enim intelligendae annorum hebdomades. Nam in scripturis tantum sunt vel annorum vel dierum hebdomades. Manifestum est autem hîc dierum hebdomadas numerari non posse. Nam septuaginta hebdomades dierum, non faciunt nisi annum, quatuor menses, et dies 14. Eo tempore satis constat, nec aedificationem Hierosolymae fuisse absolutam, nec populum universum adhuc reductam. Necesse est igitur numerari hîc hebdomadas annorum, ut Levitici 25.fit mentio hebdomadum annorum, Sunt igitur 70. hebdomades annorum intelligendae, videlicet 490. anni.

SUBDUCTIO HEBDO-MADUM.

MANIFESTUM est, angelum velle tempus patefacere, ac praefinire post exilium Babylonicum usque ad Christum seu nascentem seu praedicantem, ut & ea aetas de adventu Messiae admoneretur, & nos confirmaremur, ac certò sciremus, deleta funditus Iudaica politia, iam adparuisse Messiam.

Ac Iudaeis quidem post Danielem facilis fuit observatio annorum, praesertim cum in eo populo sacerdotes tempora diligenter annotarent, et multi essent longaevi. Nehemias qui Danielem senem viderat adolescens, Alexandrum senex vidit. Postea Antiochi tempore multi senes interfecti sunt. Servavit tamen deus posteritatem aliquam. Simeon qui Christum infantem gestavit in sinu, vidit adolescens senes qui Machabaeum viderant. Tales viri tempore quo Christus natus est, intellexerunt annos hîc praefinitos exactè quadrare ad Christi adventum.

Esset autem et nobis facilior accommodatio, si anni regni Persici certius annotati essent. Nam retro à Christi nativitate usque ad Alexandrum series annorum mediocri diligentia animadversa & perscripta est. Extat enim in Prolemaeo tempus annotatum à morte Alexandri ad primum annum regni Augusti post Antonii mortem. Anni videlicet 294. Locus est in fine libri tertii,ἀπὸ δὲ τῆς ἀλεξάνδρου τελευτῆς μέχρι τῆς Αύγούστου βασιλείας 294.

Ergo si principium Augusti post interfectum Caesarem habere voles, detrahendi erunt anni 12. Eruntque à morte Alexandri ad interfectum Caesarem, 282. His adde annos 42. Augusti, eruntque ad natum Christum à morte Alexandri, iuxta hanc computationem, anni 324. vel circiter. Adde annos Christi usque ad baptismum eius, ita erunt anni 354. Adde à morte Alexandri retro collectos annòs, usque ad secundum Longimani iuxta Graecam supputationem annos 136. Ita erunt anni à secundo Longimani usque ad baptismum Christi 490. Tot anni sunt septuaginta hebdomadum.

Cur autem ordiamur hanc computationem à secundo Longimani anno, postea dicemus. Sed si cui non placet ordiri à Longimano, sed potius à Cyri Monarchia post captam Babylonem, convenient hebdomades Danielis ad tempus quo nascitur Christus, non quo baptizatur.

Ac ut intelligat lector hanc computationem, quam dixi, probabilem esse, annotatio temporum alia conferatur. Certum est Alexandrum coepisse regnare Olympiade centesima undecima. Ita magno consensu traditur, & consentaneum est propter magnitudinem rerum gestarum Alexandri, tempus diligenter annotatum esse.

Deinde scribit Iosephus, Olympiade 184. Herodem regem creatum esse. Erunt ergo iuxta hanc computationem inter Alexandrum & Herodem anni 292. Id tempus recte congruit ad annos quos suprà ex Ptolemaeo sumpsi. Nam hîc additur initium Alexandri. Porro ab Alexandro usque ad eversionem templi factam à rege Nabogdonosor, sunt anni 256. Si tamen, ut veteres scripserunt, templi destructio facta est Olympiade 47. Quod si rursus demas tempus exilii, videlicet annos septuaginta, erunt à 70. anno post eversionem templi, usque ad Herodem regem creatum 478. Adde autem annos ad Christum natum 30. Ita habebis hebdomadum annos 490. & paulo amplius. Sed bonis mentibus hoc sufficit, quòd vident ad Danielis vaticinium congruere tempus, quod est inter reditum populi ex Babylone, & Christum, etiamsi exactissimam supputationem non habent.

Quantulum autem in his supputationibus desideratur? Testatur angelus post 490. annos Messiam se populo huic ostensurum esse. Constat autem à liberatione populi ad Christum natum tot esse annos, nec multo plures, aut pauciores. Constat enim tempus ab Augusto ad Alexandrum. Constat & hoc, Monarchiam Persicam non plus ducentis annis durasse. Venit igitur Messias filius dei, & respondit eventus vaticinio Danielis.

Sed quoniam angelus disertè dicit, Christum multis confirmaturum esse testamentum hebdomade una & dimidia, & tunc cessaturas esse hostias. Si volumus hebdomadas continuas esse, numerandae erunt hebdomades ad Christi baptismum, quod fiet, si à secundo anno Longimani numerentur, quo vaticinia Zachariae & Haggaei edita sunt de populi reditu, qui fuerat antea impeditus, nunc sublatis impedimentis, reditus concessus est. Nam angelus iubet numerari ab exitu sermonis de reditu populi, & instauratione Hierosolymae.

Porrò haec verba alii aliter acceperunt. Quidam de regum edictis interpretantur, quorum prius fuit Cyri, quod eo absente, in bello Scythico impeditum est. Alterum post annos 42. à Longimano editum est, qui in Esdra vocatur & Artaxerxes, & Darius. Nam haec fuerunt communia regum nomina, ut Romae, Caesar, Augustus. Et Herodotus libro sexto inquit, Darii voce idem significari, quod significat vocabulum cohercentis. Xerxen ait esse bellatorem, Artaxerxen, magnum bellatorem.

Porrò eo tempore quo Longimanus decretum de concessione reditus renovavit, simul Haggaeo & Zachariae facta est revelatio de liberatione & aedificatione deinceps processura. Quare hinc à secundo Longimani anno inchoatur à quibusdam computatio, eo quòd tunc sermo exierit & à deo, & à Longimano, ne liberatio & aedificatio deinceps impedirentur. Et si hinc sumemus initium, erunt continuae hebdomades usque ad Christum praedicantem.

Sed si quis mavult à Cyri decreto ordiri, perveniet ad natum Christum.

Est autem utraque sententia seu computatio concinna, & quod ad summam negocii attinet, rem eandem confirmat utraque computatio, ostendit inter liberationem populi & Christum esse 490. annos. Hoc cum declaratum est, sciunt pii, non esse postea, deleta politia Iudaica, alium Messiam expectandum. Nam hoc praecipuè agitur in hoc vaticinio, ut extet testimonium, oportere, adhuc durante politia Mosaica, nasci et conspici Messiam. Sicut idem docet versiculus, Et tu Bethlehem parvula in millibus Iuda, Ex te egredietur dominator in Israel. Oportuit stante politia Mosaica in Bethlehem Messiam nasci.

Constat autem nunc polltiam totam Mosaicam iacere deletam, Ierusalem, Bethlehem, non tam oppida, quàm casas esse aut spelea latrunculorum Arabicorum, annos mille quadringentos sexagintaseptem. Necesse est igitur Messiam natum esse.

Quid contra haec dicere Iudaei possunt, si modo prophetarum scripta non iudicant fabulosa esse? Sed quia gens Iudaica amat suas tenebras, nobis ipsis legamus, & expendamus prophetarum dicta, ut fidem nostram accendamus & confirmemus. Interim & gratias agamus deo, quòd ecclesiam curat, quòd subinde doctrinam illustravit, quòd certis testimoniis subinde erudiit.

VENIO igitur ad computationem, sed initio recensendus est catalogus Regum Persicorum.

CATALOGUS REGUM Persicorum iuxta Graecos.

Cyrus 29

Cambyses 7. annos, menses 5

Darius filius Hystaspis 36

Xerxes 20

Artaxerxes Longimanus 40

Darius nothus 19

Artaxerxes Mnemon 40

Ochus 26

Arsames 3

Darius ultimus 6

Haec series nota est eruditis omnibus, qui Graecas historias legerunt. Et inscitia Iudaeorum recentium vituperanda est, qui fingunt non plures fuisse reges Persicos, quàm quatuor, & propter hanc inscitiam, amittunt ex serie annorum mundi centum annos.

Extat autem & alius catalogus in fragmento Metasthenis, in quo appellationes magis congruunt cum Esdra. Etsi autem apud quosdam levis est autoritas Metasthenis, tamen quia congruit cum Esdra & Philone, non aspernor, praesertim cum primi tres de quibus discrepant catalogi, eodem modo recenseantur & nuncupentur ab Esdra & Philone, de caeteris ambo catalogi consentiunt, & in prioribus facilis est conciliatio.

Darius Medus & Cyrus simul regnarunt annos 2

Cyrus solus 22

Artaxerxes Assuerus 20

Darius Artaxerxes Longimanus 37

Darius nothus 19

Artaxerxes Mnemon 55

Ochus 26

Arsames 4

Darius 6

Ignotus est Graecis primus Darius qui Medus dicitur, & putant nonnulli Cyaxaren esse. Certum est autem ex Daniele, regnasse initio hunc Darium Medum cum Cyro. Et quidem Daniel ipse disceruit hunc Darium à Cyro, ait Darium esse Medum, Persam verò Cyrum. Non igitur pugnant haec cum Graecis, qui omissis Medis, numerant Persas.

Cambysen praeterit Metasthenes, ut ego existimo, quia non regnavit post mortem patris. Sed cum pater Cyrus in Scythiam duceret exercitum, more usitato prius domi regem creavit, ne absente ipso, de successione certamina orirentur.

Est ergo Artaxerxes Assuerus, is quem nominant Graeci Darium Hystaspis filium, quod ita esse ex historia intelligitur. Quia Philo narrat hunc Assuerum recuperasse Assyriam & Babylonem, quae dsfecerant. Id apud Herodotum narratur de Dario filio Hystaspis. Et scribit Herodotus Dario concubinam fuisse omnium charissimam Aristonam, quae adpellatio convenit cum adpellatione Ester.

Ex Esdra manifestum est successisse Assuero Artaxerxen, huic additur cognomen à Metasthene & Philone Longimanus, seuμακροχειρ.Nec dubium est, hunc eundem esse Longimanum, quem sic nominant Graeci. Sed cur hic omittitur Xerxes? Id rursus eo fieri iudico, quia Xerxe procul in Europam ducente exercitum, necesse fuit cooptari regem collegam domi, ne in oriente seditiones orirentur, si nemo praeesset regia autoritate, post Xerxis discessum. Deinceps convenit series cum Graecis.

Nunc redeo ad hebdomadas. Cum Cyri & Assueri tempore fuisset impedita aedificatio, ut constat ex Esdra, postea sub tertio rege, scilicet Longimano, rursus aedificatio permissa est. Nam ut Cyrus, ita hîc Longimanus benignior fuit erga Iudaeos, quàm Assuerus, etsi hunc quoque flexit Ester, quae fortassis disciplina domestica Longimanum ad veram pietatem adsuefecit. Ac fuisse amplexum Longimanum doctrinam populi Israel de deo, inde constat, quia praecipui gubernatores in aula fuerunt duo viri, vere colentes deum, & duces Israelitarum, Esdras & Nehemias.

Saepe autem iam dixi eodem tempore, Haggaeum & Zachariam coepisse vaticinari de aedificatione templi processura. De hoc vaticinio existimant loqui angelum, cum ait, Ab exitu sermonis, ut iterum aedificetur Ierusalem, &c.

Constituamus ergo initium hebdomadum à secundo anno Longimani.

Huic supputationi grave testimonium addit eventus ipse. Nam antè interruptae erant operae. Nunc primum processerunt, & anno sexto Longimani instauratio templi absoluta est. Ideo arbitrantur quidam dici in Ioanne, quadragintasex annis aedificatum esse templum. Sunt enim à Cyro usque ad secundum annum Longimani, anni 42. quibus impedita fuit aedificatio, etiamsi inchoatum erat templum. Postea verò sub Longimano rursus novo decreto concessa aedificatione templum absolutum est intra quadriennium. Ita fient anni à prima inchoatione 46.

Sunt autem iuxta Metasthenem à secundo anno Longimani, usque ad initium Alexandri, anni 145.

Septem annis regnavit Alexander post interfectum Darium.

146. post Alexandri mortem, usque ad initium ducis Iudae Machabaei, ut clare constat ex libro Machabaeorum.

127. ab initio Iudae Machabaei, usque ad Herodis initium.

30. anni ab initio Herodis usque ad natum Christum.

In hac supputatione post Alexandrum, secuti sumus librum Machabaeorum, qui numerat annos ab Alexandro usque ad Machabaeos. Nec verò dubium est, singularem fuisse diligentiam in populo Iudaico, in annotanda serie annorum, quare etiamsi haec series aliquanto brevior est, quàm altera suprà tradita, ex Olympiadum catalogo, tamen hanc propter autoritatem libri Machabaeorum, non aspernandam duco.

Sed summam colligamus.

310. ab initio Alexandri usque ad natum Christum, adde annos à Longimano usque ad Alexandrum 145. & annos à nato Christo usque ad eius baptismum 30. His annis collectis, habebis à secundo Longimani anno usque ad Christum praedicantem, annos 485. Iam 69 hebdomades continent annos 483. Disertè autem inquit angelus, usque ad Christum ducem, id est, praedicantem, esse hebdomades 69. ac deinde subiicit repetitionem, inquiens, Post sexagintaduas hebdomades occidetur Christus, quo loco ex superiore versiculo etiam repetendae erunt septem hebdomades, sicut antea dixerat, septem & sexagintaduas, quae sunt sexagintanovem. Nam postrema hebdomade, quae tantum est dimidiata, Christus suo ministerio perfunctus, crucifixus, & resuscitatus est, misit spiritum sanctum, ac publicè edidit praedicationem Evangelii, missis apostolis ad gentes. Ideo inquit angelus ad Danielem de Christo, Confirmabit testamentum multis, id est, inchoabit suum regnum, praedicabit Evangelium, fiet victima pro genere humano, resuscitatus dabit spiritum sanctum, mittet apostolos, ut Evangelium doceant, multos in illa dimidia hebdomade donabit aeterna salute. Nam antequam postrema hebdomas tota post baptismum peracta est, Christus, anno scilicet post baptismum suum quarto, crucifixus & resuscitatus est. In hac supputatione non interrumpuntur hebdomades, usque ad praedicantem & resuscitatum Christum. Et significasse continuam seriem, angelus magis videtur.

Nec comprehendendum est tempus excidii Ierosolymorum in ipso hebdomadum numero, quia post Christi resurrectionem & Evangelii promulgationem misso spiritu sancto, cum iam politia Iudaica adversaretur Evangelio, reiecta est. Sicut in textu dicitur, Et non erit ei populus, scilicet qui fuit. Praeterea angelus significat postrema hebdomade futurum, ut pactum confirmetur, id est, ut Christus fiat victima, & resuscitatus, inchoet novum regnum. Haec cum postremae hebdomadi tribuantur, manifestum est angelum non complecti tempus reliquum usque ad Titum, seu excidium Ierosolymorum. Iam antea desierat haec politia esse politia dei, cum abiecit filium dei.

Habes lector, non intricatas supputationes, quae ex testimoniis non contemnendis sumptae sunt. Sed fortassis homines contentiosi talem subductionem postulant, in qua metae hebdomadum congruant in ipsis temporum articulis, ad natalem diem Christi, aut diem baptismi. Etsi arbitror, prophetiam exactè congruere, tamen qui hanc scrupulosam diligentiam requirunt, non hoc agunt, ut rem explicent, sed ut ea quae mediocriter sunt explicata, eludant. Quare aut propiora tradant, aut haec cavillari desinant, si quid habent candoris. Hoc verò piis sufficit, quòd vident praefinitum esse tempus adventus Messiae, videlicet post reditum ex Babylone circiter annos 490. Et oportuisse nasci Messiam, adhuc durante politia Iudaica. Haec testimonia nobis hîc praecipuè observanda sunt. Et quanquam articulos temporum difficile est constituere, tamen hoc apparet, congruere hos annos 490. vel à Cyro ad nascentem Christum, vel à Longimano ad baptismum Christi. Nam series annorum post Alexandrum, notior est. Ab Alexandro, si retrò quaerantur anni iuxta Graecos ad Longimanum, magis etiam quadrabit computatio. Constat Alexandrum coepisse regnare Olympiade 111.

Iam si retrò numeres annos, certum est ex Polybio diligentissimo & fideli scriptore, captas esse Athenas à Lysandro, Olympiade 92. habebis ergo annos 76. aut circiter, nam articulum ipsum difficile est constituere. Duravit autem bellum illud tempore Periclis motum, aliquanto ante Darium nothum, annos 27. Si igitur coniunges annos ab Alexandro ad initium belli Peloponnesiaci, habebis annos 103. Haec à Graecis certa fide traduntur. Reliquum est tempus regni Longimani, qui circiter quadraginta annos regnavit. Consentaneum est igitur, quod antea posuimus ex Metasthene, à Longimano ad Alexandrum esse annos 145.

Ita collatio historiarum ostendit, has computationes omnes, quas recensui, congruere, nec procul aberrare, etiamsi ipsi temporum articuli seu momenta non exactissimè monstrantur. Summa tamen rei tenetur, & spero eruditos & candidos probaturos esse cum studium nostrum in inquirenda temporum serie, & collatione historiarum, tum verò etiam ipsas supputationes.

Illud iterum monendi sunt lectores, ut cogitent, quid piis haec concio angeli, & hoc testimonium utilitatis adferat. Perspicuè refutantur multa Iudaeorum deliramenta. Nam cum durante politia Iudaica, nasci Messiam oportuerit, ut hîc dicitur, necesse est natum & exhibitum nobis esse Messiam. Refutantur etiam somnia de mundano imperio Messiae, quod adhuc Iudaei sibi pollicentur. Docemur de passione Christi, & de veris beneficiis, de abolendo peccato, abolenda morte, de restitutione iustitiae & vitae aeternae. Denique summam Evangelii paucis versiculis angelus complexus est. Prodest igitur haec concio angeli piis mentibus, ad confirmandam fidem, quia vident eventus qui in Evangelio describuntur, cum praedicatione congruere.

Nunc breviter textum percurram. Septuaginta hebdomades praecisae sunt super populum tuum, & super urbem sanctam tuam, id est, anni 490. praefiniti sunt seu constituti. Nam id haud dubiè significat vocabulum, quo Daniel utitur. Tantisper, inquit, hic populus, & urbs vestra, hoc est, urbs & politia servabitur ad id tempus usque, quo finietur praevaricatio, & claudetur peccatum, id est, quo per adventum Messiae delebitur peccatum, cum Christus erit victima, & cum Evangelium revelabitur, delebitur enim in his, qui Evangelio credituri sunt, seu qui postea erunt membra populi dei. Etsi enim in hac mortali vita manent in his qui per Evangelium renati sunt, imbecillitas naturae carnalis repugnans legi dei, & mors corporis, tamen remissio praedicatur, & peccatum est, ut hîc dicitur, clausum, tectum condonatione, & inchoatur regnum Christi, nova agnitio dei, nova iustitia, & vita aeterna, in his qui ad Christum convertuntur.

Et expiabitur iniquitas. Hîc declarat angelus priores particulas. Quomodo finietur peccatum? Sic scilicet, quia fiet expiatio, fiet sacrificium Messiae promissum, quo solo, pater aeternus placabitur.

Et venit iustitia perpetua. Usitatum est prophetis, cum de regno Christi loquuntur, complecti integram instaurationem. Abolito peccato, abolita morte, eo tempore exhibebitur nova & perpetua iustitia & vita, quae in hac mortali vita per Evangelium, & dato spiritu sancto, inchoatur, sed postea post resurrectionem erit integra. Et signabitur prophetia & visio, id est, complebitur ac finietur, videlicet; quia promissa in prophetiis de Messia exhibebuntur.

Et ungetur sanctus sanctorum, id est, pontifex & rex ab aeterno patre unctus, id est, missus, ut fungatur munere pontificis et regis, & perfusus, spiritu sancto. Hic inquam pontifex & rex Messias ipse tunc aderit, & munere suo fungetur, docebit Evangelium, sacrificium suum offeret, ostendet se esse dominum, per quem deus aboleri peccatum & mortem velit, sanctificabit credentes, id est, remittet eis peccata, & effundet in eos spiritum sanctum. Clarè igitur adfirmat, tunc adventurum esse Messiam promissum. Ut autem pontifices & reges ungebantur, sic & filius dei cum mittitur, ungi dicitur, id est, mitti ad functionem sacerdotii & regii muneris, & perfundi spiritu sancto, utIoan. 6.dicitur, Hunc pater signavit deus, id est, peculiariter unum hunc ad tantum munus delegit, & misit. Ingens & inenarrabilis est ira dei adversus peccatum generis humani, nec ulla victima alia, nec alio modo placanda erat, nisi intercessione filii, qui se abiecit, ut fieret victima. Hanc victimam unam pater signavit, delegit, approbavit, perfudit spiritu sancto, ita ut ex eius plenitudine caeteri haurirent, ut Baptista inquit. Hactenus igitur haec dixit, Nascetur & exhibebitur promissus liberator, ac fungetur suo munere, post annos 490. durante adhuc urbe Ierusalem & politia vestra. Haec sententia pios tunc in publicis periculis uberrime consolabatur. Quid enim nobis optatius esset, quàm scire mansuram esse in his ipsis locis ecclesiam, in quibus nostra erit posteritas?

Et scies & intelliges, Ab exitu sermonis de reditu & aedificatione Ierusalem, id est, à secundo anno Longimani, quo deus per vaticinium Haggaei & Zachariae pollicetur aedificationem processuram esse, & quo anno Longimanus decretum edidit, ne impediretur aedificatio. Inde inquit angelus, numerabis hebdomadas 69. à populi reditu usque ad Christum ducem, seu natum, seu praedicantem. Cum enim nominatim dicat, usque ad ducem Christum, significat iam fungentem ministerio, quod post baptismum palàm exercuit. Admonui autem suprà non interrumpi hebdomadas, si ad baptismum Christi à Longimano numerentur.

Cur autem iubet 69. hebdomadas numerari, id est annos 483. Postremam hîc non addit, describit enim tempus quod praecedit baptismum Christi, & pios confirmat, ut sciant interim, non delendam esse urbem, etsi magni motus, magnae mutationes regnorum secuturae erant, & futurum erat, ut Iudaea duriter quassaretur inter vicinarum gentium tumultus.

Ideo additur consolatio, Usque ad Christum ducem, hebdomadibus septem & hebdomadib. 62. habitabitur Ierusalem, & aedificata erit platea & murus. Emphasis verborum in hac brevitate vaticinii expendenda est. Disertè inquit, habitabitur Ierusalem, & erit aedificata, 2 c.

hoc est, urbs et politia Iudaica inter im non delebuntur. Addit autem textus (In augustia temporum) hoc est, & tamen variè vexabitur Iudaea. Primum in instauratione urbis & templi, adversabantur proximae gentes in Samaria, & praefecti Persici. Postea Ochus crudeliter saeviit in Iudaeos. Post Alexandrum cum essent bella inter successores assidua, praesertim inter Aegyptios & Syriacos, Iudaea in medio interiecta, utrisque praedae erat. Inter haec pericula & mala, tamen hîc vox divina pollicetur, urbem Ierusalem, & politiam Iudaicam non interituram esse. Hîc pius lector imaginem verae ecclesiae dei et laborum ecclesiae consideret. Non minus miraculum fuit, reductio ex Babylone, & instauratio politiae, quàm olim fuerat eductio populi ex Aegypto. Primum à Cyro concessus est reditus, aliquanto pòst Cyrus ex regno discedens, in bello interficitur.

Amisso hoc patrono, beneficium concessum Iudaeis rursus eripitur. Hîc quanto in dolore fuit Daniel? Lugebat regem amicum, videbat inimicos verae doctrinae gratulaturos sibi esse interitum Cyri, & impiae factionis potentiam iterum confirmaturos esse, ut sunt in aulis vices.

Haec viri sapientissimi Danielis animum non leviter excruciabant. Sed is tamen se hac consolatione sustentabat, norat dei promissiones certas, veras, & ratas esse, etiam si eventus mirandi sint, nec nostris coniecturis respondeant. Nam hac doctrina oportet nos in ecclesia instructos esse, ut fide petamus & expectemus eventus. Tandem rursus concessa aedificatione quàm variae aerumnae, quàm varii tumultus intervenerunt?

Ita ecclesia semper exercetur inopinatis & variis terroribus & difficultatibus Nos nunc in instauratione ecclesiae dei, in scholis & in templis, quibus conflictamur difficultatibus Undique obiiciuntur impedimenta domi & foris, sed consolemur nos illo exemplo veteri instaurationis Ierosolymae.

Et post hebdomadas sexaginta & duas, excidetur Christus. Hîc repete ex superiore versu septem, sicut antea dixit, Post 67. & duas, id est, post 69. hebdomadas, videlicet post annos 483. Christus interficietur, & fiet victima pro ecclesia. Antea dixit, mansuram esse urbem & politiam, donec veniat dux Christus. Nunc expressè concionatur de Christi passione. Et perspicuum est testimonium de morte Messiae, qualia sunt & apudEsaiam cap. 53.Abscissus est de terra viventium. Item, Ponet animam suam hostiam pro peccato.

Haec cum sint perspicuè dicta, non sunt alienis interpretationibus depravanda, ut faciunt caeci et scelerati Iudaei, qui quia somniant Messiam occupaturum imperia mundi, & regnaturum Alexandri aut Augusti more, corrumpunt dicta de passione Messiae. Non enim intelligunt qualis sit liberatio à peccato & à morte, promissa primis patribus. Fingunt pueriliter, eo tantum mitti Messiam, ut in hac mortali, misera, & fragili vita cumulet opibus & voluptatibus posteros Abrahae. Nec cogitant longè maiorem liberationem primis patribus promissam esse, qui intelligebant quas res amiserit humana natura, scilicet firmam agnitionem dei, perpetuam iustitiam & vitam. Ideo Esaias vocat Messiam patrem aeternae vitae. Etcapite 53cum de morte eius concionatus esset, addit concionem de eius resurrectione, & regno aeterno.

Itaque repudiatis Iudaeorum corruptelis, ipsum audiamus angelum,τὸ ρητὸνteneamus, simus discipuli vocis divinae, eamque dextrè intelligamus, non adfingamus ipsi interpretationes, omissa verborum significatione. De persona loquitur, cum nominat Messiam ac de illo ipso, quem in proximo versiculo nominavit sanctum sanctorum, hîc inquit, excidetur. Sicut & Esaias ait, Abscissus est de terra viventium.

Meminerimus ergo hîc certum & perspicuum testimonium tradi de morte & supplicio Christi. Sed id cum legimus aut audimus, circumstantias etiam cogitemus, sumptas ex aliis locis prophetarum, cogitemus & de resurrectione. Ideo adficitur supplicio, ut fiat victima pro peccatis generis humani. Id Esaias declaravit pluribus verbis in eo capite, quod toties iam citavimus, Quia ponet animam suam hostiam pro peccato, videbit semen longaevum. In hoc dicto mors, ratio victimae, resurrectio, & regnum comprehenduntur.

Lex habuit antea victimas, sed non placârunt iram dei. Haec una victima placat. Hoc verè est inenarrabile consilium dei, sed utrumque ostendit irae dei & misericordiae magnitudinem. Cum enim nullius hominis virtus aut obedientia placare iram dei potuerit, nisi unius filii obedientia ac mors, necesse est veram, ingentem, & ine narrabilem iram esse. Rursus vis amoris ingens est, quòd filium nobis donavit, quod talidato pignore, nos ex aeternis cruciatibus reducit, & certos reddit nos à se diligi. Sicut inquit filius ipse, Sic deus dilexit mundum, ut filium suum unigenitum dederit, &c.

Denique in hoc brevi dicto, lector universam doctrinam Evangelii cogitet de beneficiis, quae propter Christi mortem nobis data sunt. Cogitet item doctrinam de fide & invocatione. Nam beneficia illa fide accipienda sunt, ita tibi verè placatus & propitius est deus aeternus pater, cum fide propter filii mortem statuis eum tibi propitium esse, & hac fide invocas. Hanc fidem vult, ut in invocatione adferas, utRo. 5.dicitur, Per hunc habemus accessum. Itemad Ebraeos 4.Habentes pontificem Iesum filium dei, qui potest compati infirmitati nostrae. Accedamus cum fiducia ad thronum gratiae. Haec satis sit hîc breviter attigisse, quae copiosius explicantur in Epistola ad Romanos & alibi, quoties de iustificatione dicitur.

Et non erit ei populus. In textu maior etiam brevitas est, in hoc membro. His verbis (Et non ei) seu (Et non ad eum) necesse est intelligi non esse ei gloriam, seu populum. Et, ut potius intelligamus de populo, movit sequens sententia de excidio urbis & templi. Hic populus mox desinet esse Messiae ac dei ecclesia. Sicut in Ioanne breviter dicitur, Et sui eum non receperunt. Reiecta est gens Iudaica propter Evangelii contemptum, Et rursus collecta est deo Ecclesia ex reliquiis huius populi & ex gentibus, ut saepe in concionibus Evangelii praedicitur, et postea disputat Paulus de vocatione gentium.

Et civitatem & sanctuarium destruet populus ducis venientis, id est, Titi exercitus, Et finis eius diluvium, et usque ad finem belli definita seu constituta desolatio, seu vastitas, id est, Deinde sequetur finis populi Iudaici & politiae Iudaicae, ex quo non rursus emerget, ut antea ex Babylonico exilio rediit, sed velut obrutus diluvio, funditus peribit.

Et quidem acre et difficile bellum erit. Nam Romani non sine multis & variis certaminibus Iudaeos oppresserunt, sed postquam erit debellatum, mansura est perpetua vastitas & solitudo. Nunquam sacerdotium Leviticum, nunquam regnum aut principatus Iuda, nunquam politia Mosaica, nunquam civitas Ierosolyma restituetur, sed, ut Oseas inquit, deinceps gentes vocabuntur ad societatem populi dei, eruntque populus dei, omnes qui Evangelium Messiae amplectentur, utRom. 9. & 10.dicitur.

Constat Iudaeos post Titum aliquoties conatos esse suam politiam restituere. Sub Adriano convenerat multitudo Iudaeorum, undique collecta, & patriam armis invadebat. Sed ab Adriano profligata & deleta est. Postea Iulianus odio Christiani nominis, concessit Iudaeis rursus urbem & templum condere. Inchoatum est opus, collecta ingenti pecunia, sed terraemotu aedificatio impedita est, & magna multitudo hominum saxis & ruderibus quassatis, oppressa est. Narrat etiam Nazianzenus, passim vestibus populi figuras crucis prodigiosè impressas esse. Absterriti ergo Iudaei caelestibus signis, inchoatum opus reliquerunt, quia deus decrevit, ne politia Iudaica restituatur. Vult conspici exemplum irae ab omnibus gentibus, quòd filii contemptum horribilibus suppliciis puniat. Vult etiam sepultam esse politiam Moysi, ne confirmetur opinio de ceremoniis, homines lege seu ceremoniis iustos esse.

Et confirmabit pactum seu testamentum multis. Addit declarationem superiori concioni, quia non satis est, de morte Christi, et de exitio populi dicere, Addenda sunt etiam testimonia de resurrectione, de regno, & de beneficiis Christi, item de reliquiis populi quae liberantur, sicut caeteri prophetae promiserunt, ut Esaias ait, Reliquias salvas fore, hoc est, non pontifices, non principes, non robur populi, sed coetum exiguum & spretum.

Cohorresco intuens hoc tam triste exemplum. Cur gloriantur nostri pontifices se esse ecclesiam, non posse prorsus abiici suum caetum? Cur nos confidimus propter professionem esse filios dei? Si non pepercit deus posteris Abrahae &, prophetarum & aliorum, qui res maximas gesserunt, quid fiet de nostris pontificibus, qui Evangelium contemnunt? Quid de nobis, si indulgebimus pravis cupiditatibus, ambitioni, livori, avaritiae, luxui, & tantum propter professionem arrogabimus nobis augustum ecclesiae titulum?

Sic autem inquit angelus, Confirmabit Messias testamentum multis una hebdomade, id est, postrema hebdomade palàm Christus Evangelium praedicabit, addet testimonia, vocabit multos ad Evangelium, & vitae aeternae societatem, fiet victima, & resuscitatus, inchoabit regnum aeternum, ostendet se esse datorem iustitiae & vitae aeternae, mittet apostolos ad colligendam ecclesiam, donabit eos spiritu sancto.

Haec fient in dimidiata hebdomade, id est, non confectis aunis septem. Nam à Christi baptismo ad resurrectionem, sunt anni tres & aliquot menses. Accedit & tempus pentecostes, intra hoc tempus, pactum confirmatum est, id est, exhibitae sunt promissiones novi testamenti, praedicatum Evangelium de remissione peccatorum, filius dei factus est victima, resuscitatus, inchoavit regnum aeternum, misit apostolos, dedit spiritum sanctum, coepit colligero ecclesiam, quam sanctificat, gubernat, & donaturus est vita, luce, iustitia, gloria aeterna. Haec propria Christi opera & beneficia vocat confirmationem pacti, id est, exhibitionem promissionum, quae sunt factae ad patres & prophetas. Sicut apud Ieremiam dicitur,capite 31.Ecce dies venient, & feriam cum domo Israel pactum novum. Dabo legem meam in corda eorum. Ero deus eorum, &c.

De hoc ipso foedere, quod in prophetis copiosè describitur, angelum loqui, nihil dubium est, cum ait, Confirmabit pactum seu foedus multis. Iam pius lector promissionem Evangelii & vitae aeternae datam inde usque ab initio cogitet, ut beneficia filii dei diligenter reputet animo, ut agnoscat, & verè invocet hunc Messiam, & liberatione per eum donata, fruatur.

Et in dimidio hebdomadis, cessare faciet hostiam & oblationem. O triste vaticinium, tristem vocem Iudaeis ignorantibus Evangelium, & admirantibus atque amantibus suas ceremonias, & ritus pulcherrimos à deo traditos, tantorum prophetarum & ducum consuetudine comprobatos. Amamus enim patrios mores, et aegrè ferimus eos reiici ac damnari. Sed Danieli et piis, intelligentibus Evangelium, gratissima est haec vox angeli, quae testatur prius ceremonias illas fuisse signa & typos venturi sacrificii, Exhibito autem ipso Messia, signorum finem fore, Rebus exhibitis umbrs cessaturas esse. Messias inchoavit iustitiam & vitam aeternam, dato Evangelio & spiritu sancto. Haec ingentia beneficia agnoscamus & accipiamus, nec spectacula ceremoniarum veterum, in quibus hae res adumbratae fuerunt, requiramus.

Est ergo sententia, In dimidio hebdomadis, postquam Christus suum sacrificium fecerit, resurrexerit, et dederit spiritum sanctum, postea reiectae fuerunt ceremoniae Leviticae. Ideo postea apostoli, ut in Actis narratur,capite 15perspicuo decreto abrogarunt circumcisionem, & reliquos Leviticos ritus. Et dimicat eadem de re acerrimè Paulus, ut constat. Observetur ergo apud Danielem hoc testimonium, quod adfirmat, post Messiae praedicationem cessaturas esse Leviticas ceremonias.

Et super alam abominationes desolationis, & usque ad consummationem definitam, stillabit super vastitatem. Haec ultima pars vaticinii continet verè tristem sententiam. Superiores versus res laetas promiserunt, duraturam esse politiam usque ad Messiae adventum, venturum esse Messiam, daturum beneficia aeterno foedere patribus promissa. Sed addita fuit sententia de excidio civitatis. Est autem multo tristius quod hîc de templo dicitur. Hoc ipsum templum, in quo deus olim invocari voluit, ubi exaudivit, quod defendit, iam ita diris execrationibus damnatur, ut dicat deinceps, post Christi praedicationem fore sedem execrandi idoli, vastantis cultum dei.

Et si autem & huic loco, Iudaicae corruptelae tenebras inducunt, tamen nihil dubium est hoc dicere angelum, in templo futura esse idola execranda, post quae secutura sit perpetua vastitas et solitudo. Sic interpretatus est Christus ipse hunc textum,Matth. 24.Cum videbitis abominationem desolationis, dictam per Danielem prophetam, stantem in loco sancto (qui legit, intelligat) tunc qui sunt in. Iudaea, fugiant, &c. Signum excidii Ierosolymae, & interitus gentis Iudaicae, sore inquit, cum in loco sancto stabit idolum, quod vocat Hebraica consuetudineβδέλυγμα.Quanquam autem angelus totum cultum intelligit fore idolum execrandum, postquam pontifices interfecerint Messiam, tamen ut conspici ira dei possit, permisit deus post resurrectionem templum multis tetris exemplis prophanari.

Et quanquam pii intelligebant, cultus ipsos pugnantes cum filio dei, res esse damnatas à deo, seu sacrificia eorum, qui adversabantur Evangelio, displicere deo, & veré esse idola &βδελύγματα,quia nulla invocatio placet deo, nisi ea quae sit in agnitione vera & fiducia filii mediatoris, tamen arbitror etiam exteruas prophanationes accessisse incurrentes in oculos, ut vexilla Tyberii, & caedes factas in ipso templo, cum Iudaeï vexilla eiicere conarentur, & fortasse statuas Caligulae, etsi Iosephus eam historiam aliquanto aliter narrat. Fuisse enim aliqua tetra signa incurrentia in oculos, quae pios admonuerunt de praedictione Danielis & Christi, & significârunt instare tempus excidii, consentaneum est, ut nunc ad opiniones pravas episcoporum, canonicorum & monachorum, accedunt externae notae, incurrentes in oculos, quae commonefacere mediocres homines possunt, cultus hostium Evangelii damnatos esse. Fit concursus ad certas statuas, petitur ab hominibus mortuis auxilium & salus, prorsus ut olim ab Hercule, Castore, Polluce, & similibus. Cenae domini multiplex est manifesta prophanatio, ut infrà dicam. Quid caetera commemorem? Vagae libidines sacrificulorum certèβδελύγματαsunt, & testantur deo displicere hoc genus sacrificulorum, quod penė in Epicuraeo contemptu dei vivit. Ita fuisse insignes notas externas impietatis apud Iudaeos post Christi resurrectionem existimo.

Sed verba textus expendamus. Res ipsa loquitur, Iudaeos propter inscitiam quaedam membra hîc insulsissimè interpretari. Ut autem otiosam prolixitatem fugiam, deliramenta Iudaica omittam, et breviter interpretationem recitabo.

Verba haec sunt in textu, Et super alam erunt abominationes desolationis. Mirantur multi hanc figuram, cum dicit, Super alam, sed expedita, simplex, concinna, & perspicua est enarratio, Super alam, id est, super Cherubin stantes in tabula propitiatorii, in sancto sanctorum.

Sic Christus interpretatur, qui ait,βδέλυγμα ἐρημώσεως τὸ ρηθὲν διὰ δανιὴλ τοῦ προφήτου ἐστὸς ἐν τόπῳ ἅγιῳ,id est, Seu idolum manufactum stabit in illo ipso loco Cherubim, qui fuerat sedes dei, ut constat ex25. cap. Exo.seu cultus ad illum locum & invocatio, quae fit cum contumelia filii dei, erit idolum & res damnata. Nunc proh dolor non difficile est exempla monstrare in templis. In invocatione mortuorum hominum, & prophae natione cenae domini, haud dubiė coluntur & invocantur idola, quae sunt posita in praecipuo loco, id est, sunt praecipui cultus in templis hostium Evangelii.

Erit atrocior tota haec angeli concio, si quis cogitabit totam descriptionem de propitiatorio & arca. Nihil potuit dici aut cogitari tristius apud pios, quàm pollui hanc veram dei sedem, ubi deus promiserat certò se exauditurum esse vota populi sui.

Cur nominat abominationem vastatricem? Usitata figura est Hebraeis, idolum vocare abominationem seuβδέλυγμα,id est,rem quam deus execratur & damnat. Et execratur, ac damnat, ideo, quia ei falso tribuitur honos deo debitus. Ut cogita statuam Mariae, quales multas multi viderunt, ad quam concurritur, & alligatur ad eam seu Mariae seu dei praesentia contra mandatum dei. Ibi putatur seu deus magis propitius, seu Maria benignius audire preces, quoquo modo haec fucantur, cultus est impius & damnatus à deo. Tale idolum & talis cultus estβδέλυγμα,id est, res damnata à deo, ac ideo damnata, quia invocatio non rectė fit ad deum. Recitavi exemplum, ut vocabuli usus magis intelligi possit.

Cur autem additβδέλυγμα ἐρημώσεωςEbraismus est, & significatio effectus. Quasi dicat, Idolum vastans, quia idolum positum in templo Ierosolymae post resurrectionem, fuit signum vastationis & eversion totius templi & politiae. Impius cultus erat -aussa excidii. Externae prophanationes fuerunt signa excidii. Intelligas ergoβδέλυγμα ἐρημώσεως,rem damnatam, vastatricem.

Est autem vastatio duplex, prior doctrinae, opinionum, invocationis. Altera posterior poena ipsa, scilicet exitium populi. Ut si dicas, Statua Mariae est vastatrix, primum verae doctrinae de Christo mediatore, de invocatione Christi, postea etiam attrahit poenas, sicut haud dubiè regna quae titulum ecclesiae habent, propter idolomanias evertuntur & ruunt.

Sequens particula repetit sententiam, videlicet, cum post natum Messiam evertetur urbs Ierosolyma, nunquam pòst futuram esse instaurationem aut restitutionem urbis Ierosolymae, templi, sacerdotii Levitici, & politiae Iudaicae. Hanc praedictionem comprobat eventus iam annos 1470. Id tempus aliquanto longius est, quàm tempus à Davide, qui cepit arcem, &

condidit civitatem Ierusalem, usque ad eversionem à Tito factam. Sunt enim anni à Davide ad hanc eversionem à Tito factam, 1150.

TRADIDI enarrationem huius utilissimi capitis, & temporum subductionem, quam potui; nunc quidem minime intricatam, quam tamen iudicio eruditorum libenter permitto, quos rogo ut studium meum boni consulant. Non multum discrepat haec subductio, quam exposui, ab ea quam clarissimus vir D. Lutheris in Chronico suo recenset, qui annos partim iuxta Philonem, partim iuxta librum Machabaeorum distribuit. Cum autem videbit lector ferè congruere ad eum numerum, testimonia quae & nos ex historiis certissimis adieeimus, consensu movebitur, ne has supputationes aspernetur.

Prodest autem studiosis haec temporum inquisitio, fit enim gratior & dulcior lectio pro phetarum, cum mediocriter intelliguntur. Haec autem ad alendam pietatem conducit. Deinde ivuat considerare hoc consilium dei de numero annorum, & de excidio revelato. Nobis post eversam Ierosolymam propositum est certum testimonium, quod docet Messiam exhibitum esse. Ita pios ante natum Christum numerus annorum admonebat, quo fere tempore Messiam visuri essent.

Cogitemus autem gravissimus esse caussas, cur deus, hac tanta de re, de filii missione, ecclesiam voluerit certam esse. Simus grati, quòd filium exhibuit, quòd ecclesiam his testimoniis confirmavit. Praedicemus beneficium, inquiramus testimonia, conferamus historias, ut testimonia fiant illustriora. Hoc studium certè pios decet. Et ipsarum historiarum ac temporum series obiter multa de providentia divina, de poenis impiorum, de ecclesiae forma, aerumnis, & defensione monet. Haec exempla deus vult considerari, ut nos de multis magnis rebus admoneant, sieut Psalmus inquit. Denique non retrò animo legere & contemplari rerum initia, ecclesiae exordium, mutationes, instaurationes, gubernationem, non conferre tempora depravatae religionis, ad antiquitatem verae doctrinae, negligentia est admodum vituperanda in homine literato.

POSTQUAM autem totius capitis enarrationem absolvimus, iam studiosus lector repetat animo praecipuos locos, ut quam haec lectio utilitatem attulerit, consideret. Initio traditur doctrina de paenitentia, de fide ac vera invocatione. Hic locus continet illustre testimonium consensus prophetici & apostolici. Nam prorsus congruit sententia Danielis cum Paulo, cum inquit Daniel, Precamur, non propter iustitiam nostram, sed propter miserationes tuas multas. Et quidem addit, Propter dominum, videlicet, propter promissum liberatorem.

Haec prorsus congruunt cum Paulinis concionibus, quae dicunt, nos fide recipi propter dominum mediatorem, non propter nostram dignitatem. Nec aliud docet Paulus, quàm quod monstrat exemplum Danielis. Utile est autem piis, videre consensum propheticarum concionum & Pauli, ut intelligant unam perpetuam esse sententiam catholicae ecclesiae dei, quam professi sunt primi patres, prophetae, Christus & apostoli.

Estque memoria dignus locus, quòd nominatim hîc invocat, propter dominum, id est, propter promissum liberatorem. Haec est vera ecclesiae vox, discernens invocationem nostram ab ethnica & Mahometica. Nam gentesque non norunt filium dei, non rectè invocant deum, nec statuunt suas preces recipi, quia nullam tenent promissionem. Nos verò scimus aeternum patrem per filium mediatorem placatum esse, & promissionem traditam, quòd propter hunc preces nostras recepturus sit. Ideo Christus inquit, Quidquid petieritis patrem in nomine meo, dabit vobis.

Discamus igitur hanc esse invocationem veram, petere propter filium mediatorem, & fiducia misericordiae propter ipsum promissae, aeternum patrem invocare. Econtra verò ethnicas & Mahometicas invocationes execremur, quae filium dei vel non norunt, vel contumelia adficiunt. Depravata est invocatio & apud adversarios nostros, qui doctrinam de fide obruunt, nec docent fiduciam mediatoris ad deum adferendam esse, sed vel fiduciam propriae dignitatis, vel dubitationem, quae nihil differt ab ethnica invocatione. Habes igitur in hoc capite doctrinam utilem toti vitae.

Sequitur vaticinium de tempore adventus Messiae, de morte Messiae, de delenda politia Iudaica. Hic locus manifestum testimonium continet de duobus articulis, de quibus maximè litigant Iudaei. Docet Messiam exhibitum esse, quia venturus erat, adhuc durante politia Iudaica. Docet item regnum Messiae non fore mundi imperium seu Mosaicam politiam, quia hîc manifestè & multis verbis dicitur, Mosaicam politiam delendam esse.

Ergo sic regnat Messias resuscitatus, ut dato Evangelio, vocet ad vitam aeternam eos, qui ipsum agnoscunt esse propitiatorem, & inchoet in eis per spiritum sanctum novam lucem & obedientiam, deleat peccata, & sit daturus vitam aeternam, iis, in quibus lucet fides, & iustitia nova inchoata est.

## 10:1 Argument
CHAPTER X.

In the third year of Cyrus king of the Persians, a word was revealed to Daniel, surnamed Belteshazzar, and the word is true, and great strength: and he understood the speech, for understanding is a work in a vision. In those days I Daniel was mourning for three weeks of days, desirable bread I did not eat, and flesh and wine did not enter into my mouth, nor was I anointed with ointment, until the days of three weeks were completed. And on the twenty-fourth day of the first month, I was beside the great river, which is the Tigris. And I lifted up my eyes, and I saw: and behold, one man clothed in linen, and his loins were girded with the finest gold: and his body was like the chrysolite, and his face as the appearance of lightning, and his eyes as a burning lamp: and his arms, and those things which are downward even to the feet, like the appearance of glowing brass: and the voice of his words as the voice of a multitude. But I, Daniel, alone saw the vision, moreover the men who were with me did not see, but an excessive terror fell upon them, and they fled into a hiding place. But I, being left alone, saw this great vision: and strength did not remain in me, but also my appearance was changed is in me, and I have withered, and I had no strength. And I heard the voice of his words, and hearing, I lay stunned upon my face, and my countenance was stuck to the ground. And behold, a hand touched me, and raised me upon my knees, and upon the joints of my hands. And he said to me: Daniel, man of desires, understand the words which I speak to you, and stand in your station: for now I am sent to you. And when he had spoken this word to me, I stood trembling. And he said to me: Do not fear, Daniel: because from the first day that you set your heart to understand, so that you might afflict yourself in the sight of your God, your words were heard, and I have come because of your words. But the prince of the kingdom of the Persians resisted me for twenty-one days: and behold, Michael, one of the chief princes, came to my help, and I remained there beside the king of the Persians.

But I have come to teach you what things are to come to your people in the last days: for the vision is yet for days. And when he spoke to me in these words, I cast down my countenance to the ground, and was silent. And behold, as it were the likeness of a son of man touched my lips: and opening my mouth, I spoke, and said to him who stood against me: My lord, in your vision my joints are loosed, and no strength has remained in me. And how shall the servant of my lord be able to speak with my lord? for no strength has remained in me, but my breath also is stopped. Again therefore he touched me as it were the vision of a man, and strengthened me, and said: Do not fear, O man of desires, peace be to you, take courage, and be strong. And when he spoke with me, I grew strong, and said: Speak, my lord, for you have strengthened me. And he said: Do you know why I have come to you? And now I will return, to fight against the prince of the Persians. For when I went forth, the prince of the Greeks appeared coming. But I will announce to you what is written in the scripture of truth: and there is no one my helper in all these things, except Michael your prince.

THE REVELATION which is described in the previous chapter, was made in the 1st year of Cyrus, in which some time after this revelation, the edict of Cyrus concerning the restoration of Jerusalem was proposed, and some bands of the Jews began to return and commenced the building. Everything then was full of joy, congratulations, and good hope. The pious gave thanks to God, because the ancient seat, the temple, and the worship were being restored to the church, they celebrated the prophecy of Jeremiah, they praised God, who by the voice of Jeremiah had miraculously preserved the remnants of the nation. It was sweet for the younger ones to see the fatherland of their ancestors, the temple, the worship instituted by God, to enjoy liberty, and pious studies. They also acknowledged that this exile of theirs had been profitable for the propagation of doctrine. They saw Cyrus and many others had been converted to true piety, and they rejoiced that they had a favorable king, who in power and virtue then excelled all kings. These joys also softened the exile for the old man Daniel, who had then been away from his fatherland for 86 years. For he was led away before Jeconiah, as the beginning of the first chapter testifies.

But see, reader, the image of the church. These great joys are followed after three years by the saddest mourning. Cyrus departs from the kingdom, the building of the temple is prohibited by a new edict. A little while later, Cyrus himself is killed. In the court, a reversal of all things takes place; those pious men who had previously held authority with Cyrus are excluded, pressed by hatreds, and oppressed. Ethnic superstitions are renewed. Impious princes, enemies of true doctrine, are called into the court. In what great mourning Daniel was here, no one can sufficiently explain; he grieved for the fall of the king his friend, he grieved that the name of God was being treated with insult, but he was especially tormented by the weakness and the perversities of the Jews, many of whom were already saying that there was no hope of the city being restored, and that they were being deceived by the prophets. Others, even if they were not entirely broken by despair, were nevertheless dissuaders of their own, that they should not hasten to return to their homeland, but were ordered to await more tranquil times, they laughed at the foolishness of those who, moved by uncertain hope, had immediately departed from Chaldea.

As therefore the beginnings had been full of joy, so now the good are in mourning, squalor, and sadness. On the other hand, however, the part of the Jews which preferred its own leisure to religion rages with indignation, distracts the wills of its own people, and disturbs the good.

You have an image of the church, which God wills both to be exercised by afflictions and to await deliverances by faith. And when He delivers, yet the outcomes do not correspond to our conjectures. When the kindness of Cyrus had been hindered, it could afterwards be seen more clearly that this deliverance was governed by God, since so many impediments had occurred which could not be removed by human counsels.

A new vision is therefore presented, now to Daniel, not only so that he himself and the other pious ones might be strengthened in this present danger, but also so that posterity might be forewarned about the principal changes of empires, and about those calamities which were hanging over Judea. It has, however, often been said already why it is useful for the pious to be forewarned. They learn to avoid the causes of calamities, namely those crimes by which.

The others summon punishments, to prepare minds, lest in the very calamity they assent to the impious, to await liberation, especially when they understand that the joyful outcomes of these struggles have been promised by the clear voice of God. But about these benefits it will be necessary to speak again below. Now I shall briefly run through the principal topics of this chapter.

The pious angel tells Daniel that he had fought with the prince of the Persians, that is, with the devil plotting the dissipations of the Persian kingdom. And even if we are ignorant of how good and evil spirits fight among themselves, nevertheless there is no doubt that there are struggles, whether they happen by disputation or by other means. Therefore the good angel says that the evil spirit was restrained by him, who was inciting the young Cambyses and the impious courtiers, either to destroy the Jewish nation, or to kill Daniel, or to attempt other evil things which were going to bring new disturbances into the kingdom, just as sometimes a slight error, which is despised at the beginning, afterwards drags along a huge ruin.

Others dispute differently about the meaning of this chapter, but I judge this to be the simplest exposition. Moreover, the leader Michael was present to the good angel, whom he calls the leader of the people here and below By God, I understand the Son of God himself, λόγον, as he is named by John. He writes that this leader and the one above takes part in the conversation in the eighth chapter, where the angel asks him for the interpretation of the vision.

It appears that he is present also in this conversation, and that he touches the lips of Daniel, and restores him when he is dismayed. For it is certain that the Son of God has always been present to the church, and has defended it against the fury of the devil. Therefore John says, All things were made through him. For he speaks not only of the creation of things, but also of the glorious deliverances of the church. He covered the people in the Red Sea, and in the desert, he defended Joshua, Gideon, Samuel, David, Elijah, Elisha, and other faithful governors of his people.

Therefore Jacob says, Let the angel who delivered me from all evils bless these boys. These words apply to the Son of God, who truly delivers from all evils, namely from sin, from the wrath of God, from eternal death, from the snares of the devil. For it is not without cause that it is said by name, from all evils. This glory cannot be attributed to ministering angels, who even if they protect the bodies of the pious, yet they can take away neither sin nor eternal death.

Let us learn, therefore, being admonished by these passages, that the Son of God λόγον was present with the Church even from the beginning. For we shall be better fortified against the furies of the Samosatene, when we shall consider these testimonies concerning the Son of God, even before He showed Himself in the flesh, having helped the fathers, and having spoken with them. Then also we shall establish that He is present with the Church now as well, helping the pious, driving away the devil, and, as it is said in the Epistle of John, destroying the works of the devil. Let us be kindled, therefore, so that we may seek from Him and expect help against the devil, who is raging on every side and attempting to scatter and destroy the Church with Turkish arms, with the negligence of kings and pontiffs in fostering the churches, and with depraved minds being inflamed so that they may sow or confirm false opinions. But no one can recount all the arts of the devil.

Let us rather consider examples. The universal history of the world shows how bitter an enemy he is of God and of the human race. You see from how great a height he dragged down Saul, what kind of war he brought upon David, so that he might drag him along into destruction, when he had destroyed so many others, his son Absalom, Ahithophel, and undoubtedly many excelling in wisdom and virtue.

And I think there is hardly any man to whom that serpent, the plotter, does not inflict some notable wound in his soul, so as to make his faith languish. As often as David, with the tumult now settled, thought about his fall, about wives violated by force, and about other scandals, not only was his sadness renewed, but his faith was also shaken and made to languish by the dread of doubt.

Let the pious think on these things, and let them be at their post and on watch against so dire an enemy, and let them seek defense and guidance from the Son of God. The Son of God is called Immanuel, and he is truly present with us, but he wishes to be acknowledged and invoked. He also wishes us not to rush through sloth where the enemy impels, but, being vigilant, to beware of plots and to resist allurements. If your eyes sin, do not indulge them; if an impious opinion flatters, recall your mind, flee the company and conversations of the impious. Gather in your mind the true and firm testimonies of the right opinion, and at the same time resist the enemy, and summon your leader, the Son of God, so that he may help you, so that he may destroy the works of the devil.

This is the principal point of this chapter, which clearly admonishes us concerning these struggles, and signifies that good and evil spirits are warring among themselves concerning the church and concerning empires. As then the devils were inciting the young king Cambyses and most princes, so that they might either oppress or destroy the people of the Jews, so now they incite the Turkish tyrant, so that he may utterly destroy the name of Christ, they inflame pontiffs and kings, so that they may defend idols, and extinguish the light of the gospel. And as then the devil was bringing about the dissipations of the Persian kingdom, and was impelling the Babylonians and Assyrians to stir up seditions, so now he entangles princes in domestic wars, so that even by this reason he may hinder the propagation of the Gospel.

While these things are being carried out, good and evil angels fight among themselves. But our leader, the son of God, rules and helps the good, and protects the church, and represses the attacks of the evil spirits. Although we do not see these things with our eyes, and profane men consider them to be fabulous, yet that they are in truth carried out in this way, many illustrious testimonies in histories, and in prophetic and apostolic sayings show.

The histories of Jacob are known, and of the defense of the people in the Red Sea and in the desert. Afterwards when Joshua had encamped at Jericho, while he was still observing the situation of the region, he sees a certain one standing before him with a drawn sword, who being asked who he is, he answered that he was the leader of the army of God, sent, that is to say, to bring aid to the multitude of Israel. I judge, however, that this one also was the Son of God himself. Add to this the histories of David, Elijah, and Elisha. And David saysPsalm. 91.For he has given his angels charge over you, to keep you in all your ways. And in 34. Psalm, The angel of the Lord encamps around those who fear him, and delivers them.

Since, therefore, it is certain that God has added angels as guardians to his church, let us less fear the dangers of our bodies in undertaking our calling. As I was writing these things, certain examples of our own time came to my mind. And although I could recount many certain and true histories about honest men, worthy of belief, who are still living, nevertheless I will recite a single one about Simon Grynaeus, who to excellent learning and virtue had added true reverence for God, and especially detested the philosophy of those who think they see more than others because they dare either to despise religions or are accustomed to dispute about all opinions in the Pyrrhonian manner on both sides.

IN the assembly of Speyer, which was in the year 1529.

He had wandered to me from the Heidelberg Academy. There, when he had heard Faber, the Bishop of Vienna, defending certain foul errors in a sermon, he followed him as he was leaving the temple, and greeted him reverently. Afterwards he relates that he is moved not by any petulance, but by good zeal, to say certain things. Faber does not decline the conversation. There Grynaeus says that he grieves that a man instructed in doctrine and excelling in authority should openly confirm errors insulting to God, and which it is certain can be refuted by manifest testimonies of ecclesiastical antiquity. Irenaeus writes that Polycarp was accustomed to stop his ears when hearing the portentous errors of fanatical men. With what spirit, do you think, would Polycarp have heard you now, arguing about what a mouse eats, gnawing the consecrated bread? Who would not deplore the darkness of the church? Faber interrupts him as he is about to add more, and asks his name. He conceals nothing, but calmly confesses that he is Grynaeus.

Faber was altogether, as many know, shamefully timid in the meetings of learned men, or rather he was, as was said in the old proverb, No, great, bad, fish, that is, fearful, tall, insidious, and childish. Wherefore, since he feared the learning, eloquence, abundance, and ardor of spirit of Grynaeus, especially in such a cause, he feigns that he has been called by the king, and that there is no leisure now to carry through a disputation concerning the greatest of all matters. He feigns that he is most desirous both of the friendship of Grynaeus and of a longer conversation, and he begins to entreat, both for his own sake and for the sake of the republic, that he return to him tomorrow; he points out the lodging and appoints the hour. Grynaeus, thinking these things were said seriously, promises not unwillingly.

Having therefore left Faber, he comes straight to us. He had scarcely sat down at the table, for it was already the time for dinner, and he had recited a part of the disputation to me and to others, when I, sitting at the table, was suddenly called out from the room. Here, I know not what old man, bearing before him a singular gravity in his countenance, speech, and dress—nor was I ever able to find out who he was—addresses me, and tells me that lictors would soon be present at our lodging, sent by the order of the king, before whom Faber had accused Grynaeus, to lead Grynaeus away to prison. He commands that he depart from the city immediately, and urges that we do not delay. Having thus said farewell, the old man departs.

I return to my companions, I order them to rise, concerning what the old man announced I relate. Soon we lead Grynaeus through the public square, having received him into the middle of the column, and we proceed to the Rhine, where we halted for a little while on the nearer bank, until Grynaeus with his companion was carried across in a small boat to the other bank. Afterwards, having returned to the inn, we understand that the officers had arrived immediately, when we had progressed a small distance from that house.

However, in how great a danger Grynaeus would have been, if he had been dragged off into prison, could easily be estimated from the cruelty of Faber. Wherefore we judged that that cruel plan had been eluded by divine power. And as I cannot affirm who that old man was μηνυτὴς, so great was the haste of the officers that, unless Grynaeus had been protected by angels, he could not have escaped.

But that the matter was managed as I have related is certain, and certain good men still surviving, who were present, know it. Let us therefore give thanks to God, because He adds guardian angels to us, and let us perform the duties of our calling with all the more tranquil minds.

Now I return to the text. At the end again, says the angel, he will fight with the Persian king to be, against the Greeks, and the evil spirits their leaders.

He names the Greeks Javan, or, as they themselves speak, Ionians. Nor is there any doubt that in the prophets the Ionians are everywhere understood to be the Greek nation, which both held the coast in Asia, and had seats in the neighboring islands, and in the part of Europe, which, although the names were often changed, was nevertheless finally called by the common name Greece.

Now Ion or Javan was the son of Japheth, as the catalog in Genesis chapter ten testifies, from whom we can also reason by this conjecture that the Ionians were propagated, because the memory of both names, Japetus and Ion, remained among the Greeks. The poet Euripides imagines Ion to be the great-grandson of Deucalion. But that whole narrative of Euripides speaks of more recent matters than those which can be attributed to the son of Japheth. For to the Greeks, for the most part, the ancient things done in Greece before Pelops are unknown. And I think, as Thucydides signifies, that those first Ionians who crossed into Europe, having wandered for a long time with uncertain seats, did not leave behind certain monuments of their affairs. Thus also the first doctrine concerning God was extinguished among their descendants, and oblivion followed all ancient things.

Furthermore, from the flood of Noah until the Trojan War, there are about a thousand years or a little more. If you consider the history of these, there is no doubt that the church of God remained in the two families of Shem and Japheth, while the father Noah lived, and while they themselves were survivors. Now Noah lived after the flood three hundred and fifty years. Shem indeed lived even after Abraham. It is consistent that Japheth also lived for a long time. Therefore, in the age of these men, the heavenly doctrine still shone among the nations, concerning the promise of the coming liberator, and concerning the mercy of God promised on account of him, likewise concerning good and chaste morals. And perhaps from this doctrine of the fathers there remained little verses, which prophesy concerning the conflagration of the world, concerning eternal life, concerning the judgment, and concerning the appeaser of God, which exist among Lactantius and others.

Afterwards followed a worse age and with the light of heavenly doctrine extinguished, there arose daily new ἐιδωλομανίαι, accepted shameful lusts, which God punished with huge wars, and destructions of cities and nations. These confusions of human affairs even more destroyed the memory of antiquity. And that many great and destructive changes of nations happened within the years five hundred after Japheth, it is credible. Wherefore it is not to be wondered at, that their origin is not sufficiently known to the Greeks.

Moreover, by a singular counsel, God willed that there should exist in the church the first, nay, the perpetual history, up to the coming of the Messiah, so that we might know that this is the first and perpetually consenting revelation, in which God manifested Himself. And a good mind is greatly strengthened in this profession, when it understands that this one is the first and perpetually self-consistent doctrine concerning God. For it is necessary that in the beginning there should have been some assembly, which rightly invoked God.

Since, however, in this first doctrine the series of the fathers has been handed down with certain faith, names often indicate the origins of nations, as from Asshur, the Assyrians, from Canaan, the Canaanites, from Elam, the Elamites, whose name remained in Persia, as even Greek historians testify. Thus the father Ion attributed his name to the Ionian nation, from which, since some Greek nations were propagated, it is evident that by this appellation the Greeks are everywhere signified in the prophets, as above Daniel names Alexander the king Javan, or of the Ionians. Isaiah brings him forth from Kittim. And in the book of Maccabees, Alexander is called king of Kittim. These things agree, for the book of Genesis teaches that Kittim is the son of Javan. Therefore the name Javan is somewhat more general, by which the Greek nations are signified, and Kittim is narrower, from which there is no doubt that the name μακέτ-ρ, was made, which is Macedonian. For the older appellation is μακέτης. It is certain, however, that the Macedonian nation was related to the Greek. The kings were certainly Greeks, descended from Hercules. And Alexander's mother, Olympias, was descended from Molossus, the grandson of Achilles. But that Aeacus, from whom Achilles was descended, was a Greek and held Aegina is certain. Furthermore, Alexander was not only king of the Macedonians, but was also called leader of the Greeks by the common decree of the cities of Greece. And he held a great part, Thessaly, Aetolia, Phocis. Finally, his deeds were performed in the beginning for the most part by the money, machines, and forces of the Greek nations. Wherefore he himself also wrote most modestly on the Trophy, ἀλἐξανδρος καὶ ἕλληνες πλὴν λακεδαιμονίων.. And let it be enough to have said these things concerning the name Javan or the Ionians.

That can be asked in this narration of the angel, concerning which movements of Ionia it is spoken, excited around the final times of Cyrus, or while was already reigning. With Cambyses absent from his father. The angel briefly indicates that the Ionian nation is being incited by evil spirits to stir up seditions. For the prophets briefly touch upon the foreign affairs of other kingdoms. Nor can I affirm of which movements these things are said. But it is consistent that the remnants of the war which Harpagus waged in Ionia, when he had been left there by Cyrus, had great difficulties.

The Ionian nation, which had flourished most of all in talents and wealth, was twice subdued while Cyrus was reigning. For it had rebelled when Cyrus was making for Babylon. And that there were long sieges of cities, the example of Phocaea shows, which was defended with such persistence against Harpagus that at length the entire multitude of citizens boarded ships during the very siege, and sought other seats far away. These exiles founded the city of Massilia at the mouth of the Rhone, and transported to that shore of Gaul Greek letters and a discipline full of humanity. And it can be judged from the humanity of that city how civilly the Ionian nation had lived before in Asia.

But luxury without measure had grown and had brought forth lusts and other vices. Therefore there followed huge calamities and the destruction of cities. For although there was remarkable clemency and goodness in Cyrus, yet because God establishes and changes empires, so that he may punish εἰδωλομανίας, tyrannies and lusts, all changes have some degree of cruelty; therefore, Ionia, having been subdued by great disasters, was at last with difficulty reduced to servitude, which it bore so impatiently that it stirred up frequent seditions against the Persian leaders. And the commands of the leaders were arrogant and cruel. Not even the neighboring satraps themselves were in agreement among themselves for long. For Oraetes, who ruled Ionia after Harpagus, killed his neighbor Mitrobates and his son during the reign of Cambyses. These examples show sufficiently that the province was restless. There were therefore also the struggles of the angels, who were driving away the evil spirits, the instigators of seditions and discords.

Latin:

CAPUT X.

Anno tertio regni Cyri regis Persarum, verbum revelatum est Danieli, cognomento Baltassar, & verbum verum, & fortitudo magna: intellexitque sermonem, intelligentia enim est opus in visione. In diebus illis ego Daniel lugebam trium hebdomadarum diebus, panem desiderabilem non comedi, & caro & vinum non introierunt in os meum, sed neque unguento unctus sum, donec complerentur trium hebdomadarum dies. Die autem vicesima & quarta mensis primi, eram iuxta fluvium magnum, qui est Tygris. Et levavi oculos meos, & vidi: & ecce, vir unus vestitus lineis, & renes eius accincti auro obrizo: & corpus eius quasi chrysolithus, & facies eius velut species fulguris, & oculi eius ut lampas ardens: & brachia eius, & quae deorsum sunt usque ad pedes, quasi species aeris candentis: & vox sermonum eius ut vox multitudinis. Vidi autem ego Daniel solus visionem, porrò viri qui erant mecum, non viderunt, sed terror nimius irruit super eos, & fugerunt in absconditum. Ego autem relictus solus, vidi visionem grandem hanc: & non remansit in me fortitudo, sed & species mea immutata est in me,& emarcui,nec habui quic quam virium.Et audivi vocem sermonum eius,& audiens, iacebam consternatus super faciem meam, & vultus meus haerebat terrae. Et ecce, manus tetigit me, & erexit me super genua mea, & super articulos manuum mearum. Et dixit ad me: Daniel, vir desideriorum, intellige verba quae ego loquor ad te, & sta in gradu tuo: nunc enim sum missus ad te, Cumque dixisset mihi sermonem istum, steti tremens. Et ait ad me: Noli metuere Daniel: quia ex die primoquo posuisti cor tuum ad intelligendum, ut te affligeres in conspectu dei tui, exaudita sunt verba tua, & ego veni propter sermones tuos. Princeps autem regni Persarum restitit mihi viginti & uno diebus: & ecce, Michael, unus de principibus primis, venit in adiutorium meum, & ego remansi ibi iuxta regem Persarum.
