First, what are Bishoprics everywhere, if not base and bitter quaestorships? What duties do these perform, who possess the most ample revenues of the churches?

Then look at the courts of Kings and princes. What is the study of military affairs? What are they doing against the Turkish tyranny, which has already oppressed several kingdoms of Europe, and threatens the rest not only servitude, but devastation? How do they govern discipline, how do they govern judgments at home? Nay, what do they care for these things? There is one care: of squandering and seeking money. And there is almost an infinite βουλιμία, of all kings and princes, which because the miserable common people will not be able to endure for much longer, there is no doubt that fatal changes of empires are impending. For histories show that immediately after these βουλιμίας the form of the republic has always been changed.

I recite these things so that good men may recognize that these calamities of the old age of the world have been divinely predicted, both so that warned pious governors may strive to mitigate them, and so that others may understand the punishments of sins, and learn to bear them with skill, and pray to God that He may give just and salutary empires to the church, that He may give στρατηγοὺς strong and happy, that He may give political rulers, wise and just, who may embrace their own with fatherly love. For such the angel opposes to the tax-collectors, whom he describes here. Because salutary princes ought both to drive away robberies abroad, and to govern judgments and discipline at home, and to consult for the utilities of the people, as fathers of families are able to educate children, and to instruct them in honest arts, so that true may be preserved on earth. the doctrine concerning God and concerning his Son Jesus Christ, and let other pursuits of virtue be cultivated, which are abandoned, when those βουλιμία resources of private individuals are exhausted.

Furthermore, this also was the act of a plunderer, that this Seleucus ordered the temple in the city of Jerusalem to be despoiled, and for the attempting of that matter he had Jews as instigators, as is narrated in the second book of Maccabees, capite tertio. Thus now, who they are that enjoy the wealth of the churches, it is by no means obscure. Perpetual Bacchanalia are in the colleges of the canons. Meanwhile, pious pastors of churches with their families, and poor scholars, waste away with hunger; the studies of letters, and especially of sacred ones, are abandoned, because no one helps the poor who were about to dedicate their studies to the churches.

When those highest quaestors, bishops and princes, neglect these things, or perhaps even laugh at them, let them not think that Christ will dissemble, when in that terrible judgment he will accuse them concerning their neglected office, and will say, I was hungry, and you did not feed me. Go therefore, you cursed, into eternal fire. But let us pray to the Son of the eternal God, the liberator and guardian of his church, that he himself may protect pious studies, and supply sustenance to those who are learning.

Just as in the Mosaic law, when a certain region was not given to the tribe of Levi to be possessed, as it was to the others, God said that He Himself would be the inheritance, or estate, of that tribe, so now let the estate and τροφεύς of pious teachers and scholars be, and let us not doubt that it will be.

ANTIOCHUS Epiphanes, brother of Seleucus, having learned of his father's death in Rome, departed from there secretly, so that he might snatch the kingdom from his slothful brother. Now this Epiphanes was endowed with a cunning, sharp, and savage nature, who soon, after he had begun to reign, laid snares for the Egyptians. For when Ptolemy Epiphanes had died, he came into Egypt, as if he were to be the guardian of his young kinsman. For Cleopatra, the mother of the ward, was the sister of Antiochus Epiphanes. Therefore, he placed garrisons in the cities of Egypt under the name of guardianship, while in reality he was doing this so that, having occupied them now under an honorable pretext, he might afterwards retain them. Returning from there, he came to Jerusalem, invited by Jason, to whom he had given the high priesthood against the Jewish laws. In this first journey, Antiochus despoiled the temple.

After two years, Antiochus again led an army into Egypt, because the Egyptian king Ptolemy Philometor had now grown up, and having learned by the treachery of his uncle, he had expelled the Syrian garrisons from some of his cities, and had sought aid from the Romans against Antiochus. Therefore, at that time, the legate Popilius came, who ordered Antiochus to abstain from Egypt. And when Antiochus answered ambiguously, having drawn a circle in the dust around him, he ordered him to answer clearly before he should step out of that circle. There Antiochus, terrified, promises peace, and departs raging.

But because he had made great expenses in that expedition, he came needy to Jerusalem, there he plunders the wealth of the rich, profanes the temple, with the statue of Jupiter Olympius placed in it, he proposes cruel edicts concerning the abolishing of the whole religion, delivered by the voice of God through Abraham, Moses, and the prophets. And he defends these edicts, with garrisons added, which exercised great cruelty upon the pious men who were unwilling to cast away the profession of the divine law.

On account of this great calamity, this prophecy was published among Daniel, so that the pious might be forewarned and strengthened, lest they should fall away from God. The time is also added, so that the pious might know that this calamity would not be long-lasting, namely seven years, and two months. For within this space, the temple was profaned by Antiochus, and afterwards recovered by Judas Maccabeus, and later the Syrian garrisons were expelled from the towns of Judea.

Furthermore, Antiochus Epiphanes, because he had received notable defeats, not only in Judea, but also in Persia, died from grief of mind. Others write that he was thrown from his chariot and struck the ground so hard that a lethal disease arose from it, by which he is said to have died, when he had held the kingdom for twelve years. What his character was like, I have mentioned above.

AMONG Daniel there is no mention of the following kings, because it is signified that shortly after, the Messiah would come. For there is a short time from Judas Maccabeus to the birth of Christ, namely, one hundred and sixty-six years. And immediately after Antiochus, Syria was torn apart by continuous domestic discords, until the Romans occupied it, so that it could not be called one kingdom. Just as such fatal discords of kingdoms almost always arise when the end is not far off, as this age has seen in the sad example of Pannonia.

I will add, however, briefly the remaining kings of Syria, So that the punishments may be considered, about which the first commandment preaches. There were among the descendants of Antiochus, after the profanation of the temple, continuous acts of parricide. Demetrius, who had been a hostage in Rome together with him, succeeded his brother Antiochus Epiphanes. Coming into Syria, he soon ordered the son of his brother Antiochus, who was already reigning, to be killed, Antiochus εὐπάτορα. Some time later, Demetrius pays the penalty for his parricide. For he is killed by a certain seditious Alexander, who invaded the kingdom, aided by the Egyptians.

Demetrius had two sons, Demetrius the younger, and Antiochus Sidetes. This younger Demetrius killed Alexander. Afterwards, Tryphon expelled Demetrius from the kingdom. Antiochus Sidetes was killed by the Parthians.

These brothers left children. Demetrius had a son Antiochus γρυπός. Sidetes had a son Antiochus Cyzicenus. These, fighting over the kingdom, were both killed. Afterwards, the neighboring king Tigranes invaded Syria, from whom Pompey snatched it. In this way, through domestic seditions and acts of parricide, the descendants of the impious kings paid the penalty, until at length the whole family was extinguished, and the royal name was removed, and Syria was occupied by the Romans.

KINGS OF EGYPT.

Ptolemy son of Lagus. Ptolemy Philadelphus. Ptolemy Euergetes. Ptolemy Philopator. Ptolemy Epiphanes. Ptolemy Philometor, to whom succeeded his brother Ptolemy Φύσκων, that is, the pot-bellied. Ptolemy λάθουρος. Ptolemy αὐλητὴς. Cleopatra of Antony.

These nomenclatures are handed down in this order by Strabo, who, since he himself traveled through Egypt, describes it more diligently than neighboring places.

PTOLEMY son of Lagus, who was among the principal leaders of Alexander, and led armies in India, where he also received blows from a weapon, which had been infected with poison, obtained Egypt in the distribution of the provinces, which when he had held for three years, Perdiccas, who was aspiring to the Monarchy, brought war upon him. But Ptolemy, Perdiccas having been killed, retained Egypt, and afterwards added Cyrenaica and a great part of Syria. He left sons Philadelphus and

κεραυνόν. and many others, whom Pausanias recounts.

Polybius writes that Ptolemy, son of Lagus, died in the one hundred and twenty-fourth Olympiad, that is, after the death of Alexander, when nearly forty years had passed, in which Olympiad also Lysimachus, and Seleucus, and κεραυνός perished.

PTOLEMAEUS Philadelphus, preferred to his brother older by birth, was named king by his father, and was commended to the princes and cities. He succeeded his father, therefore, and recovered Cyrenaica. Then, having obtained leisure, since he knew that the doctrine concerning celestial things had flourished in Egypt, and those arts which are joined to that doctrine, he both restored old studies, and increased them, and instructed those desirous of learning with a most copious library.

And when he had heard that the Jews preached the antiquity and doctrine of their nation, he summoned interpreters from there, who might translate the books of Moses and of other prophets into the Greek language. This old translation of the prophetic books into the Greek speech is called the work of the seventy interpreters. By this occasion it is credible it is that some in Egypt, and elsewhere, then embraced the true religion, when both books were read, and learned men from the Jews, who were living among the nations, could freely teach others. For the voice of the true church of God is never raised rashly and in vain. Moreover, Philadelphus bestowed great benefits upon the Jews here. For he granted immunity to the whole people, and gave liberty to those who were serving in Egypt.

The error of many is to be noted here, who transfer the history of the brother κεραυνοῦ to this Philadelphus. For κεραυνούς, the brother born of another mother, from Eurydice the daughter of Antipater, having left Egypt, set out for Macedonia, and having oppressed Seleucus, held Macedonia for some time, and was incestuous and impious toward his sister Arsinoe, whom he took as his wife after having deceived her by fraud, and afterwards killed her, having killed together with her the sons of Lysimachus, in the lap of their mother Arsinoe, then he paid the penalty for such great crimes, being killed by the army of Brennus. This history is copiously recounted by Pausanias.

But Philadelphus grew old in the Egyptian kingdom, in tranquility and the company of learned men, by which he soothed the pains of his joints, which he is said to have endured He is said to be sufficiently sharp. His wealth and munificence are celebrated by Theocritus, who lived there with Callimachus, Apollonius, and other poets.

PTOLEMY Euergetes waged a huge war against Syria, in order to avenge the murder of his sister Berenice, who had been married to the king of Syria, Antiochus Theos, and was afterwards killed by her stepchildren. However, having occupied many cities in Syria, Euergetes did not reluctantly grant peace to Seleucus Callinicus, who was seeking it, for he was being recalled to Egypt by domestic seditions. Daniel speaks of this Euergetes, saying, And a plant shall stand in the shoot of his roots, namely Ptolemy Euergetes, and he shall come with an army against the king of the North, that is, against Seleucus Callinicus, and he shall prevail.

At this time Judea, having been conquered by Euergetes, was forced to pay tribute, although the state of Judea was still tolerable. For when the tribute was not paid, Euergetes was easily appeased by Joseph the leader, whose mention is made in Luke chapter three, where Joseph is named as the father of John Hyrcanus, the last leader from the lineage of David.

PTOLEMY Φιλοπάτωρ was so called ironically, since his father, mother, and brother he killed. His sloth gave Antiochus the Great hope of recovering the cities in Syria which Evergetes had taken from Seleucus. Wherefore Antiochus the Great, a young man inflamed with a desire for military glory, wages war against this Egyptian king, and recovers many cities. At length, however, Philopator meets him, and since both had great forces, he defeated Antiochus. And on account of his sloth and luxury, he immediately returned into Egypt. Daniel makes mention of this victory, saying, And the king of the South, namely Ptolemy Philopator, shall be provoked, and shall fight against the king of the North, namely, Antiochus the Great.

The Jews, however, were at first joined to Antiochus; afterwards, fortune having changed, they received the Egyptian victor with hospitality and appeased him with money given. But when Ptolemy Philopator, intending to see the temple, had entered into the vestibule, the priests prayed him earnestly not to proceed, nor to penetrate into the inner sanctuary, into which it was not lawful for anyone to enter except the high priest. Although the proud king denies that he will obey the pious warnings, yet God defends the temple, and soon represses the king, struck with sudden terror, in such a way that he was carried out of the temple half-dead. Wherefore, being angry at the Jews, when he had returned to Alexandria, he proposed cruel edicts, he ordered the Jews to be seized into chains, and he threw many to be trampled by elephants in the spectacles.

Meanwhile at home, having killed his wife, he defiled himself with unspeakable lusts. And the court was ruled, not by him or the princes, but by the most base and most flagitious companions of his lusts. Such madness is never long-lasting, as is said concerning sins crying out to heaven. Wherefore after the victory, of which it has been spoken, he was not long a survivor.

PTOLEMY Epiphanes, almost an infant, succeeded his father. But when Antiochus the Great thought that the Egyptians would defend Syria more sluggishly in this infancy of the king, he moved war again. Wherefore the Egyptians, distrusting their own forces, sent legates to Rome, who might ask that the Roman senate should undertake the guardianship of the Egyptian king. Meanwhile Antiochus recovered most of the cities of lower Syria and Phoenicia, and he routed the leader Scopas, sent by the Egyptians to protect that coast. Here the Jews were gravely afflicted, first by Scopas, afterwards by the victor Antiochus.

Then when the Roman legates had arrived, they ordered Antiochus to restore the occupied cities, and they declared war upon him unless he complied. In order to elude this embassy with artifice, and not bring the Roman armies into Syria, he made peace with the Egyptian, having given him his daughter Cleopatra. But being angry at the Romans, he made a treaty with Philip of Macedon, and crossed into Greece, and waged war with the Romans.

These things Daniel predicts in order, describing two expeditions of Antiochus the Great against the Egyptians. The king of the North, he says, that is, Antiochus the Great, previously defeated by Philopator, will return, will repair the war, and will occupy many cities. Then he will turn himself to the islands, that is, he will sail into Greece, so that he may wage war against the Romans.

PTOLEMAEUS Philometor was born from the daughter of Antiochus the Great, and when his age, upon the death of his father, was not yet suitable for government, Antiochus Epiphanes, his uncle, came into Egypt, pretending that he would be the guardian of his nephew, so that under that pretext he might impose garrisons upon the cities of Egypt, and despoil the ward of his kingdom. But some time after the departure of Antiochus, aid from the senate The mother of Ptolemy sought the Roman, and with the garrisons of Antiochus driven out, she recovered the cities.

Antiochus therefore returned in anger, intending to crush his nephew either by deceit under the pretense of friendship, or by arms. But the Romans sent the legate Popilius, who ordered Antiochus to abstain from Egypt. Thus frustrated in his hope, and in need, Antiochus returned to Syria, as was narrated above. This history is recited at length in the prophecy of the angel. For because of the disasters which the Jewish people received in these journeys of Antiochus, the angel commemorates this series of events. And therefore he omits the other kings.

But because the following history contains illustrious examples of divine judgment, I shall briefly add it. Antiochus Epiphanes had invaded Egypt, as has been said, and had laid snares under the pretext of the guardianship of his nephew's life. But these unjust attempts were not only vain, but were also punished by a singular example. For after Antiochus had died, and his son and brother had been killed, when there was a war between the seditious Alexander and the younger Demetrius struggling for the kingdom of Syria, this Ptolemy Philometor moved war against Alexander, king of Syria, and defeated him in battle he conquered. Wherefore the Antiochenes received Ptolemy, and crowned him with a double crown, one of Syria, the other of Egypt.

As therefore Antiochus had plotted against the life and kingdom of the other, so his descendants were killed by dire parricides, and he himself, against whom the plots had been laid, obtained the kingdom of the plotter. This is a just punishment, as is said in the verse, ἔικε πάθοι ἃ ἔρεξε, δίκη ἴθεια γένοιτο.

But Ptolemy, fearing the Romans, yielded the kingdom of Syria, and was the author that the younger Demetrius should be summoned. To him Ptolemy gave his daughter, snatched away from Alexander, his former son-in-law. They say, however, that Ptolemy received a wound in battle, and was with difficulty defended and saved, but that Alexander, having lost his army, came fleeing to the king of the Arabs. By him, when he had been killed, his head was sent to Ptolemy, who not long after died from his wound. This was the end of both kings, father-in-law and son-in-law, who contended among themselves with impious arms.

PTOLEMY Physcon, so called from the stuffing of his belly, which was shamefully inflated. For φύσκη signifies a thicker intestine, the use of which is in making sausages. Now this Physcon was the brother of Philometor, of whom it has already been spoken. And although in the Chronicle of Eusebius a certain Euergetes is inserted between Philometor and Physcon, nevertheless Strabo testifies that he is the same as Physcon, who was ironically named εὐεργέτης, for he surpassed all in cruelty and obscenity. He married his sister, who had previously been married to his elder brother, and on the wedding day he killed the boy, his brother's son, in the mother's embrace. Some time later, he married the daughter born to his wife from his brother, having repudiated the mother. Moreover, he had a little son born from his former wife and sister. This one he killed and dismembered, and sent to the mother on her birthday during the banquet. Nor was he milder toward the citizens, for at Alexandria he caused almost a desolation by daily slaughters. Wherefore the Romans sent legates to Alexandria, among whom were both Scipio the Younger and Polybius the historian, so that by the authority of the senate they might command him to cease from unjustly attacking his own people, because the senate would not defend him, nor restore him to the kingdom, if he were cast out on account of his cruelty. And the legates, having seen the ridiculous deformity of the king, not only execrated his most foul morals, but also the monstrous appearance of his body. Shortly Afterwards, the tyrant Physcon paid the penalty for his crimes, having been cast out of the kingdom by the Alexandrians.

Let such histories admonish readers, not only concerning the judgment of God, who testifies that He punishes atrocious crimes in this life with manifest punishments, but also concerning the common misery of the human race, and the fury of the devil, who, out of hatred for God and contempt for the human race, deforms the images of God with such great outrages, and indeed in the highest place, in empires where virtue ought to excel, so that the whole nature of things abhors those horrendous spectacles of the devil. Wherefore let us pray to the Son of God, that He may destroy the works of the devil who is raging, both in the whole human race, and truly especially in the highest orders, just as it is written, that the Son of God appeared, that He might destroy the works of the devil.

PTOLEMAEUS λάθουρος, for so he is named by Strabo, I think for the reason that, as an exile, he secretly attempted a return. For his mother, moved by sedition, expelled this Ptolemy from Egypt, and handed over the kingdom to her younger son Alexander, whom she hoped would be more obedient to her. However, he is written to have done these things as an exile. When the Jews saw that they had obtained an opportunity of occupying neighboring cities, on account of domestic discords, having arisen both in Egypt and in Syria, they moved war against their neighbors. These summoned Ptolemy from Cyprus, who, hoping that he would return to Egypt through that opportunity, brought moderate forces into Phoenicia, and immediately made an incursion into Judea, and captured a certain town of Galilee on the Sabbath. Afterwards, he clashed with the Jews in a very fierce battle at the Jordan. And when a great multitude of Jews had been slaughtered in the battle line, the victor Ptolemy ravaged the villages in the borders of Judea, and ordered the women and infants to be killed, and their limbs to be cut up and cooked, and he forced the Jews to eat them boiled and cooked.

Such cruelty in victory did not have lasting successes. For the Jews sought aid against him from Ptolemy's mother, who sent an army there the more quickly, so that she might keep her son away from Egypt. When, therefore, Ptolemy attempted a return in vain, and could not protect the cities occupied in Phoenicia, he fled back to Cyprus. Some time later, Alexander, who was reigning in Egypt, killed his mother. Therefore, with Alexander expelled, this Ptolemy was recalled to Egypt λάθουρος, whom new seditions there they excepted, perhaps because some favored the brother. It is said here that Ptolemy utterly destroyed the greatest and most celebrated city, Egyptian Thebes.

PTOLEMY αὐλητὴς, that is, the flute-player, so called because he was accustomed to compete publicly in royal attire on the flutes with artists, having been expelled from the kingdom on account of his sloth and cruelty, he came to Rome, where Pompey persuaded him to be restored. And although contentions were drawn out for a long time regarding that matter, nevertheless he was finally restored by Gabinius, who killed Archelaus, who had reigned in the meantime. This αὐλητὴς left a son, Ptolemy Dionysius, who as a youth ordered Pompey, fleeing into Egypt from the Pharsalian battle, to be killed. Afterwards, when he had laid snares for Julius Caesar, he was killed by Caesar, who gave the kingdom to his sister Cleopatra. Cleopatra, at the end, when Augustus had come into Egypt, inflicted death upon herself.

This is the series of Egyptian kings, in which not only is this to be deplored, that descendants born from men most excellent in virtue degenerate so shamefully, but that also, that the devil rages so insolently in these who hold empires. But these are the punishments εἰδωλομανιῶν, as it is written to theRomans. 1.However, there was nowhere more monstrous idol-mania than in Egypt and in Syria.

In this commemoration, this also must be observed, that we see the church of God, whose seat was then between those two kingdoms, is here small, and harshly afflicted, and is not defended by human aid or human protections, but is miraculously ruled and preserved by God. This is an image of the final old age of the world, in which the true church of God will be in servitude, oppressed by manifold tyranny. But after I have reviewed the histories of the kings in order, I will briefly adapt the prophecy of the angel to them.

TEXT.

And the king of the South shall be strengthened, who is from his leaders, that is, Ptolemy, who was one of the leaders of Alexander, will occupy Egypt, which is situated to the south of Judea. And another shall be strengthened from his region, and his dominion shall be great, that is, Seleucus from his region will hold Syria and Babylonia, who, after Antigonus was defeated, when he held Asia, excelled all others in power.

And in the end of days they shall be joined together, that is, after some time they shall be bound between themselves by treaties.

and the affinities of the Egyptian and Syrian Kings. And the daughter of the King of the South, Berenice, daughter of Ptolemy Philadelphus, King of Egypt, shall come to the King of the North, that is, she shall marry Antiochus Theos, King of Syria, so that there may be friendship, that is, so that by this affinity between the kings there may be peace, and the Egyptians may retain the cities in Phoenicia occupied by the first Ptolemy.

And she shall not obtain the strength of the arm, and his seed shall not stand, and she shall be delivered up, and her attendants, and her child, and he who had strengthened her, that is, Berenice shall not retain the kingdom of Syria, nor her son, born of Antiochus Theos. But the step-sons Seleucus Callinicus, and Antiochus ἱέραξ shall kill the step-mother, and her son. But the father who had strengthened the Egyptian, that is, who had made her a partner of the kingdom, Antiochus Theos, shall be killed by poison by his repudiated wife Laodice.

Latin:

DE SUCCESSORIBUS Alexandri.

CUM post Alexandri mortem teneret regium nomen frater Aridaeus, apud quem summus gubernator erat perdiccas, provinciae inter duces distributae sunt, ita, ut omnes Aridaeo regi & Perdiccae parere iuberentur. Ambit igitur Perdiccas, quia autoritate plus quàm caeteri valebat, coniugium sororis Alexandri Cleopatrae. Et ut tolleret è medio eos, quos obsistere suis consiliis arbitrabatur, iubet interfici Meleagrum, struit insidias Antigono, qui cum fugisset ad Antipatrum in Macedoniam, missus est Eumenes, ut bellum inferret Antipatro & Antigno, cum quibus & Ptolemaeus se coniunxerat. Ducit igitur exercitum Perdiccas in Aegyptum, ut Ptolemaeum inde expelleret. Sed desertus ab exercitu suo, Perdiccas interficitur à suis equitibus, tertio anno post Alexandri mortem. Ita primus autor seditionis dedit poenas, quae latè udgatae sunt. Mox enim & frater Perdiccae Alcetas interfectus est, & eius soror.

Alibi verò, cum Eumenes dimicasset cum Antipatri exercitu, perierunt Craterus & Neoptolemus. Haec fuerunt initia ingentium motuum, tot enim interfectis ducibus, iam odia exarserant, & nova foedera fiebant, & se singuli ad nova certamina praeparabant. Duo autem erant omnium acerrimi, Antigonus & Eumenes. Hi inter se dimicantes, religuos reliquos in partes traxerunt. Tandem Antigonus victor cepit Eumenem proditione, deditum à militibus, eumque interfecit.

Fuerat autem Eumenes scriba apud Philippum & Alexandrum, et in his tumultibus ostendit se non suae utilitati servire, sed propugnare baeredes, Alexandri matrem, sororem, coniugem & filios. Et quia multum valet in conciliandis & retinendis voluntatibus mediocrium hominum, species offitii, ac praesertim pietatis erga pupillos, hoc titulo veteranos Macedonas sibi adiunxit & devinxit.

Sed secuta sunt longe maiora & difficiliora bella. Mortuo Aridaeo, singuli duces sibi vendt care provincias opportunas conabantur. Cassander filius Antipatri, ut retineret Macedoniam, interfecit duos filios Alexandri magni, ut suprà narravi. Asiam tenebat Antigonus cum filio Demetrio, Ptolemaeus Aegyptum, Seleucus Syriam et vicinas provincias ad Perfidem usque. Caussam verò novi belli inter caeteros Cassandri furiae praebuerunt, qui ut scelerum poenas daret, bellum movit adversus Antigonum, erepturus ei quasdam in Asia provincias. Fecerat autem societatem cum Lysimacho, Ptolemaeo & Seleuco, qui crescentem Antigoni potentiam metuebant.

Etsi Antigonus videbat, quanta belli moles impenderet, tamen non frangebatur animo. Et primi successus adversus Cassandrum eum confirmaverunt. Hunc iudicavit hostem Antigonus, propter interfectam Olympiada, & caegit tradere urbes & exercitus in Asia.

Postea Seleucum & Ptolemaeum adortus est. Id fuit longum & difficile bellum. Demetrius Antigoni filius, primum à Ptolemaeo ad Tyrum acie victus est, ubi cum tabernaculum Demetrii instructum apparatu regio, cepisset Ptolemaeus, integrum ei adparatum unà cum captivis restituit, significans se de gloria, non de opibus certare.

Aliquanto pòst Demetrius, cum Ptolemaei ducem Cillen, et octo millia militum subitò oppressisset, ut sine certamine fieret deditio, memor accepti beneficii, cumulate reddidit gratiam. Nam et ducem incolumem, & exercitum Ptolemaeo remisit, iussum Syria excedere. Ita inter se, non solum virtute in acie, sed etiam humanitate & moderatione, principes illi certabant.

Ad extremum, de summa rei dimicatum est ab omnibus regibus, non procul ab Epheso. Erant in alteris castris Seleucus, filius Antiochus, Lysimachus, & Ptolemaei auxilia. In alteris castris erant Antigonus & filius Demetrius. Nocte ea quae praetessit pugnam, visus est per somnium Alexander magnus Antigono dicere, se iam ad hostes transire. Eo somnio eventum sequentis pugnae, Antigonus significari arbitratus, filium successorem dixit coram exercitu. Postea in praelio, dum Antiochum Seleuci filium Antigonus effusius insequitur, circumventus ab equitatu Seleuci, interficitur, cum iam octogesimum annum attigisset. Dissipato exercitu, filius Demetrius fugit Athenas, Et aliquanto post, bellum instauravit, sed captus à Seleuco, non diu superstes fuit patri.

IAM restabant ex ducibus Alexandri duo tantum, qui hactenus socii fuerant, Seleucus & Lysimachus tenens Thraciam & Macedoniae partem. Hic Lysimachus, cum satelles esset Alexandri, cum Callistheni fuisset familiarior, leoni obiectus est. Sed cum interfecto leone mansisset incolumis, postea Alexandro propter virtutem carior fuit.

Ut autem reliquam historiam Lysimachi omittam, hoc tantum adiiciam. Strabo scribit bellum gerentem cum vicinis Getis, in acie captum esse, & ductum in Geticam, ubi rex Geticus, cum ostendisset quotidianam vitae & gentis consuetudinem, quae durissime victitans, &

robore praestans, facile caeteris nationibus in re militari antecellebat, adhortatus est Lysimachum, ut tales vicinos mallet amicos, quàm hostes habere & habiturum amicos, si ipsis arma non inferret. Deinde honorificè donatum, dimisit, nihil pactus nisi pacem. Pausanias filium narrat captum esse, sed uter fuit, humanitas laude digna est, & significat in ea gente, quam quidem Gotticam fuisse constat, virtutis intellectum & studium fuisse.

Sed hic quoque Lysimachus libidine virtutes suas deformavit, sorores duas duxit, & ex utraque liberos suscepit, Sed alterae alterius filium interfecit. Postea mater profuga, latebras quaesivit apud cognatos reges.

Hinc caussa belli orta est, inter Seleucum et Lysimachum quam recitat Pausanias. Periit igitur Lysimachus victus à Seleuco, qui tenens iam maximam Monarchiae partem, solus ex ducibus Alexandri superest. Itaque tantis elatus successibus, sperat se etiam Macedoniae & Graeciae imperio potiturum esse. Cumque bellum transfert in Europam, septimo mense post Lysimachi mortem, interficitur à Ptolemaeoκεραυνῷ,qui cum esset frater Ptolemaei Philadel phi, regnantis iam in Aegypto, navigârat in Europam, ut Macedoniam occuparet. Nec multo post hicκεραυνὸςà Brenno duce Celtico, vastante Macedoniam, in acie interfectus est.

PENE defatigatus sum, recitanda horum gladiatorum laniena, in qua si quis humano iudicio confusionem aspiciat, fortuitas existimet esse regnorum constitutiones et mutationes. At pii aliter iudicent. Sciant à deo puniri tum principum tum populorum scelera, & tamen postea restitui politicum or dinent, aliàs felicius, aliàs tristius.

TANDEM verò post tot ingentia bella, deus haec regna ita partitus est. Ptolemaei posteritas retinuit Aegyptum, usque ad Iulii Caesaris tempora. Seleuci filius Antiochus possedit Asiam & Syriam. Sed posteri Asiam amiserunt, occupatam partim ab Eumene Bithynio, partim à Galatis. De Macedoniae possessione diutius certatum est. Sed ad extremum posteritas Demetrii, qui fuit Antigoni filius, captus à Seleuco, ut dixi, tenuit Macedoniam, donec eam Romani occupârunt. Cassandri & Lysimachi filii adolescentes, teterrimis parricidiis perierunt.

Ptolemaeusκεραυνὸς,ducta sorore Arsinae & deinde interfecta, invaserat & ipse Macedoniam. Sed à Brenno interfectus, poenas incestae consuetudinis cum sorore, & parricidiorum dedit.

Postea Antigonus filius Demetrii moderatissimus rex, natus laudatissima matre Phila, filia Antipatri, Macedonia potitus est, quam etsi aliquoties amisit, tamen postea recuperavit. Expulit ex Graecia Gallos, postea gessit bella cum Pyrrho. Cumque interfecti Pyrrhi caput ad eum allatum esset, & filius Helenus deditus, hunc in regnum paternum remisit, eique patris caput et ossa dedit, regio more condenda. Matrem Philen praedicat his verbis Diodorus Siculus,αὐτὴ δὲ ἡ γυνὴ συνέσει ἐδόκει διενηνοχέναι, τούς τε γὰρ ταραχώδεις ἐν τῷ στρατοπέδῳ κατέπαυσεν, τάς τε ἀδελφὰς καὶ θυγατέρας τῶν ἀπόρων ἐξεδίδου ἴδίοις δαπανήμασι, πολλοὺς καὶ τῶν διαβολαὶς περιπιπτόντων μὴ δικαίως, ἀπηλλαξε κινδύνων,,&c.

Satis longum catalogum regum texuimus, in quorum historia utile est cuiusque exitum ad mores conferre, ut de iudicio dei admoniti, diligentius mores nostros regamus.

Porrò Daniel omissis caeteris regnis, iam vaticinatur tantum de duobus, de Syriaco & Aegyptio, quia Iudaea in medio horum sita, duriter quassata est, annos fere trecentos. Aliis regnis interim nihil fuit negocii cum Iudaea. Quare caeteris praeteritis, horum duorum praecipuos motus praedicit. Et quia vaticinium pene est historia aliquot seculorum, prodest addere catalogos regum Syriacorum & Aegyptiorum, ut narratio aptè ad regum nomina accommodari possit.

REGES SYRIAE.

Seleucus.Antiochusσωτὴρ.Antiochusθεός.Seleucusκαλλίνικος,& frater Antiochusἱέραξ.

Antiochus magnus, natus patre Seleuco, fratrem habuit Seleucum, maiorem natu, qui tamen statim, postquam regnare coepisset, interfectus est.

Seleucusφιλοπάτωρ,& fratres Antiochus Epiphanes & Demetrius.

Antiochusεὐπάτωρnatus patre Epiphane.

Demetrius iunior & frater Antiochus filii Demetrii.

Antiochusγρυπὸςfilius Demetrii iunioris.Antiochus Cyricenus filius Sedetis.Extincta Seleuci posteritate, non multo pòst Romani Syriam occupaverunt.

SELEUCUS natus est in Macedonia mediocri loco. Patrem habuit quendam ex Philippi ducibus. Aiunt Seleuco & eius posteris femur nativa anchora insignitum fuisse. Cum autem post mortem Perdiccae praefectus esset Babyloniae, orta dissensione inter Antigonum & caeteros, adiunxit partem Syriae, camque magnis certaminibus adversus Antigonum defendit, quem tandem ingenti praelio superavit, postea & filium Antigoni Demetrium cepit. Deinde acie victum, Lysimachum interfecit. Tandem circumventus insidiis, à Prolemaeoκεραυνῶfratre Philadelphi interfectus est.

Reliquit filiis amplissimum regnum, Babylonem, Persidem, Medos, Bactra, Parthiam, Asiam & Syriae partem. Tenebat enim partem Ptolemaeus, qui fuerat socius contra Antigonum.

Iosephus scribit fuisse beneficum erga Iudaeos, quos permisit uti propriis legibus in civitatibus suis. Laudat & Pausanias eius iustitiam & clementiam.

Antiochusσωτὴρinterfuit praelio patris adversus Antigonum, nec diu post patrem regnavit.

Antiochusθεὸςhabuit initio coniugem Laodicen, ex qua suscepit duos filios, Seleucum Callinicum, et Antiochum cui fuit cognomenἱέραξ.

Postea Ptolemaeus Philadelphus, ut per eam occasionem recuperaret civitates in Syria amissas, dedit Antiochoθεῷfiliam Berenicen. Post has alteras nuptias, non diu fuit superstes maritus. Et hoc connubium praebuit occasionem novis motibus & discordiis regum. Laodice repudiata, hortata est filios, ne sibi regnum cripi paterentur. Quare Seleucus maior natu, cinxit ob sidione urbem, in qua erat Berenice cum parvo filio. Cumque ei persuasisset, ut deditionem faceret, deditam interfecit cum filio. Haec fuerunt auspitia regni Seleuci, qui postea parricidii poenas dedit.

Seleucus Callinicus, postquam novercam interfecit, hoc scelere sibi ingens bellum accersivit, Nam Ptolemaeus Evergetes, ut sororis ulcisceretur,bellum Seleuco intulit,eique pulso & fugato,magnam Syriae partem ademit.

Erat autem in altera regni parte, videlicet in Asia,frater Antiochusἱέραξ.Ab hoc Seleucus auxilium petit. Hierax cupidè suscipit expeditionem, sperans se unum toto regno potiturum esse. Adducit mercede conductas Galatarum copias, quae iam stipendiis in Asiam pertractae erant. Seleucus quia fratris voluntati diffidebat,cum Evergete pacem fecit. Quare iratusἱέραξ,fratri bellum infert, quem cum acie victum,Syria expulisset, exorti sunt novi motus in Asia, quam vacuam Eumenes Bithynius occupabat,et passim in ea Galatae vagabantur. Et rex Aegyptius Ciliciam invaserat.

His motibus cum rediens in Asiam,ἱέραξoccurrisset,magnis acceptis cladibus, fugit ad socerum in Cappadociam, à quo sentiens insidias sibi strui,brevi pòst, alias latebras quaesivit,seque ad hostem in Aegyptum contulit.Huic quoque diffidens, cum fugiens in deviis locis erraret,interfectus est. Seleucus frater, eodem ferè tempore excussus ex equo, extinctus est. Hic fuit exitus duorum impiorum fratrum,qui non amplius triennio regnârunt.

Horum bellorum caussam recitat Daniel, inquiens, Filia regis Austri, scilicet Berenice, filia regis Aegyptii, venict ad regem A quilonis,id est,Syriacum, Antiochumθεόν,facere amicitiam. Et non erit firmum brachium eius, & non stabit semen eius, id est, Berenice cum filio interficietur.Deinde addit venturum Aegypti regem, scilicet Evergeten,& occupaturum regni Syriaci partem.

Ita breviter positis causis,& facta mentione bellorum, significat magnas fore calamitates illarum regionum, in quibus & vioina Iudaea malè adflicta est, ut dicitur, Aliquid mali propter vicinum malum.

Antiochus magnus fratrem habuit Seleucum maiorem natu,qui statim postquam regnare coepisset in transitu Tauri, interfectus est à suis, ut narrat Polybius,apud quem extant initia rerum gestarum Antiochi magni. Caetera desiderantur,quae si haberemus, multum lucis accederet Danielis vaticinio,& supputatio,de qua suprà dictum est, illustrior fieret.

Primum bellum gessit Antiochus adolescens cum Molone duce, qui ab ipso defecerat. Hunc tandem acie victum et captum, in crucem egit.

Postea Syriae civitates, quas Aegyptii priore bello occupaverant, partim vi recepit, partim ad deditionem certis pactionibus invitavit.

Haec fuit caussa novi & magni belli inter Antiochum magnum,& Ptolemaeum Philopatorem, quod hîc à Daniele praedicitur. Apparatus verò & praelium regum à Polybio describuntur. Ptolemaeus exercitum duxit in Syriam, septuaginta millia peditum, & quinque millia equitum. Antiochi copiae fuerunt, sexaginta millia peditum,& sex millia equitum. Hi exercitus in Syria conflixerunt, fuitque victor Ptolemaeus, & amissas civitates recuperavit. Antiocho petenti, pax data est. Nam Ptolemaeus, etsi progredi, & plures regni partes Antiocho eripere poterat,tamen propter ignaviam gaudebat se labore militiae liberari.Sicut & Polybius narrat,συνεχώρησε σπονδὰς ἐνιαυσίους ὀυκ ἀλλότριος ὢν τῆς ἡσυχίας ἀλλὰ ἐἰπερ τὸ δέον οἰκείως ἑλκόμενος, ὑπὸ τῆς συνήθους ῥαθυμίας καὶ καχεξίας.

Cum autem post paucos annos domi mortuus esset Ptolemaeus Philopator, relicto filio impubere Ptolemaeo Epiphane, rursus bellum movet Antiochus. Nam propter hostis infantiam, videbat se facilius inclinaturum voluntates civitatum ad defectionem.

Miserunt autem Aegyptii legatos Romam, ac petiverunt,ut senatus Romanus tutelam regii pueri susciperet. Quod cum esset factum, senatus iussit Antiochum ab Aegypto abstimere. Id aegrè ferens,& hospitem habens Hannibalem, qui erat hortator, ut caeteri reges se coniungerent ad reprimendos Romanos, societatem cum Philippo Macedonum rege fecit. Haec fuerunt semina belli cum Romanis, quod à Livio describitur. Tandem autem victus Antiochus, cessit Romanis tota Asia citra Taurum montem. Coactus est & obsides dare duos filios, Antiochum Epiphanem, & Demetrium. Aliquanto pòst, spoliaturus templum quoddam in Syria, à suis interfectus est.

Facilis est autem coniectura, Iudaeam in his Syriae bellis graviter vexatam esse, nunc à Syriacis, nunc ab Aegyptiis ducibus. Ptolemaeus Philopator venit Ierosolymam victor, cumque usitata regibus curio sitate visurus esset templum celebratissimum, iamque in vestibulum ingressus esset,deprecati sunt sacerdotes ne pergeret, nec prophanaret adytum. Cumque subita consternatione divinitus repressus esset, ac è templo eductus, vehementer irasci Iudaeis caepit, & postea in eos atrociter etiam in Aegypto saeviit. Haec recitantur in tertio Machabaeorum.

Postea Scopas dux ab Aegyptio Epiphane missus, occupavit quaedam Iudaeae oppida. Sed cum ad Iordanem ab Antiocho magno victus esset, Iudaei dedunt se victori, qui, etsi nondum crudeliter eis imperavit, tamen tributa indixit,& partem sibi, partem genero Ptolemaeo Epiphani attribuit. Haec fuerunt initia servitutis Iudaicae sub his Syriacis regibus. Ideo hîc dicitur in Daniele, Stabit in terra desider abili, scilicet Antiochus magnus. Deinde convertet faciem suam ad insulas, & capiet multas, id est,discedet inde,bellum gesturus cum Romanis in Graecia & in Asia.

Antiochus magnus reliquit tres filios, Seleu cum Philopatora, Antiochum Epiphanem, & Demetrium. Seleucus exiguo tempore post patrem regnavit. Hunc Seleucum describens angelus, inquit, Stabit in loco eius, vilis vel ignavus quaestor regii honoris. Vilem vel ignavum vocat,regem negligentem officia sua, nec domi iudicia diligenter exercentem, nec disciplinam graviter tuentem, nec populi utilitatibus consulentem, nec foris depellentem hostes, sed deditum voluptatibus, & profundentem publicas & privatas pecunias in res inutiles,pompas,convivia, aedificationes luculleas. Et quia nullae opes his ineptiis sufficiunt, expilationibus infinitis exhaurientem privatorum facultates. Ideo Quaestorem vocat, sed regio honore, id est, titulo regio dominantem. Nec sine caussa angelus depingit hunc Quaestorem. Significat enim ultimis temporibus talia fore imperia regum & principum, quibus ecclesia subiecta erit.

EST autem bonae mentis considerare res humanas,& adfici communibus aerumnis.Ideo lector te adhortor,ut ad hanc imaginem, Europae imperia iam conferas. Primum, quid sunt Episcopatus ubique, nisi turpes & acerbae quaesturae? Quae offitia faciunt hi, qui amplissimos reditus ecclesiarum possident?

Deinde Regum & principum aulas vide. Quod est studium rei militaris? quid gerunt adversus Turcicam tyrannidem, quae iam aliquot Europae regna oppressit, & caeteris minatur non modo servitutem, sed vastitatem? Domi quomodo disciplinam, quomodo iudicia regunt?imò quid ista curant? Una cura est profundendae & quaerendae pecuniae. Estque penè omnium regum & principum infinitaβουλιμία,quam quia diutius sustinere misera plebs non poterit,non dubium est impendere fatales imperiorum mutationes. Nam historiae ostendunt, statim post hasβουλιμίαςsemper mutatam esse formam reipub.

Haec recito, ut boni agnoscant has calamitates senectae mundi divinitus praedictas esse,tum ut admoniti pii gubernatores mitigare eas studeant, tum ut caeteri poenas peccatorum intelligant,& ferre arte discant,ac deum orent,ut det imperia iusta & salutaria ecclesiae, detστρατηγοὺςfortes & felices, det rectores politicos, sapientes & iustos, qui paterno amore suos complectantur. Tales enim angelus opponit quaestoribus,quos hîc describit. Quia salutares principes oportet & foris depellere latrocinia,& domi iudicia ac disciplinam regere, & utilitatibus populi consulere, ut patresfamilias educare liberos, & honestis artibus erudire possint, ut conservetur in terris vera de deo & de filio eius Iesu Christo doctrina,et colantur alia virtutis studia, quae deservutur, cum illaβουλιμίαexhauriuntur privatorum facultates.

Porrò & hoc expilatoris erat, quòd hic Seleucus spoliari templum in urbe Ierosolyma iussit, & ad eam rem tentandam incitatores habuit Iudaeos, ut in secundo Machabaeorum narratur, capite tertio. Ita nunc qui fruantur opibus ecclesiarum, minimè obscurum est. Bacchanalia perpetua sunt in collegiis canonicorum. Interim pii pastores ecclesiarum cum familiis suis,& pauperes scholastici fame contabescunt,studia literarum, & praecipuè sacrarum, deservutur, quia nemo ivuat pauperes, qui studia dedicaturi erant ecclesiis.

Haec cum negligant, aut fortasse rideant etiam illi summi quaestores,episcopi & principes, non existiment Christum dissimulaturum esse, cum in illo terribili iudicio accusabit eos de neglecto offitio,ac dicet,Esurivi,& non cibastis me. Ite igitur maledicti in ignem aeternum. Nos verò filium dei aeterni,liberatorem & custodem ecclesiae suae oremus, ut ipse pia studia tueatur,& suppeditet discentibus victum.

Sicut in lege Mosaica, cum tribui Leviticae non daretur certa regio possidenda,ut caeteris, deus inquit, sese fore eius tribus haereditatem, seu fundum,ita nunc sit fundus &τροφεύςpiorum doctorum & scholasticorum,& futurum esse non dubitemus.

ANTIOCHUS Epiphanes frater Seleuci, Romae cognita morte patris, clàm inde discessit,ut ignavo fratri regnum eriperet. Fuit autem hic Epiphanes callido, acri, & saevo ingenio praeditus, qui mox, postquam regnare coepisset,insidias struxit Aegyptiis. Nam mortuo Ptolemaeo Epiphane, venit in Aegyptum, quasi tutor futurus cognati pupilli. Nam Cleopatra mater pupilli, erat soror Antiochi Epiphanis. Collocat igitur praesidia in urbibus Aegypti tutorio nomine,cum re ipsa hoc ageret,ut occupatas nunc honesto praetextu, postea retineret. Inde rediens, Ierosolymam venit,invitatus à Iasone, cui pontificatum dederat contra Iudaicas leges.In hoc primo itinere Antiochus spoliavit templum.

Post biennium Antiochus rursus duxit exercitum in Aegyptum, quia rex Aegyptius Ptolemaeus Philometor iam adoleverat, et cognita fraude avunculi, ex aliquibus suis urbibus Syriaca praesidia expulerat, & auxilium à Romanis adversus Antiochum petiverat. venit igitur eo tempore legatus Popilius, qui iussit Antiochum ab Aegypto abstinere. Cumque Antiochus ambiguè responderet,ducto circulo in pulvere circa eum,iussit eum clarè respondere antequam ex eo circulo egrederetur. Ibi Antiochus perterrefactus, pacem promittit, & fremens discedit.

Quia verò magnos sumptus in ea expeditione fecerat,egens venit Ierosolymam, ibi facultates locupletum diripit, prophanat templum, statua Iovis Olympii in eo collocata, proponit crudelia edicta, de abolenda totà religione, tradita voce dei per Abraham, Moysen & prophetas. Et haec edicta defendit, additis praesidiis, quae magnam saeviciam exercebant in homines pios, qui professionem legis divinae abiicere nolebant.

Propter hanc magnam calamitatem, hoc vaticinium apud Danielem editum est, ut pii praemonerentur & confirmarentur, ne à deo deficerent.Additur & tempus, ut pii scirent hanc calamitatem non diuturnam fore, videlicet anni septem, & menses duo. Nam intra hoc spacium, templum ab Antiocho prophanatum est, et deinde à Iuda Machabaeo recuperatum, & postea ex oppidis Iudaeae expulsa sunt Syriaca praesidia.

Porrò Antiochus Epiphanes, quia acceperat insignes clades, non solum in Iudaea, sed etiam in perfide, animi dolore extinctus est. Alii scribunt excussum è curru, tam duriter allisum esse solo, ut inde morbus letalis ortus fit, quo extinctus dicitur, cum tenuisset regnum annos duodecim. Quales fuerint eius mores, suprà commemoravi.

APUD Danielem non fit mentio sequentium regum, quia significatur brevi pòst, venturum esse Messiam. Est enim breve tempus Iuda Machabaeo ad natum Christum, videlicet, anni centum sexagintasex. Et statim post Antiochum, Syria domesticis, discordiis continuis dilacerata est, donec eam Romani occuPârunt, ut unum regnum dici non posset. Sicut ferè existunt tales regnorum discordiae fatales, cum finis non procul abest, ut vidit haec aetas triste exemplum Pannoniae.

Adiiciam autem breviter reliquos Syriae reges, ut poenae considerentur, de quibus concionatur primum praeceptum. Fuerunt inter posteros Antiochi, post prophanationem templi, continua parricidia. Successit Antiocho Epiphani frater Demetrius, qui unà fuerat obses Romae. Hic veniens in Syriam, mox iubet interfici filium fratris Antiochi iam regnantem, Antiochumεὐπάτορα.Aliquanto pòst, Demetrius dat poenas parricidii. Interficitur enim à quodam Alexandro seditioso, qui regnum invasit, adiutus ab Aegyptiis.

Demetrio fuerunt duo filii, Demetrius iunior, & Antiochus Sedetes. Hic iunior Demetrius inter fecit Alexandrum. Postea Tryphon ex regno Demetrium expulit. Antiochus Sedetes à Parthis interfectus est.

Hi fratres reliquerunt liberos. Demetrio fuit filius Antiochusγρυπός.Sedeti filius fuit Antiochus Cyricenus. Hi dimicantes de regno, ambo interfecti sunt. Postea Syriam invasit vicinus rex Tygranes, cui eam Pompeius eripuit. Hoc modo domesticis seditionibus & parricidiis, poenas dederunt impiorum regum posteri, donec tandem familia tota extincta est, & regium nomen sublatum, & Syria à Romanis occupata est.

REGES AEGYPTI.

Ptolemaeus Lagi filius.Ptolemaeus Philadelphus.Ptolemaeus Evergetes.Ptolemaeus Philopator.Ptolemaeus Epiphanes.Ptolemaeus Philometor, cui successit fra ter PtolemaeusΦύσκων,id est, ventricosus.Ptolemaeusλάθουρος.Ptolemaeusαὐλητὴς..Cleopatra Antonii.

Hae nomenclaturae hoc ordine traduntur à Strabone, qui cum ipse perlustraverit Aegyptum, diligentius eam quàm vicina loca describit.

PTOLEMAEUS Lagi filius, qui inter praecipuos Alexandri duces fuit, & exercitus in India duxit, ubi & vidnus accepit ictus telo, quod veneno infectum fuerat, in distributione provinciarum nactus est Aegyptum, quam cum triennio tenuisset, bellum ei Perdiccas intulit, qui Monarchiam adfectabat. Sed Ptolemaeus, interfecto Perdicca, retinuit Aegyptum, ac postea Cyrenaicam & magnam Syriae partem adiecit. Reliquit filios Philadelphum &

κεραυνόν.& alios plures, quos recenset Pausanias.

Scribit Polybius mortuum esse Ptolemaeum Lagi filium Olympiade centesima vicesima quarta, hoc est, post Alexandri mortem, exactis ferè quadraginta annis, qua Olympiade et Lysimachus, & Seleucus, & κεραυνός perierunt.

PTOLEMAEUS Philadelphus, praelatus fratri maiori natu, rex à patre nuncupatus est, & commendatus principibus & civitatibus, Successit igitur patri, & recuperavit Cyrenaicam. Deinde nactus ocium, cum sciret in Aegypto floruisse doctrinam de rebus caelestibus, & eas artes, quae coniunctae sunt illi doctrinae, vetera studia & restituit, et auxit, et instruxit discendi cupidos bibliotheca copiosissima.

Cumque audisset Iudaeos praedicare suae gentis antiquitatem & doctrinam, accersivit inde interpretes, qui libros Moysi & aliorum prophetarum in Graecam linguam converterent. Haec vetus translatio propheticorum librorum in Graecum sermonem, vocatur septuaginta interpretum opus. Per hanc occasionem credibile est aliquos in Aegypto, & alibi, tunc veram religionem amplexos esse, cum & libri legerentur, & viri docti è Iudaets, qui versabantur inter gentes, liberè alios docere possent. Nam vox verae ecclesiae dei, nunquam temere & frustra excitatur. Magnis autem beneficiis hic Philadelphus Iudaeos adfecit. Nam & populo universo immunitatem concessit, & iis qui in Aegypto serviebant, libertatem donavit.

Observandus est hîc error multorum, qui historiam fratrisκεραυνοῦin hunc Philadelphum transferunt. Namκεραυνούςfrater ex alia matre natus, ex Eurydice Antipatri, relicta Aegypto, profectus est in Macedoniam, et oppresso Seleuco, aliquandiu tenuit Macedoniam, fuitque incestus & impius in sororem Arsinoen, quam fraude circumventam duxit uxorem, & postea interfecit, necatis unà filiis Lysimachi, in matris Arsinoae gremio, deinde poenas dedit tantorum scelerum, interfectus à Brenni exercitu. Haec historia copiose recitatur à Pausania.

At Philadelphus in Aegyptio regno consenuit, in tranquillitate & doctorum consuetudine, qua leniit dolores articulorum, quos sustinuisse satis acres dicitur. Eius opes & munificentia celebrantur à Theocrito, qui ibi cum Callimacho, Apollonio & aliis poetis vixit.

PTOLEMAEUS Evergetes ingens bellum intulit Syriae, ut ulcisceretur necem sororis Berenices, quae fuerat nupta regi Syriae Antiocho Theo, & postea à privignis interfecta est. Occupatis autem multis urbibus in Syria Evergetes, non gravatim Scleuco Callinico, pacem petenti, dedit, revocabatur enim in Aegyptum domesticis seditionibus. De hoc Evergete loquitur Daniel, inquiens, Et stabit in germine radicum eius plantatio, videlicet Ptolemaeus Evergetes, & veniet cum exercitu contra regem Aquilonis, id est, contra Seleucum Callinicum, & obtinebit.

Hoc tempore Iudaea victori Evergete, tributum pendere coacta est, etsi adhuc quidem tolerabilis fuit Iudaeae status. Nam cum tributum non solveretur, facile placatus est Evergetes à Iosepho duce, cuius mentio fit in Luca capite tertio, ubi nominatur Ioseph pater Iannis Hircani ultimi ducis ex stirpe David.

PTOLEMAEUS Φιλοπάτωρsic dictus est ironicè, cum patrem, matrem, & fratrem interfecerit. Ignavia eius Antiocho magno spem fecit recuperandarum in Syria, quas Seleuco Evergetes ademerat. Quare Antiochus magnus, ivuenis incensus cupiditate gloriae militaris, bellum infert huic regi Aegyptio, & multas civitates recipit. Tandem verò occurrit Philopator, & cum uterque magnas haberet copias, vicit Antiochum. Et propter segniciem & luxum statim in Aegyptum rediit. Huius victoriae mentionem facit Daniel, inquiens, Et provocabitur rex Austri, scilicet Ptolemaeus Philopator, & pugnabit adversus regem Aquilonis, scilicet, Antiochum magnum.

Erant autem Iudaei initio coniuncti Antiocho, postea mutata fortuna, hospitio victorem Aegyptium exceperunt ac placârunt, data pecunia. Cum autem Ptolemaeus Philopator visurus templum, in vestibulum ingressus esset, sacerdotes obnixè, orant, ne pergat, nec penetret in adytum interius, quò non erat fas ingredi cuiquam praeter pontificem. Etsi negat se obtemperaturum piis monitis rex superbus, tamen deus templum defendit, & mox subito terrore consternatum regem ita reprimit, ut semivivus ex templo exportaretur. Quare Iudaeis iratus, cum Alexandriam redisset, crudelia edicta proponit, Iudaeos iubet in vincula rapi, & multos elephantis conculcandos obiicit in spectaculis.

Interea domi ipse, interfecta coniuge, infandis se libidinibus contaminat. Et regebatur aula, non ab ipso aut principibus, sed à turpissimis & flagitio sissimis libidinum sociis. Talis furor numquam est diuturnus, ut dicitur de peccatis clamantibus in caelum. Quare post victoriam, de qua dictum est, non fuit diu superstes.

PTOLEMAEUS Epiphanes penè infans, patri successit. Cum autem Antiochus magnus existimaret Aegyptios segnius defensuros esse Syriam in hac regis infantia, rursus movit bellum. Quare Aegyptii diffidentes suis viribus, Romam legatos miserunt, qui peterent, ut Romanus senatus tutelam regis Aegyptii susciperet. Interim Antiochus plerasque urbes inferioris Syriae ac Phoenices recepit, & ducem Scopam, missum ab Aegyptiis, ad tuendam eam oram, profligavit. Hîc Iudaei graviter afflicti sunt, primum à Scopa, postea à victoro Antiocho.

Deinde cum legati Romani advenissent, iusserunt Antiochum restituere occupatas urbes, eique bellum denunciârunt nisi obtemperaret. Hanc legationem ut arte eluderet, nec Romanos exercitus in Syriam adduceret, pacem fecit cum Aegyptio, data ei filia Cleopatra. Iratus verò Romanis, foedus fecit cum Philippo Macedone, & traiecit in Graeciam, & bellum gessit cum Romanis.

Haec ordine praedicit Daniel, describens duas expeditiones Antiochi magni adversus Aegyptios. Revertetur, inquit, rex Aquilonis, id est, Antiochus magnus, antea victus à Philopatore, bellum reparabit, & multas urbes occupabit. Deinde vertet se ad insulas, id est, navigabit in Graeciam, ut bellum gerat adversus Romanos.

PTOLEMAEUS Philometor natus est ex filia Antiochi magni, cumque eius aetas mortuo patre, gubernationi nondum idonea esset, venit in Aegyptum Antiochus Epiphanes avunculus, simulans, se tutorem fore nepotis sui, ut eo praetextu urbibus Aegypti praesidia imponeret, & pupillum regno spoliaret. Sed aliquanto post discessum Antiochi, auxilium à senatu Romano mater Ptolemaei petivit, & pulsis Antiochi praesidiis, urbes recepit.

Redit igitur Antiochus iratus, nepotem vel dolo per simulationem amicitiae, vel armis oppressurus. Sed Romani legatum miserunt Popilium, qui iussit Antiochum ab Aegypto abstinere. Ita frustratus sua spe Antiochus, & egens rediit in Syriam, ut suprà narratum est. Haec historia prolixè recitatur in vaticinio angeli. Nam propter clades quas accepit populus Iudaicus in his itineribus Antiochi, hanc seriem rerum angelus commemorat. Ideoque caeteros reges omittit.

Sed quia exempla iudicii divini illustria, sequens historia continet, breviter eam adiiciam. Invaserat, ut dictum est, Antiochus Epiphanes Aegyptum, & nepotis vitae praetextu tutelae insidias struxerat. Sed hi iniusti conatus non solum irriti fuerunt, sed etiam singulari exemplo puniti sunt. Nam postquam Antiochus mortuus erat, & filius ac frater interfecti, cum bellum esset inter Alexandrum seditiosum, & Demetrium iuniorem de regno Syriae certantes, hic Ptolemaeus Philometor bellum movit adversus Alexandrum regem Syriae, eumque acie vicit. Quare Antiocheni receperunt Ptolemaeum, & duplici corona, altera Syriae,altera Aegypti coronaverunt.

Ut igitur Antiochus alterius vitae & regno insidiatus erat, ita posteri ipsius diris parricidiis necati sunt, & regno insidiatoris potitus est ille ipse, cui insidiae structae fuerant. Haec est iusta poena,ut in versu dicitur,ἔικε πάθοι ἃ ἔρεξε, δίκη ἴθεια γένοιτο.

Caeterum Ptolemaeus metuens Romanos, cessit regno Syriae,fuitque autor ut iunior Demetrius accerseretur. Huic dedit Ptolemaeus filiam ereptam Alexandro priori genero. Aiunt autem Ptolemaeum in praelio vulnus accepisse, & vix defensum ac servatum esse, Alexandrum verò,amisso exercitu, fugientem ad Arabum regem venisse. Ab hoc, cum interfectus esset, caput Ptolemaeo missum est,qui non multo pòst ex vulnere mortuus est. Hic fuit exitus utriusque regis, soceri & generi, qui inter se impiis armis certârunt.

PTOLEMAEUS Physcon sic dictus à sagina ventris turpiter inflati. Namφύσκηsignificat crassius intestinum, evius usus est in faciendis farciminibus. Fuit autem hic Physcon frater Philometoris, de quo iam dictum est. Et quanquam in Eusebii Chronico inter Philometorem & Physconem inseritur quidam Evergetes, tamen Sirabo testatur eundem esse Physconem, qui ironicè nominatus estεὐεργέτης,vicit enim omnes crudelitate & obscoenitate. Duxit sororem,quae antea priori fratri nupta fuerat, & in die nuptiali puerum filium fratris, in complexu matris interfecit. Aliquanto poòst, duxit filiam suae coniugis ex fratre natam, repudiata matre. Erat autem ei filiolus ex priore coniuge & sorore natus. Hunc interfectum & dissectum, misit matri in die natali inter epulas. Nec fuit mitior in cives, Nam Alexandriae quotidianis caedibus penè vastitatem effecit. Quare Romani legatos Alexandriam miserunt, inter quos fuerunt & Scipio posterior & Polybius historicus, ut autoritate senatus imperarent ei, ut iniuste grassari in suos desineret,quia senatus eum defensurus non esset, nec in regnum reducturus, si propter crudelitatem excuteretur. Ac legati ridicula deformitate regis conspecta, non solum foedissimos ipsius mores, sed etiam monstrosam speciem corporis execrati sunt. Brevi pòst, scelerum poenas dedit tyrannus Physcon, eiectus regno ab Alexandrinis.

Tales historiae lectores admoneant, non solum de dei iudicio, qui testatur se atrocia delicta in hac vita manifestis suppliciis punire, sed etiam de miseria communi generus humani, & de diaboli furore, qui odio dei & contemptu generis humani,tantis flagitiis deformat imagines dei, & quidem in summo loco, in imperiis ubi excellere virtus debet,ut illa horrenda diaboli spectacula universa rerum natura abominetur. Quare filium dei oremus, ut opera diaboli grassantis, cum in toto genere humano, tum verò praecipuè in summis ordinibus, destruat, sicut scriptum est, filium dei apparuisse, ut destruat opera diaboli.

PTOLEMAEUS λάθουρος,sic enim à Strabone nominatur,opinor ideo, quòd exul occultè tentavit reditum. Nam mater seditione mota, hunc Ptolemaeum Aegypto expulit, & filio minori Alexandro, quem sibi magis obtemperaturum esse sperabat, regnum tradidit. Exul autem haec gessisse scribitur. Cum Iudaei viderent se nactos esse occasionem, vicinas urbes occupandi, propter domesticas discordias, cum in Aegypto, tum in Syria ortas, bellum adversus vicinos moverunt. Hi ex Cypro accersunt Ptolemaeum, qui sperans se per eam occasionem in Aegyptum rediturum esse, mediocres copias in Phoenicen adduxit, ac statim in Iudaeam irruptionem fecit, cepitque in sabbato quoddam Galilaeae oppidum. Postea acerrimo praelio cum Iudaeis ad Iordanem conflixit. Cumque magna multitudo Iudaeorum in acie trucidata esset, victor Ptolemaeus, in finibus Iudaeae populatus est villas, & iussit occidi mulieres & infantes, & dissecta eorum membra, coqui, quibus elixis & coctis, vesci Iudaeos coegit.

Tanta immanitas in victoria non habuit diuturnos successus. Nam Iudaei à Ptolemaei matre, auxilia adversus eum petiverunt, quae misit exercitum eo celerius, ut filium ab Aegypto arceret. Cum igitur frustra tentaret reditum Ptolemaeus, nec tueri occupatas urbes in Phoenice posset, rursus fugit in Cyprum. Aliquanto pòst, Alexander, qui in Aegypto regnabat, matrem interfecit. Pulso igitur Alexandro, revocatus est in Aegyptum hic Ptolemaeusλάθουρος,quem ibi novae seditiones exceperunt, fortassis quia aliqui favebant fratri. Dicitur hic Ptolemaeus funditus delevisse maximam & celebratissimam urbem Thebas Aegyptias.

PTOLEMAEUS αὐλητὴς,id est tibicen, sic dictus, quia publice in habitu regio tibiis certare cum artificibus solebat, Regno propter ignaviam & crudelitatem expulsus, venit Romam, ubi Pompeius reduci eum suasit. Et quanquam diu ea de re contentiones extractae sunt, tamen per Gabinium tandem reductus est, qui Archelaum, qui interea regnaverat, interfecit. Hicαὐλητὴςreliquit filium Ptolemaeum Dionysium, qui adolescens Pompeium ex Pharsalica pugna fugientem in Aegyptum, iussit interfici. Postea cum Iulio Caesari struxisset insidias, interfectus est à Caesare, qui regnum dedit eius sorori Cleopatrae. Cleopatra ad extremum cum Augustus venisset in Aegyptum, sibi mortem conscivit.

Haec est series regum Aegyptiacorum, in qua non solum hoc deplorandum est, quòd posteri nati ex hominibus virtute praestantissimis, adeo turpiter degenerant, sed illud etiam quòd diabolus tam petulanter grassatur in his qui imperia tenent. Sed hae sunt poenaeεἰδωλομανιῶν,ut adRoma. 1.scribitur. Nusquam autem plus fuit portentosae idolomaniae, quàm in Aegypto & in Syria.

In hac commemoratione & hoc observandum est, quòd ecclesiam dei, cuius sedes tune erat inter illa duo regna, videmus hîc exiguam esse, & duriter adfligi, nec humana ope, humanis praesidaiis defendi, sed mirabiliter divinitus regi & servari. Haec imago est ultimae senectae mundi, in qua ecclesia vera dei in servitute erit, oppressa multiplici tyrannide. Postquam autem ordine historias regum recensui, vaticinium angeli ad eas breviter accommodabo.

TEXTUS.

Et confortabitur rex Austri, qui est ex ducibus eius, id est, Ptolemaeus, qui fuit unus ex ducibus Alexandri, occupabit Aegyptum, quae est Iudaeae ad meridiem sita. Et alius confortabitur è regione, & erit eius magna dominatio, id est, Seleucus è regione Syriam & Babyloniam tenebit, qui post Antigonum victum cum Asiam teneret, antecelluit caeteris omnibus potentia.

Et in fine dierum sociabuntur, id est, post aliquod tempus devincientur inter se foederib.

& affinitatibus Reges Aegyptii & Syriaci. Et filia regis Austri, Berenice filia Ptolemaei Philadelphi regis Aegyptii, veniet ad regem Aquilonis, id est, nubet Antiocho Theo regi Syriae, ut fiat amicitia, id est, ut hac adfinitate inter reges fiat pax, & retineant Aegyptii urbes in Phoenice occupatas à primo Ptolemaeo.

Et non obtinebit robur brachii, & non stabit semen eius, & tradetur ipsa & comites eius, & puer eius, & is qui eam confortaverat, id est, Berenice non retinebit regnum Syriae, nec eius filius, natus ex Antiocho Theo. Sed privigni Seleucus Callinicus, et Antiochusἱέραξinterficient novercam, & filium eius. Pater verò qui confortaverat Aegyptiam, id est, qui regni sociam fecerat, Antiochus Theos, necabitur veneno à repudiata coniuge Laodice.

## 11:1 Concerning Ptolemy Euergetes
CONCERNING PTOLEMY Euergetes.

And there shall stand from the sprout of her root in his place, and he shall come with an army against the King of the North, that is, Ptolemy Euergetes, brother of Berenice, shall avenge the slaughter of his sister, and shall wage war against Seleucus Callinicus, he shall capture the great cities of Syria, and shall take away the treasures and

Syrian images. Therefore it is said in the text, Also their gods and their princes, and their precious vessels of silver and gold shall be carried into Egypt. But the king of the South, Ptolemy Euergetes, returned into Egypt. For being recalled by internal seditions, he made peace with Seleucus Callinicus. And at this time Euergetes imposed tributes upon Judea.

Latin:

DE PTOLEMAEO Evergete.

Et stabit de germine radicis eius in loco suo, & veniet cum exercitu contra regem Aquilonis, id est, Ptolemaeus Evergetes frater Berenices ulciscetur sororis necem, & geret bellum adversus Seleucum Callinicum, magnas urbes Syriae capiet, & auferet thesauros &

simulacra Syriaca. Ideo dicitur in textu, Etiam dii illorum & principes, & vasa preciosa argenti & auri vehentur in Aegyptum. Sed rex Austri, Ptolemaeus Evergetes rediit in Aegyptum. Nam intestinis seditionibus revocatus, fecit pacem cum Seleuco Callinico. Et hoc tempore Evergetes tributa indixit Iudaeae.

## 11:1 Of Antiochus the Great & Ptolemy Philopator
OF ANTIOCHUS THE GREAT & Ptolemy Philopator.

And his sons shall be stirred up, and shall assemble a multitude of forces. And one shall certainly come, that is, Antiochus the Great, son of Seleucus Callinicus, burning with a desire for military glory, he shall wage war against Ptolemy Philopator, and indeed in the very beginning he shall recover some cities in Syria, he shall also occupy some in Phoenicia, which had obeyed the Egyptians. And although Philopator met the enemy slowly, spending almost a year in the preparation of war, yet at length having undertaken the expedition, he leads an army to Raphia. There, the battle having been joined, he shall conquer, he says, Antiochus the Great, and shall recover the cities lost in Syria and Phoenicia, and shall compel Antiochus to seek peace.

And his heart shall be lifted up, and he shall cast down many, and he shall not be strengthened, that is, Philopator He will be more insolent and more furious. In the city of Jerusalem he will attempt to profane the temple, and having returned into Egypt, he will give himself over to shameful lusts, his wife having been killed. He will also exercise cruelty against the Jews, he will expose them to be crushed by elephants. Therefore he will quickly perish. The text signifies this when it says, He shall not be strengthened, that is, he will not be a survivor for long, he will perish miserably. At this time Judea was harshly afflicted, for first it had joined itself to Antiochus the Great, afterwards it was fined a great sum of money by the Egyptian, and in Egypt cruelty was practiced against the Jews everywhere, as I have said.

Latin:

DE ANTIOCHO MAGNO & Ptolemaeo Philopatore.

Et filii illius irritabuntur, & congregabunt exercitus. Et veniet veniens, id est, Antiochus magnus, filius Seleuci Callinici, flagrans cupiditate gloriae militaris, inferet bellum Ptolemaeo Philopatori, & quidem initio statim aliquas urbes in Syria recipiet, occupabit etiam aliquas in Phoenice, quae parverant Aegyptiis. Etsi autem Philopator tardè occurrit hosti, ferė annum in apparatu belli consumens, tamen tandem expeditione suscepta, ad Rhaphiam ducit exercitum. Ibi praelio commisso, vincet, inquit, Antiochum magnum, & recuperabit urbes in Syria & Phoenice amissas, & coget Antiochum pacem petere.

Et elevabitur cor eius,& prosternet plurimos, & non confortabitur, id est, Philopator erit insolentior & furiosior. In urbe Ierosolyma templum prophanare conabitur, & reversus in Aegyptum, dedet se flagitiosis libidinibus, interfecta uxore. Exercebit & in Iudaeos crudelitatem, obiiciet conterendos elephantis. Ideo citò peribit. Id significat textus, cum ait, Non confortabitur, id est, non diu superstes erit, misere peribit. Hoc tempore duriter adflicta est Iudaea, primùm enim se adiunxerat Antiocho magno, postea ab Aegyptio magna pecunia mulctata est, & in Aegypto passim in Iudaeos saevitum est, ut dixi.

## 11:1 Concerning Antiochus the Great and Ptolemy Epiphanes
CONCERNING ANTIOCHUS THE GREAT AND Ptolemy Epiphanes.

And the king of the North shall return, and shall bring a greater multitude than before, that is, Philopator being dead, Antiochus the Great will renew the war, hoping that the victory would be easy, because the age of Ptolemy Epiphanes was not yet suitable for conducting affairs. Although however the Roman senate had undertaken the guardianship of the Egyptian king, yet because the aids of allies are accustomed to be slower, Antiochus the Great recovered Syria and Phoenicia, the army having been routed, which the Egyptian had sent into Syria, with Scopas as leader.

And at that time many shall stand against the king of Egypt, that is, Antiochus the Great shall make a league with Philip the king of Macedon, who shall send auxiliaries against the Egyptians.

And the children of the transgressors of thy people shall be lifted up, that they may fulfill the vision, and they shall fall. Because in these wars they were ravaging Syria on both sides, both the Syrian armies and the Egyptians, many Jews who were serving in the armies of the impious kings fell away from the divine law, as the third book of Maccabees also testifies. And a great multitude of Jews migrated into Egypt, that they might seek seats in a more tranquil place.

Onias was then high priest in the city of Jerusalem, whose virtue and piety is celebrated in the second book of Maccabees. Jason, his brother, attempted to snatch the high priesthood from him, of whom mention is made later in the history of the Maccabees. These struggles moved Onias, the son of the high priest Onias, to withdraw from his fatherland into Egypt, because he had perceived that snares were being laid for him at home by his uncle. Since, therefore, there was a great multitude of Jews in Egypt, and the high priest's son Onias was there together with them, so that their exile might be less sad, having obtained seats at Heliopolis, they established a Jewish polity there, and as it were Holding a second Jerusalem, they build a temple there to offer sacrifices according to the Levitical rite, although God willed that there be only one tabernacle or temple, and expressly prohibited more from being built, and indeed willed that they be built nowhere except in the land of Canaan. Thus the consensus of doctrine could be more easily retained, and it was established in what place the voice of God would sound and be propagated. But although the building of a new temple was disapproved of by the Jews who were at Alexandria, nevertheless superstition prevailed. Onias, against the command of God, builds a temple, performs sacrifices in a foreign place, and, so that a pretext would not be lacking, citesIsaiah, who in chapter 19.says, in that day there shall be an altar to the Lord in the midst of the land of Egypt, etc. Since this is said concerning the calling of the Gentiles and concerning the Gospel, which, just as it was going to call the other nations, so also the Egyptians to the acknowledgment of the Son of God and of eternal life, Onias twisted it to his own design. Thus impostors, or those fascinated by depraved opinions, in all ages abuse the voice of God, and transfer it from its genuine meaning to confirm their own delusions.

But the angel in this place condemns the building of that Egyptian temple, and calls that design prevarication or violation of the divine law and audacity. Let them observe this judgment in all ages, and that those who serve dogmas against the word of God, and who institute worships of God without a divine mandate, are corruptions of doctrine, a prevarication, and a violation of the first and second commandment, there is no doubt.

Then it is a horrible audacity, when most certain testimonies exist, that God is offended by no wickedness more than by corruptions of doctrine, with all threats and examples despised, yet to play with false opinions and those insulting against God. How great was the audacity of Jeroboam, when against the open command of God, he builds a certain temple of his own, and institutes a new priesthood? How great was the fury in the heathens, when they devised monstrous cults, of Priapus and others? How great was the audacity of Mahomet, who utterly abolishes the entire doctrine of the prophets and apostles? But this audacity is not human, they are singular furies, sent into the breast of Mahomet by the devil. Nor is the audacity of the pontiffs and monks small, who defend manifest idols, such as the invocation of the dead and their masses. Wherefore let us learn to fear God, and

Let us restrain ourselves within the divinely prescribed boundaries, let us also curb the impulses of our talents and minds, lest by ambition, by emulation, Φιλονεικία we disturb true opinions.

AND the king of the North will come, and will surround cities with a rampart, and will take them, etc. Here the angel returns to the history of Antiochus the Great, whom he says will occupy many cities, while Ptolemy Epiphanes is reigning, but still a boy.

Briefly, however, he inserts a particle concerning Judea, which in those wars was vexed on both sides. And he will stand in the desirable land, and his hand will fail, that is, Antiochus the Great will come into Judea, and with Scopas the Egyptian leader defeated, he will impose tribute on the Jews. Whose hands will fail, that is, they will not fight back, because without any struggle they did what was commanded, they gave tribute on this agreement, that they might retain their laws and divinely delivered cults.

Again, therefore, the church is in servitude, and yet God so mitigates the calamity that the liberty of teaching and hearing the divine law remains, nor is the priesthood changed, nor are pious cults abolished. Nor is it without cause that this people is so often in servitude toward the end, even though it had promises concerning the liberty and preservation of his own polity. But that servitude of the miseries of the church in the final old age of the world is an image.

And he will come so that he may make friendship with him, and he will give him a daughter, so that he may destroy him, that is, Antiochus the Great will give a daughter to the Egyptian king, so that on that condition he may both make peace, and retain the cities of Syria, and increase the wealth of his kingdom. Which matter, however, deceived him, because after Antiochus the Great there followed a notable decline of the Syrian kingdom.

Furthermore, in the treaty made with the Egyptians at this time by Antiochus the Great, there was also this condition: Antiochus assigned a part of the tributes to the kingdom of Syria, and a part to his son-in-law Ptolemy Epiphanes. Thus kings divided among themselves the wealth of the church, just as now kings hand over the titles and revenues of bishops to their courtiers.

And he will turn his face to the islands, and will capture many, that is, he will sail into Greece, where some Greek cities will join themselves to him, as he moves war against the Romans. But a certain prince will make him cease with disgrace, says the angel, that is, a Roman consul will defeat him, and will force him to seek peace, and to accept harsh conditions, namely, that he should cede all of Asia, on this side of Mount Taurus.

Afterwards, he says, he will return to his cities and will perish. For when Antiochus the Great had returned to Syria, and on account of poverty was about to plunder a certain temple, he was killed there, when the common people had run together for the defense of the temple. This was the end of King Antiochus, whom either the restless nature of his character, or ambition, had inflamed to stir up many wars, although he experienced adverse fortune almost always. It is, however, the ruin of a republic to stir up unnecessary wars, especially when fortune is adverse.

Latin:

DE ANTIOCHO MAGNO & Ptolemaeo Epiphane.

Et revertetur rex Aquilonis, & adducet maiorem multitudinem quàm antea, id est, mortuo Philopatore, Antiochus magnus instaurabit bellum, sperans facilem fore victoriam, quòd aetas Ptolemaei Epiphanis nondum idonea esset rebus gerendis. Quanquam autem senatus Romanus tutelam regis Aegyptii susceperat, tamen quia sociorum auxilia solent esse tardiora, Antiochus magnus recuperavit Syriam & Phoenicen, profligato exercitu, quem Aegyptius in Syriam, duce Scopa, miserat.

Et in tempore illo multi stabunt contra regem Aegypti, id est, Antiochus magnus faciet foedus cum Philippo rege Macedonico, qui mittet auxilia adversus Aegyptios.

Et filii praevaricatores populi tui extollentur, ut impleant visionem, & corruent. Quia in his bellis Syriam utrinque populabantur, & Syriaci exercitus & Aegyptii, defecerunt à lege divina multi Iudaei, qui militabant apud impios reges, ut testatur & tertius liber Machabaeorum. Et magna multitudo Iudaeorum commigravit in Aegyptum, ut tranquilliore loco sedes quaereret.

Erat tunc pontifex Onias in urbe Ierosolyma, cuius virtus & pietas celebratur in secundo libro Machabaeorum. Huic pontificatum eripere Iason frater conabatur, cuius postea mentio fit in historia Machabaeorum. Haec certamina filium Oniae pontificis Oniam moverunt, ut ex patria in Aegyptum secederet, quòd domi à patruo sibi insidias strui sensisset. Cum igitur magna in Aegypto multitudo esset Iudaeorum, & esset unà pontificis fillius Onias, ut exilium minus triste esset, nacti sedes Heliopoli, politiam Iudaicam ibi constituunt, & quasi Ierosolymam alteram tenentes, templum ibi condunt sacrificaturi ritu Levitico, cum tamen deus non nisi unicum seu tabernaculum seu templum esse voluerit, & plura condi expresse prohibuerit, & quidem nusquam nisi in terra Canaan condi voluerit. Ita doctrinae consensus facilius retineri poterat, & constabat quo loco vox dei sonaret & propagaretur. Etsi autem à Iudaeis qui Alexandriae erant, improbabatur aedificatio novi templi, tamen superstitio vicit.
