Ex hac imagine intelligi potest quid dicat textus, Non curabitστοργὴνmulierum, id est, confirmabit contumelias sexus muliebris. Est autem contumelia duplex. Altera est neglectio, quam in caelibe viro sequuntur multae turpes flammae & alia graviora peccata. Altera est, cum miserae mulieris corpus & animam contaminas. Nam omnis concubitus extra coniugium, est contumelia sexus. Non enim uteris eo iuxta dei ordinationem, imò etiam abstrahis hominem à deo, quae est summa contumelia.

David rapiens alienam coniugem, quantum in ipso fuit, non solum sese perdidit, sed etiam illam miseram à deo avulsit, quae tunc quidem non vidit, quo modo ex hoc scelere eluctatura esset.

Accedunt deinde poenae ingentes, quibus plectuntur impudici. Ut adulterium Davidis secuta sunt domestica parricidia, exilium, seditio, stupra coniugum, caedes civium. Haec mala erant atroces contumeliae, quae ita deformabant Davidem, ut gloriam ingentem, qua floruerat, amittere se sentiret ipse.

In his exemplis cogitemus, quid sit contumelia sexum adficere, & discamus reverenter uti sexu, iuxta ordinationem dei, frenemus libidines, vitemus poenas, caveamus, ne polluta conscientia impediamus invocationem. Denique; legem iniustam, quae incendit vagas libidines, tanquam diaboli laqueum excutiamus. NamPaulus prohibitionem coniugii expressè nominat doctrinam daemoniorum. Illud etiam adiiciendum est, peculiarem poenam esse cultus idolorum, labes libidinum, ut primo capite ad Romanos significatur.

Postea hîc in textu repetita est descriptio contemptus dei, cum ait, Super universa magnificabit se. Describit furorem Epicuraeum, qui omnium malorum summum & extremum est, fingere nihil esse deum, nihil esse providentiam, fingi posse ad utilitatem praesentem religiones arbitrio potentum.

Mirum est autem, cum hic furor tantum malum sit, ut magnitudinem eloqui nemo possit, tamen innumeŕabiles homines delectari Epicuraeis opinionibus. Sed haec scelera reteget & puniet filius dei, cum se ostendet mundo, & resuscitabit mortuos, ut ecclesiam ornet gloria, & impios in aeternos cruciatus abiiciat.

ET deum Maosim in loco dei venerabitur, & deum, quem nescierunt patres sui, colet auro, &c. Et praesidiis Maosim defendentibus deum alienum quem elegit, dabit magnam gloriam.

Intelligo simpliciter deum Maosim deum templorum variorum commenticium, alium à deo, qui se in uno verbo & cultu populi Israel patefecit. Nam ut suprà templum populi Israel vocat Maosa, id est, arcem, ita plurali vocat gentium aliarum templa Maosim, id est, arces seu munitiones, quia suum cuique templum, suus cuique cultus, seu deus, arx est.

Populo Israel arx erat templum, verbum et cultus, propter haec iubebantur petere & expectare defensionem. Ita nobis Evangelium arx est, propter id speramus & certò statuimus, ecclesiam non prorsus interituram esse. Acuera arx est unica, unus est verè deus aeternus pater Iesu Christi, qui se in promissionibus suis inde usque ab initio patefecit. Hanc nos habemus unicam arcem, unum deum patrem Iesu Christi, unum Evangelium, unum cultum.

Sed ethnici finxerunt innumerabiles. Attici colebant Palladem. Thebani, Bacchum. Eluesinii, Cererem. Siculi, Proserpinam. Argivi, Iunonem. Delii, Latonam. Delphici, Phoebum. Lemnii, Vulcanum. Lampsaceni, Priapum. Phrygii, Cybelen. Denique finis nullus erat fingendorum numinum & cultuum.

Quanquam autem haec numina prorsus commenticia erant, tamen humana coecitas, cum finxit sibi ceremonias, confidit his suis operibus, & fiducia confirmatur successibus. Ut Romani quia vincebant caeteras gentes, cum ivuarentur divinitus, existimabant etiam se verè deum colere. Simili argumento confirmantur Turci. Porrò in tanta caligine humanae naturae, vehementer moventur animi fortuna et successibus. Sed nos scimus deum agnoscendum esse per verbum suum, et restare aliud iudicium, in quo ecclesia ornabitur gloria.

Intelligo igitur deum Maosim, deum variorum templorum ethnicorum commenticium, alium ab eo qui in uno templo colitur, in quo sonat vox dei, seu Evangelium. Ita Antiochus Iovis Olympii statuam Ierosolymis coluit, Iovis Cretaei statuam Samariae, Herculem in Tyrio templo, Adonidem in vicinis.

Hae commenticiae adpellationes, etsi nihil sunt nisi inanes tituli statuarum, tamen dicebantur dii illorum variorum templorum. Ita mihi videtur adpellatio Maosim ex antithesi interpretanda, ut sit Maosa verum templum unicum, Maosim verò multa ethnica diversarum statuarum & cultuum.

Alii rationem adpellationis hanc esse iudicant, deum Maosim dici, quòd vi & armis cultus ille constitutus & defensus sit. Antiochus armis repressit Iudaeos, cum statuam in templo collocaret, postea addidit praesidia, quae cogebant populum ad hanc statuam colendam. Haec interpretatio etiam mihi concinna videtur.

Nunc accommoda ad Antichristum. Mahometus commenticium numen reiecto verno dei, colit, & vi ac armis sua mendacia & propagare & defendere solet.

Quid fit in regno pontificio? Nomen Maosim alludit ad missam, & adfine est nomen Mazon, cibus seu panis. Praecipuum verò idolum est in regno pontificio, multiplex prophanatio cenae domini. Deinde sunt & alia multa, statuae innumerabiles, ad quas concurritur, tanquam ibi deus magis propitius sit, aut ibi velint exaudire animae sanctorum.

Et haec ethnica, quae in textu describuntur, colet auro & argento deum commenticium, ubi fiunt impudentius quàm in pompis, in quibus circumfertur & adoratur panis? Et ethnico more praecedunt magna agmina, quae gestantstatuas aureas, argenteas, gemmatas. O tristia & execranda spectacula.

Deinde adpellatio idoli Maosim, complectitur omnes errores pugnantes cum Evangelio. Cultus sine vera cognitione Evangelii, est ethnicus. Ut invocabat numen aliquod aeternum Xenophon, sed dubitans, ita invocant deum cum dubitatione, hi qui reiiciunt doctrinam de fide. Item, ut Argiva sacerdos invocat Iunonem fiducia sui sacrificii, & somniat propter hos ritus servari totam gentem Argolicam, sic Franciscanus invocat deum fiducia suorum rituum, & totas civitates propter tales ritus servari putat, id quod saepe audivi iactari.

Et si autem haec idola, hi errores, hae imposturae, magna autoritate defenduntur, et in magna parte orbis terrarum haerebunt usque ad finem, tamen admonendi sunt homines, de praecepto divino, quod est immutabile, Fugite idola. Fugiant pii & execrentur illa spectacula prophanatae cenae domini, mortuorum invocationem, & contumelias doctrinae Evangelii. Fugiant societatem saevitiae pontificum, & discant ex Evangelio veram invocationem, & vera officia quae deus postulat. Haec moneri ecclesiam, necesse est, etiamsi fractus illabatur orbis.

Quòd verò in textu additur, daturum esse opes, his, qui tuentur deum alienum, non est obscura sententia. Ut Antiochus tradebat pontificatum Epicuraeis sacerdotibus, qui prophanationem templi ridebant, ita nunc honores et opes dantur his, qui fortiter seu scriptis, seu sententiis tuentur idola, & fingunt praetextus, Etiamsi quid vitii sit in ritibus, tamen autoritatem episcoporum non labefactandam esse, non tollendum esse ordinem, qui cum imperio aliquo religionem regat. Sed has disputationes suprà attigi, & hac una voce prolata, apud pios facile refutantur, Fugite idola.

ET in tempore finis, geret bellum cum et rex Austri, & instar turbinis veniet super illum rex Aquilonis.

Haec postrema pars plus habet obscuritatis, et aliquia de bellis ultimae senectae mundi significat. Et ut Ezechiel praedicit vastationem futuram per Gog & Magog, ita hoc loco angelus significat barbariem regnantem ultimo tempore, scilicet, Turcicam, versus Austrum, longius progressuram esse, & venturam esse in terram desiderabilem, videlicet, in loca verae ecclesiae. Magnum profectò periculum nobis denunciatur, sed addentur postea consolationes.

Porrò historica quaedam de Antiocho admixta esse, res ostendit. Haec prius recitabo. Duraverunt post Epiphanem bella cum Aegyptiis. Etsi autem Aegyptus non est deinceps occupata à Syriacis regibus, tamen in illis duabus expeditionibus, quarum suprà facta est mentio, Antiochus aliquas Aegypti civitates & vexavit & cepit, praedatus est & ex vicinia, ex Cyrenaica quam vocat hîc Lybiam, & ex proxima Acthiopia, quam vocat Cusim. Sed haec bella Antiochi non adflixerunt Idumaeos, imò salutaria eis fuerunt. Nam Hircanus postea domuit Idumaeos, & hac conditione cum eis pacem & societatem fecit, ut veram de deo doctrinam amplecterentur. Haec recitat Iosephus in narratione rerum gestarum ab Hircano, quare & Danielis dictum de hac historia vaticinari iudico.

Sunt & haec historica, Rumores terrebunt cum ab Oriente, & veniet usque ad finem suum, & nemo ei auxiliabitur. Talis enim fuit Antiochi exitus. Audierat in Perside profligatos esse suos exercitus, & videbat valde diminutam esse Syriaci regni potentiam, defectione Persarum, Parthorum & Babylonis. Has provincias amisit, dum alibi impium & infoelix bellum gerit in Iudaea. Hi remores defectionis & seditionum, qui ex Oriente, ab Aquilone adferebantur, ita moverunt animum eius, ut vel metu ac desperatione rerum suarum, vel moesticia extinctus sit.

Eodem pertinet, quòd ait neminem ei opem tulisse. Nam provinciae ab eo defecerant, & bellum ei inferabant, Iudaea verò iam victrix, etiam pellebat Syriacos exercitus. Ut autem tune deus frenum iniecit tyranno grassanti, & oppressit iam molientem novum & maius bellum, ita saepe mirabili modo liberat ecclesiam.

In latina versione legitur, Et figet tabernaculum suum Apedno, inter duo maria super montem inclytum & sanctum.

Hîc sciant studiosi, hanc peregrinam vocent non esse nomen proprium, sed fuisse vertendam hoc modo, Et figet tabernaculum palatii suiNam apedna significat palatium, seu regiam, seu, ut vulgo loquimur, aulam. Significat enim Antiochum in loco sancto, ubi erat templum dei, castra facturum esse, cum videlicet in templo collocasset idolum, & id praesidiis defenderet. Sed quia addit, inter duo maria, significat aliquid de domicilio Antichristi.

DIFFICILIS est autem accommodatio huius postremae partis in vaticinio, quare breviter quaedam communia dicemus. In ultima mundi senecta, Mahometica tyrannis figet tabernaculum sui palatii inter duo maria, ut Bizantii regiam Turcicam esse donstat, inter Aegeum mare, & Euxinum, quo in loco prius Ecclesia dei fuit, ac inde ex Aquilone progrediens, latius vastabit ecclesiam. Sed Idumaei aliqui erunt, id est, in regionibus horridis & asperis, erit adhuc sedes aliqua ecclesiarum dei. Non opprimet totum orbem Turciea tyrannis, non enim erit quinta Monarchia, etsi horrendas vastitates faciet.

Tandem verò eam rumores ex Oriente terrebunt. Reprimetur, non humanis viribus, sed divinitus. Res ostendit iam annis ducentis, reges Europae raro feliciter dimicasse cum Turcis.

Quanquam autem mansurum est regnum Gog & Magog, id est, Turcicum, usque ad mundi finem, ut Apocalypsis monet, tamen ante finem aliqua fiet inclinatio regni Turcici, ne ecclesiam dei prorsus obruat. Et fiet ea inclinatio divinitus, sicut postea dicet, filium dei dimicaturum esse pro populo suo. Stabit, inquit, dux magnus, Michael, id est Christus, pro populo suo.

SIMILIA fient & in regno pontificio. Aula pontificis est inter duo maria, Adriaticum & Tyrrhenum. Et quidem haec aula titulo ipso profitetur se in ecclesia dominari, cum eam latè prophanaverit idolis. Sed Idumaei aliqui, taxabunt errores, id est, homines aerumnosi, quos episcopi tenentes ordinariam potestatem, tanquam alienos à populo dei, aspernabuntur, ut Iudaei contemnebant Idumaeos, Tales aliquos pios & doctos viros subinde deus excitavit, qui Evangelii doctrinam repurgârunt, idola reiecerunt, & ecclesiam de vera invocatione docuerunt.

Etsi autem doctrinae certamina labefactabunt aut oritatem episcoporum, tamen mansurae sunt reliquiae pontificii regni. Nec armis constituetur melior status, sed quo magis illucescet veritas, eo magis ruet autoritas eorum, qui idola defendunt.

Sciant igitur scholastici, fore doctrinae certamina,& ad haec se praeparent, confirment animos Evangelii cognitione, & sciant sibi ducem Christum adfuturum esse, qui, ut infrà dcitur, hoc ipso tempore stat pro filiis populi sui, id est, defendit & servat eos, ne à diabolo & eius organis prorsus opprimantur & extinguantur. Quòd enim deus subinde excitaturus sit aliquos, qui idola taxaturi sint, & quod sint futura doctrinae certamina, testatur dictum suprà recitatum, Docti docebunt multos. Cogitent igitur pii scholastici, deum praecipuè hoc officium flagitare à genere humano, aidelicet studium discendi & explicandi Evangelii, sicut Christus inquit, In hoc glorificatur pater meus, ut fructum copiosum feratis, & fiatis mei discipuli, id est, discatis & propagetis Evangelium. Cogitent poenas, quas deus hisqui studia piae doctrinae negligunt, minatur, cum ait, Quia repulisti scientiam, repellam te. Econtra doctis hîc caelestia & aeterna praemia proponuntur, Qui ad iustitiam multos erudiunt, fulgebunt ut stellae in perpetua aeternitate.

## 12:1 Argument
CHAPTER XII.

But at that time, Michael the great prince shall arise, who stands for the children of thy people: and there shall come a time, such as has not been from the time, from which nations began to be, even until that time. And at that time, thy people shall be saved, everyone who shall be found written in the book. And many of those, who sleep in the dust of the earth, shall awake, some to life everlasting, and others to reproach, to see always. But they that are learned shall shine as the brightness of the firmament: and they that instruct many to justice, as stars for all eternity. But thou, O Daniel, shut up the words, and seal the book, even to the time appointed: many shall pass over, and knowledge shall be manifold. And I Daniel looked, and behold, as it were two others stood: one on this side upon the bank of the river, and another on that side on the other bank of the river. And I said to the man that was clothed in linen, that stood upon the waters of the river: How long shall it be to the end of these wonders? And I heard the man that was clothed in linen, that stood upon the waters of the river, when he had lifted up his right hand, and his left hand to heaven, and had sworn by him that liveth for ever, that it shall be unto a time, and times, and a half. And when shall be completed when the dispersion of the hand of the holy people shall be accomplished, all these things shall be finished. And I heard, and I understood not. And I said: O my lord, what shall be after these things? And he said: Go, Daniel, because the words are shut up, and sealed until the appointed time. Many shall be chosen, and made white, and shall be tried as fire: and the wicked shall deal wickedly, and none of the wicked shall understand, but the learned shall understand. And from the time when the continual sacrifice shall be taken away, and the abomination unto desolation shall be set up, there shall be a thousand two hundred and ninety days. Blessed is he that waiteth, and cometh even to the thousand three hundred and thirty-five days. But go thou thy ways until the time appointed: and thou shalt rest, and stand in thy lot unto the end of the days.

THE LAST part of the prophecy, he says, will be huge tribulations for the church shortly before the resurrection. I said, however, above that we are forewarned, so that our minds may be strengthened, lest they fall away from God, either broken by the cruelty of the wicked, or by the examples and judgments of hypocrites. And it is always necessary for this doctrine to be known to us, and fixed in our minds, that the church is subject to the cross, and why it is subject, namely, because God wishes the wrath against sin, which the world despises, to be understood by the church. There are also other causes, which I do not recount now.

And as the nature of the human race, worn out by old age, is more languid, so the impulses toward virtue are more sluggish. What warriors, orators, and craftsmen does the world now have to be compared with the ancients? Therefore, in this weakness of nature, vices grow: sloth, love of pleasures, softness of mind, impatience, inconstancy, perfidy, foolishness as in those who are delirious, and the persuasion of one's own wisdom. From these fountains arise great tumults both in empires and in churches. The true church therefore becomes narrower. And this very church, as if by the feebleness of old age, has very many diseases.

David, looking forward to this last age.

He prays with the voice of the universal church, Psalm 71. Do not cast me off in the time of old age; when my strength fails, do not forsake me. It is a most sorrowful prayer, if one compares the times, and considers the miseries of the final age. Great were the calamities of the church at the time of the destruction of Jerusalem, but yet God raised up excellent governors in that very exile, Daniel, Ezekiel, Haggai, Zechariah, Ezra, and adorned them with great glory. And although God has scattered some flame of the Gospel in this last age, yet the governors are weak.

Let the pious therefore acknowledge the distresses of the church, and for the sake of the glory of God, and their own salvation and the public necessity, let them apply themselves more keenly to the study of the Gospel, and with their whole heart call upon God, that He may preserve, defend, and increase the church.

Four consolations, moreover, are delivered in this place, which ought always to be in the sight of all the pious.

THE FIRST is, that the church is not to perish utterly, but is to endure even then in those dangers. For therefore he says, The learned shall teach many.

Here other testimonies concerning the perpetual preservation of the church must be considered. As Christ says, I am with you, even unto the consummation of the world.

AndIsaiah chap. 59.This is my covenant with them, says the Lord. My spirit that is in you, and my words, which I have put in your mouth, shall not depart out of your mouth, nor out of the mouth of your seed, says the Lord, forever.

It is a clear description of the church, which warns of both things, that it is going to remain, and what kind of assembly it is, namely, one in which the voice of the Gospel sounds, and the Holy Spirit rules hearts.

The second consolation is that there will be members of the church there, wherever those embracing the pure doctrine of the Gospel will be. For there will be, as he says here, a dispersion of the people.

Under the Turkish empire, under other kings and pontiffs, enemies of the pure doctrine of the Gospel, some pious ones will be dispersed here and there. Let these hold to this consolation, that the church is not a polity bound to certain kingdoms, but that all, wherever they are, who call upon God by the true knowledge of Christ and faith, and flee idols, are members of the church of God. By opinion, by will, by confession let each one join himself with the other members.

These sayings teach it, My sheep hear my voice. Likewise, if you abide in me, and my words abide in you, ask whatever you will, and it shall be done for you. Likewise, Wherever two or three are gathered in my name, I am in the midst of them.

These sentences have been handed down for this reason, that the scattered pious may have consolation, and may not think that the church is a polity, bound to one empire, but to the voice of the Gospel and the true invocation of God.

THIRD consolation, that in these such great dangers the church will have the Son of God as a defender. Therefore it is said here in the text, At that time Michael the great prince shall stand for the children of his people.

By this voice let all the pious confirm themselves, which indeed Christ himself also inculcates to us, saying, I am with you even to the consummation of the world. In such great dissipation, in such great miseries, the pious seem deserted by God. The successes of the Turkish tyranny are so great that God seems to have forgotten his church. Great are the riches, great is the power of the other enemies of the Gospel.

Under these everywhere there are some pious, truly invoking God, and professing the Gospel, who are seized for punishments, as if they were seditious. Although the impious think this is neglected by God, yet it is certain that they have a helper and defender, the Son of God. He drives away the devil, so that he cannot utterly destroy the whole assembly of the pious, and he confirms those who are seized for punishments with the Holy Spirit, so that they do not fall away from the doctrine, as he says, I will not leave you orphans.

I said, however, above, that the Son of God has always been present to his church. Therefore, he is called here the great leader Michael, for thus he names the Son of God. The appellation is well-known. Who is like God, that is, how great is he, who is like God, who is the image of the eternal Father, powerful, merciful, a liberator, an avenger.

Therefore, so that we may rouse ourselves to invocation and faith, let us diligently impress upon ourselves these sayings, which testify that the Son of God is present to his church, defending us, bringing us aid. And let us cast away those dreams of human reason, which imagines that God is far away, sitting idle in heaven, such as many things which are invented in poems, for this very reason, because the human mind without the light of heavenly doctrine and without faith, hallucinates in this way about God in all things. But the true knowledge of God is kindled in the pious mind through the Gospel, and

it is confirmed by constant invocation.

THE FOURTH consolation is that which the angel proposes here also, since miseries will not be perpetual, we may bear them more easily with this hope, because a glorious liberation and eternal joy is promised to the pious, but eternal torments are denounced to the impious.

Therefore Daniel says here, Many shall rise from the dust of the earth, some to eternal glory, others to everlasting torments. To this let similar testimonies concerning eternal life be added. For we bear the miseries of this life more easily when we look forward to the goal, as it were, and know that the church will at some time struggle out of the existing evils.

THEREFORE the pious in all ages have also inquired about the time, when the church shall reign, with all the impious oppressed? how long this confusion, in which the impious dominate, will similarly endure. Here the time is indicated, Immediately, he says, after these struggles, after the dispersion of the holy people, the resurrection of the dead shall take place. We see now indeed that the people of God are miserably scattered, wherefore the resurrection of the dead is not far off.

The Evangelists call it the last hour, this the time which remained until the end of this world from then on by the preaching of the apostles. It is therefore necessary that it be shorter than the age which preceded it.

THE LIMITS are established by the wonderful counsel of God, and although Christ says that that day is known to the Father alone, and does not wish us to inquire curiously about a certain day or year, but always to be prepared as if on watch, to await that most joyful day on which He will show Himself to the whole human race, and will triumph with His church, yet the brevity of the times of this world has been signified in various ways.

To Daniel God showed the series of Monarchies and kingdoms, which it is certain is now running to its end. Four Monarchies have passed. There remains the savage kingdom arisen in the fourth Monarchy, namely, the Turkish, which will not equal the power of the Roman Monarchy. Since therefore it has now almost come to its summit, it is necessary that it will soon fall, and then that day will dawn on which the dead will be recalled to life.

IT IS CELEBRATED also the saying of the prophet Elijah, which ought to be most known to all, and it was useful to be written on all walls, in all vestibules.

The world shall last six thousand years, and afterwards its destruction.

Two thousand, void. Two thousand, the law. Two thousand, the Messiah.

Even if anything should be lacking from these years, it will be lacking on account of our sins.

It is certain, however, that Christ was born around the end of the fourth millennium. And already 1542 years have passed. We are not, therefore, far from the end. And the saying of Elijah, and the sayings of Christ, signify nevertheless that this time is to be shortened, just as the course toward the flood was shortened, so that wickedness might be cut off more quickly.

HE ASKS and Daniel here concerning the time of the end. And a number is set, which, although it seems to prophesy concerning the wars of the Maccabees, nevertheless without doubt signifies something concerning the end of this world. And the application is easy, if you change days into years. There will be, however, 2625 years.

Nor indeed do we seek the very article, or moment, in which the last day will dawn, which is not to be scrutinized. But because this number most aptly agrees with the saying of Elijah, I judge that the years of the world remaining are signified by Daniel. The years are six hundred or thereabouts, from Daniel to the birth of Christ. The remaining are two millennia, the final age of this world.

The division also fits. For the former number is distinguished from the latter so that it may be signified that the beginnings of the final impious and hypocritical Mahometan kingdom are to come soon after the first number. For Mahomet began to reign after the birth of Christ, in the year six hundred and thirty. Now if you compute the time from the age of Daniel, the first number is completed. The beginnings of ecclesiastical errors were almost the same. For then superstitions, masses, invocations of the dead, and vows grew little by little. The following number, therefore, is the course of the impious kingdom, up to the end of the world.

I affirm nothing in this comparison, but I recite conjectures. For although God wished us to be reminded by this prediction, nevertheless He does not wish the articles of times to be established. Let us therefore be content with a certain common admonition, and let us pray to God that He may rule and preserve His church, and as soon as possible break off this course of human nature, drive away the darkness of this life, and restrain all the impious, raging with contempt for God, and show Himself openly to His church, so that He may henceforth be celebrated with eternal praises.

I have reviewed the consolations which the angel delivered in this place, which indeed Christ also repeated in His final sermons. But let this doctrine or precept be added, namely, that since in this old age of the world, many delusions of doctrine have been and will be, let us be vigilant and constant in retaining the purity of the Gospel. There is one doctrine, the first of all, delivered through the prophets, Christ, and the apostles, in which the eternal God has revealed His will. And those who seek God without that doctrine wander away from Him.

To this, therefore, He calls us, and promises that His church will be there only, and that He will hear us there, where the voice of the Gospel sounds, as John 15 it is said, If you abide in me, and my words abide in you, whatever you wish, you shall ask, and it shall be done for you. Let us therefore constantly retain this doctrine, and with it shining before us, let us call upon God, and ask that He may rule and preserve us.

Now I return to the historical matters. The number, although it has a hidden meaning, nevertheless corresponds to the history of the Maccabees, as above in chapter 8 He predicted the time of the war, namely six years, three months, and twenty days. Thus in this place he distinguishes that number, signifying the different moments of the war, and discerning the time of the profaned temple from the following time, in which the Syrian garrisons were entirely driven out from all of Judea. He says, therefore, From the time of the removed sacrifice, and the placed idol, there will be one thousand two hundred and ninety days, that is, three years, six months, and fifteen days.

He signifies that this first intensification of the war will be more difficult for the pious. For during this three-year period the pious were in the greatest calamity. The Syrian armies held all the citadels, they were slaughtering the unarmed multitude in the towns and fields, they were dragging old men and women to punishments, and for some time there were neither any armies nor leaders who could repress this cruelty. Afterwards, Mattathias and Judas began to repress this cruelty a little. There fortune inclined, the Syrians were driven out, they could not rage as before. For in very fierce battles, the temple was recovered after the first three years. The remaining matters were easier.

Therefore, concerning the outcome of the war he says, Blessed is he who waits, and reaches one thousand three hundred and thirty-five days, that is, three years and eight months. We shall therefore encompass seven years and almost three months.

For in the year 145 after the death of Alexander, an idol was placed in the temple, in the month of November, when a little before Antiochus had begun to exercise cruelty of every kind against the Jews. Afterwards in the year 151 in the month of February, Nicanor was defeated, who had most proudly threatened the temple. This was the last victory of Judas Maccabaeus, which so raised up Judea, that although the Syrians renewed the war several times, yet they were easily repelled. In the following year, which was the seventh, Jonathan and Simon recovered all of Judea, and drove out the Syrian garrisons from the Jewish towns on every side. The prediction of the number therefore agrees with the time of the war, I think this should be considered here first, afterwards let the fitting meanings be sought.

The last particle in the text is to be diligently observed, because the Angel says expressly that these prophecies pertain to the last age of the world. You shall rest and stand in your lot at the end of the days. These words, although others have twisted them elsewhere, yet clearly signify that the doctrine of Daniel embraces all the times of the world, up to the resurrection of the dead, because he prophesies in order concerning the Monarchies, and the last kingdoms, and concerning the miseries of the church of the latter time. Therefore he says, You shall stand in your lot, you shall teach and confirm the church in the last time of the world.

This particle ought to exhort good minds to the reading of Daniel. Nor is there no reason why God wishes the church to be forewarned about its own miseries. The minds of many are moved by the multitude of the impious, they are moved also by fortune and glory, and they argue that so many other nations ignorant of the Gospel, and indeed flourishing in empires and glory, are not neglected by God, especially since among them also there are many who excel in virtue. Minds are offended also by many other arguments.

Therefore God forewarns us, so that we may know that the church will be small, and that we may firmly believe that this one doctrine, handed down through the prophets, Christ, and the apostles, is saving, and that the church is nowhere else, except in that assembly which embraces this doctrine. Let us confirm ourselves with this consolation, which, when diligently pondered, greatly helps pious minds.

τέλος, δόξα θεῶ.

THE DICTUM OF THE PROPHET ELIJAH, of which mention is made on page 374. It exists in the book, סנהדרין Chapter 11. which begins, כל ישראל ישלהם חלק לעולם הבא׃ And in the book, עבודה זרה Chapter one, which begins, לפני אידהן

תנא רבית אליהוששת אלפים שנה הוי עלמאשני אלפים תוהושני אלפים תורהשני אלפים ימות המשיח

TRADITION OF THE HOUSE OF ELIJAH.

Six thousand years, the world. Two thousand, Void. Two thousand, Law. Two thousand, the days of the Messiah.

These are the six days of the week before God, according to that which is in Psalm. 90. And in 2. Pet. 3. A thousand years in your eyes are as one day.

The seventh day is the eternal Sabbath, concerning which Psalm. 95. & Hebr. 4. To this pertains also that saying of Christ, My Father is still working etc.

But there has been added in the Talmudic books to this saying of Elijah such a sentence, ובעונותינו שרבו יצאו מה שיצאו This is, But on account of our sins, which are many, there are missing from these years, those which are missing. Which sentence, although it has no inconvenience among us, when we interpret it through that which Christ said, that those days must be shortened on account of the elect, that is, that the end must be accelerated by the multitude and abundance of sins, and the pious must be snatched from danger, nevertheless because the Jews usurp this appendix to defend their own impiety, as if the coming of the Messiah is deferred on account of their sins, therefore it seemed good to warn pious readers, that that sentence is not of Elijah, nor of his disciples, but is an addition and a fiction of some Rabbi infatuating himself and his own, of which kind many others are found. Which thing also before us Porchetus noted, showing that this appendix is found otherwise in another place, which nevertheless now in corrupted printed codices is read everywhere in the same way.

You who marvel at riches, you who marvel at yellow gold, the wealthy gifts of the Pactolus or the Tagus, and you who search the cold waters, search the sands, and the wells, mountains, deep pools, and the sea, and you who marvel at the foreign Seres and Indians and carry the wares of the sea through blind shallows, why do you not rather marvel at eternal honors and seek the wealthy gifts of the heaven above? Why do you not read through the heavenly books with a studious mind, and gather good gems from them? Believe me, the final times of the world about to collapse are already before the doors and at your doors. These things could be said neither more conveniently nor more learnedly anywhere than they have been commemorated in this book, which the renowned muse of the prophet Daniel wrote, and which he wishes you to always have in your hand. For those about to prophesy, what kind of empire will that of Christ be? And does it proceed by human strengths? How turbulent will that status of the extreme time be, and what is the image of the pious assembly? And how sad and miserable ruins of kingdoms will this last order of time drag along with it?

ON DANIEL, ILLUSTRATED BY PHILIP MELANCHTHON, AN ELEGY BY GASPAR BRUSCH, POET LAUREATE, TO THE READER.

Does the renowned muse of Melanchthon recount these and more things, and teach in what manner all things were made? Does it explain the histories of various nations, and add what punishment has always followed the wicked? What rewards God has given to the upright, what rewards to the just, what kind of life will be for the pious after this one? Who is the savior? And who can free us from so many waves and reconcile us to an angry God? We ought to have preferred these gems, these golden threads, to the goods of the whole world. And that stone will be to me, or iron, or the Marpesian rock, harder than any oak, and harder than rocks, and more sluggish than the brutes themselves, to whom this golden book can be displeasing. Which now comes forth to you, studious reader, more recent, expressed by the diligence of a busy hand. Which you ought not to lay down from your hands sooner than black death drags you into its snares. Then let it also always cling to your mindful mind, since it teaches and contains so many good things. You also, Melanchthon, who give us such writings and illustrate the true dogmas of the Father God, and instruct the rude peoples and the falling world, live and be well for Pylian days, unharmed. The sunset and the sunrise will always praise you, and indeed the whole assembly of the Olympian choir.

If there are any times also, after these of ours, that are to come, these too shall be pleasing to your merits. The eighth of April was the date.

In the argument of the book of Daniel, line 14, read 'caeteris' instead of 'certis'. Page 21, line 10, 'cutem', read 'autem'. In the same place, line 14, 'ferreum', read 'ferrum'. Page 121, in the margin read, 'Regnum Othomanicum'. Page 302, line 5 from the end, 'vicinia Cetorum', read 'Ceteorum'. LEIPZIG, printed by Nicolaus Wolrab, 1543.

ERRATA.

Latin:

CAPUT XII.

In tempore autem illo, consurget Michael princeps magnus, qui stat pro filiis populi tui: & veniet tempus, quale non fuit ab eo, ex quo gentes esse coeperunt, usque ad tempus illud. Et in tempore illo, salvabitur populus tuus, omnis qui inventus fuerit scriptus in libro. Et multi de his, qui dormiunt in terrae pulvere, evigilabunt, alii in vitam aeternam, alii in opprobrium, ut videant semper. Qui autem docti fuerint, fulgebunt quasi splendor firmamenti: & qui ad iustitiam erudiunt multos, quasi stellae in perpetuas aeternitates. Tu autem Daniel, claude sermones, & signa librum usque ad tempus statutum: plurimi pertransibunt, et multiplex erit scientia. Et vidi ego Daniel, & ecce, quasi duo alii stabant: unus hinc super ripam fluminis, & alius inde ex altera ripa fluminis. Et dixi viro, qui erat indutus lineis, qui stabat super aquas fluminis: Usquequo finis horum mirabilium? Et audivi virum, qui indutus erat lineis, qui stabat super aquas fluminis, cum elevasset dexteram & sinistram suam in caelum, & iurasset per viventem in aeternum, quia in tempus & tempora, & dimidium temporis. Et cum completa fuerit dispersio manus populi sancti, complebuntur universa haec. Et ego audivi, et non intellexi. Et dixi: dominemi, quia erit post haec? Et ait: Vade Daniel, quia clausi sunt, signatique sermones, usque ad praefinitum tempus. Eligentur, & dealbabuntur, & quasi ignis probabuntur multi: & impiè agent impii, neque intelligent omnes impii, porrò docti intelligent. Et à tempore cum ablatum fuerit iuge sacrificium, & posita fuerit abominatio in desolationem, dies mille ducenti nonaginta. Beatus qui expectat & pervenit usque ad dies mille trecentos trigintaquinque. Tu autem Daniel, vade ad praefinitum: & requiesces, & stabis in sorte tua, in finem dierum.

EXTREMA pars vaticinii, ait paulò ante resurrectionem futuras ingentes aerumnas ecclesiae. Dixi autem suprà praemoneri nos, ut animi confirmentur, ne deficiant à deo, vel saevicia fracti impiorum, vel exemplis & iudiciis hypocritarum. Ac semper oportet nobis notam esse, & infixam animis hanc doctrinam, quòd ecclesia sit subiecta cruci, & cur sit subiecta, videlicet, quia vult deus ab ecclesia intelligi iram adversus peccatum, quam mundus contemnit. Sunt & aliae caussae, quas nunc non recenseo.

Et ut languidior est natura generis humani confecta senio, ita impetus ad virtutem sunt segniores. Quos habet iam mundus bellatores, oratores, artifices conferendos veteribus? Crescunt igitur in hac infirmitate naturae vitia, ignavia, amor voluptatum, mollicies animi, impatientia, inconstantia, perfidia, stulticia ut in delirantibus, persuasio propriae sapientiae. Ex his fontibus oriuntur & in imperiis & in ecclesiis magni tumultus. Fit igitur vera ecclesia angustior. Et haec ipsa, tanquam senectae imbecillitate, plurimum habet morborum.

Hanc ultimam aetatem David prospiciens.

orat voce universae ecclesiae,Psalmo. 71.Ne proiicias me tempore senectutis, cum defecerit virtus mea, ne derelinquas me. Tristissima oratio est, si quis tempora conferat, & consideret ultimae aetatis miserias. Magnae erant calamitates ecclesiae, tempore excidii Ierosolymitani, Sed tamen excitavit deus excellentes gubernatores in ipso exilio, Danielem, Ezechielem, Haggaeum, Zachariam, Esdram, & hos ornavit ingenti gloria. Etsi autem deus aliquam Evangelii flammam sparsit hac ultima aetate, tamen gubernatores sunt infirmi.

Agnoscant igitur pii ecclesiae aerumnas, & propter gloriam dei, & propriam salutem & publicam necessitatem, acrius incumbant in Evangelii studium, & toto pectore deum invocent, ut ecclesiam conservet, defendat & augeat.

Quatuor autem consolationes hoc loco traduntur, quae piis omnibus semper in conspectu esse debent.

PRIMA est, quòd ecclesia non sit penitus interitura, sed tunc quoque in illis periculis duratura. Ideo enim inquit, Docti docebunt multos.

Hîc cogitanda sunt & alia testimonia de perpetua conservatione ecclesiae. Ut Christus inquit, Ego vobiscum sum, usque ad consummationem mundi.

EtEsaias cap. 59.Hoc foedus meum cum eis, dicit dominus. Spiritus meus qui est in te, & verba mea, quae posui in ore tuo, non recedent ab ore tuo, nec ab ore seminis tui, dicit dominus, usque in sempiternum.

Luculenta descriptio est ecclesiae, quae utrumque monet, & mansuram esse, & qualis coetus sit, scilicet, in quo sonat vox Evangelii, & spiritus sanctus regit pectora.

Secunda consolatio, quod ibi sint futura ecclesiae membra, ubicunque erunt amplectentes puram Evangelii doctrinam. Erit enim, ut hîc inquit, dispersio populi.

Sub Turcico imperio, sub aliis regibus & pontificibus, inimicis purae doctrinae Evangelii, passim dispersi erunt aliqui pii. Hi teneant hanc consolationem, quòd ecclesia non sit politia certis regnis alligata, sed quòd omnes, ubicunque sunt, qui deum vera agnitione Christi & fide invocant, & idola fugiunt, sint membra ecclesiae dei. Sententia, voluntate, confessione se quisque cum caeteris membris coniungat.

Id docent dicta, Oves meae vocem meam audiunt. Item, si manseritis in me, & verba mea manserint in vobis, quicquid volueritis, petite, & fiet vobis. Item, Ubicunque duo aut tres sunt congregati in nomine meo, in medio eorum sum.

Hae sententiae ideo traditae sunt, ut pii disper si consolationem habeant, nec putent ecclesiam politiam ese, uni imperio alligatam, sed voci Evangelii & verae invocationi dei.

TERTIA consolatio, quòd in his tantis periculis habitura sit ecclesia defensorem filium dei. Ideo hîc in textu dicitur, Illo tempore stabit Micael dux magnus pro filiis populi sui.

Hac voce omnes pii se confirment, quam quidem & Christus ipse nobis inculat, inquiens, Ego vobiscum sum usque ad consummationem mundi. In tanta dissipatione, in tantis aerumnis, pii videntur deserti à deo. Successus Turcicae tyrannidis sunt tanti, ut videatur deus oblitus suae ecclesiae. Magnae sunt opes, magna est potentia & aliorum hostium Evangelii.

Sub his passim aliqui sunt pii, verè invocantes deum, & profitentes Evangelium, qui ad supplicia rapiuntur, tanquam seditiosi. Hoc etsi impii existimant à deo negligi, tamen certum est habere opitulatorem & propugnatorem filium dei. Hic depellit diabolum, ne totum coetum piorum prorsus delere possit, & eos qui ad supplicia rapiuntur, confirmat spiritu sancto, ne à doctrina deficiant, Sicut inquit, Non relinquam vos orphanos.

Dixi autem suprà, semper adfuisse filium dei ecclesiae suae. Ideo hîc vocatur dux magnus Micael, sic enim nominat filium dei. Adpellatio nota est. Quis sicut deus, id est, quantus est hic, qui est sicut deus, qui est imago aeterni patris, potens, misericors, liberator, vindex.

Itaque, ut excitemus nos ad invocationem & fidem, diligenter haec dicta nobis inculcemus, quae testantur adesse ecclesiae suae filium dei, nos defendere, nobis opem ferre. Et illa somnia humanae rationis abiiciamus, quae fingit deum procul abesse, ociosum sedere in caelo, Qualia multa in poematis ficta sunt, hanc ipsam ob caussam, quia mens humana sine luce doctrinae caelestis & sine fide, in omnibus ita hallucinatur de deo. Sed vera dei notitia, per Evangelium in mente pia accenditur, &

assidua invocatione confirmatur.

QUARTA consolatio est, quam hîc quoque proponit angelus, cum aerumnae non sint futurae perpetuae, hac spe facilius eas feramus, quòd piis promittitur gloriosa liberatio, & aeterna laeticia, Impiis verò denunciantur aeterni cruciatus.

Ideo hîc Daniel in quit, Multi resurgent ex pulvere terrae, alii ad aeternam gloriam, alii ad cruciatus sempiternos. Huc addantur similia testimonia de vita aeterna. Facilius enim ferimus huius vitae miserias, cum quasi metam prospicimus, & scimus aliquando ecclesiam extantis malis eluctaturam esse.

IDEO pii omnibus aetatibus, de tempore etiam quaesiverunt, quando ecclesia regnatu-a sit, oppressis omnibus impiis? quamdiu confusio haec, in qua dominantur impii, duratura similiter. Hîc tempus indicatur, Statim, inquit, post haec certamina, post dispersionem populi sancti, fiet resuscitatio mortuorum. Videmus nunc quidem miserè dissipatum esse populum dei, quare non procul abest resuscitatio mortuorum.

Evangelistae vocant novissimam horam, hoc tempus, quod ad finem huius mundi inde usque praedicatione apostolorum restabat. Necesse est igitur brevius esse, quàm fuit aetas quae praecessit.

METAE sunt temporum mirabili consilio dei constitutae, Et quanquam Christus diem illum soli patri notum esse inquit, nec vult nos curiosè quaerere certum diem aut annum, sed semper velut in statione paratos, expectare illum laetissimum diem, quo se ostendet universo humano generi, & cum sua ecclesia triumphabit, tamen brevitas temporum huius mundi, variè significata est.

Danieli deus monstravit seriem Monarchiarum & regnorum, quam certum est iam decurrere ad finem. Quatuor Monarchiae praeterierunt. Restat regnum saevum ortum in quarta Monarchia, videlicet, Turcicum, quod non aequabit potentiam Romanae Monarchiae. Cum igitur iam penè ad fastigium suum venerit, necesse est brevi ruiturum esse, ac tunc illucescet dies ille, quo mortui revocabuntur in vitam.

CELEBRATUR & dictum Eliae prophetae, quod omnibus notissimum esse debet, et in omnibus parietibus, in omnibus vestibulis scribi proderat.

Sex millia annorum mundus, & postea destructio.

Duo millia, inane.Duo millia, lex.Duo millia, Messiae.

Etsi quid ex his annis deerit, deerit propter nostra peccata.

Constat autem Christum natum esse circa finem quarti millenarii. Et iam exacti sunt anni 1542. Non igitur procul à fine absumus. Et dictum Eliae, ac Christi dicta, significant tamen de curtandum esse hoc tempus, sicut & curriculum ad diluvium decurtabatur, ut citius abrumpantur flagitia.

INTERROGAT & Daniel hîc de tempore finis. Et numerus ponitur, qui, etsi videtur de bellis Machabaeorum vaticinari, tamen haud dubie aliquid significat de fine huius mundi. Ac facilis est accommodatio, si dies in annos commutaveris. Erunt autem anni 2625.

Nec verò quaerimus articulum ipsum, seu momentum, quo illucescet ultimus dies, quod non est scrutandum. Sed quia hic numerus aptissimè congruit cum dicto Eliae, arbitror significari annos mundi à Daniele reliquos. Anni sexcenti sunt aut circiter, à Daniele ad natum Christum. Reliqui sunt duo millenarii, postrema huius mundi aetas.

Congruit & divisio. Ideo enim prior numerus à posteriore discernitur, ut significetur mox post primum numerum ventura esse initia postremi regni impii Mahometici & hypocritici. Nam Mahometus regnare coepit post Christum natum, anno sexcentesimo & tricesimo. Iam si à Danielis aetate computes tempus, exactus est primus numerus. Eadem fere initia fuerunt errorum ecclesiasticorum. Tunc enim paulatim superstitiones missae, invocationes mortuorum & vota creverunt. Sequens igitur numerus, est curriculum regni impii, usque ad finem mundi.

Nihil adfirmo in hac collatione, sed coniecturas recito. Quanquam enim commonefieri nos deus hac praedictione voluit, tamen non vult articulos temporum constitui. Simus igitur contenti quadam communi admonitione, & deum oremus, ut regat ac servet ecclesiam suam, & quàm primum abrumpat hunc naturae humanae cursum, pellat tenebras huius vitae, compescat impios omnes, furentes contemptu dei, & ostendat se ecclesiae suae coràm, ut deinceps aeternis laudibus celebretur.

Recensui consolationes, quas angelus hoc loco tradidit, quas quidem & Christus in concionibus suis postremis repetivit. Sed addatur doctrina seu praeceptum hoc, videlicet, Cum in hac senecta mundi, multa & fuerint & sint futura doctrinae deliria, simus vigilantes & constantes in retinenda puritate Evangelii. Una est doctrina prima omnium, tradita per prophetas, Christum & apostolos, in qua deus aeternus patefecit voluntatem suam. Et qui sine ea doctrina deum quaerunt, aberrant ab eo.

Ad hanc igitur nos vocat, & promittit ibi tantum fore ecclesiam suam, ibi se exauditurum esse, ubi sonat vox Evangelii, utIoan. 15.dicitur, Si manseritis in me, & verba mea manserint in vobis, quicquid volueritis, petetis, & fiet vobis. Hanc igitur doctrinam constanter retineamus, et ea praelucente, deum invocemus, ac petamus, ut nos regat & fervet.

Nunc ad historica redeo. Numerus, etsi arcandam significationem habet, tamen ad Machabaeorum historiam congruit, ut suprà capite 8. belli tempus praedixit, videlicet sex annos, tres menses, & viginti dies. Ita hoc loco numerum illum distinguit, significans diversa belli momenta, & tempus prophanati templi discernens à sequenti tempore, quo prorsus pulsa sunt ex tota Iudaea praesidia Syriaca. Ait ergo, A tempore ablati sacrificii, & collocati idoli, fore dies mille ducentos nonaginta, hoc est, annos tres, menses sex, & dies quindecim.

Hanc primam belli epitasin significat fore difficiliorem piis. Nam hoc triennio pii in maxima calamitate fuerunt. Tenebant Syriaci exercitus arces omnes, trucidabant in oppidis & agris inermem multitudinem, rapiebant ad supplicia senes & mulieres, & aliquandiu nec exercitus ulli nec duces erant, qui hanc saeviciam reprimerent. Postea Matathias & Iudas, aliquantulum reprimere hanc crudelitatem caeperunt. Ibi inclinata fortuna, pulsi Syri, non poterant grassari ut antea. Nam acerrimis praeliis primum post triennium recuperatum est templum. Reliquae res fuerunt faciliores.

Ideo de belli exitu inquit, Beatus qui expectat, & attingit dies mille trecentos trigintaquinque, id est, tres annos & octo menses. Complectemur ergo annos septem et ferè tres menses.

Nam anno 145. post mortem Alexandri, idolum in templo collocatum est, mense Novembri, cum paulò ante Antiochus crudelitatem omnis generis exercere adversus Iudaeos coepisset. Postea anno 151. mense Februario, Nicanor victus est, qui superbissimè minitatus fuerat templo. Haec fuit postrema victoria Iudae Machabaei, quae ita erexit Iudaeam, ut quanquam Syri bellum aliquoties instaurarent, tamen facile repellerentur. Sequenti anno, qui fuit septimus, Ionathas et Simon totam Iudaeas receperunt, & Syriaca praesidia undique ex oppidis Iudaicis expulerunt. Congruit igitur ad belli tempus praedictio numeri, id arbitror prius hîc considerandum esse, postea quaerantur concinnae significationes.

Ultima particula in textu diligenter observanda est, quòd disertė dicit Angelus, haec vaticinia pertinere ad ultimam aetatem mundi. Quiesces & stabis in sorte tua in fine dierum. Haec verba, etsi alii aliò detorserunt, tamen clarè significant Danielis doctrinam complecti omnia mundi tempora, usque ad resurrectionem mortuorum, quia vaticinatur ordine de Monarchiis, & ultimis regnis, & de aerumnis ecclesiae postremi temporis. Ideo inquit, Stabis in sorte tua, docebis & confirmabis ecclesiam ultimo tempore mundi.

Haec particula bonas mentes ad lectionem Danielis hortari debet. Nec nihil est caussae cur deus praemoneri ecclesiam de suia aerumnis velit. Multorum animi moventur multitudine impiorum, moventur & fortuna ac gloria, & disputant tot gentes alias ignaras Evangelii, & quidem florentes imperiis & gloria non negligi à deo, praesertim cum in illis etiam multi sintqui virtute excellunt. Offenduntur animi & aliis multis argumentis.

Ideo praemonet nos deus, ut sciamus ecclesiam fore exiguam, & certò stamamus hanc unam doctrinam, traditam per prophetas, Christum, & apostolos, esse salutarem, nec alibi esse ecclesiam, nisi in eo coetu, qui hanc doctrinam amplectitur. Hac nos consolatione confirmemus, quae diligenter cogitata, pias mentes plurimum ivuat.

τέλος, δόξα θεῶ.

DICTUM ELIAE PROPHEtae, cuius fit mentio pagina 374. extat in libro,סנהדריןCap. 11. quod incipit,כל ישראל ישלהם חלק לעולם הבא׃Et in libro,עבודה זרהCapite primo,quod incipit,לפני אידהן

תנא רבית אליהוששת אלפים שנה הוי עלמאשני אלפים תוהושני אלפים תורהשני אלפים ימות המשיח

TRADITIO DOMUS ELIAE.

Sex millia annorum, mundus.Duo millia, Inane.Duo millia, Lex.Duo millia, dies Messiae.

Isti sunt sex dies hebdomadae coram Deo, iuxta illudPsalm. 90.Et2. Pet. 3.Mille anni in oculis tuis sicut dies unus.

Septimus dies Sabbatum aeternum est, De quoPsalm. 95.&Hebr. 4.Huc pertinet etiam illud Christi, Pater meus adhuc operatur etc.

Adiecta autem est in libris Thalmudicis huic Eliae dicto sententia talis,ובעונותינושרבו יצאו מה שיצאוHoc est,Sed propter peccata nostra, quae multa sunt, ex his annis desunt, qui desunt. Quae sententia etsi nihil incommoditatis habet apud nos, cum interpretamur eam per id quòd Christus dixit, abbreviandos esse dies illos propter electos, id est, multitudine & copia peccatorum accelerandum esse finem, & eripiendos periculo pios, tamen quia Iudaei usurpant hunc appendicem ad impietatem suam defendendam, quasi propter peccata ipsorum Messiae adventus differatur, ideo monere visum est pios lectores, illam sententiam Heliae non esse, neque discipulorum eius, sed adiectitiam & commentum alicuius Rabini infatuantis se et suos, cuiusmodi aliae multae reperiuntur. Id quod & ante nos Porchetus annotavit, ostendens aliter alio loco hanc appendicem inveniri, quae tamen nunc depravatis impressis codicibus ubique similiter legitur.

Qui miraris opes fulvum miraris & aurum,Ditia Pactoli munera sive Tagi,Et gelidas scrutaris aquas scrutaris arenasEt puteos, montes, stagna profunda, mare,Quique peregrinos miraris Seras & IndosEt pelagi merces per vada coeca vehis,Cur non aeternos potius miraris honoresExpetis & superi ditia dona poli?Cur non caelestes studiosa mente libellosPerlegis, & gemmas colligis inde bonas?Ultima crede mihi, ruituri tempora mundiPrae foribus iam sunt in foribusque tuis.Haec possent neque commodius neque doctius usquamDici, quàm sunt hoc commemorata libro,Inclyta quem scripsit Danielis musa prophetae,Inque tua vult te semper habere manu.Vaticinaturis, imperium sit quale futurumChristi? & an humanis viribus illud eat?Temporis extremi quàm sit status ille futurusTurbidus, & coetus qualis imago pii?Et quàm regnorum tristes miserasque ruinasUltimus hic secum temporis ordo trahet?

IN DANIELEM A PHILIPPO MELANTHONE ILLUSTRATUM, ELEGIA GASPARIS BRUSCHII, POETAE LAUREATI AD LECTOREM.

Haec et plura Melanthonis inclytamusa recensetAtque docet, quo sint omnia facta modo?Explicat historias variarum gentium, & additQuae semper pravos poena sequuta fuit?Quae deus integris dederit quae praemia iustisQualis post istam, vita futura piis?Quis salvator? & è tot nos qui liberet undisPossit & irato conciliare deo?Has debebamus gemmas haec aurea filaTotius mundi praeposuisse bonis.Et lapis ille mihi, ferrum aut Marpaesia rupesDurior & quovis robore truncus erit,Durior & saxis, brutis & inertior ipsisCui liber aureus hic displicuisse potest.Qui tibi nunc lector studiose recentior exitExpressus gnavae sedulitate manus.De manibus quem non citius deponere debesIn casses quàm te mors trahat atra suos.Tum memori menti quoque semper inhaereat illeCum res tot doceat contineatque bonas.Tu quoque qui nobis das talia scripta MelanthonIllustrasque patris dogmata vera dei,Instituisque rudes populos mundumque cadentem,Incolumis Pylios vive valeque dies.Te semper collaudabunt occasus & ortusQuin & Olympiaci concio tota chori.

Tempora si qua etiam sunt, post haec nostra, futuraHaec quoque sunt meritis grata futura tuis.Octava Aprilis faciebat.
